---
description: Execute an approved plan note from `Plans/` — for each item, isolate in a worktree, spawn an agent, push, open a PR, monitor CI, park until a batched merge gate at the end of each dependency level. Status tracked in-band on the plan note. On board-enabled projects (stageFind), mirrors each item onto the GitHub Projects board: creates a tracking issue if missing, claims it In Progress while working (claim-first), moves it to Done on merge, and closes the parent epic when its last sub-issue closes.
argument-hint: <vault-path-to-plan-note> [--unattended] [--no-workflow] [--dual-build <tier>=<candidate>]
---

You are running the **build** command. Goal: drive every item in a `Plans/<date> <project> - <title>.md` plan note from `[ ]` to `[x] merged in #N` via one isolated worktree per item. Items inside a dependency level run unattended through agent → push → PR → CI-watch; on the `--no-workflow` conversational path a clean-disjoint item may merge at its own green (Step 3h.5, `BUILD_MERGE_AS_YOU_GO`), and everything else — including *every* item on the default Workflow path, where 3h.5 does not run — parks `[m]` (merge-pending) for the single batched merge gate that fires at the end of each level before the next level starts. This is the **execution-lifecycle** stage of the pipeline (`/triage` → `/assess --epic N` → `/build`); it owns the epic's runtime and **death** (close on last child), per [[Decisions/foundation - Triage stage and the logical-technical pipeline split]].

## Inputs

- `$1` — vault path to a plan note (e.g. `Plans/2026-05-16 stagefind - sweep follow-up.md`). Must conform to the plan-note schema (`~/.claude/plan-schema.md`) and have frontmatter `status: approved`.
- `--unattended` (optional) — auto-start mode, set when launched by the `assess` approval poll (the user approved and walked away). Skips the Step 1.5 "work all / subset / cancel" prompt and defaults to **work all pending items** (items the user pre-marked `[-]` in the doc stay skipped), and selects the Step 1.7 pipeline-overlap **freeze** default (recorded, never silent). It does **not** bypass any downstream human gate — see the operating principle below.
- `--dry-run` (optional) — rehearsal mode: validate + parse, then print the full execution plan (issues that would be created, the level DAG, spike items, merge-gate count) with **zero mutation** — no status flip, no issue/epic creation, no claims, no worktrees. See Step 1.6.
- **The per-level Workflow execution path is the DEFAULT** (`claude/workflows/build-level.mjs`, foundation #422 — flipped ON by default in temperloop#998). With no flag, Step 3's within-level loop runs through the Workflow; the orchestrator's context then stays pinned to one small `{parked, escalations}` object per dependency level regardless of how many items the level holds (the bounded-context win the Workflow re-homing buys, per [[Decisions/foundation - build workflow-machinery invocation seam]]), and the path's batched machinery executors (`prelude` / `pr-batch` / `ci-batch`, temperloop#942) are what the default now actually spends. This remains a **dual path, not a replacement** — see `--no-workflow`.
- `--workflow` (optional) — **accepted as a NO-OP, retained for compatibility.** It used to *select* the Workflow path; that path is now the default (above), so passing this flag asks for exactly what `/build` already does and changes nothing. It MUST stay accepted — an existing invocation, wrapper, or muscle-memory command line that passes it explicitly must not break. Use `--no-workflow` for the opt-out.
- `--no-workflow` (optional) — **opts OUT** of the default Workflow path, selecting the **conversational two-sweep loop** (the two-sweep / `parallel()` fanout of Step 3, driven inline by this session) for Step 3's within-level loop instead. Both paths call the **same deterministic machinery scripts** (`worktree.sh` / `pr.sh` / `ci-poll.sh` / `quality-gates.sh` / the board `claim.sh`) for every mechanical step, so they cannot diverge on *mechanics* — only on *orchestration* (who drives the 3a–3h loop: the conversational session, or the bounded Workflow process). The orchestrator still owns Step 4 (merge gate), ALL plan-note writeback, and escalation resolution under both paths — see the **Step 3 dual-path** note and the **orchestrator↔workflow boundary** below. **What `--no-workflow` buys back — speculative next-level execution.** Speculative cross-level overlap is a documented **conversational-path-only NON-GOAL** under the Workflow path in v1 (the per-level Workflow quantizes the level boundary), so making that path the default **DISABLES speculative overlap by default**: `--no-workflow` is the only way to get speculative next-level overlap back. See the **Speculative next-level execution** section's NON-GOAL block below.
- `--dual-build <tier>=<candidate>` (optional) — **per-invocation** opt-in to the new-work dual-build harness (epic temperloop#2065): every plan item whose `model:` stamp equals `<tier>` is built twice inside its level — once on that tier's current **baseline** model, once on the `<candidate>` under test — and the level's own pick decides which arm's work ships. **The flag is the only thing that arms it.** No setting, env var, plan-note field or prior run turns dual-build on, so a `/build` invocation without this flag is the byte-identical single-arm build it was before the harness existed. `<tier>` is required; the `=<candidate>` half may be omitted, in which case `DUAL_BUILD_CANDIDATE_MODEL` supplies the candidate, and the baseline arm always comes from `DUAL_BUILD_BASELINE_MODEL` — both **named here and never valued**, with `workflows/scripts/build/build.config.sh` (sourced at Step 0 item 6) their single home. **Those two are literal defaults, never a `$HOME`-inherited or inherit-the-invoking-session value:** each arm's model has to stay fixed and disclosed for the whole level (it is what the `Model-comparison-arms:` PR trailer publishes), so neither arm may resolve to "whatever model this session happens to be running" or to one read out of the operator's `$HOME` config. Step 1.9 owns the pre-flight, the consent, the `dualBuild` hand-off and the partial-dual-build refusal.

## Operating principles

- **Orchestrator owns the branch, push, PR, and merge.** Workers only edit + commit (in their own worktree); they never push. <!-- cite: B.1 incident:F#17 -->
- **Worker write-isolation is orchestrator-enforced, not model-discretion.** Leaving isolation to the model (the harness runs an `Agent { subagent_type: "claude" }` in the **parent cwd by default**) is **nondeterministic and has failed in practice** — workers stacked commits on one branch and leaked uncommitted edits into the parent tree. So the orchestrator **pre-creates a per-item worktree at a deterministic path** (`workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh create <repo-root> <item.slug>` — path `<repo>.wt/<item.slug>`, branch `build/<item.slug>`, based on `origin/<default>`) and hands the worker its **explicit absolute worktree-root prefix** — the worker creates nothing and owns nothing about isolation (**Option A**). A **PreToolUse guard hook** (`claude/hooks/build-worktree-guard.sh`, matcher `Bash|Edit|Write|MultiEdit`, self-armed by the per-worktree `.build-guard` marker that `worktree.sh create` drops in the worktree root) then **structurally rejects** any file-tool write — or any destructive **Bash** verb (`rm`/`rmdir`/`mv`/`shred`/`truncate`/`dd of=`) — that resolves outside that root (**Option B**), so a stray parent-root path can no longer leak and worker Bash can no longer `rm -rf` its way out of the jail (F#932). The orchestrator stays in the **parent checkout** and owns the branch via **push-by-SHA** from the pre-created worktree (`git push origin "$(git -C <worktreePath> rev-parse HEAD)":refs/heads/<item.branch>` — the shared object store makes the worker's commit reachable). No `Agent { isolation: "worktree" }`. See Step 3 for the full flow and re-spawn handling. Failure history (#17 stacked commits, #10 parent-leak), the marker-vs-env-var arming (#171), the two registration surfaces (#72), and Options C (per-worker parent-clean assertion) / D (unimplemented upstream harness write-jail FR): [[Decisions/foundation - build worker write-isolation (worktree failure history + Option A-D)]]. <!-- cite: B.2 incident:F#17 -->
- **Classifier-denial recovery is parent-side, never a retry (unattended runs).** When the auto-mode permission classifier denies a **worker or build-machinery git operation** on an `--unattended`/pipeline run — a `pr.sh` push/rebase, a force-push, or a merge/close of a PR the agent didn't author — do **NOT** retry it or force past the denial: a retry burns cycles and risks denial escalation (this recurred across the Epic C–F unattended runs). The orchestrator instead **completes the step itself, parent-side, from the worker's journal verdicts** — inspect the worktree state directly, **push by SHA** from the pre-created worktree (as 3f already does), **open the PR itself**, and **comment on (never close)** another agent's PR. The load-bearing de-obfuscation that keeps the classifier from denying the machinery steps in the *first* place is the pre-resolved **`machineryBinDir`** hand-off (Step 3 `machineryBinDir` bullet — passing an absolute machinery path instead of a nested `$(dirname "$(readlink -f …)")` command-substitution the classifier read as an obfuscated-command bypass, temperloop#72); this recovery playbook is the fallback for the residual denials de-obfuscation doesn't pre-empt. The root-cause defect for over-broad auto-mode denials is tracked separately (foundation#826). See [[Patterns/foundation - Classifier-denial recovery in unattended builds]]. <!-- cite: B.3 incident:K#72 -->
- **Within a level, items run concurrently (independent by construction); merges stay gated.** Honor `depends-on`/`after` edges strictly for *level assignment* (see Step 1.4), but within a level run items concurrently: by default the Workflow path's own `parallel()` fanout (Step 3 dual-path), or — under `--no-workflow` — the conversational two-sweep / `parallel()` fanout of Step 3 (gated by a width setting; `--serial` forces sequential). The merge gate (Step 4) remains the single human checkpoint.
- **Merge gate is batched per dependency level for the set that needs a human**, not per item — with one scoped exception. All items in a level run unattended through CI-green, then a single gate at the end of the level approves whatever is still parked. The exception is the **as-you-go tier (Step 3h.5, `BUILD_MERGE_AS_YOU_GO`)** — **conversational path (`--no-workflow`) only** (temperloop#1452; see 3h.5's own SCOPE paragraph): there, an item the *existing* Step-4a regime partition already puts in the **clean-disjoint** tier may take its consent and merge at its own green, instead of waiting for the slowest sibling and then hitting the merge queue in one burst with it. On the **default Workflow path the exception does not apply at all** — the workflow neither merges nor writes the plan note and returns only at the level boundary, so every item batches to the Step-4 gate, a documented accepted trade-off rather than an oversight. Nothing about the risky path moves — a risky / structurally-overlapping set is still batched to the level boundary and still hard-blocks modally there — and the level boundary remains a **dependency barrier for starting the next level** regardless.
- **Status sentinels go in-band on the plan note** so a future session can resume:
  - `[ ]` untouched
  - `[~]` in-progress (worker active, or PR opened but CI not green)
  - `[m]` merge-pending (PR open, CI green or no CI configured, parked for the level's batch gate — **awaiting** consent)
  - `[>]` merging as-you-go (Step 3h.5, **conversational path only**: consent already recorded and the merge issued/enqueued, awaiting confirmed `MERGED` — **not** `[m]`, which is the un-consented parked state; a default Workflow-path run never produces it)
  - `[x]` merged
  - `[v]` verdict-captured (`kind: spike` — note written + issue routed; no PR)
  - `[-]` skipped
- **Worker self-verifies against `acceptance:` before returning `done`; the orchestrator then confirms *activation* — that a `class: A` item's built output is wired into the running path, not merely correct (Step 3e.6).**
- **Blocked / failed items stay synchronous.** A worker that returns `blocked` halts the level for an immediate clarification; a worker that returns `failed` surfaces retry/skip/abort. Only the merge gate is batched, because merges are the only step that has no further work to drive.
- **A design fork is a first-class halt that keeps implementation in the worker.** When a worker discovers mid-build that ≥2 viable designs exist and choosing between them changes the item's *contract or approach* (not merely a missing fact it could be told — that's `blocked`), it returns `design-fork` rather than guessing or quietly forcing the run into a hand-driven main-session build. The orchestrator surfaces the fork via `AskUserQuestion`, **captures the verdict durably** (a `notes:` stamp on the plan item, or a `Decisions/` note for an architectural call), and **re-spawns the same worker against the same worktree** with the verdict appended — so the design call is ratified by a human while the *build* stays delegated and the orchestrator stays lean. This is the seam that stops a design-laden epic from silently degrading into a context-blowing manual build across sessions. See [[Mistakes/foundation - build design-fork drops to manual, orchestrator context blowup]]. <!-- cite: B.27 class:context-blowing-manual-build -->
- **Branch names come from the plan's `branch:` field.** Do not derive — the plan is authoritative. The plan's branch values conform to [[Decisions/foundation - Branch naming convention]].
- **`--unattended` controls the *start*, not the *gates*.** When auto-started by the `assess` approval poll, build skips the initial subset prompt (Step 1.5, working all pending items) and selects the Step 1.7 pipeline-overlap **freeze** default (both start-time defaults, both recorded — Step 1.5 to pending-decisions, Step 1.7 to `## Questions` + pending-decisions); it never changes which gate regime Step 4 selects. The downstream gates run in two distinct regimes: <!-- cite: B.4 incident:K#526 -->
  - **Merge gate (Step 4) is regime-selected, not unconditionally modal.** A clean, disjoint, independent set is **timed** — it auto-merges after the `BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW` window **unless an operator objects**, where absence of objection = consent (the walk-away case). A **structurally risky** set (Step 4b) is **modal** — it hard-blocks on an `AskUserQuestion` for explicit approval and is *never* timed-auto-merged.
  - **Blocked / design-fork / failed halts (3d) are always modal** — they issue an `AskUserQuestion` and wait for the user, in every run, with no timed variant.
  - **The keystone-spike review halt (4e-keystone) is never bypassed.** When a `keystone: true` spike captures its verdict this run (temperloop#526), the run **halts before dependent levels build** so the operator reviews the contract-reshaping verdict. It is `ask-now` with **no safe default** and no timed variant — on `--unattended`/`PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` it parks the plan (`status: executing`) and posts a verdict-review decision issue on the epic rather than auto-proceeding. Re-running `/build` after review is the operator's approval; the approval poll authorizes the *start*, never this mid-run gate.
  (The prior "merging into protected `main` is never automated" invariant is **deliberately superseded** by the timed gate — see [[Decisions/foundation - Timed merge-gate auto-merge (supersedes never-auto-merge-main)]].) See also [[Decisions/foundation - Approval-poll handoff for batch workflow]].
- **Non-blocking in-run decisions defer to the plan note's `## Questions` section, not an interrupting prompt.** A **`ask-at-gate`** decision (per `[[Context/foundation - AskUserQuestion severity taxonomy]]`) — a non-blocking choice that arises *during an active run* and carries an obvious **default** (Step 2.5 issue creation, Step 2.6 epic creation, the Step 0.5 reconcile auto-fix offer, a Step 4c per-PR conflict disposition, a Step 4b managed-merge EJECTED disposition) — does **not** issue an interrupting `AskUserQuestion` mid-level. It **appends one entry to the plan note's `## Questions` section** (the `## Questions` schema in `~/.claude/plan-schema.md`: question + bold `default:` + a one-line `auto-proceed:`), then proceeds on that default. The whole batch is surfaced as ONE question at the next **level merge gate** (Step 4), before merge consent is recorded — in the **timed** regime an entry still `[ ]` at window-elapse takes its default (same auto-proceed as the timed gate's absence-of-objection consent); in the **modal** regime the entries are shown alongside the merge `AskUserQuestion`. **An unanswered entry at gate resolution takes its default — never a silent stall** — which is what makes a non-blocking question safe on an unattended run instead of hanging it. This is **disjoint** from the `ask-now` halts (3d blocked/design-fork/failed, 3a claim conflict, the 4b risky-set modal): those have *no* safe default and MUST keep interrupting, every run. See [[Context/foundation - AskUserQuestion severity taxonomy]] and `~/.claude/plan-schema.md` § Orchestrator-written `## Questions` section. <!-- cite: B.5 class:unattended-silent-stall -->
- **Every `ask-now` human-gate ask routes through the `decision_sink_ask(...)` seam.** The orchestrator never calls `AskUserQuestion` directly at a gate; it calls the documented seam **`decision_sink_ask(question, options, severity)`** — *question* = the prompt text to surface, *options* = the offered choices (each a label + its trade-off/consequence, and which is the default/recommended one), *severity* = the taxonomy class (`ask-now` for every gate enumerated below; per [[Context/foundation - AskUserQuestion severity taxonomy]]). The seam has **two wired backends, selected by operator-presence and severity:**
  - **Modal backend (default — operator-present runs):** `AskUserQuestion` with the inputs as given — byte-for-byte the same interactive prompt and result as before. This is the default for every run that does not explicitly set the operator-absent driver flag. An operator-present run is **never** affected by the async backend; its call-sites behave identically to before this backend was added.
  - **Async decision-issue backend (operator-absent + `ask-now` only):** selected when the run carries the **operator-absent driver flag** (a `--unattended` flag or an equivalent `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` env signal set by the `pipeline-tick` cron) **AND** `severity == ask-now`. The backend: (1) posts a comment on the item's GH issue with the question text, the offered options, and the typed-reply grammar (fenced ` ```decision ``` ` block or `/choose <label>` / `/approve` shorthands, per `~/.claude/decision-queue-contract.md` § 3); (2) applies the `decision` label to the issue (`gh issue edit <n> -R "$REPO" --add-label decision`); (3) assigns the issue to the operator (`gh issue edit <n> -R "$REPO" --add-assignee "$ASSIGNEE"`, where `$OPERATOR` = the repo's configured operator handle read from `PIPELINE_OPERATOR` env, default `@example-operator`, and `$ASSIGNEE` is `$OPERATOR` with a leading `@` stripped — `ASSIGNEE="$OPERATOR"; [ "$ASSIGNEE" = "@me" ] || ASSIGNEE="${ASSIGNEE#@}"` — because GitHub's `replaceActorsForAssignable` cannot resolve an `@`-prefixed login like `@example-operator` (foundation #977; the special `@me` token is preserved, gh resolves it to the authenticated user)); (4) parks the plan item (`[?]` in the plan note) and stops processing this item for this tick. **No auto-default is ever taken** — the item stays parked until the operator replies, unassigns, and the drain step picks it up. The `decision` kind label and assignee-baton semantics follow `~/.claude/decision-queue-contract.md` exactly.
  - **Selection rule (load-bearing, non-negotiable):** `if operator_absent AND severity == ask-now → async backend; else → modal backend`. The two batch-severity surfaces (`ask-at-gate` → `## Questions` with gate-elapse default; `ask-at-checkin` → `pending-decisions.md` + `check-in`) are **not routed through this seam** and are **not affected** — severity remains the routing axis, operator-presence is only the within-`ask-now` tie-breaker. An operator-present run behaves **byte-for-byte identically** to before this backend was added. <!-- cite: B.7 incident:F#977 -->
  - **v1 exclusion — `design-fork` on an operator-absent run does NOT use the async backend.** A `design-fork` result holds a live worktree and requires a re-spawn against the same worktree after the operator answers; that continuation cannot survive a cron gap without persisting the worktree indefinitely. So: on an **operator-absent run**, a `design-fork` outcome **parks the item back to Ready** (status → Ready via board adapter), **discards the worktree** (removes the `.build-guard` marker and cleans the worktree path), stamps a `notes:` entry with the fork's `design_fork.decision` and `design_fork.options` for the next claim to see, and **stops** — no decision issue is opened, the fork is not handed to the async backend, and the item will be re-spawned fresh on the next claim. See the 3d bullet below for the concrete operator-absent design-fork path.
  - **Operator-phone reach — every `ask-now` seam entry pings the operator (foundation#863).** A halt that parks the run on a human decision must also *reach* an operator who has walked away: the #804 design-fork halted correctly but had no reach — its live modal ask sat ~4.5h overnight (38% of the run's wall clock) until the operator woke (the Epic-B retro, F#847). So **on seam entry — before surfacing the modal ask or posting the async decision issue** — the orchestrator runs `decision-notify.sh ask-now "<one-line summary>"` (a `worktree.sh` sibling in `workflows/scripts/build/`) and relays its printed line to the operator via the harness **`PushNotification`** tool. The summary names the repo, the gate kind, and the parked item — e.g. `temperloop /build halted — design-fork on <slug> needs your decision` (`decision-notify.sh` truncates to `PushNotification`'s 200-char cap). `PushNotification` pushes to the phone when Remote Control is connected and **self-suppresses (a "not sent" result) when the operator is actively at the terminal**, so the ping is a harmless no-op on an *attended* modal gate and reaches the operator only when they are away — exactly the modal-path gap this closes. A second, OPTIONAL channel — the `BUILD_DECISION_NOTIFY_CMD` setting (default empty; an ntfy/pushover/webhook for phone reach independent of Remote Control) — is fired by `decision-notify.sh` itself with the same summary. **No timed gate and no non-blocking question ever notifies** — the two batch severities never enter this seam (per the selection rule above), so `decision-notify.sh` exits `10`/silent on them and the exclusion is *structural*, not a rule the prose re-asserts at each of the five gate sites. The ping is best-effort and fail-open (a failing scriptable channel never aborts the gate) and is **not consent** — it changes no gate regime; the ask, the default, and the park-and-stop all behave exactly as below. **One carve-out:** the keystone-spike review halt (4e-keystone) is `ask-now` but parks with *no inline ask*, so it never enters this seam — it calls `decision-notify.sh` **directly** at its own halt point (4e-keystone step 3) for the identical reach. So "every ask-now halt pings the operator" holds; "at the seam" holds for every ask-now gate *except* that one, which pings just outside it. <!-- cite: B.8 incident:F#863 -->
  **Load-bearing invariant: the seam carries ONLY the ask.** Every durable side-effect around a gate — the `## Design verdict` / `## User answers` append, the `escalated: true` stamp, the verdict-block writes, the post-announce objection scan, the deferred `## Questions` (4f) drain, the merge incantation — stays **OUTSIDE** the `decision_sink_ask(...)` call and in its **existing order** relative to the call. The seam is a pure ask: inputs in, the operator's choice out (modal) or void (async, parks and stops); it writes nothing and scans nothing. (The one seam-entry effect is the ephemeral operator-phone ping above — stateless, idempotent, carrying no plan state, so it does not affect resume ordering; it is a reach mechanism, not a state write.) The gates that route through it: **3a** claim-conflict, **3d** blocked / failed (and design-fork on operator-present runs only — see v1 exclusion above), **3d-esc** continuation (per escalation kind), **4b** risky-set merge approval + the timed-path objection fallback. (**3e** pre-push review carries *no* human-gate ask — its blocking findings auto-loop to 3c — so there is nothing to route there. **Step 1.5**'s subset prompt is an `ask-at-checkin`-adjacent *start* question with a safe default, **not** an `ask-now` gate, so it keeps its direct `AskUserQuestion` and does **not** route through the seam.) `/assess` Step 6 (approval poll) routes its one ask through the same seam. <!-- cite: B.6 class:resume-order-corruption -->
- **Board integration is claim-first, and only when the project provides it.** When the shared board toolkit is present and this repo is a registered board (the Step 0 probe resolves the adapter `lib/board.sh` plus a board number — see `claude/CLAUDE.kernel.md` § The board glossary), build mirrors the plan onto the board: at start it ensures every worked item has a GH issue (Step 2.5 creates one when `gh_issue:` is missing), **claims** each item In Progress as the *first* per-item action — before the worker investigates, per [[Decisions/stageFind - Task workflow (board + WIP-3 gate)]] — **best-effort** clears the tmux claim marker when the item parks (the board claim is **held until Done** — 3h step 2, K#275 — so the marker clear is optional, never a required park step), and moves the item to **Done** on merge. A failed or contended claim **halts** that item (retry/skip/abort) — build never works an unclaimed or another session's item. All board reads/writes go through the adapter (`board_resolve` / `board_resolve_item` / `board_item_id` / `board_set_status` / `board_capture_item`), never a hardcoded board number or a hand-rolled `gh issue`/`gh api` call against tracker state. **Single-item board ops use `board_resolve_item "$BOARD" <issue#>`, not `board_resolve`** — it resolves just the one issue's item with a targeted read instead of pulling the whole-board list, which drained the shared API budget in a per-item burst (GH #53). Reserve the full `board_resolve` for whole-board scans (the Step 0.5 reconcile). Repos with no adapter, or that aren't a registered board, run exactly as before; every claim/release/issue-creation step is skipped. See [[Decisions/foundation - build board integration]]. <!-- cite: B.10 incident:F#53 -->
- **External gates lift by predicate, never by issue-closed-state inference.** An item gated on work *outside this plan* (a cross-plan leg, an unplanned upstream issue) carries its gate as prose in `notes:` ("don't start until #N lands") plus a machine-checkable `gate_check:` predicate on the **consumable, not the tracker** (`/assess` emits both; plan-schema rule 11 requires the predicate whenever the prose gate is present). **Any gate-lift or re-open decision — Step 3a's claim gate (3a-0) and the Step 0.5 reconcile re-open path — MUST run the `gate_check:` predicate and gate on its result; it must NEVER declare a gate lifted because the gated-on issue is closed.** *"Issue closed" ≠ "dependency consumable exists"* — an issue can close one half of its work while deferring the half this item needs (the ELT #492 false gate-lift: *"#380 CLOSED → roster gate lifted"* was wrong because #380 deferred product wiring; `configs/artists.toml` still listed 3 of 40 artists — costing the run's most expensive replan). Run the predicate against the consumable; infer nothing from the tracker. See 3a-0 and `~/.claude/plan-schema.md` § Optional `gate_check:` field. <!-- cite: B.9 incident:ELT#492 -->
- **5-hour quota gate — pause at the level boundary, auto-resume after reset.** After each level's merge gate, before advancing (Step 4e-quota), the run checks the remaining 5-hour usage quota via `quota-gate.sh` (which reads the snapshot `status-line.sh` persists to `~/.claude/rate-limits.json`). If the remaining quota is below `BUILD_QUOTA_PAUSE_PCT` (set in `workflows/scripts/build/build.config.sh`), the run **pauses at that clean boundary and auto-resumes after the window resets** — a background `sleep <wait_secs>` (the duration `quota-gate.sh` emits, = `reset_ts − now + buffer`), then re-check and continue — rather than getting cut off mid-merge in the next level. The signal **fails open**: a missing/stale/unreadable snapshot never pauses a run. This is an `ask-now`-adjacent *self-throttle*, not a human gate — it interrupts nothing and asks nothing; it only defers the *next level's* work to after reset.
- **Epic lifecycle (board ON + plan has `epic:`): build owns runtime + death, not birth.** On the triaged path the epic already exists (`/triage` created it, `/assess` wrote `epic:` into the plan) and its children are already linked as sub-issues — so Step 2.6 is a **backstop**, creating an epic only for a plan that did *not* come through triage. At runtime build moves the **epic → In Progress** when it claims the *first* child (3a), and **closes the epic → Done** when its **last open sub-issue** closes (4d). Epic close is **data-driven** (open-children == 0), never "this plan finished" — so it is correct for partial-coverage plans and for children closed by triage-cull or by hand. See [[Decisions/foundation - build epic creation at run-start]] (epic creation now demoted to a backstop) and [[Decisions/foundation - Triage stage and the logical-technical pipeline split]]. <!-- cite: B.11 incident:F#130 -->

## Step 0 — Validate

Run in parallel:

1. `git status --porcelain` on the parent checkout, then branch by operator presence. **Attended run:** if dirty, don't blanket-abort — offer the operator **stash → build → restore** (stash the parent's changes, run the build, restore them afterward), so their in-progress work is out of the way but never discarded. **Unattended run:** abort and record the item `failed` — never stash, reset, or otherwise touch the operator's own uncommitted work when no one is watching. Either way the orchestrator does not *reason about* the uncommitted changes; it either sets them aside intact (attended) or refuses to run (unattended). <!-- cite: B.12 class:co-tenant-work-destruction -->
2. `gh auth status` — needed for PR creation and merge.
3. `Read` on `$1`, resolved against the knowledge store root — confirm plan note exists. (`$1` and other document paths cited throughout this file — `Plans/`, the priorities note (`Projects/<project>/Priorities.md`, falling back to the legacy `Priorities/<project>.md`), the pending-decisions surface (`Pipeline/pending decisions.md`, falling back to the legacy `Context/pipeline - pending decisions.md`) — path fallback convention, `claude/commands/check-in.md` — are relative to **the knowledge store root**, resolved per `workflows/scripts/lib/knowledge_store.contract.md`. Reads use the `Read`/`Glob` file tools directly on the resolved path; writes stay on the Obsidian MCP tools — per that contract's Obsidian-mode note.)
4. Read project `CLAUDE.md`; extract `## Branch policy` and `## Subagents` sections verbatim for passing to workers. Also extract the `## PR verification surface` rule — it lives in the global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (source: `claude/CLAUDE.md`), with a project `CLAUDE.md` taking precedence if it defines its own.
5. **Board-integration probe.** Canonical predicate: [[Decisions/foundation - Project capability probes]] — a project is **board-wired** iff the shared board adapter resolves **and** the repo maps to a registered board number. The operational resolution mechanics follow; every board step goes through the adapter, never a hardcoded project number or a hand-resolved field/option id.
   - Set `BOARD_LIB` = the first of `scripts/lib/board.sh` (a consuming repo like stageFind, which vendors the toolkit) or `workflows/scripts/board/lib/board.sh` (foundation, the toolkit's home) that exists. Set the claim/release entrypoints `CLAIM`/`RELEASE` = the `claim.sh`/`release.sh` next to `BOARD_LIB` (i.e. `$(dirname "$BOARD_LIB")/../claim.sh`), or the `claim`/`release` commands on `PATH` (put there by `make install-board` in the foundation fleet — a kernel checkout has no such target and resolves the scripts next to `BOARD_LIB` as above).
   - **Adapter found** → resolve the board number from the adapter's own registry, so this works on any registered board with no hardcode:
     `source "$BOARD_LIB"; repo="$(gh repo view --json nameWithOwner -q .nameWithOwner)"; BOARD=""; for b in $(board_registered_boards); do [ "$(board_repo "$b")" = "$repo" ] && BOARD="$b"; done`
     (the candidate list is the adapter's own `board_registered_boards` — the single source of truth for the registered set: stageFind=3, foundation=4, ssmobile=5, subsetwiki=6, temperloop kernel=7 (issues-only), plus any `boards.conf`-registered board. Onboarding a board needs no edit here — it flows from `board_repo()`'s map. Mirrors `/triage` Step 3's identical reverse-lookup. Iterating a hardcoded `3 4 5 6` literal here is exactly what dropped board 7 and left `/build` board-OFF on the kernel's own tracker — temperloop#352.) <!-- cite: B.13 incident:K#352 -->
   - **`BOARD` resolved** → board integration is **ON**. **No `project` gh scope is needed — never check for it.** Every board is issues-only (`ISSUES-ONLY-BACKEND.md`; the Projects-v2/GraphQL arm was removed by ADR 0004 / epic temperloop#524, so there is no second backend left to branch on), which drives Status / claim-stamp / Done / mirror entirely through **plain-REST label writes + issue-close + the sub-issues API** — all covered by the default `repo` scope, so a token carrying only `repo` runs every board step (temperloop#391). Record `BOARD_LIB`, `BOARD`, `CLAIM`, `RELEASE`; **`source "$BOARD_LIB"` at the top of every board bash block** to get `board_resolve` / `board_resolve_item` / `board_item_id` / `board_set_status` / `board_capture_item` / `board_stamp` / `board_backend` and the `BOARD_OPT_*` constants. Pass `--board "$BOARD"` to `CLAIM` so the claim targets the right board on any repo (it defaults to 3). **Plan home-repo legibility check (no-op absent `epic:`).** `<repo-root>` (3b) defaults to **the plan's home repo**, not necessarily this launch checkout — so when the plan frontmatter (item 3) carries `epic:`, resolve its actual home repo by probing each registered board for it: `for b in $(board_registered_boards); do board_resolve_item "$b" "$epic" >/dev/null 2>&1 && { homeRepo="$(board_repo "$b")"; break; }; done`. If `homeRepo` resolves and differs from this checkout's own `$repo` (above), print ONCE at run start: `NOTE: plan's home repo ($homeRepo) differs from this checkout ($repo) — <repo-root> (3b) defaults to this checkout unless items carry repo:.` Informational only, never a hard block — cross-checkout invocation is legitimate and the `repo:` per-item honor point (3b) already handles it when set. <!-- cite: B.14 incident:K#391 -->
   - **No adapter, or no registered board matches this repo** → board integration is **OFF**: **board integration unavailable — claim/mirror steps skipped** (2.5, 2.6, the 3a claim, the 3h release, the 4d Done move + epic-close). The run behaves exactly as the pre-board version.
6. **Source the batch-pipeline config + resolve the quota gate (best-effort).** `source workflows/scripts/build/build.config.sh` (bare repo-relative, the same convention the machinery scripts use — `worktree.sh`/`pr.sh` etc.) — the worked example of the kernel's Step-0 config-sourcing convention (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` § Named-setting convention). This pulls the central `BUILD_*` defaults into scope — the merge-gate window, the queue timeout, the as-you-go merge switch (Step 3h.5), and the **5-hour quota-gate** settings (Step 4e-quota) — with any pre-set env value still overriding. It also resolves **`BUILD_MACHINERY_SOLO_MODEL`** / **`BUILD_MACHINERY_BATCH_MODEL`** (the Workflow path's per-machinery-executor model tiers, one per executor kind — a paired setting, not a general-case-plus-carve-out; empty = unchanged) for hand-off as `input.machinerySoloModel` / `input.machineryBatchModel` in Step 3's args (below), and **`BUILD_GATE_SLICE_SECS`** (§3e.5's per-SLICE acceptance-gate budget) for hand-off as `input.gateSliceSecs` and **`BUILD_REVIEW_BLOCKING_MAX_ROUNDS`** (§3e's review-blocking convergence bound — temperloop#1970) for hand-off as `input.reviewBlockingMaxRounds`, both on the same seam, plus **`BUILD_MACHINERY_STEP_CEILING_SECS`** / **`BUILD_MACHINERY_STEP_SLOW_SECS`** (the machinery-step wall-clock liveness bound and its progress-notice threshold — temperloop#1071) for hand-off as `input.machineryStepCeilingSecs` / `input.machineryStepSlowSecs`, and **`BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_CEILING_SECS`** / **`BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_SLOW_SECS`** (the §3e review-agent wall-clock liveness bound and its progress-notice threshold — temperloop#2003) for hand-off as `input.reviewAgentCeilingSecs` / `input.reviewAgentSlowSecs`, and **`BUILD_WORKER_SUMMARY_MAX_WORDS`** / **`BUILD_WORKER_EVIDENCE_MAX_WORDS`** (§3c's worker return-value word bounds — temperloop#1080) for hand-off as `input.workerSummaryMaxWords` / `input.workerEvidenceMaxWords`; belt-and-suspenders forms `${BUILD_MACHINERY_STEP_CEILING_SECS:-900}` / `${BUILD_MACHINERY_STEP_SLOW_SECS:-300}` / `${BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_CEILING_SECS:-1200}` / `${BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_SLOW_SECS:-300}` on a non-vendoring checkout — the Workflow runtime has no shell of its own to source this file, so this Step-0 resolution is the only place these nine settings are read (`build-level.mjs` keeps its own in-file defaults, so an omitted key still yields a bounded worker prompt). Then resolve the gate script and **guard its existence**: `quotaGate="workflows/scripts/build/quota-gate.sh"; [ -x "$quotaGate" ] || quotaGate=""`. If the config/script isn't found (a consuming repo that doesn't vendor the toolkit), proceed: the prose keeps its inline `${VAR:-default}` fallbacks, and an empty `$quotaGate` makes Step 4e-quota a no-op (proceed) — the **optional** quota gate must never be the thing that breaks or stalls a run. This same source is also what pulls the `DUAL_BUILD_*` defaults into scope for **Step 1.9** — the two arm-model settings the `--dual-build` flag falls back to, and the floors/ceilings `dual-build-preflight.sh` reads when it re-sources this file itself; on a run with no `--dual-build` flag nothing reads them and this sentence is inert. <!-- setting-prose:allow — this line names BUILD_REVIEW_BLOCKING_MAX_ROUNDS (default 3) alongside bare section references (§3e, 3c, tier-3, the list number) that check-setting-prose.sh's int heuristic reads as a restatement of that default. The value is stated nowhere here: build.config.sh remains its single home. -->
7. **Build-machinery staleness check (read-only, no-op when not a symlink).** A consuming repo's `workflows/` directory is often a dev-local symlink into a separate foundation checkout — whichever branch that checkout is on becomes the live build-machinery for this run. If the machinery is stale (foundation on a feature branch or behind `origin/main`), every machinery script (`worktree.sh`, `pr.sh`, `ci-poll.sh`, etc.) may be missing merged fixes, with no visible signal. This check makes staleness loud. **It is strictly read-only and must never mutate, fetch into, or check out the foundation checkout.** <!-- cite: B.15 class:foreign-checkout-mutation -->

   ```bash
   # Resolve the symlink target, then walk up to find the foundation repo root.
   machineryLink="workflows"
   if [ -L "$machineryLink" ]; then
     machineryTarget="$(readlink -f "$machineryLink")"   # absolute, canonical
     # Walk up from the symlink target to find a git root that is NOT the current repo.
     foundationRoot=""
     probe="$machineryTarget"
     currentRoot="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || true)"
     while [ "$probe" != "/" ]; do
       if [ -d "$probe/.git" ] && [ "$probe" != "$currentRoot" ]; then
         foundationRoot="$probe"
         break
       fi
       probe="$(dirname "$probe")"
     done
     if [ -n "$foundationRoot" ]; then
       machineryBranch="$(git -C "$foundationRoot" symbolic-ref --short HEAD 2>/dev/null || echo "(detached)")"
       # Count commits behind without fetching — compare against the already-known remote ref.
       machineryBehind="$(git -C "$foundationRoot" rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main 2>/dev/null || echo "unknown")"
       if [ "$machineryBranch" != "main" ] || [ "$machineryBehind" != "0" ]; then
         echo "WARNING: build-machinery symlink (workflows/ -> $foundationRoot) is STALE"
         echo "  branch:        $machineryBranch  (expected: main)"
         echo "  commits behind origin/main: $machineryBehind"
         echo "  Machinery scripts may be missing merged fixes. Switch foundation to main and pull before building."
       fi
     fi
   fi
   # If workflows/ is not a symlink, or the symlink target is within the current repo,
   # the check is a no-op — the machinery is the repo itself (e.g. running inside foundation).
   ```

   When no warning fires, proceed silently. This check never aborts the run — it is advisory only.

**Merge incantation (canonical).** `gh pr merge <pr> --merge --delete-branch` — `--merge` preserves the repo's `--no-ff` history convention; `--delete-branch` cleans the remote. This is the single source of truth for the merge strategy *on the MANAGED backend* (the 4a `gate.sh backend` verdict): `gate.sh managed-merge` runs exactly this incantation internally — **not** `--auto` — after establishing mergeability itself (its `--strict` arm folds an `update-branch` + SHA-pinned CI re-poll in front of it; `--non-strict` merges directly). The batch merge gate (Step 4b, "Merge — MANAGED backend") references it. <!-- cite: B.16 incident:F#83 -->

A repo with GitHub's **native merge queue** (the 4a `gate.sh backend` probe returning `NATIVE`) uses a **different, smaller** incantation: **`gh pr merge <pr> --auto`** — no `--queue`, no `--merge`, no `--delete-branch`. Rationale (verified on **gh 2.93.0**): `--queue` **does not exist** as a flag (it errors `unknown flag`; the gh 2.93 `pr merge` flags are `--auto`/`--disable-auto`/`--admin`/`--merge`/`--rebase`/`--squash`/`--delete-branch`), and on a queue-required branch the **queue owns the merge strategy** (the ruleset's `merge_method`, so `--merge` is ignored) and the **branch lifecycle** (so `--delete-branch` is rejected). `--auto` arms auto-merge, which enqueues the PR once its required checks pass — the build case, where a parked PR may not yet be green. So the NATIVE path changes *both* when GitHub lands it *and* drops the strategy flags (the queue supplies them).

If any check fails, surface in one line and stop.

## Step 0a — Plan-approval drain (both run modes; the attended arm reports, it does not launch)

**Runs on every tick that is deciding what to work** — i.e. any run where **no plan-note path `$1` was given**. Skip it entirely when `$1` is already set: that run is resuming a named plan, not choosing one. It has **two arms, selected by operator presence on exactly the predicate the `decision_sink_ask` seam already uses** (§ Operating principles — a `--unattended` flag or `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` ⇒ operator-absent; anything else ⇒ attended). The **operator-absent arm** is the cron-tick continuation of the `assess` Step 6 async handoff and drives the approved plan into `/build --unattended`. The **attended-tick drain** applies the same answer, stops at `status: approved`, and reports the plan — it **never** invokes `/build`. Both arms share the procedure below and diverge only at step 4.

**Why both arms exist.** `assess` Step 6's `ScheduleWakeup` poll runs inside a single live session and cannot span cron-tick gaps, so on an operator-absent run the async backend posts the approval offer on the epic issue and stops (no `ScheduleWakeup` is armed); this step is what picks it up at the next tick. But gating the drain on *that run mode alone* strands the answer on any board no funnel ticks — the operator's `approve` lands on the issue, nothing ever reads it, and the plan sits `draft` indefinitely (foundation#1496: six days, with no surface showing it was stuck). The **attended-tick drain** closes that strand. It deliberately stops short of launching, for blast radius: widening *when* this step fires already changes behavior for every repo that adopts the kernel, and letting an attended tick auto-start a build would widen what that firing can *do* (`docs/principles.md` § 7 Bound the blast radius, § 6 Automate the reversible; human-gate the irreversible) — whereas an operator who is right there can run `/build` themselves the moment the plan reads `approved`. `/check-in` § Stranded plan approvals is the visibility backstop for a strand no drain has reached yet.

**Procedure:**

1. **Query answered plan-approval decision issues.** Run:
   ```sh
   gh issue list -R "$REPO" \
     --label decision \
     --state open \
     --assignee "" \
     --json number,title,body,comments
   ```
   **"Answered" is mechanical, and the `--assignee ""` filter is what makes it so:** the operator signals a reply by **unassigning themselves** (`~/.claude/decision-queue-contract.md` § 3), so a still-assigned issue is **unanswered** and never appears in this list — in either arm. Filter the returned results to those whose body contains the magic marker line `plan-approval-poll: [[Plans/` (the marker the async backend stamps into the issue comment body — see `assess` Step 6, async path: the comment body must include this marker followed by the vault path of the plan note, e.g. `plan-approval-poll: [[Plans/2026-06-20 foundation - my plan]]`). A decision issue without this marker is a different kind of decision (e.g. a blocked/failed item) and is **not** drained here — leave it in the queue for the appropriate drain step.

2. **For each matched issue, apply the contention pre-check.** Re-read the issue's current assignee count (`gh issue view "$N" -R "$REPO" --json assignees --jq '.assignees | length'`). If non-zero, another tick already re-assigned it — skip this tick.

3. **Parse the operator's reply** (the most recent comment on the issue, per `claude/decision-queue-contract.md` § 3):
   - A `decision` block with `chosen: approve` (or `/approve` shorthand) → approved.
   - A `decision` block with `chosen: skip` (or `/choose skip`) → declined; the plan stays draft.
   - A `/hold #N` → defer; leave the issue in the queue.
   - Any other comment or parse miss → follow the contract's parse-miss rule (re-assign to operator with a "couldn't parse" comment; skip this tick).

4. **Apply the answer.** Sub-steps (a)–(c) are identical in both arms; only (d) differs. **(a)** Remove the `decision` label — `gh issue edit "$N" -R "$REPO" --remove-label decision`. **(b)** Extract the vault path from the marker line (`plan-approval-poll: [[Plans/<path>]]` → `Plans/<path>`). **(c)** Set that plan note's frontmatter to `status: approved` with a full-file `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_write` rewrite — never a frontmatter-scalar patch, which reports success while silently dropping the field (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` § Obsidian vault). Then **(d)**, by arm:
   - **Operator-absent, approved:** post the confirmation comment "Decision applied: plan approved. Invoking `/build --unattended`." and invoke `/build Plans/<path> --unattended`. **The `/build` invocation replaces the `ScheduleWakeup` poll's role — it is the continuation of the `assess` Step 6 approval-poll handoff, fully re-homed onto the decision-issue backend.**
   - **Attended-tick drain, approved:** post the confirmation comment "Decision applied: plan approved." and emit **exactly one line** naming the plan — `plan approved: Plans/<path> — run /build to execute it`. **Do NOT invoke `/build`**, and do not offer to: this arm's job is to un-strand the answer, not to start work the operator did not ask this tick to start.
   - **Not approved, either arm** — sub-step (c) is skipped and the plan note stays `status: draft`. *Declined (`skip`):* remove the `decision` label and post "Decision applied: plan not approved — skipping." *Deferred (`/hold #N`):* leave the issue exactly as it is (label present, no assignee) and skip it this tick.

5. **One plan per tick-start drain.** If multiple answered plan-approval issues are found, process them in issue-number order, one per tick. This prevents a single tick from launching unbounded concurrent builds (the pipeline's `PIPELINE_DRIVE_CONCURRENCY` governor bounds per-tick drive concurrency, and the per-item claim-first lock governs concurrency once items are claimed). Log which issues were found and which was processed.

## Step 0.5 — Reconcile state (resume only)

Runs only when the plan's frontmatter `status` is `executing` at start (i.e. a resume). Cross-checks the four state stores that can diverge under a crash — plan-note sentinels, git/remote (worktrees, branches, open PRs), the board, and the workflow journal — and surfaces drift **before** parsing levels. **Report-first, fix-on-confirm:** never silently mutate outward state. <!-- cite: B.18 class:silent-outward-mutation -->

**Code cross-check (board ON), mandatory on every resume (temperloop#1910 L6), non-authoritative this level.** Gated exactly like item 3 below: when `$BOARD` is empty (board integration OFF, Step 0 item 5), skip both calls and print `state-graph: skipped — board integration OFF` in the Step 6 line — a boardless resume behaves exactly as it did before this item. Otherwise, run `workflows/scripts/build/state-graph.sh build --board "$BOARD"` then `workflows/scripts/build/state-graph.sh query resume --board "$BOARD"`, redirecting the resume query's stdout to `resume-<run-id>.json` under the `lib/cache.sh` kind=`state-graph` per-board directory (`cache_repo_dir "$BOARD" state-graph`) so its ranked-merge verdict can later be soak-compared against this prose without ever overriding a decision made here; key degradation on CONTENT, not exit code (the queries' own in-band convention) — no valid JSON on the build call's stdout, or no `resume-<run-id>.json` file materialized after the query call, degrades to `state-graph: unavailable (<reason>)` in the Step 6 line and never blocks items 1–4, otherwise carry the build call's per-source tally plus the resume-artifact path into that line.

1. **Worktrees.** `git worktree list` — flag `<repo>.wt/<slug>` worktrees (on `build/<slug>` branches) not owned by an in-flight (`[~]`) item (offer pruning via `workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh prune <repo-root>` — removes only `<repo>.wt/*` worktrees whose branch is merged into `origin/<default>`, marker included, skipping dirty worktrees unless `--force`, one structured outcome per worktree; each path maps back to a plan item by slug).
2. **PRs vs sentinels.** `gh pr list --state open --json number,headRefName` cross-mapped to plan `branch:`/`pr:` values — flag open PRs with no `[m]`/`[>]`/`[~]` item, and `[~]`/`[m]`/`[>]` items whose `pr:` PR is closed or gone. **Speculative-worker sentinel:** a `[~]` item carrying `  - speculative: true` and **no `pr:`** is a *held speculative worker* (worktree built, awaiting the 3f base-currency check), **not** a stuck worker to reset — its worktree holds real build work that a reset would discard. Route it to the **3f step-0.5 base-currency check** (rebase-then-reverify, or discard-and-respawn on conflict), not a re-spawn. Its worktree (flagged in item 1) is therefore *expected*, not orphaned — do not offer to prune it.
3. **Board vs sentinels (board ON).** Read the board via the adapter (`source "$BOARD_LIB"; board_resolve "$BOARD"`, then inspect `BOARD_ITEMS_JSON` — each item's `.status` and `.["host/Session"]`) — flag In-Progress cards for `[x]` items (should be Done) and Done cards for non-terminal items. **Self-claim recovery:** an item In Progress under *this host* but a *prior* `Host/Session`, with no live tmux marker, is a reclaimable dead-session self-claim — re-claim it rather than treating it as a foreign conflict (cf. 3a). **Epic drift:** if the plan has `epic:`, flag an epic that is closed while a non-terminal child remains (re-open it), or open with zero open children (the 4d close was missed — close it on confirm).
4. **Workflow journal vs sentinels (intra-level recovery).** The Workflow runtime journals each `agent()` call as `agent-<id>.jsonl` in the transcript directory; a crash mid-level leaves that journal as the only record of intra-level progress the plan note had not yet been updated to reflect. Consult it after the three stores above, using the following **authority ordering**:

   | Priority | Store | Scope of truth |
   |---|---|---|
   | 1 (highest) | **Plan-note sentinels** (`[ ]`/`[~]`/`[m]`/`[>]`/`[x]`/`[v]`/`[-]` + `pr:`/`pushed_sha:`) | Durable truth **at level boundaries** — written by the orchestrator on each state transition; survives any crash. |
   | 2 | **Workflow journal** (`agent-<id>.jsonl` in the transcript dir) | Truth for **intra-level** progress of the **currently-running level only** — recovers facts (e.g. a `pr:` number) the orchestrator had not yet stamped to the plan note before a crash. |
   | 3 (lowest) | **Git + board** | External cross-check (stores 1–3 above). |

   **Invariant — journal NEVER overrides `[x]`/`[-]`:** a human merge (`[x]`) or human skip (`[-]`) sentinel on the plan note outranks any intra-level journal entry. A journal record showing a PR was opened or pushed does NOT let you flip an `[x]` back to `[~]` — that transition is one-way and human-owned. This is the authority ordering's load-bearing guarantee. <!-- cite: B.17 class:resume-reverses-human-decision -->

   **Crash-quantization.** A crash between levels lands in a clean reconcile state: no intra-level journal progress to recover (the prior level's sentinel writes are durable, the new level's journal is empty). A crash mid-level leaves the currently-`[~]` level's journal with per-`agent()` entries the orchestrator can mine. Net effect: recovery either sees nothing intra-level (clean) or sees the structured journal for exactly one active level (recoverable).

   **Concrete reconcile step.** On resume, if the currently-`[~]` level has a workflow journal (the transcript directory is the Workflow runtime's per-run transcript dir, surfaced in the `Workflow` launch result as `Transcript dir: …` and stable across a `resumeFromRunId`; the `agent-<id>.jsonl` files live under it):
   - Read the journal for the `pr-batch:<slug>` executor entries. Since temperloop#942 the 3f steps ride ONE batched executor per item whose result is `{results:[…]}` — one closed-outcome object per step that ran, in `rebase, scan, push, pr-open` order (the entry's prompt carries a `Steps:` manifest) — so the `{outcome:"PR_OPENED", pr_number, url}` object is an **element of that array**, not a standalone entry. Scan each entry's `results` for it.
   - For each such entry whose item **does not yet carry `pr:` in the plan note** — and whose sentinel is still `[~]` (not `[x]`/`[-]`) — **recover the `pr:` pointer**: stamp it via the deterministic machinery writer `workflows/scripts/build/plan.sh writeback <plan> --slug <slug> --sentinel '[~]' --pr <N>` (the same sub-line writer 3f uses — no `patch_vault_file` em-dash-heading misfire on the item's sub-lines). This lets Step 1.4's `[~]`-with-`pr:` path re-attach to the open PR instead of re-spawning a worker.
   - Similarly recover `pushed_sha:` from the `{outcome:"PUSHED", sha}` element of the same `pr-batch` entry (or a standalone `push-retry:<slug>` entry, which stays unbatched) for any `[~]` item that has no `pushed_sha:` yet — add `--pushed-sha <SHA>` to the same `plan.sh writeback` call. This anchors the CI re-poll to the correct SHA.
   - **Do NOT touch any `[x]`/`[-]` item** — the invariant above is absolute; the journal cannot reverse a terminal sentinel.
   - Include the journal recovery (items recovered, pointers stamped) in the Step 6 summary.

5. Emit one reconciliation report; the offer to auto-fix the **safe** divergences (adopt an orphan PR's number, prune a dead worktree, move a stale board card, close an all-children-closed epic) is a **`ask-at-gate`** decision — **append it to the plan note's `## Questions` section** rather than issuing an interrupting `AskUserQuestion`, then proceed on its default. Entry: `` `step: 0.5` — Auto-fix the safe reconcile divergences (<the specific safe fixes>)? **default: don't auto-fix (report only)** ``, with an `auto-proceed:` sub-line (unanswered → leave the divergences as reported; nothing is mutated). The batch surfaces at the next level merge gate (Step 4). Never auto-close issues or rewrite human merge decisions — the conservative default (report only) is what keeps a deferred reconcile from silently mutating outward state on an unattended run. **A gate-lift / re-open decision here is bound by the gate-predicate rule (operating principles, 3a-0):** if reconcile considers re-opening or un-parking an externally-gated item whose `gate_check:` reads **false**, do NOT treat the gated-on issue being closed as evidence the gate lifted — *"issue closed" ≠ "dependency consumable exists"* (the ELT #492 trap). Re-evaluate the predicate; gate on its result, not the tracker's state. Echo the result in the Step 6 summary.

**Also emit a resume-recovery record — mandatory whenever this reconcile pass recovered or flagged anything** (temperloop#1908, the resume-recovery stream — a baseline instrument for the graph-of-record work; a `/build` resume is not a drive and never writes a command-run, so this rides its own stream rather than folding into command-runs, whose disposition-partition invariant has no slot for "a resume found drift"; the call has nothing to fire on, and is rightly skipped, only when items 1–4 above recovered and flagged nothing): `workflows/scripts/emit-resume-recovery.sh --plan <plan-note-stem> --recovered-count <N> [--recovered <kind>:<ref>]... || true` — one `--recovered <kind>:<ref>` per divergence found across items 1–4 (`kind` ∈ `worktree`|`pr`|`claim`|`sentinel-journal`|`board-drift`, one per check above). Telemetry only, same `|| true`-isolated contract as the issue-touch/item-efficiency emits elsewhere in this doc; its presence here is asserted by `workflows/scripts/validate-resume-recovery-emit.sh` (wired into `scripts/quality-gates.sh`).

## Step 1 — Parse and validate the plan note

1. Read `$1` via `Read` on the resolved file path.
2. Enforce the validation rules from `~/.claude/plan-schema.md`:
   - Frontmatter `status` must equal `approved`.
     - If `draft`: tell the user to promote it and stop.
     - If `executing`: ask whether to resume.
     - If `done` / `abandoned`: ask whether to re-open.
   - Every item must have a `slug:` and a non-empty `acceptance:` block.
   - Every item's `slug` is unique within the plan and matches `[a-z0-9-]+` (≤40 chars).
   - Every `depends-on` slug must exist in this plan.
   - Every `after:` slug (logical-ordering edge) must exist in this plan; the union of `depends-on` + `after` edges must be acyclic.
   - No item's `acceptance:` block still contains the placeholder line `- (no acceptance criteria derivable from source — fill in during review)` — a remaining placeholder is a validation FAILURE (fill it in before approving).
   - Every `branch:` must match `<type>/<slug>` where type ∈ {feat, fix, chore, refactor, docs, test} (per [[Decisions/foundation - Branch naming convention]]).
   - **Activation-block consistency (rule 13):** an item carrying an `activation:` block must declare `class:` ∈ `A|B|C`, and a **`class: A` block MUST carry a `proof:` predicate**. A class-A block with no `proof:` is a validation FAILURE — never a runtime fallback: 3e.6 has no non-predicate arm to fall back to (temperloop#1451), so this check is what keeps that gate from silently no-op'ing on a dormant feature. Class B/C need no `proof:` (ledger-discharged, temperloop#317). `workflows/scripts/build/plan.sh validate` enforces this mechanically — prefer running it over eyeballing the note.
   - **External-gate predicate (rule 11):** an item whose `notes:` carry an external/cross-plan gate (a "don't start until #N lands" prose gate — the schema forbids a cross-plan `after:` ref, so the gate can only ride `notes:`) MUST carry a `gate_check:` predicate. A prose-only external gate is a validation FAILURE — it forces a gate-lift to be *inferred* from issue-closed-state (the ELT #492 trap), which 3a-0 forbids. (A `gate_check:` is optional on an item with no external gate.)
   - Every `source:` target must resolve. A **vault wikilink** source (`[[path#heading]]`) is checked via `Read` on the path part, resolved against the knowledge store root (heading anchor isn't strictly verifiable). A **GitHub issue ref** source (`#<n>` or an issue URL — the form `/assess --epic` writes for a board-native sub-issue with no originating doc) is considered **resolved by existence** — skip the vault-path check for it (optionally confirm with `gh issue view <n>`).
3. On any validation failure, list all problems in one block and stop. Do not start work.
4. Build the **dependency-level list** (not a flat sequence):
   - Topologically sort by the **union** of `depends-on` (merge-safety) and `after` (logical-ordering) edges. Each level contains items whose edges are all satisfied by prior levels (level 0 = items with neither `depends-on` nor `after`).
   - **Edge semantics differ:** a `depends-on` edge requires its target be `[x]` **merged** before the dependent's worker starts (it builds on merged code). An `after` edge only requires its target to have reached a **terminal state** — `[x]` merged, `[-]` skipped, or `[v]` verdict-captured — so a spike (`[v]`) satisfies an `after` edge with no merge.
   - Within a level, preserve plan-note source order — defines the per-item processing order inside the level.
   - The level list is the unit of **dependency** ordering: level *k+1* starts only after level *k*'s merge gate. It is the unit of *merge batching* for everything the gate still has to consent to — on the conversational path an as-you-go-eligible item (Step 3h.5) instead takes its consent and lands at its own green, and the rest of level *k* park as `[m]` and pass through the single gate; on the default Workflow path 3h.5 does not run, so the whole level parks `[m]`.
   - Items already `[x]` (merged) **or `[v]` (spike verdict-captured)** are terminal and skipped (resuming a partial run) — their level is not re-processed and their kind-fork does not re-run. This is load-bearing for the keystone-spike gate (temperloop#526): a keystone spike already `[v]` at run start is excluded here, so its level is never re-entered on a resumed RUN 2 and the 4e-keystone halt cannot double-fire.
   - Items `[~]` are surfaced: if the item carries a `pr:` sub-line (3f recorded it), **re-attach to that open PR** (re-watch CI / re-check mergeability) instead of re-spawning a worker. **Else if it carries `speculative: true` and no `pr:`** — it is a *held speculative worker* (its worktree has real build work, holding before the 3f currency check); **do NOT reset/re-spawn** (that discards the build). Resume it at the **3f step-0.5 base-currency check**: `BASE_CURRENT` → promote to push-by-SHA; `BASE_STALE` → rebase-then-reverify (push the rebased SHA, re-run 3e.5, re-watch CI) or discard-and-respawn on conflict. **Else if it carries `escalated: true` and no `pr:`** — it is an *escalated item awaiting continuation* (its worktree is intact, a verdict block was appended in 3d-esc step 3, and it is waiting for a re-spawned worker to drive it to `[m]`); **do NOT reset/re-spawn** (that discards the worktree and the human verdict). Re-enter the **3d-esc continuation**: cross-reference the `## User answers — <slug>` / `## Design verdict — <slug>` blocks in the plan note, then re-invoke `build-level.mjs` with this slug in `onlySlugs` and the `verdicts` map. This case must be distinguished from both the `speculative: true` held-speculative case and the plain `[~]`+no-`pr:`+no-sentinel "stuck worker → ask resume/reset" default. Otherwise (no `pr:`, no `speculative:` sentinel, no `escalated:` sentinel) ask whether to resume or reset.
   - Items `[m]` from a prior session are **revalidated before** rolling into the gate: `gh pr view <pr> --json state,mergeable,mergeStateStatus,statusCheckRollup`. `state != OPEN` → surface (re-open / mark `[-]` / re-run); `statusCheckRollup` not `SUCCESS` (and not `PENDING`) → demote to `[~]` and re-enter 3g — **except an item carrying a `  - no_ci: true` sub-line** (3h stamps this at park time for a `NO_CI`-outcome item, temperloop#605): with zero check-runs ever configured, its `statusCheckRollup` reads neither `SUCCESS` nor `PENDING` **by construction**, which is that item's normal, stable `[m]` state — not a demotion signal. Leave it `[m]` and do **NOT** re-enter 3g (there is nothing for 3g to poll; re-entering would spin until the grace window re-fires `NO_CI` again, for no benefit). Only PRs that re-confirm `OPEN` + green, **or** `OPEN` + the `no_ci: true` sentinel, roll into the gate (`PENDING` = re-poll, not failure). **Also read the plan note's `## Merge gate log` section** (the orchestrator-written consent lines, `~/.claude/plan-schema.md`): a `[m]` item whose PR is covered by a consent line for its level resumes **without re-consent** — route it by the 4b "Merge — MANAGED backend" resume state table (mark-merged / resume the managed loop / fresh gate, incl. after a re-park / escalate) instead of re-entering a fresh gate unconditionally.
   - Items `[>]` from a prior session (an as-you-go merge in flight when the session died, Step 3h.5 — necessarily a prior *conversational-path* run, since the default Workflow path never mints `[>]`; this row still runs on either path, because resume reads the note, not the path that wrote it) are revalidated the same way, but **with consent already recorded**: read the live PR, then route by the *same* 4b resume state table the `[m]` case uses — `MERGED` → run the 4d treatment and flip `[x]`; still `OPEN` and covered by a `## Merge gate log` consent line for its level → **resume the in-flight merge without re-asking** (re-issue the backend's per-PR call; both `--auto` and `managed-merge` are idempotent); `CLOSED` without `mergedAt` → the `ask-now` escalation that row already specifies. A `[>]` item whose PR is **not** covered by a consent line is a corrupted state, not a fast path: demote it to `[m]` and let it take a fresh gate.
5. Show the parsed level list + per-level item ordering.
   - **Flag expensive items in the preview (foundation#1059).** For each item carrying a `cost:` block (`~/.claude/plan-schema.md` § Optional `cost:` block — the expensive-work flag), append a visible cost tag to its preview line: `💰 expensive: <because>` plus `· budget: <N>` when a `budget:` is set (e.g. `💰 expensive: agent-fanout · budget: 500000`). This is the whole point of the field — the operator sees an outsized token/time spend **before** approving the plan, instead of it hiding inside a plain-looking item. Purely a display concern: `cost:` drives no gate or control-flow (it is advisory), so nothing else about Step 1.5 or the run changes.
   - **Unattended start (`--unattended`):** skip the question — proceed as if **Work all N pending items** was chosen (items pre-marked `[-]` in the doc stay skipped). Print the level list for the record and continue to Step 2. **This is an `ask-at-checkin` deferral** ([[Context/foundation - AskUserQuestion severity taxonomy]]): no live operator, a safe default. So don't *silently* auto-proceed — record the auto-taken default to the **pending-decisions surface** (`claude/CLAUDE.md` § Unattended pending-decisions surface) so the next `check-in` reviews it. Append one `### open` entry to the pending-decisions surface (`Pipeline/pending decisions.md` vs the legacy `Context/pipeline - pending decisions.md` — target pinned by the append-target resolution rule of the path fallback convention, `claude/commands/check-in.md` — in the knowledge store) via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_append`:
     ```markdown
     ### <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM> · build Step 1.5 · <host>:<sess8>
     - **Decision:** work-all / subset / cancel for plan `<plan path>`
     - **Default taken:** work all N pending items (N of M)
     - **Disposition:** auto-taken (unattended start; no live operator)
     - **Status:** open
     ```
     (Interactive runs ask the question instead and never write here.)
   - **Interactive (default):** ask via `AskUserQuestion` (**not** routed through `decision_sink_ask(...)` — this is an `ask-at-checkin`-adjacent *start* prompt with a safe default, not an `ask-now` gate, so it is intentionally outside the seam — see § Operating principles):
     - **Work all N pending items** (default)
     - **Pick a subset** (then prompt for slugs)
     - **Cancel**

### Step 1.6 — Dry-run preview (`--dry-run` only)

If `--dry-run` is set, print the full execution plan and **STOP** before any mutation (no Step 2 status flip, no 2.5/2.6 issue/epic creation, no claims, no worktrees):
- the **level DAG** (level *k* → item slugs), each cross-item edge tagged `depends-on` (merge) or `after` (logical);
- per item: `size`, `branch`, `kind` (flag spikes — no PR; flag a `keystone: true` spike as a **review-halt boundary** — the run will stop after its level for operator verdict-review before dependents build, temperloop#526), **flag a `cost:`-bearing item as `💰 expensive: <because>[ · budget: N]`** (foundation#1059 — same visibility as the Step 1.5 approval preview, so a large spend is not hidden on the `--dry-run` inspection path either), and whether `gh_issue:`/`epic:` already exist or would be created;
- counts: issues that *would* be created, board claims, and **merge gates** (= levels containing ≥1 `code` item; a spike-only level needs no merge);
- whether the epic pre-exists (triaged path — `epic:` set) or would be created as a backstop;
- the **pipeline-overlap verdict** (the Step 1.7 predicate, run read-only — print its one-line JSON; no offer, no mutation).

Reuses the Step 1 parse verbatim, so the preview cannot drift from a real run. End with: "Re-run without `--dry-run` to execute."

### Step 1.7 — Pipeline-overlap check (run start; foundation #864)

Before any mutation (Step 2 onward), check whether this run and the autonomous pipeline are about to interfere — the Epic B failure (retro #847): the pipeline merging PRs into `main` while a plan rewrites the pipeline's own machinery cost repeated rebase/re-gate cycles plus a stale-seed design fork (~2.5h). The freeze levers already exist (the schedule note's `enabled: no` is the fail-closed kill switch — every `pipeline-schedule-gate.sh` tick skips); this step's job is to surface the **offer at run start**, not at whatever mid-run item first collides. <!-- cite: B.19 incident:F#864 -->

1. **Run the predicate.** Aggregate the **worked** items' `files:` entries (post-Step-1.5 subset; user-`[-]`-skipped items excluded), then:
   ```bash
   overlapCheck="workflows/scripts/build/pipeline-overlap.sh"
   [ -x "$overlapCheck" ] || overlapCheck=""   # consuming-repo fallback, like the quota gate
   [ -n "$overlapCheck" ] && "$overlapCheck" --board "$BOARD" <file...>
   ```
   Script absent → skip the step silently (a repo that doesn't vendor the toolkit has no pipeline to collide with). **Exit 0 (`no-overlap`)** → proceed silently; note the reason in the Step 6 summary line only when it was `inconclusive` (no `files:` fields). **Exit 10 (`overlap`)** → the offer below. The predicate is **fail-open** by contract — it can decline to fire, never stall the run.
2. **Overlap, operator-present:** one direct `AskUserQuestion` (deliberately **outside** `decision_sink_ask(...)` — a *start* prompt with a safe default, same rationale as Step 1.5): **Freeze the pipeline for this run (recommended)** — flip the kill switch now, un-freeze surfaced at epic close — vs **Proceed without freeze** — accept the interference risk (the matched paths are shown). Record a declined offer durably by appending a `## Pipeline freeze` section to the plan note via `vault_append` (`offer declined — proceeding unfrozen (<ts>, operator choice; matched: <paths>)`), so a later interference post-mortem has the decision on record. (**Not** the `## Run status` section — Step 2 writes-or-resets that moments later, which would silently destroy the record. A declined-offer section records no freeze, so 4d-pipeline's unresolved-freeze condition correctly never fires on it.)
3. **Overlap, operator-absent — split default (never silent, never self-defeating):**
   - **`--unattended` (approval-poll epic start):** default **freeze**. A freeze is bounded and reversible (ticks skip until re-enabled; the un-freeze is owed at epic close and surfaced there), while interference is unbounded (#847). Append a `## Questions` entry (`` `step: 1.7` — Freeze the pipeline for this run (overlap: <matched paths>)? **default: freeze** ``, with an `auto-proceed:` sub-line) **and** one `### open` entry to the pending-decisions surface (`Pipeline/pending decisions.md` vs the legacy `Context/pipeline - pending decisions.md` — target pinned by the append-target resolution rule, `claude/commands/check-in.md`; ask-at-checkin format) so `check-in` reviews the auto-taken freeze. Then perform the freeze (step 4).
   - **`PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` (a pipeline tick driving its own item):** default **proceed without freeze** — **the pipeline never freezes itself** (a routine singleton touching its own scripts would otherwise turn off every subsequent tick). Append the `## Questions` entry recording the overlap and the taken default; no freeze, no pending-decisions entry. The Step 6 `Pipeline:` line derives this case's `overlap acknowledged — ran unfrozen` value from that `step: 1.7` `## Questions` entry (there is no `## Pipeline freeze` section on this arm).
4. **Freeze action** (operator accepted, or the `--unattended` default):
   - Flip the schedule note (`PIPELINE_SCHEDULE_FILE`, default `Context/foundation - pipeline schedule.md`): `enabled: yes` → `enabled: no` inside the fenced ```pipeline-schedule block, via **full-file `vault_write`** (read → modify → write; never `vault_patch` into the fenced block, per the vault safe-targeting contract). The note flip alone is sufficient — the gate is fail-closed, so every tick skips. Do **not** automate a teardown of the deploy host's LaunchAgent/cron wake itself; that belt-and-suspenders step stays a manual operator option.
   - Append a `## Pipeline freeze` section to the plan note via `vault_append` (reliable EOF op): timestamp, who/what decided (operator choice vs unattended default), the lever used (`schedule note enabled: no`), and the standing obligation line `un-freeze owed at epic close (4d-pipeline)`. This section is the durable marker 4d-pipeline keys off.

## Step 1.8 — Resolve the effective engineering principle set (once per run)

Before Step 2 mutates anything, resolve what §3c's `workerPrompt()` and §3e's pre-push reviewer both need: the **effective (kernel ∪ project) engineering principle set**, once per distinct `(repo, project)` pair this run touches. This is **orchestrator** work, not `.mjs` work: the Workflow runtime has no filesystem or vault access (`build-level.mjs`'s own DESIGN NOTE 1 — the same wall `machinerySoloModel`/`gateSliceSecs` hit at Step 0 item 6), so only this live session — with `Read` on the checkout's own files and on the resolved knowledge-store paths — can do the merge; the rendered result is handed to `build-level.mjs` as `principlesSummaries` / `principlesDefaultRepo` on that identical Step-0-hand-off seam (Step 3's `args`, below). **Resolved once per run, point-of-use, never cached across runs or persisted into a future `/build` invocation** — matches `claude/engineering-principles.md`'s "Both-active rule."

1. **Enumerate the distinct `(repo, project)` pairs.** The **default/primary pair**: `repo = ownerRepo` (Step 0's `gh repo view` probe), `project = basename(ownerRepo)` (the segment after the final `/` — the same repo→project derivation `next.md`/`check-in.md` already use for a board-registered repo's `Projects/<project>/Priorities.md`). Then, across **every** item in the **whole plan** (all levels, not only the level about to run — the enumeration is run-scoped), collect each **distinct** `repo:` value that differs from `ownerRepo`; each is an **additional pair**, `project = basename(that repo)`. Most runs have exactly one pair — the default; a cross-repo item (`~/.claude/plan-schema.md` § Optional `repo:` field, the kernel-vs-overlay split) adds one pair per distinct target repo.
2. **Resolve each pair's kernel set and project extension.** For the default pair, `Read` `claude/engineering-principles.md` at this checkout's root; for an additional pair, resolve a local checkout of that `repo:` the same way 3b's `repo:` honor point does and `Read` **its own** copy — **unlike 3b's hard block, a missing foreign checkout/file here degrades rather than halts the run** (principles are advisory — `claude/engineering-principles.md` § Advisory posture, this run's own principle 7 — never worth stalling a build over): reuse the default pair's kernel file content, noting the substitution in that pair's rendered summary (`kernel file unavailable for <repo> — reused <ownerRepo>'s copy`). Parse the `### N. <Title>` headings under "## The seven principles" as the `[kernel]` entries (`<Title>` is the name; the one-line criterion is that heading's own `**Criterion:**` sentence). Then `Read` `Projects/<project>/Priorities.md`, resolved against the knowledge store root, falling back to the legacy `Priorities/<project>.md` (path fallback convention, `claude/commands/check-in.md`), for the project extension.
3. **Merge per `claude/engineering-principles.md`'s own § Merge semantics — the single statement site; implement it here, do not restate or re-derive it.** **Absent** (both paths missing, a read fails, or no `## Principles` section) → kernel set alone, project slot empty, **no** degradation notice (the ordinary, expected case). **Present but empty or malformed** → kernel set alone **plus** a degradation notice (`project § Principles present but empty/malformed — treated as absent`) — same set as absent, but named so the gap stays visible. Otherwise, exactly as that section states: **extend** (default — dedupe a duplicate to the project's phrasing, name a genuine contradiction `kernel principle overridden by project: <name>`), **`mode: replace`** (project's own set wholesale, kernel discarded), or **named exclusions**.
