4. **Render, per pair, computed once and reused by both 3c and 3e (never re-derived at either site):** a compact numbered summary (`<name> — <one-line criterion> [kernel]`/`[project]`), **followed by a trailing provenance line** in the exact form the Step 6 `Principles:` bullet uses (below), so a worker or reviewer sees not just *what* applies but *why* — a `none` opt-out renders as an **empty** numbered list plus that line alone, still non-empty and distinguishable from "not resolved at all" (`build-level.mjs`'s fallback, its own comment). One Step-6 bullet per pair, the default unlabeled, each additional pair named by its `(repo/project)`.
5. **Hand off for Step 3, and let `--no-workflow` reuse it too.** Collect every pair's summary into `principlesSummaries = { "<repo>": "<rendered summary text>" }` (keyed by each pair's `repo`, `ownerRepo` for the default), and keep `ownerRepo` itself as `principlesDefaultRepo`; pass both in Step 3's `args` (below) — `build-level.mjs`'s `workerPrompt()` looks up `item.repo || principlesDefaultRepo`, falling back to an in-file kernel-only snapshot **plus a legible `DEGRADED` notice** only when `principlesSummaries` is entirely absent (an older orchestrator or a consuming-repo caller that omits the keys — **`sweep.md` and `fix.md` resolve and pass this same hand-off in their own Step 0** as of temperloop#1460, each with the one-pair simplification their single-repo scope allows, so the DEGRADED path is no longer any first-party caller's steady state). The conversational path's own 3c/3e (Within-level execution, below) read from these SAME pairs rather than re-deriving them per item.

## Step 1.9 — Dual-build: flag resolution, pre-flight, consent, and the partial-dual-build refusal

1. **Resolve the flag, per invocation (`--dual-build` runs only).** Parse `--dual-build <tier>=<candidate>`: `<tier>` is the plan `model:` tier under test — an item is **in scope** iff its own `model:` field equals it, and an item carrying no `model:` at all is never in scope — and `<candidate>` is the model being evaluated against it. Resolve `baseline` from `DUAL_BUILD_BASELINE_MODEL`, and `candidate` from `DUAL_BUILD_CANDIDATE_MODEL` only when the `=<candidate>` half was omitted: **defaults only, read only from the `build.config.sh` sourced at Step 0 item 6** — never from the invoking session's own model, never from a `$HOME`-side config or a prior run's choice, so the pair of models a run compares is fully determined by this invocation's flag plus this checkout's tracked config. A malformed value — no `<tier>`, an empty `<candidate>`, or a `<tier>` no worked item carries — is a **Step 1 validation failure**: list it with the other problems and stop. It is never quietly downgraded to a single-arm run, because a dropped comparison flag reads exactly like a normal build and the operator would learn about it only from a missing report.
2. **Project every level, then take consent ONCE for the run — before Step 2 mutates anything.** For **each** level in the Step 1.4 level list, run `workflows/scripts/build/dual-build-preflight.sh --tier <tier> --items-file - --baseline <baseline> --candidate <candidate>` (that script's own usage header owns its contract — this is the invocation, not a second spec), piping it that level's items as a JSON array of `{slug, model}`. **Dispose all three outcomes, and never silently.** `PREFLIGHT` + `stop: false` → the level is dual-buildable; keep the `dualBuild` object it emitted. `PREFLIGHT` + `stop: true` → the level is **declined**; print `dual-build declined for level <k> — <stop_reason>` naming the script's own closed reason set (`below_min_inscope` / `no_credential` / `ceiling_exceeded`) and build that level single-arm. `CANNOT_EVALUATE` → print its `error` field **verbatim** and build that level single-arm too: an unevaluable projection is **not** a pass, and the safe reading of an unknown spend projection is to decline to double it. Then surface the whole run in **one** `ask-now` ask through `decision_sink_ask(...)` (§ Operating principles): per level, the in-scope slugs and the declines by reason; both arms by name; the script's own `cumulative_spend_line`; and the standing consequence that a dual-built level opens **no** PR until every in-scope arm has a gate result and a judge result. **Doubling a run's spend carries no safe default**, so this gate never auto-proceeds and is never timed — on an operator-absent run (`--unattended` or `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`) the seam's async decision-issue backend posts the question and **parks**, taking no default, because a timeout is not consent (`claude/CLAUDE.kernel.md` § Merge autonomy & consent). **Honor an epic-scoped grant:** an autonomy grant the operator has already given for **this** epic in **this** run covers its remaining levels, so ask once rather than per level — but a grant is never inherited from an earlier session (a fresh run re-earns it) and any later explicit pause overrides it. On consent, each consented level's `dualBuild` object is exactly what Step 3 hands the engine as `input.dualBuild`; a declined, unconsented or out-of-scope level passes no such key at all.
3. **A flag-less resume over a partially dual-built level REFUSES — and this check runs on every run, flag or not.** At each level's **3-launch**, before the level is driven, read the dual-build ledger (`workflows/scripts/model-comparison/dual-build-ledger.sh read`). If it holds arm rows for any of this level's slugs with **no** recorded `pick`, the level was interrupted mid-barrier and both of its arms are still live. Finishing it single-arm would pick a side by omission — the one outcome the harness exists to prevent — so **stop the run** and print exactly: `REFUSED: level <k> of <plan> was left partially dual-built (<n> arm rows, no level pick). /build will not finish a barriered level single-arm and will not pick a side for you. Re-run with --dual-build <tier>=<candidate> to finish the comparison, or clear the level's dual-build state (dual-build-ledger.sh purge, then remove the <slug>@<arm> worktrees) to resume single-arm.` A run that **does** carry the flag re-enters that level through the barrier normally and never sees this refusal. A ledger that is absent, empty or unreadable means there is nothing to refuse over — proceed silently, so a repo that has never dual-built can never have a halt invented for it.

## Step 2 — Promote plan status

Patch frontmatter `status: approved` → `executing` via `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file`. Lets a parallel session see this plan is in flight.

Also write (or reset) a `## Run status` section near the top of the plan note — a compact live dashboard the orchestrator patches at each state transition (3a claim, 3h park, 3d skip/fail, 4d merge): `run started <ts> · session <id> · level k/N active · items: x done / y parked / z in-flight / w skipped`. One small section-scoped patch per transition — it is the at-a-glance progress surface for a returning user and a durable post-hoc trail that survives transcript loss.

## Step 2.5 — Mirror plan items onto the board (board integration ON only)

Skip this whole step when board integration is OFF. Goal: every item that will be worked carries a `gh_issue:`, so it can be claimed (3a) and `Closes #N`'d (3f). **Idempotent** — items that already have `gh_issue:` are left untouched, so a resumed run creates nothing twice. (A plan triaged the **normal** way — `/triage` made native sub-issues, `/assess --epic` prefilled each item's `gh_issue:` from them — arrives with every `gh_issue:` already set, so this step is normally a no-op for it. **Two triaged variants are the exception** and arrive with `gh_issue:` *unset* by design — split items, and a **Contract-derived epic-decomposition plan** (foundation #526; a pre-designed epic decomposed from its `## Contract` body, every item `gh_issue:`/`split_from:` unset with `epic:` in frontmatter). Both are the *expected* set here — minted below, then linked under the existing epic in Step 2.6.)

1. Collect the **pending** items to be worked this run (state `[ ]`; not user-`[-]`-skipped) that have **no** `gh_issue:` field. On the triaged path this set is normally empty, with **two deliberate exceptions:** (i) **split items** — `/assess` leaves `gh_issue:` unset and stamps `split_from: #N` whenever it splits one coarse sub-issue #N into multiple items, precisely so this step mints a **fresh issue per item** and the one-item↔one-issue invariant holds (#73); and (ii) **Contract-derived items** (epic-decomposition mode, foundation #526) — `/assess` leaves `gh_issue:` **and** `split_from:` unset on every item of a pre-designed epic it decomposed from the epic's `## Contract`, so this step mints a fresh issue per item, which Step 2.6 then links under the **existing** epic (there is no coarse parent to close — unlike split items). Both kinds are expected here; mint them like any other.
2. If that set is empty, skip to Step 3. Otherwise the creation gate is a **`ask-at-gate`** decision — **append it to the plan note's `## Questions` section** instead of interrupting the level, then proceed on the default (**create**). Entry: `` `step: 2.5` — Create N tracking issues for the unmilestoned items (<slug list>)? **default: create** `` with an `auto-proceed:` sub-line (unanswered → create the N issues; this is the same disposition `--unattended` takes). Choosing **Skip creation** at the gate leaves those items untracked (they run normally but are never claimed and emit no `Closes`); **Cancel the run** is also offered at the gate. So this step still **creates the issues now** (proceeding on the default) so the items can be claimed and worked this level — the deferred question only lets the operator *retract* that at the gate; an unattended run never stalls here. *(Resume idempotency is unchanged: 2.5 probes for an already-created issue before creating, so a default-create that is later retracted at the gate is the operator's explicit choice, not a duplicate.)*
   - **Unattended (`--unattended`):** proceed and create them — the approved plan is the authorization; no `## Questions` entry is appended (there is no operator to surface it to at the gate).
3. For each item in the set:
   - **Probe before create (crash-safe idempotency), corpus-rg-first.** Search for an issue a prior crashed run may already have created, matching on the exact `Tracked in plan: [[Plans/<date> <project> - <title>#<slug>]]` back-link string (unique per item) — **not** the title (titles collide across plans). Resolve the marker-probe helper once per run (mirrors 2.6/2.5's other single-resolve lib seams): `PROBE_LIB="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || echo .)/workflows/scripts/lib/issue-marker-probe.sh"`; if it exists, `source "$(dirname "$PROBE_LIB")/../board/lib/cache.sh"; source "$(dirname "$PROBE_LIB")/knowledge_store.sh"; source "$(dirname "$PROBE_LIB")/issue-corpus.sh"; source "$PROBE_LIB"` and probe with `issue_marker_probe "$repo" "Tracked in plan: [[Plans/<date> <project> - <title>#<slug>]]"` — it searches the rendered issue corpus first (zero `gh` calls when fresh) and falls back to the identical live `gh issue list --search "<slug> in:body" --state all --json number,title,body` this step always used whenever the corpus is absent or stale-beyond-limit. **Graceful degrade:** if `PROBE_LIB` doesn't exist in this checkout, use that direct `gh issue list` call exactly as before this item. If a match exists (`jq -r '.[0].number // empty'` off the returned array), **adopt** its number and skip creation. <!-- cite: B.20 class:duplicate-artifacts-on-resume -->
   - Otherwise create it: `gh issue create --title "<item.title>" --body "<body>"`, where `<body>` = the item's one-line scope, the `acceptance:` bullets, `Derived from: <item.source>`, and `Tracked in plan: [[Plans/<date> <project> - <title>#<slug>]]`. Apply the repo's default issue label if project `CLAUDE.md` names one. **If the item carries `split_from: #N`,** add a `Split from #N` line to the body (human lineage back to the coarse sub-issue) — the operative close-out of #N happens in Step 2.6.
   - **Write order — durable pointer before board mutation:** immediately patch `  - gh_issue: <N>` into the item via `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file` — and **in the same patch, when the item carries `split_from: #N`, remove the `split_from:` line**, so the item ends with `gh_issue:` set and `split_from:` gone (the "1:1 restored" end-state: e.g. `split_from: #40` / no `gh_issue:`  →  `gh_issue: #57` / no `split_from:`). This atomic `split_from:`→`gh_issue:` swap is what keeps `plan-schema.md` **rule 10**'s mutual-exclusion invariant always true, so a resumed run's Step-1 re-validation of rule 10 passes instead of failing on an item that carries both. **Then** place it on the board through the adapter — `source "$BOARD_LIB"; board_capture_item "$BOARD" "<issue-url>" <N>` (rides the board's auto-add workflow and lands it in **Backlog**, falling back to an explicit add only if auto-add never fires — GH #53; dedups by URL). Landing the vault pointer first shrinks the duplicate window to just the board-add. New items land in Backlog — the per-item 3a claim moves them to In Progress. This is what makes resume idempotent and lights up the `Closes #N` path in 3f.
4. Record the created `(slug → issue#)` set for the Step 6 summary.

## Step 2.6 — Ensure the epic + sub-issue linkage (board integration ON only) — backstop on the triaged path

Skip when board integration is OFF. Goal: the plan is tracked as one unit by a parent **epic**, and every per-item issue is a child of it. **Idempotent on the plan's `epic:` frontmatter field**, exactly as 2.5 is idempotent on per-item `gh_issue:` — a resumed run creates nothing twice. See [[Decisions/foundation - build epic creation at run-start]].

**On the triaged path this step is a verification no-op.** `/triage` owns epic **birth**: it creates the epic and links survivors as native sub-issues, and `/assess --epic` writes `epic: <N>` into the plan frontmatter. So a triaged plan arrives with `epic:` set and its children already linked — there is nothing to create. Epic **creation here is now a backstop**, firing only for a plan that did *not* come through triage (no `epic:` frontmatter — e.g. a hand-written plan). Child-linkage stays as an idempotent safety net (it skips already-linked children, which is all of them on the triaged path).

1. **Ensure the epic exists (backstop).** If the plan frontmatter has no `epic:` (the non-triaged path):
   - The epic-creation confirmation is a **`ask-at-gate`** decision — **append it to the plan note's `## Questions` section** rather than interrupting, then proceed on the default (**create**). Entry: `` `step: 2.6` — Create a parent epic for this plan? **default: create** `` with an `auto-proceed:` sub-line (unanswered → create the epic and link the children; same disposition `--unattended` takes). **Skip — no epic this run** / **Cancel** are offered at the gate. As with 2.5, the step proceeds on the default *now* (creating the epic so children can be linked and the 4d epic-close can converge); the deferred question only lets the operator retract it at the gate. **Unattended:** create it (the approved plan authorizes); no `## Questions` entry is appended.
   - `gh issue create --title "Epic: <plan title>" --body "<body>"`, where `<body>` = the plan's `## Summary` plus a task-list of every item (`- [ ] <title> (#<gh_issue or TBD>)`). Apply an `epic` label if the repo defines one. Place it on the board through the adapter (`source "$BOARD_LIB"; board_capture_item "$BOARD" "<epic-url>" <epic-N>` — auto-add aware, explicit add as fallback; GH #53).
   - **Patch `epic: <N>` into the plan frontmatter immediately** (`mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file`) — this is what makes the step idempotent across resumes.
   - If `epic:` is already present (triaged path), **adopt it** — skip creation entirely; just run the linkage check below.
2. **Link each per-item issue as a child of the epic.** For every worked item with a `gh_issue:` not yet linked: attach via the adapter — `board_add_sub_issue "$BOARD" <epic> <child>` (resolves the child's node/database id and busts the issue-cache entry internally; never a raw `gh api .../sub_issues` POST). Skip children already linked (the common case on the triaged path).
   - **Linkage failure warns-and-continues** (does NOT halt) — the typed parent link is cosmetic relative to closure; `Closes #N` (3f) + the 4d board→Done move + the 4d epic-close still work without it. Note the warning in the Step 6 summary.
   - **Fallback when the sub-issues API is unavailable:** append `- [ ] #<child>` to the epic body's task-list and prepend `Part of #<epic>` to the child body — GitHub renders that as a tracked relationship, and the 4d epic-close fallback can read the task-list.
   - **Split items** (no `split_from:` parent yet linked as a leaf): link each minted leaf as a **direct child of the epic** exactly like any other item — *not* nested under its coarse `split_from` parent. The 4d epic-close counts the epic's one-level open children, so the leaves must sit at that level for it to converge.
3. **Close out each coarse `split_from` parent (board ON, split items present).** For every distinct `#N` appearing as a `split_from:` value among this run's worked items: once **all** of #N's split leaves have been minted (Step 2.5) and linked as epic children (step 2 above), retire #N so the data-driven 4d epic-close doesn't hang on a bucket issue no item will ever close. **Idempotent — skip #N if already closed.**
   - **De-link #N from the epic** (best-effort, cosmetic): `gh api -X DELETE repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<epic>/sub_issue -f sub_issue_id=<N-db-id>` (resolve the db id with `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/issues/<N> --jq '.id'`). Warn-and-continue on failure.
   - **Close #N** (load-bearing — this is what 4d's open-children count reads). **Comment first, then land Done** — the ordering `/triage` Step 4.8 uses, so a failed comment leaves #N open for the next sweep. Post the reason **only when #N is not already closed** (`gh issue view <N> -R <repo> --json state -q .state` != `CLOSED`, then `gh issue comment <N> -R <repo> --body "Split into <space-separated #leaf list> by /build; tracked by those sub-issues."`), then land Done via `if declare -F board_close_done >/dev/null 2>&1; then board_close_done "$BOARD" <N>; else board_resolve_item "$BOARD" <N> >/dev/null && board_set_status "$(board_item_id <N>)" "$BOARD_OPT_DONE"; fi`. `board_close_done` (temperloop#1217/#1337) closes #N *and* strips its `fnd:status:*` label and claim stamp in one idempotent call; the adapter owns this close outright (a coarse split parent has no PR whose `Closes #N` is owed, so the helper's misuse warning does not apply). It takes no `--comment` by design — hence the separate comment, **state-gated because a bare `gh issue comment` is not idempotent** while this step is re-entrant by design, so an ungated split would repost "Split into…" every re-run. The `declare -F` guard is the cross-repo skew fallback (a consumer whose vendored `board.sh` predates the helper). (Same unchecked-return family as the 3a epic stamp, foundation#1220 — a non-zero return warns naming #N and continues; `reconcile.sh` backstops the residual label.)
   - **Never close #N while any of its leaves is unminted** — that would reintroduce the premature-close failure #73 fixes. Gate on all leaves having a `gh_issue:` first. <!-- cite: B.21 incident:F#73 -->
3. Record `(epic #, created vs adopted, linked children)` for the Step 6 summary.

## Step 3 — For each dependency level, process items then run the batch merge gate

For level *k* in the level list, process every item through 3a–3h, then run Step 4 (batch merge gate) before moving to level *k+1*.

### 3-launch. Print the level's composition BEFORE the level is driven (both paths; temperloop#1310)

`/workflows` is an **opt-in** surface the operator has to go look at, and temperloop#1294's two remaining asks (a collapsed row drawing from the active `phase()` title; running agent labels) are harness-side and unfixable from this repo — so `/build`'s own transcript is the only surface the kernel controls that **pushes** run context. Printing all three level rosters — this one, 4a's disposition roster and 4d's close-out — on every level and both paths is **mandatory**; each is the only record that a given item was ever accounted for at that boundary.

**Do not narrate a roster by hand.** Render it with `plan.sh` and print its stdout verbatim — that script owns totality (one row per level member, terminal and filtered-out items included), the closed stage vocabulary derived from `claude/plan-schema.md`'s seven sentinels, the never-infer arms (`no issue`, the report-only `[?]`), and the header counts, which it **derives from the rows** rather than restating beside them (its own header is the contract). Here, before the `build-level.mjs` invocation on the default path and before the first item's 3a on `--no-workflow`, run:

`"$machineryBinDir/plan.sh" roster "<planFile>" --level <k> --stage launch --owner-repo "$ownerRepo" --plan-link "<planLink>" [--only-slugs "<csv>"] --driver "<Workflow path (build-level.mjs) | conversational (--no-workflow)> · merge gate fires at level end"`

It is **free** and that is load-bearing: it re-reads the plan note this run already parsed, makes no `gh` call, and so has no unavailable arm to degrade into — so **re-print on every invocation of the level, a 3d-esc continuation included** (a continuation narrows `--only-slugs`, which changes what is being driven: re-driven items render `continuation`, still-open siblings `parked`). A non-zero exit (`ROSTER_INCOMPLETE`) means the level could not be fully accounted for — investigate before driving it, never proceed on a short roster. Worked example, a **continuation re-drive** of level 1 (`--only-slugs flaky-ci-detector`), which is why one row reads `continuation` and its siblings do not:

```
=== Level 1 launch: 4 items (1 active) · Towheads/temperloop · Plans/2026-08-24 temperloop - level report ===
[ ] level-composition-report  #1310     code   parked → not driven this pass
[~] flaky-ci-detector         #1201     code   continuation → 3c re-spawn (escalation continuation)
[v] keystone-changelog-spike  #1311     spike  terminal → not driven this pass
[m] pr-recap-round-trippable  no issue  code   terminal → not driven this pass
# driver: Workflow path (build-level.mjs) · merge gate fires at level end
```

### Step 3 is dual-path: the Workflow path (default) vs `--no-workflow` (conversational opt-out)

**Branch on the `--no-workflow` flag once, per level, here** — the two paths run the *same* 3a–3h mechanics over the *same* machinery scripts; they differ only in *who drives the loop*. **The default is the Workflow path** (temperloop#998); `--workflow` is still accepted and selects that same default, i.e. it is a no-op:

- **DEFAULT — no flag (or the no-op `--workflow`): the per-level Workflow path.** **Immediately before invoking**, print the **Workflow launch line** template (`claude/message-schema.md`) — this is what makes concurrent drives distinguishable in the transcript, since the tool's own `meta.description` is a pure literal and cannot carry run context (temperloop#903, temperloop#1941). Invoke the per-level Workflow once for **this level** via the Workflow tool — by **`scriptPath`** to `$workflowPath` (resolved by the first bullet below, foundation #422), **NOT** by `name:` (the tool's `name:` resolves built-ins ONLY — deep-research/code-review — never deployed user workflows; #437). `args` is delivered to the script as a JSON **string** (the `.mjs` parses it itself). Pass:
  ```
  args = { repoRoot, planLink, board, items, verdicts, ownerRepo, claimCmd, machineryBinDir, machinerySoloModel, machineryBatchModel, gateSliceSecs, reviewBlockingMaxRounds, machineryStepCeilingSecs, machineryStepSlowSecs, reviewAgentCeilingSecs, reviewAgentSlowSecs, workerSummaryMaxWords, workerEvidenceMaxWords, principlesSummaries, principlesDefaultRepo, reviewerRoutingTsv, requireDiscriminationEvidence: true, onlySlugs[, dualBuild][, levelPick] }
  ```
  where `items` is this level's **full** item array (each `{ slug, branch, title, kind, ghIssue, alsoCloses, repo, model, acceptance, source, scope, notes, dependsOn, activation }`; `repo` is the item's plan-schema `repo:` field straight through, when present — `build-level.mjs` uses it for exactly one thing today (temperloop#852): the 3f cross-repo `Closes` qualification below, emitting `Closes <ownerRepo>#<gh_issue>` (and the same for each `also_closes:` entry) instead of bare when `repo` differs from `ownerRepo`, matching the "Cross-repo `repo:` honor point" that same 3f step names — it does **not** (yet) retarget worktree creation or CI polling per item, so a `repo:` item that needs its work to actually *land* in a different checkout still requires `--no-workflow`). **`dualBuild` and `levelPick` are the two CONDITIONAL keys.** `dualBuild` is present only on a level that Step 1.9 both projected as dual-buildable and got consent for, carrying that level's `{tier, baseline, candidate, inScope: [slug…]}` object verbatim as `dual-build-preflight.sh` emitted it (Step 1.9 is the single statement site for its shape, its declines and its refusals — do not re-derive either here). `levelPick` (temperloop#2083) is the operator's answer to a `level-pick` escalation — `{verdict: confirm | override-level | override-item, arm?, items?: [{slug, arm, reason}]}` — passed ONLY on the continuation that answers one, with 3d-esc's level-pick handler below its single statement site for the grammar and every disposition; an engine that ignores it HOLDS the level for a confirm that can never arrive, a visible stall rather than a silent auto-merge. **Omit both keys entirely** on every declined level and on every flag-less run; that omission is what keeps a single-arm run byte-identical to the pre-harness behavior. How each arg is resolved:
  - **Resolve `workflowPath` FIRST — the SINGLE statement site; `sweep.md` and `fix.md` point here (temperloop#2027).** `wp="$(bash workflows/scripts/build/workflow-path.sh resolve "$repoRoot")" || { printf '%s\n' "$wp"; exit 1; }` then `workflowPath="$(printf '%s' "$wp" | jq -r .path)"`. It resolves the **CHECKOUT copy** and **REFUSES** a drifted installed one (`WORKFLOW_PATH_STALE`, non-zero, **no path**, so nothing stale is left to invoke), reusing `doctor.sh`'s `check_installed_workflow_drift` (temperloop#1397) rather than growing a second detector. `WORKFLOW_PATH_INSTALLED_IN_SYNC` and `WORKFLOW_PATH_INDETERMINATE` proceed but **print their `notice` verbatim** — indeterminate is never a pass. This spec previously named `~/.claude/workflows/build-level.mjs`, which the Workflow tool **rejects** (a `scriptPath` outside the working directory) and which nothing installs or refreshes, so it drifted silently. **Nothing here installs one.** The script's usage header owns the full contract.
  - **`dependsOn`** — the **dep-merge precondition** input (#108), an array of `{ slug, sha }`: for each of this item's `depends-on` targets, the dep's **merged head sha** (read from that dep's plan-note `pushed_sha:` sub-line — a `depends-on` target is by construction in a **prior**, already-merged level, so its `pushed_sha:` is recorded). Empty/omitted for level-0 items and items whose only cross-item edges are `after:` (logical-order, not merge). `build-level.mjs`'s 3b-0 gate runs `worktree.sh deps-merged` on these shas **before** `worktree.sh create`: the worktree is created only once **every** dep sha is an ancestor of `origin/<default>` (the depended-on PR has MERGED), so the worker builds and self-verifies against merged dependency code — not a pre-merge base. If a dep has not merged, the item escalates `dep-not-merged` (no worktree, no worker) rather than silently building on stale code. The orchestrator's level ordering (Step 4 merges level *k* before this call for *k+1*) is the primary guarantee; `dependsOn` is the mechanical backstop against a resume race / partial merge / ordering bug. The 3f unconditional rebase (#525) does **not** replace it — that only repairs the branch *textually* at push, too late for the worker's own build/verify.
  - **`activation`** — the item's plan-schema `activation:` block straight through (`{ class, proof, locus }`), omitted for an item that declares none. This is what carries the block ACROSS the orchestrator→workflow boundary; without it §3e.6 below cannot run at all on the Workflow path, and every gate rule 14 forces onto a product-source item is inert (temperloop#1219 — the block was missing from this contract, so `build-level.mjs` had no `activation` reference anywhere). Only **`class: A`** does anything here: `driveItem` runs its `proof:` predicate against the worker's worktree at §3e.6, strictly between 3e.5 and 3f. An absent block, or `class: B`/`C` (ledger-discharged at 4d-epic step 2a, orchestrator-side), takes the byte-identical pre-#1219 path. A `class: A` block's `proof:` is **also** passed through into the worker's own brief — `workerPrompt()`'s `activationProofSection()` (temperloop#1934) renders it verbatim as the reachability predicate §3e.6 will run, so the worker names and wires its artifacts to satisfy the exact command it is gated on rather than guessing at a paraphrase.
  - **`repoRoot`** / **`planLink`** / **`board`** — from the run context (the parent checkout's top-level path, the plan note's vault link, the board id or OFF).
  - **`ownerRepo`** — `"owner/repo"` for `ci-poll.sh` / `gh` ops; resolved by the **Step 0 probe** (`gh repo view --json nameWithOwner -q .nameWithOwner`). **The workflow has no shell to derive it**, so the orchestrator MUST pass it — without it every CI poll gets `''` and resolves to `ERROR`.
  - **`claimCmd`** — the absolute path to the board **`claim.sh`** entrypoint, resolved by the **Step 0 `CLAIM` probe**; consumed by 3a (board-ON claim). Absent → 3a falls back to a bare `claim.sh` on `PATH`.
  - **`machineryBinDir`** — the **already-resolved absolute path** to the build-machinery script directory (`worktree.sh`/`pr.sh`/`ci-poll.sh` live here). Resolve it in the orchestrator's **own** shell (Step 0): `machineryBinDir="$(cd workflows/scripts/build 2>/dev/null && pwd)"` — a plain `cd`+`pwd` that follows a `workflows/` → foundation symlink to the real path. Passing it lets `build-level.mjs` emit **plain absolute machinery paths** (`'<dir>/pr.sh' push …`) instead of resolving them **inline** with a nested `$(dirname "$(readlink -f …)")` command-substitution — which the auto-mode safety classifier read (together with the runMachinery "run exactly this command" instruction) as an obfuscated-command bypass and **denied** on `--unattended`/pipeline runs, blocking every push/worktree machinery step and then crashing `driveItem` on the null result (temperloop#72). **Absent → `build-level.mjs` falls back to its in-bash `machineryBin` resolver** (the #560 repo-local→foundation chain), so this is a classifier de-trip optimization, **not** a hard dependency — an older/consuming-repo invocation that omits it still runs, just without the de-obfuscation. **`sweep.md` and `fix.md` resolve and pass it too** (temperloop#1460), on the same seam and in the same plain `cd`+`pwd` form — the classifier trip is a property of the *machinery command text* `build-level.mjs` emits, not of `/build`, and `--unattended` is `/sweep`'s default posture, so a caller that omitted it was the one most exposed to the denial.
  - **`machinerySoloModel`** / **`machineryBatchModel`** — the model tiers for `build-level.mjs`'s two machinery-executor agent spawns (`runMachinery` solo / `runMachineryBatch` prelude-pr-batch-ci-batch — that file's own DESIGN NOTE 1; a paired setting, not a general-case-plus-carve-out — each covers exactly half of the file's four machinery call sites), resolved from `$BUILD_MACHINERY_SOLO_MODEL` / `$BUILD_MACHINERY_BATCH_MODEL` at **Step 0 item 6** (empty on both by default — the Step-0-source shell seam every other setting in this file uses is structurally unavailable *inside* the Workflow runtime, which has no filesystem/Node/shell, so this Step-0 hand-off is the only place they are read; temperloop#982). Pass the resolved value straight through, empty or not — **the empty-vs-omitted distinction is NOT this prose's responsibility**: the .mjs reads `input.machinerySoloModel || 'haiku'` / `input.machineryBatchModel || 'haiku'` (`||`, not `??`), which collapses an empty value exactly the same as an absent/omitted key, so there is no failure mode here left for the orchestrator to get wrong (omitting the key when unset is a harmless nicety, not a requirement). This is a pure model-tier override with **no other behavior change**; both sites keep their `'haiku'` literal as the fallback default. **`sweep.md` and `fix.md` resolve and pass the same two settings** in their own `build-level.mjs` invocations (their own Step 0 already sources this file) — the setting governs the *machinery-executor seat* inside `build-level.mjs`, not `/build` alone, so all three callers wire it.
  - **`gateSliceSecs`** — the §3e.5 acceptance gate's per-**slice** wall-clock budget, resolved from `$BUILD_GATE_SLICE_SECS` at **Step 0 item 6** and handed in on the same seam, and for the same structural reason, as the two model settings above (the Workflow runtime has no shell/filesystem to source `build.config.sh` itself). It is a **slice** length, not a suite deadline: `build-level.mjs` hands it to `scripts/quality-gates.sh` as that script's own per-invocation budget, the suite stops cleanly *between* gates when the budget is spent and reports a resume index, and the `.mjs` loops slices — so total suite runtime is **not** bounded by the executor agent's ~10-minute Bash cap and a growing gate list cannot manufacture a false `GATE_FAIL` (the flat-timeout design decayed twice, temperloop#115 then temperloop#1021). Pass the resolved value straight through, empty or not — the `.mjs` keeps its own in-file default, ignores a non-positive value, and **clamps** the derived Bash-tool timeout under the agent cap, so no orchestrator or operator value can reintroduce the hard-kill. **`sweep.md` and `fix.md` resolve and pass it too** (their own Step 0 already sources this file), exactly as they do the model pair. **`reviewBlockingMaxRounds`** rides the identical seam (resolved from `$BUILD_REVIEW_BLOCKING_MAX_ROUNDS`, passed by all three callers): it caps how many §3e review ROUNDS one item's worktree may spend before a HIGH finding stops escalating `review-blocking` (which loops the item back to 3c for another build-review round) and is instead **carried** into the PR body's `## Review notes` and the parked record's `review.residual_blocking` tally for the human at the merge gate — advisory, never a suppression. Unbounded before temperloop#1970: one live item spent five consecutive passes, four distinct HIGHs, zero repeats, ~2h45m and ~1.05M subagent tokens, with the reviewed spec growing across the rounds so each round enlarged the surface the next one read. Its companion half is the reviewer seat itself (`claude/agents/workflow-reviewer.md` now enumerates every HIGH in one pass). Pass it straight through, empty or not: a non-positive value falls back to the `.mjs`'s in-file default and a floor of 1 means no value can restore an unbounded loop. **`workerSummaryMaxWords` / `workerEvidenceMaxWords`** ride this same seam for the same reason — §3c's worker return-value word bounds, resolved from `$BUILD_WORKER_SUMMARY_MAX_WORDS` / `$BUILD_WORKER_EVIDENCE_MAX_WORDS` at Step 0 item 6, interpolated by `build-level.mjs` into the single shared `workerPrompt()`, so all three worker spawns (main, spike, CI-fix) — and `sweep.md` / `fix.md`, which do **not** resolve these settings — inherit the bounded shape from the `.mjs`'s own in-file defaults; the shape is universal, only the tuning is `/build`'s. Pass them straight through, empty or not: a non-positive value falls back to the in-file default, and a floor stops any operator value collapsing a slot to nothing. — **`machineryStepCeilingSecs`** / **`machineryStepSlowSecs`** ride the identical seam (resolved from `$BUILD_MACHINERY_STEP_CEILING_SECS` / `$BUILD_MACHINERY_STEP_SLOW_SECS` at Step 0 item 6, passed by all three callers): the first is a per-**step** wall-clock **liveness ceiling** that `build-level.mjs` compiles into the machinery command text itself — a `sleep`/`kill` watchdog **inside** the invoked shell, deliberately independent of and additional to the Bash tool's own `timeout` parameter, because temperloop#1071 observed a `pr-batch` step run 9h49m with that tool timeout never firing and nothing else bounding the call; a step that outlives it is killed, reported as `STEP_TIMEOUT`, and treated as **LOST** — disposed through the existing `pr.sh recover-probe` side-effect ladder (an already-opened PR is **adopted**, never re-opened) and escalated as `machinery-step-timeout`, never blind-retried, so no bounded step can double-push or double-open. The second is its observability half: a step slower than that threshold emits a `log()` progress notice so a long stall is **visible** well before it reaches the ceiling. Both are clamped in the `.mjs` (a floor no smaller than one CI-poll/gate slice) so no operator value can manufacture a false timeout on healthy work. — **`reviewAgentCeilingSecs`** / **`reviewAgentSlowSecs`** are that same pair one layer up, for the §3e REVIEW AGENTS (resolved from `$BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_CEILING_SECS` / `$BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_SLOW_SECS` at Step 0 item 6, passed by all three callers). A reviewer is an `agent({agentType})` call with no shell to wrap, so the emitted-shell watchdog above cannot reach it and it had **no** liveness bound at all: temperloop#2003 observed a spawned reviewer never return, the workflow stop writing its journal for ~41 minutes, and the **mandatory** `workflow-reviewer` for that item's `claude/commands/*.md` diff never launch — with `review.mandatory_ok` never even evaluated, because the pass never resolved. `build-level.mjs` now spawns every routed reviewer **concurrently** and bounds the whole fanout's wall clock: a reviewer still unreturned at the ceiling is **abandoned** (this runtime cannot cancel an agent) and dispositioned by kind — an **advisory** one degrades to the documented `skipped — <agent> unavailable` notice and the item carries on, a **mandatory** one escalates `review-agent-timeout` carrying the computed tally, so the gate can never read as if it passed. The slow threshold is its observability half and also the timer's first slice, so a healthy review costs one cheap executor spawn. Both are clamped in the `.mjs` (the ceiling floored at one CI-poll/gate slice), and the bound **fails open** to the pre-#2003 unbounded await if its own timer cannot run. <!-- setting-prose:allow — this line names BUILD_REVIEW_BLOCKING_MAX_ROUNDS (default 3) alongside bare section references (§3e, 3c, tier-3, the list number) that check-setting-prose.sh's int heuristic reads as a restatement of that default. The value is stated nowhere here: build.config.sh remains its single home. -->
  - **`reviewerRoutingTsv`** — the verbatim text of `workflows/scripts/config/reviewer-routing.tsv`, read by the orchestrator at Step 0 and handed through on the same structural seam as `principlesSummaries` below (temperloop#1982). The routing table is a **static repo file** — identical every run, independent of anything the worktree computes — so only the *changed-file list* ever genuinely needed to come from the worktree. Relaying the table itself through §3e's `review-diff` machinery step put a multi-line tab-delimited table in front of a `machinery-executor` agent bound to echo it byte-for-byte, and it was mangled three distinct ways across eight observed occurrences: silently omitted, replaced by an English sentence *describing* the table, and returned double-JSON-encoded (surrounding quotes plus literal two-character `\t`/`\n` in place of real tabs and newlines, parsing as one row rather than eleven). `tsv_rows` and `tsv_checksum` — both small integers — were correct in every one of those, which is the tell: the string field is the unreliable part, not the step. Supplying it here removes the agent from this data's path rather than adding another guard behind it. **Pass it whenever you can read the file**; when the key is absent (an older orchestrator, or a consuming-repo caller that has not wired it) `build-level.mjs` falls back to the relayed table plus its row-count/checksum gap check and one retry, so this is additive and cannot regress an un-migrated caller. Two things changed on that fallback path in temperloop#2020, and neither weakens a detector. **The relayed field is now `tsv_lines`** — the table's data rows as a JSON *array of strings*, the same `jq` idiom `files` uses, which arrived intact in every one of the manglings above that destroyed the `tsv` scalar; the array joined on newlines is byte-identical to the blob it replaces, so `tsv_rows`/`tsv_checksum` compare exactly as before, and the legacy scalar is still *accepted* so a payload produced by an older copy keeps routing. **And a gap that survives the retry now DEGRADES rather than escalating `review-diff-error`:** §3e runs *after* the worker has committed and passed acceptance, so halting there stranded finished work over an advisory pass that is explicitly never a `checks` gate — it now emits a `skipped — …` line naming the withdrawn axes (`claude/message-schema.md` § Degradation notice), carries the gap figures on the parked record as `review.routing_degraded`, and lets the drive finish. That degradation is **partial, and deliberately so**: only the *table-dependent* axes — the extension axis and the prose-`*.md` fallback — are withdrawn. The **mandatory command-doc rule** (foundation#1007), the `review:` override and the `kind: architectural` axis are computed from the item and its changed-file list, never from the table, so they still route and still run, and `review.mandatory_ok` keeps meaning "workflow-reviewer actually ran on this command-doc diff". Withdrawing them alongside the table would have reported a `claude/commands/*.md` diff as `mandatory_ok: true` with no reviewer run at all — byte-identical to a clean pass, i.e. the silent-skip class this rule exists to prevent. **`sweep.md` and `fix.md` resolve and pass this hand-off in their own Step 0 too** (temperloop#1992), exactly as they already do `machineryBinDir` and `principlesSummaries`: §3e's routing lives inside `build-level.mjs`, so the relay governs every caller, not `/build` alone — and `--unattended` is `/sweep`'s default posture, so a mangled table there stalls the mandatory §3e reviewer pass with no operator watching.
  - **`principlesSummaries`** / **`principlesDefaultRepo`** — the §3c **effective engineering principle set**, resolved ONCE for the whole run at **Step 1.8** (below), on the identical structural seam as the settings above: `build-level.mjs` has no filesystem or vault access to resolve `claude/engineering-principles.md` ∪ a project's `Projects/<project>/Priorities.md` § Principles itself, so the orchestrator resolves the merge and hands the **already-rendered text** through. `principlesSummaries` is a map keyed by each resolved `(repo, project)` pair's `repo` string — `principlesDefaultRepo` (`== ownerRepo`) for the plan's primary/default pair, plus one more key per distinct `repo:` a cross-repo item carries — to that pair's rendered summary (a compact numbered list, each entry tagged `[kernel]` or `[project]`, plus a trailing provenance line in the Step 6 `Principles:` format). `build-level.mjs`'s shared `workerPrompt()` looks up `item.repo || principlesDefaultRepo` and embeds the match verbatim in a self-contained `## Effective engineering principles` section (own comment there); an item whose own `repo:` has no resolved pair falls back to the default pair's summary, never to silence. Pass both straight through, empty or not: an entirely absent/empty `principlesSummaries` — an older orchestrator or a consuming-repo caller that omits the keys — makes the `.mjs` fall back to its own static, kernel-names-only in-file snapshot **plus an explicit `DEGRADED` notice**, never a silent kernel-only list that reads identically to "principles applied." **`sweep.md` and `fix.md` resolve and pass this hand-off in their own Step 0 too** (temperloop#1460), exactly as they already do the model pair and `gateSliceSecs`: `workerPrompt()` is shared, so the set governs the *worker seat inside `build-level.mjs`*, not `/build` alone, and leaving those two un-wired pinned their workers on the DEGRADED path permanently. Their pair enumeration is the one-pair degenerate case (a sweep drains one board; a `/fix` drives one target), so each resolves `{ ownerRepo: <summary> }` with `principlesDefaultRepo = ownerRepo`. **`requireDiscriminationEvidence`** rides the identical shared-`workerPrompt()`/scoped-to-`/build` shape (temperloop#1319): a plain **boolean**, always `true` for this caller, read as `input.requireDiscriminationEvidence === true` (strict equality, not `||`/`??`, so only an explicit `true` arms it) to gate the `## Discrimination evidence` section and its `discrimination_evidence` acceptance-result field (see the §3c bullet below for the full requirement) — `sweep.md`/`fix.md` deliberately omit this key, and (unlike `principlesSummaries`, which they now DO pass — temperloop#1460) that omission is the one remaining deliberate one: an OPERATIONAL SCOPE DECISION (temperloop#1319 scopes this requirement to `/build` only), not a structural one — `sweep.md`/`fix.md`'s own `acceptance:` field is "checkable bullets from the issue body", falling back to a bare-string placeholder only when the issue body carries none, so a real per-criterion array from either caller would have exactly the shape this requirement needs; nothing stops a future item from wiring them in.
  - **`verdicts`** — the escalation-continuation map. **Empty/absent on a fresh level**; on a 3d-esc continuation, keyed by slug as `{ [slug]: { kind, verdict_section } }` (the full appended markdown block — see 3d-esc step 4). The workflow reads it **only** for slugs in `onlySlugs`.
  - **`onlySlugs`** — the continuation filter. **Absent on a fresh level** (drive everything); on a 3d-esc continuation, set to the array of still-unresolved slugs to re-drive (siblings already parked are left untouched). A slug in `onlySlugs` is driven in **continuation mode** — 3a (claim) and 3b (worktree create) are **skipped** and the captured verdict is injected at 3c (see 3d-esc step 4).
  - **Probe the engine's hand-off capability IMMEDIATELY BEFORE invoking (temperloop#2018) — the single statement site; `sweep.md` and `fix.md` point here.** Every bullet above can say "pass it straight through, an absent key cannot regress an un-migrated caller" because this hand-off is **additive**: `build-level.mjs` falls back to an in-file default for any key it does not find. The converse is silent too — a **stale** installed engine, or a consuming repo's older **vendored** copy, simply IGNORES a key you pass, with no signal on either side (live case: an installed copy 18 days behind carried zero `reviewerRoutingTsv` support, so following this spec literally dropped the key and fell back to the very agent relay the run was fixing). So, naming the key set this spec passes, run the probe — `workflows/scripts/build/handoff-capability.sh`'s own usage header owns its contract; this is the invocation, not a second spec:
    ```bash
     # The keys THIS spec passes. Static per spec — it changes only when an author edits
     # the args block above, never per run — so run-time derivation had nothing to read
     # (`args` is a Workflow-tool parameter, never a shell string). Sync is an
     # AUTHORING-time obligation: temperloop#2024's registry + lint reads the SAME
     # declaration this probe does and REPLACES this literal. /sweep and /fix pass 18.
     # On a level Step 1.9 consented for dual-build, append `dualBuild` — passed ONLY then, so a DEGRADED verdict naming it correctly means this engine has no dual-build arm support.
     # On a 3d-esc level-pick continuation, append `levelPick` — passed ONLY then, so a DEGRADED verdict naming it correctly means this engine has no level-pick support (without it, the confirm is silently ignored and the level HOLDS on every continuation with no legible notice).
     handoffKeys="repoRoot,planLink,board,items,verdicts,ownerRepo,claimCmd,machineryBinDir,machinerySoloModel,machineryBatchModel,gateSliceSecs,reviewAgentCeilingSecs,reviewAgentSlowSecs,reviewBlockingMaxRounds,machineryStepCeilingSecs,machineryStepSlowSecs,workerSummaryMaxWords,workerEvidenceMaxWords,principlesSummaries,principlesDefaultRepo,reviewerRoutingTsv,requireDiscriminationEvidence,onlySlugs"
    probe=workflows/scripts/build/handoff-capability.sh
    if [ -f "$probe" ]; then bash "$probe" check "$workflowPath" "$handoffKeys"; else echo '{"outcome":"CAPABILITIES_INDETERMINATE","reason":"handoff-capability.sh absent from this checkout"}'; fi
    ```
    **Dispose all FOUR outcomes, and never silently.** An **`ERROR`** verdict (a missing `jq`, or no valid key parsed) is **never the permissive branch** — surface its `.error` field verbatim and treat capability as unknown, exactly as `CAPABILITIES_INDETERMINATE` below, the same conservative rule every other closed-outcome consumer in this file already states. `CAPABILITIES_OK` → proceed, print nothing. **`CAPABILITIES_DEGRADED`** → **proceed anyway** (the named keys fall back; halting a level over an advisory probe is worse than the drop) but print the verdict's `notice` field **verbatim** — it is a `claude/message-schema.md` § Degradation notice naming exactly WHICH keys this engine drops, which a generic "your engine is old" would not — and carry it into the Step 6 summary and each resulting PR body, where the verdict's `remedy` field rides along (mode 6 carries the remedy slot; the live line does not). **`CAPABILITIES_INDETERMINATE`** → proceed and print the notice the same way, but read it as what it is: the probe **could not tell**, which is NOT a pass. An unknown must never read as "all keys supported" — that collapse is the failure this check exists to end — so its `reason` (engine absent, unreadable, no declaration, unterminated, empty) is reported too, and an absent probe script is itself this outcome, never a silent skip.

  The Workflow drives **every active** item's 3a–3h via the `runMachinery`/`runMachineryBatch` bridge plus an `agent({schema})` worker per item, expresses the level as a `parallel()` over `driveItem` (= the `pipeline()` that owns intra-level concurrency), and **returns only** a small `{ parked, escalations }` summary. Mechanically-adjacent machinery steps ride **one** `agent({schema})` each — `prelude:<slug>` (claim + deps-merged + worktree), `pr-batch:<slug>` (rebase + scan + push + pr-open), `ci-batch:<slug>#n` (the interleaved merge-state probe + CI poll slices) — returning `{results:[…]}`, one closed-outcome object per step that ran; the 3e.5 gate stays a solo call because its own runtime is minutes-scale. Every branching decision still reads an individual step's outcome in the `.mjs` (temperloop#942 cut an L0-shaped 3-item level from 40 agent spawns to 15):
  ```
  { parked:      [{ slug, pr, pushed_sha, acceptance_results }],
    escalations: [{ slug, kind, payload }] }
  ```
  **Immediately after the call returns**, print the **Workflow return line** template (`claude/message-schema.md`) — the launch line's pair, so the transcript shows this invocation's outcome without the reader re-deriving it from the JSON above. The Workflow **NEVER writes the plan note** and **NEVER merges** — it returns *what to write*; the orchestrator does the writeback (serialized, single-writer) and runs Step 4. Both halves together are why **Step 3h.5's as-you-go merge is a conversational-path-only step** and has no actor here (temperloop#1452): its `[>]` flip is a plan-note write and its merge is a merge, and the workflow does neither. The orchestrator then partitions the return: `parked` items get their 3h sentinel writes + worktree removal + claim release; `escalations` items re-enter the **3d-esc** continuation loop (below). See the **orchestrator↔workflow boundary** statement under 3d-esc. **THIRD ARM — `zeroDisposition` present: the level disposed of NOTHING, and that is a contradiction, not a completion (temperloop#2004).** The two arms above cover `parked` non-empty and `escalations` non-empty. A return with **both empty** for a level that was asked to drive at least one item matches neither, and before this item it fell straight through as "the level completed with nothing to report" — the item then vanished with no PR, no park, no escalation and no signal (observed on a stopped-and-resumed run that re-ran nothing while its issue was still In Progress under a live claim). `build-level.mjs` now names that case itself, below all three drivers, so this arm reads a field rather than re-deriving the condition: when **`zeroDisposition`** is present on the return, **conclude nothing from the empty result and re-probe real state first.** The field carries `slugs` (exactly the items this run was asked to drive — the `onlySlugs` subset on a continuation, never the level's full membership), `requested`, `continuation`, and per slug its `issue`, `branch`, `worktree` and a ready-to-run **`reprobe`** command line. Run each slug's `reprobe` — issue status, open PRs for its branch, and the worktree — then dispose on **what those reads actually say**: a PR already open and green finishes through Step 4 normally; an intact worktree resumes via the 3d-esc continuation loop; nothing landed at all re-drives the slug. Never write a `[m]`/`[x]` sentinel, never move the board item to Done, and never advance to the next level on the strength of this return. Its absence is the ordinary case and costs a normal level nothing. Carry the contradiction into the Step 6 summary either way — a level that reported nothing is exactly the thing that must not go unmentioned. <!-- cite: B.28 class:concurrent-write-races -->

  A parked record may additionally carry **`acceptance_unverified: true`** + **`recovered_from: <RECOVER_* stage>`** (#939 — see the "LOOK before you classify" paragraph in 3d). That pair means the worker's return channel failed and the record was reconstructed from observable side-effects: `pr`/`pushed_sha` are ground truth, but its `acceptance_results` are **UNKNOWN, not passing**. The orchestrator MUST **re-verify that item's acceptance itself before the Step 4 merge gate** (and stamp `  - acceptance_unverified: true` on the plan item alongside `pr:`), never merging it on the strength of a self-check that never ran.

- **OPT-OUT — `--no-workflow`: the conversational path.** This session drives the within-level loop inline: the two-sweep / `parallel()` fanout described in **Within-level execution** below, running each item's 3a–3h directly and parsing each worker's verdict JSON (§3c) by hand. The 3a–3h prose below **is** that path, executed conversationally. It is also the **only** path that can run the speculative next-level overlap (a Workflow-path NON-GOAL in v1 — see the NON-GOAL block below), so `--no-workflow` is what an operator reaches for when they want that overlap.

**The 3a–3h prose below is retained verbatim** — it is (a) the **spec the Workflow implements** (`build-level.mjs`'s `driveItem` is the as-built encoding of 3a–3h; read its header DESIGN NOTES) and (b) the **conversational path** run inline under `--no-workflow`. Do **not** delete it.

### Per-item processing (3a–3h)

**Branch on `kind` first.** A `kind: spike` item has no PR — its deliverable is a Context/Decision note + a routed follow-up issue. For a spike: run 3a (claim, mark `[~]`), then spawn a **read-only** worker whose return reports `{ verdict_note_path, routed_issue }` instead of commits; verify the note exists (`Read` on the resolved file path) and the issue is routed (`gh issue view`), then **skip 3b–3h entirely** and mark the item `[v]` (verdict-captured). On board-ON projects, move its issue to Done on verdict-capture (cf. 4d) and close it with a comment linking the note — no PR `Closes` — then **run the 4d epic-close check** (a spike closing can be the epic's last open child); if 4d-epic closes the epic here, run 4d-retro immediately after (same trigger/guard). A **routine** spike (`keystone:` absent) runs **fully autonomously** — no operator gate — even when its verdict reopens a previously-locked decision; it produces findings, not merges, and the downstream merge gates stay intact.

**Keystone-spike review gate (`keystone: true` — temperloop#526).** A spike whose plan item carries `keystone: true` (§ plan-schema `keystone:`) is a *keystone* spike — its verdict **reshapes downstream contracts/scope**, so its findings must **not** flow into dependent builds unreviewed. Capture its `[v]` verdict exactly as above (note + routed issue, Done-move, 4d/4d-retro epic checks all run identically — the marker changes nothing about verdict capture). The halt itself is **not** raised here in the kind-fork — it is raised at the level boundary by **Step 4e-keystone**, which is **plan-note-driven and path-agnostic** (it re-reads the plan note and keys off the `keystone: true` + `[v]` sentinels the kind-fork just wrote, so it works identically whether the kind-fork ran conversationally or inside `build-level.mjs`, and needs no in-memory flag to cross the orchestrator↔workflow boundary). Do not halt *inside* the kind-fork: let the spike's whole level finish its per-item processing and its no-op merge gate first, so a level that happens to pair the keystone spike with an independent sibling still completes that sibling before the boundary halt (in the isolated-level authoring pattern the level is spike-only, so this is moot). The halt is `ask-now` (no safe default) and is **NOT bypassed by `--unattended`** — see 4e-keystone.

A `kind: code` item (the default) runs the full 3a–3h below.

### Within-level execution — two-sweep, optionally parallel

Items in a level are independent by construction (no merge edge between them — that is what put them in one level), so the orchestrator does **not** block on each item's CI before starting the next. Two modes:

- **Two-sweep (default for ≥2 items).** *Spawn-push sweep:* run 3a–3f for every item (claim, worker, push-by-SHA, open PR), kicking off the **REST CI poll (3g)** as a background job per PR and recording the `pr:` pointer (3f). *Collect sweep:* join each background CI poll, then run 3g (failure handling) + 3h (park). This overlaps the long CI waits across items instead of serializing them. If interrupted between sweeps, `[~]` items with `pr:` re-attach to their open PRs on resume (Step 1.4) rather than re-spawning.
- **Parallel fanout (≥3 items; the width setting).** Express each item's **kind-appropriate** pipeline as a `parallel()` over the level's items on the Workflow substrate — `code` items run claim → worker → push → PR → CI-watch → park; `spike` items run their read-only kind-fork to `[v]` (no push/PR/CI). Three coordination rules are load-bearing: (1) **all plan-note patches are serialized through the orchestrator** — workers never patch the note (concurrent `patch_vault_file` on one file races); (2) **blocked/failed items are collected into one batched `AskUserQuestion` at the join** and do not halt siblings; (3) the parent-tree **leak-check (3h step 0) runs once at the join**, not per item — if dirty, bisect by each branch's committed paths. A `--serial` escape hatch forces one-at-a-time for debugging.

Below the width threshold (single-item levels), process the one item through 3a–3h directly.

**By DEFAULT this within-level concurrency is owned by the Workflow, not this section.** On the default (Workflow) path — i.e. unless `--no-workflow` is passed (Step 3 dual-path above) — `build-level.mjs` expresses the level as a `pipeline()` = `parallel(activeItems.map(driveItem))` and owns the intra-level fanout itself, so the two-sweep / `parallel()` orchestration described here is **superseded by the workflow's `pipeline()`** for that run. The two coordination guarantees still hold identically (and are the workflow's responsibility on the default path): (1) **the orchestrator is the single plan-note writer** — the workflow returns `{parked, escalations}` and writes nothing, so concurrent `patch_vault_file` races cannot occur; (2) **blocked/failed/design-fork items are collected, not halted inline** — they ride back as `escalations[]` and are batched at the level boundary (3d-esc) rather than interrupting siblings. The text in this section is therefore the **conversational-path** (`--no-workflow`) behavior, driven inline by this session.

##### Speculative next-level execution (opt-in optimization, `--no-workflow` only — overlap build with merge latency)

This is an **opt-in** optimization layered on top of the sequential level flow, and it is available **only on the conversational path** (`--no-workflow`; see the NON-GOAL immediately below) — it changes *when* a worker spawns, never the one-worktree-per-item isolation or the per-level merge gate. With it off, levels run strictly sequentially as above; with it on, the orchestrator overlaps the next level's build with the current level's merge-wait. Governed by the ratified [[Decisions/foundation - Speculative next-level execution worktree-isolation contract]] — implement exactly that contract.

> **NON-GOAL on the Workflow path (v1) — and therefore OFF BY DEFAULT.** Speculative cross-level overlap is **out of scope for the Workflow path** in v1. The per-level Workflow model (`build-level.mjs` drives exactly one level and returns `{parked, escalations}`) **quantizes the level boundary** — the orchestrator invokes the workflow per level and reasons only over its summary, with no in-process handle to pre-spawn the *next* level's workers during this level's merge-wait. So this entire speculative section is **conversational-path-only** (it applies only when `--no-workflow` is passed). **Consequence of the temperloop#998 default flip, stated plainly:** because the Workflow path is now the DEFAULT, **speculative overlap is disabled by default** — a plain `/build <plan>` runs levels strictly sequentially with no speculative pre-spawn, and **`--no-workflow` is the only way to get speculative overlap back**. Lifting the NON-GOAL (making speculation work under the Workflow path) is separate, larger work, still deferred past v1.

- **What may overlap.** When level *k* reaches its CI-poll / merge-wait (all its items pushed and in 3g, or parked at the Step 4 gate awaiting merge), the orchestrator **MAY pre-spawn level *k+1* workers** rather than waiting for level *k* to fully merge — running their 3a–3e build (claim, worktree, worker, review) against `origin/<default>` while level *k* is still in flight. This recovers the merge-latency window the default flow leaves idle.
- **Eligibility — what may be speculated.** ONLY level *k+1* items with **(a) no `depends-on` into the in-flight level *k*** AND **(b) no *unsatisfied* `after:` edge into level *k*** — i.e. every level-*k* item the candidate points to via `after:` has already reached a **terminal state** (`[x]` merged, `[-]` skipped, or `[v]` verdict-captured), per the `after:`-satisfaction rule in Step 1.4. A `depends-on` item is **NEVER** speculated — it needs the dep's *merged* code, which by definition isn't on `origin/<default>` until level *k*'s gate lands. An item with an **unsatisfied `after:` into level *k*** is likewise **NEVER** speculated — its logical-ordering predecessor hasn't yet reached a terminal state, so spawning it would violate the ordering the `after:` edge encodes. **Note:** during level-*k*'s merge-wait, most level-*k* `after:`-targets are still in-flight (`[~]`/`[m]`), not terminal — so this naturally excludes most `after:`-dependent items from speculation; a candidate qualifies via (b) only when *all* of its level-*k* `after:`-targets happen to be terminal already (e.g. a spike that captured a `[v]` verdict before the level's merge gate). **Eligibility is necessary but NOT sufficient:** two items can be edge-independent (no `depends-on`/`after` between them) yet edit **overlapping lines** of the same file — so eligibility gates *spawning*, never *pushing*. The sufficient half is the base-currency check in 3f (step 0.5 below), enforced before any speculative worker's PR is promoted.
- **Isolation invariants hold identically (#17, #10).** Each speculative worker still gets its **own** orchestrator-pre-created worktree on its **own** `build/<slug>` branch (3b) — speculation changes *when* the worker spawns, not the one-worktree-per-item isolation that #17 fixed (no stacked commits on one branch). The write-jail guard (armed by the worktree's own `.build-guard` marker, dropped at 3b creation) + worktree-qualified paths (3c, #10) hold for a speculative worker exactly as for a sequential one (no parent-tree leak).
- **Promotion is gated on currency, not spawn.** A speculative worker that finishes its build does **not** push immediately. It holds at the boundary before 3f until level *k* has merged and its base can be checked for currency (3f step 0.5) — only then is it rebased/respawned as needed and promoted to a real PR. See 4e for how the in-flight speculative set is handed into the next level.
- **Crash-safe sentinel — stamp `speculative: true` (load-bearing for resume).** A speculative worker sits as `[~]` with a worktree but **no `pr:` yet** (it holds before 3f). That state is indistinguishable from a *stuck non-speculative worker* (`[~]`, no `pr:`) — and the Step 1.4 default for such an item is to *reset/re-spawn*, which would **silently discard the speculative worker's real build work**. To make the two distinguishable across a crash, **when the speculative worker is claimed and its worktree is created (3a/3b), stamp a `  - speculative: true` sub-line on the plan item** — same durable-sentinel pattern as `pr:`/`pushed_sha:`, written via `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file`. Step 0.5 reconcile and Step 1.4 resume both key off this sentinel (below) to route a `[~]` + `speculative: true` + no-`pr:` item to the **3f step-0.5 base-currency check** (rebase/respawn as needed) instead of a discard-and-re-spawn. **Clear the sentinel** (remove the sub-line) once the item promotes to a real PR (when `pr:` is recorded in 3f step 3) or merges — past that point it is an ordinary `[~]`/`[m]` item and the `pr:` pointer governs resume. <!-- cite: B.29 class:resume-discards-held-work -->

#### 3a. Claim the item (claim-first), then mark `[~]`

**3a-0. External-gate predicate check (gate-lift by predicate, NEVER by inference) — runs FIRST, before the claim proceeds.** If this item carries a `gate_check:` field (the external-gate predicate — set by `/assess` for an item whose `notes:` hold a cross-plan "don't start until #N lands" gate; see `~/.claude/plan-schema.md` § Optional `gate_check:` field), **evaluate the predicate now and gate the claim on it**:

1. **Run the predicate against the consumable, not the tracker.** `gate_check:` is a command or file-check on the **dependency consumable** (an artifact / file / schema / count) — e.g. `gate_check: "configs/artists.toml lists >=40 artists"`. Evaluate it directly (run the command / check the file). **PASS** → the gate is genuinely lifted; proceed to the claim (3a item 1 below). **FAIL** → the consumable does not yet exist; **do NOT claim or spawn** — park the item (leave `[ ]`, append a one-line `- external gate not yet lifted: <predicate> read false <date>` note) and surface it to the user (skip-for-now / abort), exactly as a `blocked` halt. The gate re-evaluates on the next run.
2. **NEVER infer gate-lift from issue-closed-state.** Do **not** substitute "the gated-on issue #N is closed" for the predicate — **"issue closed" ≠ "dependency consumable exists."** An issue can close one half of its work (data capture) while explicitly deferring the half this item needs (product wiring), so a closed tracker is **not** evidence the consumable exists. This is the **ELT #492 false gate-lift**: #492's gate rode `notes:` as prose, the orchestrator declared *"#380 CLOSED → roster gate lifted"*, but #380 had deferred product wiring (`configs/artists.toml` still listed 3 of 40 artists) — #492 was re-opened on that false premise, re-parked, two prereqs filed (#519/#520), and the leg re-decomposed: the costliest replan of the run. The `gate_check:` predicate exists precisely so the gate-lift decision reads the consumable, not the tracker — run it, don't infer.
3. An item with an external gate in `notes:` but **no `gate_check:`** failed plan-schema **rule 11** (Step 1 validation) and should not have reached here; if one slips through, treat the missing predicate as a halt (surface it) rather than inferring lift from issue state.

(Most items carry no `gate_check:` — this sub-step is a no-op for them. It binds only items with an external/cross-plan gate.)

**Board integration ON** — claiming is the next per-item action (after 3a-0), before the worker spawns (claim-first — canonical: stageFind project `CLAUDE.md` § Task workflow → "Claim first"):

1. **Contention pre-check.** Read this issue's current board state via the adapter (`source "$BOARD_LIB"; board_resolve_item "$BOARD" <gh_issue>`, then read its `.status` and `.["host/Session"]` from `BOARD_ITEMS_JSON` — the single resolved item). If `.status` is already **In Progress** under a *different* `Host/Session` than this run, treat it as a **claim conflict** — halt the item via `decision_sink_ask(<the conflict + the owning Host/Session>, [retry, skip `[-]`, abort], ask-now)` (operator-present → modal `AskUserQuestion` surfacing the identical retry / skip / abort prompt; operator-absent → async decision-issue posted on this item's GH issue, item parks, tick stops for this item). Don't steal another session's claim. (`claim.sh` itself would silently overwrite, so this pre-check is what honors the lock.)
2. **Claim.** Run `"$CLAIM" <gh_issue> --board "$BOARD"` (the entrypoint resolved by the Step 0 probe) — it sets In Progress, stamps this run's `Host/Session` (`$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`), and sets the tmux claim marker in the orchestrator window. If it exits non-zero (`gh` unauthenticated, issue not on the board, network) → **halt the item** (retry / skip / abort); do **not** spawn the worker on an unclaimed item.
   - An item with **no** `gh_issue:` (creation was skipped in 2.5) can't be claimed — note that and proceed unclaimed.
3. **Epic → In Progress + claim-stamp (once per run, if the plan has `epic:`).** When claiming the **first** child of this run, move the epic's board item to **In Progress** if it isn't already **and stamp it with this run's `Host/Session`** — the epic is the lock unit for the *run*, so it must carry a real ownership stamp, not a bare status flip. Via the adapter: `source "$BOARD_LIB"; board_resolve_item "$BOARD" <epic#>; eid="$(board_item_id <epic#>)"; board_set_status "$eid" "$BOARD_OPT_INPROGRESS"; board_stamp "$eid" "$BOARD_FIELD_HOSTSESSION" "<run-stamp>"` — where `<run-stamp>` is the **same `host:sess` value `claim.sh` writes** (`host="$(board_host_label)"` — the shared helper in `workflows/scripts/board/lib/board.sh`, so this doc names it rather than restating its resolution chain — `sess="${CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID:-manual}"`, `stamp="${host}:${sess:0:8}"`); read it back from any child this run just claimed if convenient. Idempotent and cheap — re-stamping an epic this run already owns is a no-op, so do it on the first claim — but **the once-per-run skip latches on success only**: check both adapter calls' exit codes (`board_set_status` / `board_stamp` return non-zero on real failure — e.g. the API-rate-limit exhaustion that also kills claims, foundation#1220). If either exits non-zero → **warn loudly, naming the epic and the failure** (`WARNING: epic #<epic#> In-Progress stamp failed: <error> — will retry at next child claim`), do **not** latch, and **retry the full status+stamp pair at the next child claim** this run makes; skip thereafter only once both calls have succeeded. A run that ends with the stamp never landed **must report the unstamped epic in the Step 6 summary** (the Board line's `epic stamp:` slot) rather than ending clean — a silently-unstamped epic is exactly the unattributable-owner state this stamp exists to prevent, and it also means Step 5's park-back will (correctly) decline to park an epic that doesn't carry this run's stamp. **Why stamp, not just status:** without it, "which run owns this epic" is unknowable, and a crashed run leaves the epic In Progress with no attributable owner (observed: an epic kept a 3-days-dead session's stamp because nothing re-stamped it). The epic's Done transition is the data-driven 4d check; the epic's **park-back** is the new Step 5 step (both below). (Rollup of the epic's own `Status` beyond In-Progress/Done is deferred — see the triage decision's "Open / deferred".)

Then patch the item's checkbox to `[~]`. Lets `git log` and the vault agree on what's in-flight. (Board OFF: just patch `[~]`.)

#### 3b-0. Dep-merge precondition gate (before create — #108)

**A `depends-on` edge requires its target be `[x]` MERGED before this item's worker starts** — the worker must build and self-verify against the dependency's *merged* code, not a pre-merge base. Level ordering (Step 4 merges level *k* before this call for level *k+1*) is the primary guarantee, but it is enforced only by orchestrator sequencing; this gate makes it **mechanical**. For each `depends-on` target of the item, read that dep's **merged head sha** from its plan-note `pushed_sha:` sub-line (the dep sits in a prior, already-merged level, so `pushed_sha:` is recorded) and, **before** calling `worktree.sh create`, verify it has landed:

```
workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh deps-merged <repo-root> <dep-sha,dep-sha,...>
# → {"outcome":"DEPS_MERGED"}  (every sha is an ancestor of origin/<default> — all deps merged)
#   {"outcome":"DEPS_UNMERGED","unmerged":[...]}  (≥1 dep has NOT merged)
# The script fetches origin/<default> first (like create), then tests ancestry. An unknown/
# unfetched sha reads UNMERGED (conservative — never a false green). Branch on .outcome.
```

Only on `DEPS_MERGED` proceed to 3b (`worktree.sh create`). On `DEPS_UNMERGED`, **do NOT create the worktree and do NOT spawn a worker** — escalate `dep-not-merged` (a resume race, a partial merge, or an ordering bug left a dep unlanded; surface it rather than build against a stale base). Items with no `depends-on` (level-0) or only `after:` edges (logical-order, not merge) skip this gate entirely — `dependsOn` is empty. **The 3f unconditional rebase (#525) is not a substitute:** it repairs the branch *textually* at push, which is too late for the worker's own build/self-verify against merged code. (On the Workflow path, `build-level.mjs`'s `driveItem` runs this gate from `item.dependsOn`; see the `dependsOn` arg above.)

#### 3b. Pre-create the per-item worktree (orchestrator-owned, deterministic path)

The orchestrator — **not** the worker — creates one dedicated worktree per item via the deterministic-machinery script, at a **deterministic path** so 3f (push-by-SHA) and 3h (cleanup) reference it without depending on anything the worker reports:

**`repo:` honor point — resolve `<repo-root>` before calling `create`.** By default `<repo-root>` is **the plan's home repo** — resolved explicitly in Step 0's plan-home-repo check (item 5, above), not assumed to be this launch checkout — matching `~/.claude/plan-schema.md`'s own "absent = the plan's home repo" reading. When the item carries `repo:` (`~/.claude/plan-schema.md` § Optional `repo:` field — the kernel-vs-overlay case), resolve `<repo-root>` to a **local checkout of `repo:` instead** — e.g. a sibling checkout at a conventional path, or wherever the operator's environment keeps it — and pass *that* root to `worktree.sh create`, so the item's worktree, branch, and every commit land against the target repo, never the plan's own. **If no local checkout of `repo:` exists, this is a hard block** — surface it via `decision_sink_ask` (missing checkout path + the item) rather than silently falling back to the plan's own repo, which would build the fix in the wrong place. This is a no-op for the common case (`repo:` absent — everything below runs against the plan's own repo exactly as before).

**Backstop — a kernel-classified item that reached build with `repo:` absent (foundation#1050).** When `repo:` is **absent** *and* this checkout carries `claude/CLAUDE.kernel.md` but is **not** the kernel repo itself (`claude/CLAUDE.overlay.md` present — a consumer), defensively resolve-classify the item's `files:` before `create`: source `workflows/scripts/kernel/lib.sh`, load the manifest, and for each path classify `kernel_lib_classify "$(kernel_lib_resolve_for_classify <repo-root> <path>)"` (the symlink-resolving map — see `/assess`'s seam-straddling check). If any file classifies to `kernel`, **hard-block** via `decision_sink_ask` (the item + its kernel-classified files) rather than build kernel content in the plan's home repo — `/assess` should have set `repo: <org>/temperloop` (its resolve-then-classify routing), so an all-kernel item arriving here with no `repo:` is a routing miss that must surface, never silently mis-land (the #1050 failure this backstops: epic #54's worker edits leaked into the vendored `kernel/` subtree before git's "beyond a symbolic link" error caught them). No-op in the kernel repo itself and on any item that already carries `repo:`. **Treat "cannot evaluate" as a HARD ERROR, never as "not kernel" (temperloop#1177):** read the EXIT CODE, not just stdout — **rc 0** = classified (class on stdout), **rc 1** = evaluated fine, nothing matched, **rc 2** = `CANNOT EVALUATE`, the classifier had no answer to give (a failed `kernel_lib_load_manifest`, or a shell whose glob matcher the lib's own selftest rejected). A non-zero load, or **any** rc-2 classify, means this backstop **did not run**: do not proceed to `create` as though nothing classified `kernel` — **hard-block via `decision_sink_ask`** exactly as on a positive kernel hit, naming it `kernel backstop: CANNOT EVALUATE — kernel_lib_classify rc 2 (<stderr line>)` plus the item and its `files:`. An un-evaluable guard must never return the safe answer: before #1177 this call ran under the agent's zsh and answered rc 1 + empty for *every* path, so the backstop passed everything it was built to stop.

```
workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh create <repo-root> <item.slug>
# → {"outcome":"CREATED","path":"<repo>.wt/<item.slug>","branch":"build/<item.slug>","base":"origin/<default>","guard":"ARMED"|"UNARMED"|"UNKNOWN","guard_detail":"registration=… matcher=… bash_arm=… write_arm=…"}
# worktreePath = <repo>.wt/<item.slug>; worktreeBranch = build/<item.slug> (throwaway local
# branch; the plan's branch: is honored at PUSH time). Closed outcome set: CREATED, or
# {"outcome":"ERROR",...} + non-zero exit — branch on .outcome, never parse prose.
```

- The script branches it from `origin/<default>` so it starts clean from `main` (a `depends-on` item's base is `main` *after* that dep merged at the prior level's gate — see 4e; the **3b-0 gate above** enforces that "after that dep merged" precondition mechanically before this create runs).
