- **`create` drops a `.build-guard` marker file in the new worktree root, then PROVES the jail is armed before the worktree is handed to a worker (foundation#1352).** The marker is what arms the write-jail guard (3c) for any worker running in that worktree — per-worktree state, so concurrent sessions on one host arm independently; the orchestrator exports no env var, and `worktree.sh remove`/`prune` clean the marker up with the worktree. But a marker only arms a hook that is actually *reached*, and **every** real failure of this jail has been a reachability failure, not a logic failure: a stale vendored hook body, a `settings.json` matcher still reading `Edit|Write|MultiEdit` (which runs the file-tool jail but leaves worker **Bash** un-jailed — the exact F#932 shape), an unregistered consuming repo, a non-executable hook file, a missing `jq`. Each of those exits 0 silently and is individually invisible, so `create` self-tests **both** halves at the moment it matters: it asserts that a **PreToolUse entry whose matcher covers `Bash` points at an existing, runnable build-worktree-guard hook file** (a correct hook can still be unwired) *and* pipes a **synthetic deny-shaped payload at that registered hook file** — the verbatim F#932 `rm -rf "$(dirname "$(pwd)")"` for the Bash arm plus an out-of-jail `Write` for the file-tool arm — requiring `permissionDecision:"deny"` back from each. **Record the returned `guard` verdict in the item's spawn record** alongside `worktreePath`/`worktreeBranch`, and carry it into the level summary (4a). `ARMED` is the only clean verdict and prints one quiet stderr line; `UNARMED` (nothing registered, the matcher omits `Bash`, the hook file is missing or non-executable, or it silently passed) and `UNKNOWN` each additionally print a **loud** stderr banner naming the remedy. **`UNKNOWN` means "could not prove it", never "probably fine"** — the hook hung, its path was unresolvable, or the arming registration was found *only* in an untracked/gitignored parent-checkout settings file that this worktree does not carry (`registration=parent-only`), so the probe cannot show a worker here loads it; every such ambiguity resolves away from `ARMED`, because asserting safety that isn't there is strictly worse than reporting doubt. Surface a non-`ARMED` verdict in the run output rather than swallowing it, and treat that level as running on Option A (pre-created worktrees) + Option C (parent-clean assertion) alone, the weaker footing 3c's guard-enablement note describes. **The probe never blocks a build:** it is bounded and fails open by construction — and it never executes the registration *string*, only the hook file it names — so a non-`ARMED` verdict is a signal to fix the hook registration, never a reason to halt the item.
- **`create` also self-heals a legacy-tracked `.build-verification.md` (#529).** The verification-surface artifact is meant to be a dev-local, uncommitted file (kept out via `info/exclude`), but in consuming repos that committed it before the exclude existed, `info/exclude` is powerless and every item re-commits it → a multi-item level's serial-merge conflicts on it. So `create` untracks it as its **own** `chore: untrack …` commit on the fresh branch. **Expect that one extra deletion commit** on the first build branch in any such repo — it's intentional machinery hygiene, not the worker's doing; all level branches make the identical removal (merges delete-vs-delete cleanly), and once that repo's `main` is clean the step is a silent no-op.
- The worktree is created in the **parent checkout's** working tree but is a separate checkout dir; the orchestrator itself stays in the parent checkout. Two parallel level-0 workers each get their **own** pre-created worktree on their **own** `worktreeBranch`, so they **cannot** stack commits on one branch or commit onto the parent's branch (the #17 failure mode).
- The plan's `branch:` name is honored at **push** time (3f), not worktree-creation; remote-branch-name collisions are handled there.
- If the deterministic path already exists from a prior aborted run (Step 0.5 didn't prune it), `create` force-removes and re-adds it — the path is a pure function of the slug, so re-deriving it is always safe.
- **Speculative spawn (opt-in optimization on):** when this worktree is being created for a **speculatively pre-spawned** next-level item (per "Speculative next-level execution"), the orchestrator also stamps a `  - speculative: true` sub-line on the plan item now — via `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file`, alongside the `[~]` flip — so a crash between here and the 3f currency check leaves the item legibly distinguishable from a stuck non-speculative `[~]` worker (Step 0.5 / Step 1.4 key off it). The sentinel is cleared when the item promotes to a real PR (3f step 3 records `pr:`) or merges.

#### 3c. Spawn the implementation agent

`Agent { subagent_type: "claude" }` — no `isolation` param; when the item carries a `model:` field, pass it through to the spawn (`Agent { subagent_type: "claude", model: <item.model> }`), and when it's absent pass nothing (the worker inherits the session model — see **Model tiering** below). The worker runs against the **`worktreePath` the orchestrator pre-created in 3b** (a deterministic, orchestrator-owned value — *not* something the worker reports back). The write-jail guard is **already armed** for this worker: `worktree.sh create` (3b) dropped a `.build-guard` marker in the worktree root, and the worker's PreToolUse hook self-arms from that marker (rejecting any write that resolves outside that root — see Option B below); the orchestrator exports nothing. Tell the worker its Bash cwd is `<worktreePath>` and to do all work there.

**Model tiering — tier by measured rounds, not by an assumed gate.** The item's optional `model:` field (`~/.claude/plan-schema.md` § Optional `model:` field, stamped by `/assess` Step 2) is advisory routing for this spawn; the stamp, its carve-outs and the measurement behind it live there, not here. The principle, for this seat and any future seat: a seat moves to a cheaper model only on a **paired measurement** (the `--dual-build` harness, or rounds-to-merge over a window) showing it costs less per merged item — never on the inference that a downstream mechanical gate (CI at 3g, the 3e.5 acceptance gate, the acceptance bullets) will catch a weaker model's mistakes. That inference was the worker seat's original rule and it was falsified: the mistakes surfaced in the advisory §3e reviewers instead, at a full round each (plan-schema § Optional `model:` field). A seat whose output *is* the gate (judgment nothing downstream checks) stays on the session model without needing the measurement.

- **Escalate on retry — a retry never stays on the cheap model.** Any re-spawn of this item after a **`failed` return** (3d) or a **CI failure** on its branch runs on the **top tier** — omit `model:` so the re-spawn inherits the session model — regardless of the item's stamped `model:`. The first failure is evidence the cheap tier was insufficient for this item. Re-spawns that are *not* failures — a `blocked` answer round-trip or a `design-fork` verdict — keep the stamped tier. <!-- cite: B.26 class:weak-model-retry-loop -->
- **Seat tiers (current policy).** Worker seats: per the stamp (`size: S/M` + `kind: code` → `opus`; `kind: spike` or `size: L` → absent, inherit). Review seats: `requirements-auditor`, `workflow-reviewer`, and `docs-reviewer` → `sonnet` (set in their `claude/agents/` frontmatter — their findings are advisory inputs the orchestrator and human filter). Explore-style locate-and-report fan-outs → `haiku` (their structured returns are checked by the caller; the built-in Explore agent has no def file in this repo, so route it at spawn time with `model: haiku` — and set the frontmatter wherever such defs exist downstream, e.g. a consuming repo's `.claude/agents/`, as deploy-time follow-through). `architecture-reviewer` explicitly **pins the strong tier** (`model: opus` in its def) — its boundary calls are judgment whose output *is* the gate, so it is never down-tiered; a declared tier holds no matter which tier the calling drive itself runs on, which `inherit` could not guarantee (temperloop#1456). The orchestrator/assess/triage seats (the `/build`, `/assess`, and `/triage` sessions themselves) always keep the session model — they are sessions, not agent defs, and their judgment is what everything else gates on.
- **Seat scoping — nested review delegation (#225 residual).** A worker MAY delegate a focused review to a **nested** agent for context control (keeping a bulky read-and-judge pass out of its own window) where the harness allows. Known constraint: subagent contexts have historically been unable to dispatch further subagents (observed empirically on stageFind PR #38), so this is an **aspiration gated on harness capability**, not a mandated step — a worker that can't nest does the review inline. **Do not misread this as licensing a context-inheriting research fork (temperloop#1072) — the two are distinct.** BANNED: spawning a context-inheriting `fork` for a narrow READ-ONLY sub-task (gathering conventions, reading code) — a fork inherits this entire prompt, including "implement the item, drive to done, and commit," so a fork spawned for research still carries that mission and may edit, commit, or fabricate a completion report instead of returning findings (observed: temperloop#635, [[Mistakes/foundation - research fork inherits drive-to-done context and commits to shared worktree]]). SANCTIONED: a FRESH, explicitly-scoped read-only subagent (`Explore`/`general-purpose`) with a return-findings-ONLY prompt and no write/commit instructions, or a `fork` whose own prompt explicitly OVERRIDES the inherited mission ("read-only; return findings ONLY; make no edits and no commits") — the nested-review delegation just above is this sanctioned pattern, not the banned one. Treat any nested-agent report as UNTRUSTED until independently re-verified. Both paths carry this as a first-spawn instruction: the Workflow path embeds it in `build-level.mjs`'s `workerPrompt()` (`## No context-inheriting research forks`); the conversational path MUST include it in the **first** worker prompt it authors.

The prompt includes:

- Branch policy from project CLAUDE.md.
- **Effective engineering principles (summary form) — resolved once per run at Step 1.8, embedded here.** The **merge** (kernel set ∪ the item's project's own `## Principles` extension, per `claude/engineering-principles.md`'s own § Merge semantics) is resolved **once per run, point-of-use, never cached across runs**, at **Step 1.8** (above) — not here, and not per item: the Workflow runtime `build-level.mjs` runs on has no filesystem or vault access to do that merge itself (that file's own DESIGN NOTE 1), so Step 1.8 is the only place it can happen, and this step's job is narrower — **embed** the already-resolved set. For each item, take the pair Step 1.8 resolved for `item.repo` (falling back to the run's default pair when the item carries no `repo:` of its own, or when its own `repo:` has no resolved pair) and embed that pair's rendered summary **verbatim, in summary form** — a compact numbered list of principle names/one-line criteria in force, each tagged `[kernel]` or `[project]`, in `build-level.mjs`'s `workerPrompt()` (its own self-contained `## Effective engineering principles` section) on the Workflow path, or the same lookup inline on `--no-workflow`. This is the **same merged set** § 3e hands the pre-push reviewer for this item's `(repo, project)` pair — **reuse Step 1.8's resolution there too; do not re-resolve it at either site.** Tell the worker to weigh its own choices against this set and, if its own summary cites a principle-shaped concern, to name the principle and its origin. **Degradation is the `.mjs`'s job, not this step's:** if `principlesSummaries` never reached it (an absent hand-off — an older orchestrator or a consuming-repo caller; `sweep.md`/`fix.md`, which share `workerPrompt()`, resolve and pass it in their own Step 0 as of temperloop#1460), `build-level.mjs` falls back to its own static kernel-names-only snapshot plus an explicit `DEGRADED` notice — never a silent set that looks identical to "principles applied" (that file's own comment).
- The full item block: title, slug, scope, source wikilink, files, acceptance, notes.
- **Source-doc excerpt:** read the `source:` target (path + heading for a vault source, or `gh issue view <n>` for an issue-ref source) and embed the relevant section verbatim, so the worker doesn't have to chase the reference itself.
- **Worktree-qualified file paths — load-bearing, do NOT skip.** Every repo file path you hand the worker (in `files:`, the scope, examples, verification commands) MUST be written under the pre-created `worktreePath` (`<worktreePath>/evals/scorer.py`), never the bare parent-repo-root path (`/…/<repo>/evals/scorer.py`). The worker's `Edit`/`Write` echo the path you give them; a bare parent-root absolute path resolves against the parent checkout and **leaks an uncommitted write into the orchestrator's tree** even though the worker's Bash cwd is the worktree (the #10 parent-leak failure — history in [[Decisions/foundation - build worker write-isolation (worktree failure history + Option A-D)]]). Because the orchestrator now **pre-creates** the worktree (3b), you **know the exact `worktreePath` up front** — prefix every handed path with it, and additionally tell the worker: "make ALL Edit/Write/path references either relative to your Bash cwd (the worktree root) or absolute under `<worktreePath>/…`; never write to the parent `/…/<repo>` checkout." Relative paths from the worker's Bash cwd are safest. **This prose instruction is now backstopped structurally:** with the `.build-guard` marker in place (dropped by 3b's `worktree.sh create`), the worker's PreToolUse guard *rejects* an out-of-worktree write outright (Option B) — so a path-discipline slip fails loudly at the worker instead of silently leaking into the parent (the prose alone was the discretion this item removed). **The same discipline extends to destructive filesystem commands (F#932).** Tell the worker: any destructive filesystem commands — removing, moving, or overwriting a tree, e.g. scratch cleanup or a stale copy — MUST target an absolute literal path under `<worktreePath>` (or a path relative to your Bash cwd), never a command substitution, a variable expansion, or an uninspected glob; never `rm -rf` a directory you did not yourself create this session; never suppress `rm` errors with `2>/dev/null`; and for a scratch copy, `cp` a single file out to `/tmp` and back rather than copying or deleting a whole tree. The incident that forced this: a worker ran `rm -rf "$(dirname "$(pwd)")" 2>/dev/null`, whose computed target resolved to the parent of every checkout on the host, and whose `2>/dev/null` hid the wipe until it was too late (F#932). This is backstopped structurally by `build-worktree-guard.sh`'s Bash arm (Option B below), which denies a non-literal destructive-command target outright — but two of these rules (session-created-only, and error suppression) are things the guard cannot see, so the prompt instruction carries weight the hook cannot replace. <!-- cite: B.22 incident:F#10 -->
- **Guard enablement (Option B), worker-side — load-bearing.** The PreToolUse hook `build-worktree-guard.sh` (matcher `Bash|Edit|Write|MultiEdit`) is **inert by default** and **self-arms from a per-worktree marker file**: it enforces iff the tool cwd's `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` contains a `.build-guard` marker AND that toplevel sits under a `<repo>.wt/` directory (the deterministic-path convention from 3b). `worktree.sh create` drops the marker; `remove`/`prune` clean it up with the worktree. **The orchestrator exports nothing** — the marker replaces a prior env-var arming (`BUILD_WORKTREE_GUARD`) that was never armable for Agent-tool workers (why: [[Decisions/foundation - build worker write-isolation (worktree failure history + Option A-D)]] §#171). The marker is per-worktree state, so N concurrent build sessions on one host (a shared cron/deploy host) arm independently and correctly. A marker found **outside** the `.wt` convention (a stale or hand-copied marker) makes the hook warn on stderr and fail **open**; a toplevel with no marker leaves the hook inert — so a normal interactive session, even one with the hook installed via the foundation fleet's `make install` (a kernel checkout installs via `workflows/scripts/install-claude-md.sh` / `install-settings.sh`), is unaffected. **Hook *registration* is two distinct surfaces (#72):** (1) for **foundation-repo** runs, foundation's `claude/settings.json` — symlinked to the user-global `~/.claude/settings.json` by `make install-claude` — registers it with an absolute command path; (2) for a **consuming repo** (e.g. stageFind, whose build runs may execute on a different host), the guard is **vendored into that repo** at `.claude/hooks/build-worktree-guard.sh` and registered in the repo's **project-level** `.claude/settings.json` with a repo-relative command path (path-portable across machines). foundation is the source of truth; `make sync-stagefind-hooks` copies the banner-stamped hook (the settings.json registration is a one-time per-repo edit). **A consuming repo with no project-level registration runs the write-jail un-armed** — build then relies on Option A (pre-created worktrees) + Option C (per-worker parent-clean assertion) alone, which is weaker; verify the registration exists before trusting Option B there. The guard denies any `Edit`/`Write`/`MultiEdit` whose resolved absolute path is outside the marker's worktree toplevel — and, on the **Bash** arm (F#932), any destructive filesystem verb (`rm`/`rmdir`/`mv`/`shred`/`truncate`/`dd of=`) whose target it cannot prove stays inside the worktree, including a non-literal target (`$`-expansion, `$(…)` substitution, or glob) it cannot resolve statically — with an allow-list for `/tmp`, `$TMPDIR`, and gitignored source copies (e.g. a `.env` copied in from the parent). **Matcher-registration caveat:** the Bash arm only fires where the hook's settings.json matcher includes `Bash`; a consuming repo still registered with the old `Edit|Write|MultiEdit` matcher runs the file-tool jail but leaves worker Bash un-jailed until its matcher is updated.
- Instruction to commit on the current branch but NOT push and NOT open a PR.
- **Changelog fragment instruction — load-bearing, do NOT skip (temperloop#1530).** Tell the worker: if this item touches contract surface (`VERSIONING.md` § The contract surface), add its own `changelog.d/<slug>.<category>.md` fragment as part of this change, in the same or a follow-up commit on this branch — the same way it is told to run the gates. Point the worker at `changelog.d/README.md` for the filename grammar and body rules; do **not** restate that shape here, so the two surfaces (this prompt and that file) cannot drift out of sync. Name the recorded opt-out too — `Changelog: none — <reason>` as a commit-message trailer, the one escape-hatch channel that works **before a PR exists** (`workflows/scripts/check-changelog-entry.sh`'s own header: a PR label and PR-body line are the other two, both unavailable pre-PR) — so a worker that judges its change genuinely non-shipping records that choice instead of silently omitting the fragment. **Why this belongs on the worker, not a parent-side gate:** the issue's own prose names both options; telling the worker is *prevention* (the fragment lands in the worker's own commit, no round trip) where a parent-side `check-changelog-entry.sh` run before `pr.sh open` would still be *detection* (a re-spawn once it fires, merely earlier than CI) — and the gate itself is not the bug (it already documents that it skips cleanly with no resolvable base), so the fix belongs in the prompt contract, not a second enforcement site. **Do NOT weaken `check-changelog-entry.sh` itself** — this bullet is purely additive prevention; the gate stays the authority. Both paths carry this as a first-spawn instruction, exactly like the clauses above: the Workflow path embeds it in `build-level.mjs`'s `workerPrompt()` (its own self-contained `changelogFragmentSection()`, mirroring `principlesSection()`'s shape); the conversational path MUST include it in the **first** worker prompt it authors. Keep build.md §3c and `workerPrompt()` in lockstep here too (static guard in `test_workflow.sh`). <!-- cite: B.53 incident:temperloop#1663,temperloop#1455,temperloop#1528 -->
- **No issue-closing keywords in commit messages — load-bearing, do NOT skip.** Tell the worker: its commit messages and commit titles MUST NOT contain any GitHub issue-closing keyword (`Closes`/`Fixes`/`Resolves`/`Close`/`Fix`/`Resolve` followed by `#<N>`). Use a plain descriptive message only. **Why:** GitHub scans the messages of all commits merged to the **default branch** for closing keywords, not just the PR body — so a worker's stray `Closes #153` in a commit message auto-closes that issue on merge, even when the PR body omits it (observed: commit `ec8d5fd` carried a `Closes #153` that auto-closed an issue whose *other half* of the work wasn't done). Issue linkage is the **orchestrator's** job and belongs in exactly one place — the PR body's `Closes #<gh_issue>` line (3f) — never a worker commit message. This is backstopped by the orchestrator's pre-push scan in 3f (the prompt prohibition alone was the discretion this rule removed). <!-- cite: B.23 incident:commit-ec8d5fd -->
- **Self-verification requirement:** before returning `done`, the worker must explicitly check each acceptance bullet and report which passed with evidence — **including discrimination evidence that the check can actually FAIL, not just that it passed (temperloop#1319).** A `passed: true` self-report is worthless if the check could never have gone red — a mistargeted assertion or an untouched-path fixture looks IDENTICAL, from the returned verdict alone, to one that genuinely discriminates — so for every bullet the worker also reports, in `acceptance_results[].discrimination_evidence`, at minimum **which mechanism it removed/broke**, **that the suite went RED without it**, and **that restoring it went GREEN** (a criterion genuinely too coarse to discriminate this way says so explicitly rather than leaving the field empty). **Scoped to `/build` only — it does NOT widen to `/sweep` or `/fix`**, which share this file's `workerPrompt()` (`claude/workflows/build-level.mjs`). This is an **operational scope decision, not a structural one — correct the record here** (an earlier version of this bullet claimed the opposite): `claude/commands/sweep.md` and `claude/commands/fix.md` both define `acceptance:` as *"checkable bullets from the issue body"*, falling back to the bare-string placeholder (`"(self-verify the issue is resolved)"`) **only** when the issue body carries none — and `acceptanceList()` (`build-level.mjs`) already handles the array case for any caller. So a real, multi-bullet acceptance array from `/sweep` or `/fix` DOES have exactly the per-criterion shape this field targets; the key genuinely never reaches those callers today only because temperloop#1319 scoped the requirement to `/build`, not because the shape doesn't exist. The gate is the `requireDiscriminationEvidence` input flag (Step 3 `args`, above) — `/build` passes `true`, `/sweep`/`/fix` omit the key. That omission is now a *deliberate* scope choice standing on its own, **not** a side effect of those callers being un-wired generally: as of temperloop#1460 they resolve and pass `principlesSummaries`/`principlesDefaultRepo` (and `machineryBinDir`) on the very same seam, so wiring this flag too is a one-line change whenever temperloop#1319's scope is widened.
- **Never run the BARE, repo-wide gate in the worker's own context — a path-scoped subset only (#997).** The worker's self-verification MUST NOT invoke bare `scripts/quality-gates.sh` (nor a whole-suite `make` equivalent). That suite is minutes-scale (measured ~7 min / 105 gates on this repo) and one blocking turn that long **exceeds the ~5-minute prompt-cache TTL**, so the worker's next API call is a total cache miss: its entire accumulated context is re-*written* at weight 1.25 instead of re-*read* at 0.1, a 12.5x penalty (measured 491,043 excess weighted units on a single item = 22.8% of that level; corpus-wide 49 such misses = 4.84% of all workflow-agent spend). Instead the worker runs the **changed-file-scoped mode** (temperloop#957): `scripts/quality-gates.sh --scoped` resolves the worker's own working-tree changes (committed, staged, unstaged **and** untracked — this runs mid-work, not after a commit) and selects only the gates those paths reach per `workflows/scripts/config/gate-paths.tsv`, plus that map's enumerated **global-by-nature floor** (the completeness / cross-file / total verdicts no glob can scope honestly); every resolution failure widens to the full set, and the run **names each gate it skipped** and stamps its verdict line `[SCOPED SUBSET — NOT a full-suite pass]`, so a green scoped run cannot be misread as a green full one. Where the project's gate script predates that flag, fall back to hand-picking — `scripts/quality-gates.sh --list` prints every gate as a `[layer] <make target>` line — and either way keep **each** foreground verification call to seconds, well inside the TTL; if it cannot cheaply tell which gates apply, it runs none and says so. **What makes that "seconds per call" ceiling OBEYABLE rather than self-contradictory — the worker's scoped run carries a per-invocation BUDGET (temperloop#2145).** Un-budgeted, the two halves of this bullet contradicted each other exactly where it mattered: `--scoped` resolves its gate set from the *diff*, so a diff touching an escalation-row path (`workflows/scripts/config/gate-paths.tsv` and its kin) widens straight back to the full suite and the "scoped" run is minutes-scale again — and the worker had no lever to make it obey. The lever already existed; this was the only invocation not given one. `scripts/quality-gates.sh` implements a soft per-invocation budget (`QUALITY_GATES_BUDGET_SECS`) plus a resume index (`QUALITY_GATES_START_AT`): it stops cleanly *between* gates, with an exit-75 partial protocol and `QUALITY_GATES_RESUME_AT=` / `QUALITY_GATES_FAILED=` stdout markers — the same temperloop#1021 seam §3e.5 already drives slice by slice. So the worker prompt instructs the scoped run **under that budget**: `QUALITY_GATES_BUDGET_SECS=<budget> scripts/quality-gates.sh --scoped`, where `<budget>` is the value the orchestrator resolved from **`${BUILD_GATE_SLICE_SECS:-300}`** at Step 0 item 6 — written in the belt-and-suspenders fallback form its five siblings there already use, and that fallback is **load-bearing, not decoration**: on a checkout that does not vendor `build.config.sh` the Step 0 `source` no-ops, a bare `$BUILD_GATE_SLICE_SECS` expands to the **empty string**, and `scripts/quality-gates.sh` normalises an empty `QUALITY_GATES_BUDGET_SECS` to `0` — which means **no budget at all**, silently reinstating the very unbounded run this bullet exists to bound, on precisely the non-vendoring checkout shape this spec treats as first-class everywhere else — the very same setting §3e.5's own per-slice budget uses (`input.gateSliceSecs` on the Workflow seam; `build-level.mjs`'s in-file default on a checkout that doesn't vendor `build.config.sh`, exactly as Step 0 item 6 notes for its siblings). **Never write that budget's number into this prose or into the worker prompt** — name the setting and leave `workflows/scripts/build/build.config.sh` its single home (`~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` § Named-setting convention). It is an **env var, not a flag**, for the same reason the scoping seam is (§3e.5): a consuming repo vendoring an older `scripts/quality-gates.sh` ignores an unknown env var and runs the suite unbudgeted exactly as before, whereas an unknown *flag* exits 2 `usage` and reads back as a gate **failure**. With the budget the call is bounded **by construction** rather than by the worker's estimate, so the seconds-per-call ceiling is something the worker is *given* rather than something it must guess at — that is the whole resolution. **Exit 75 is a PARTIAL, not a Fail — and the worker DEFERS the remainder rather than looping slices.** A budgeted run that stops with gates remaining exits **75** (never 0, never 1) after printing `PARTIAL — ran gates A..B of N`, `QUALITY_GATES_FAILED=<failures seen in THIS slice>` and `QUALITY_GATES_RESUME_AT=<next index>`. Slice-looping is §3e.5's job, parent-side; the worker never re-runs to exhaustion and never resumes at the next index itself. It reads the markers and splits on the failure count. **`QUALITY_GATES_FAILED=0`** — every gate that ran was green and the rest never ran: report that criterion `passed: true`, with `evidence` naming which gates ran (the `ran gates A..B of N` line) and saying plainly that the repo-wide check was **deferred to the parent-side §3e.5 gate**, and — when `requireDiscriminationEvidence` is armed — `discrimination_evidence` reading exactly `deferred to §3e.5; discrimination not established worker-side`. That is the *same* deferral wording as the bare-suite case below, deliberately: it is the same deferral, to the same actor, for the same reason. **`QUALITY_GATES_FAILED=<n>` with n > 0** — a gate that DID run went red, which is a real red on the worker's own change: fix it and re-run, exactly as for exit 1. A slice that found failures still exits 75 so the remaining gates keep running, so the exit code alone must never be read as "nothing was found". Never report `passed: false` for a merely *partial* run — 3d reads that as `blocked` and stalls the level on a check that was never meant to finish worker-side. **That subset is FAST LOCAL FEEDBACK ONLY — it is NOT the acceptance authority.** §3e.5's parent-side run is — it is the orchestrator's own run against the worker's commit rather than the worker's self-report, which is what the PR #309 silent-red lesson actually turns on. **Be precise about what §3e.5 does and does not catch since temperloop#1663 scoped it.** It changes WHICH gates run, not WHO decides acceptance: a red inside the item's scoped set is caught there and loops back to 3c, costing one worker round-trip — the trade accepted here, because that round-trip is paid only when the gate actually fails, whereas the cache miss was paid on *every* item. A red **outside** that set is no longer caught pre-push at all, because §3e.5 and the worker's own `--scoped` run now resolve the same diff through the same map and therefore select nearly the same gates (§3e.5 adds the terminal-state and freshness gates the worker's mid-work run cannot produce, not a wider path scope). That residual gap is **disclosed and accepted**, not overlooked: it is the same gap CI's own `pull_request` run has carried since temperloop#1024, and the unscoped **`merge_group`** run closes it before anything reaches `main` — later than pre-push, and batched, but before the default branch. If that catch ever fires, the fix is the wrong `gate-paths.tsv` row, not a wider gate. So when an acceptance bullet names the bare repo-wide suite, the worker does **not** run it: it reports that criterion `passed: true` **only** if its targeted subset is green, and its `evidence` must say plainly that the repo-wide check was **deferred to the parent-side §3e.5 gate** (itself diff-scoped since temperloop#1663, so the deferral is to a different actor, not to a wider path scope). Never report `passed: false` for a merely *deferred* criterion — 3d reads that as `blocked` and stalls the level on a check that was never meant to run worker-side. **This is also the exemption for `discrimination_evidence` (temperloop#1319) — distinct from "too coarse to discriminate" (the bullet above): a deferred criterion never ran red-without/green-with at all, having been delegated to a different actor, not judged unable to discriminate.** So when `requireDiscriminationEvidence` is armed, a deferred criterion's `discrimination_evidence` reads exactly `deferred to §3e.5; discrimination not established worker-side` — never left empty (which would misread as the degraded case above) and never fabricated. **Both paths carry this as a first-spawn instruction, exactly like the foreground clause below:** the Workflow path embeds it in `build-level.mjs`'s `workerPrompt()` (inside the same `## Quality gate & long-running work — FOREGROUND ONLY (#1219)` section) and in `FOREGROUND_CURE`; the conversational path MUST include it in the **first** worker prompt it authors. **The BUDGET half of this bullet is not yet in lockstep — disclosed, not overlooked (temperloop#2145).** `workerPrompt()`'s embedded copy still instructs an unbudgeted `--scoped` run, so **only** the conversational (`--no-workflow`) path and any hand-authored first worker prompt carry the budget today. **State that scope honestly rather than letting this read as a system-wide fix:** the per-level Workflow execution path is this command's **DEFAULT** (see the top of this spec), so the unbudgeted copy is what virtually every `/build` run will send its workers the moment that path is reachable again — what ships here is the narrower half. Wiring `workerPrompt()`'s copy is held back solely to avoid colliding with the in-flight rewrite of `build-level.mjs` (temperloop#2141), and is tracked as **temperloop#2147**. **Do not mis-attribute the measured stalls to the Workflow path's own `driveItem`/`RECOVER_DIRTY` auto-resume — they were not on it.** The epic temperloop#2133 level-0 incident ran under `--no-workflow` throughout, because temperloop#2126/#2141 leave the engine over the Workflow tool's script cap; all four stalls were **conversational-path**, with the orchestrator driving each recovery by hand. That inverts the usual reading and makes the gap *more* urgent, not less: the path that has actually been taking this damage is the one now protected, while the path that will carry almost all future traffic is untouched and returns to service the instant temperloop#2141 lands. Because this is a knowingly half-shipped invariant — and unlike every sibling clause in this section it cannot yet carry the usual static lockstep guard — it is pinned instead by a **self-discharging debt guard** in `workflows/scripts/build/tests/test_workflow.sh` (run by `make test-build`): it fails if this disclosure is deleted while the gap is still open, and fails again if `workerPrompt()` closes the gap without retiring the disclosure, so the debt can neither be lost nor quietly outlive its fix. **Ruled out first — a longer per-agent prompt-cache TTL:** the harness has a 1-hour TTL, but every `agent()` call site in `build-level.mjs` passes only `{label, phase, agentType, model, schema}` (`bashTimeoutMs` is prompt *text*, not an agent option), so no per-agent TTL knob is reachable from this repo. <!-- setting-prose:allow — this line names QUALITY_GATES_BUDGET_SECS and QUALITY_GATES_START_AT (both default 0) alongside bare section references ("Step 0 item 6") and gate exit codes, which check-setting-prose.sh's int heuristic reads as a restatement of that default. Neither setting's value is stated here: scripts/quality-gates.sh's own env defaults remain their single home, and the worker's budget is named as ${BUILD_GATE_SLICE_SECS:-300} — the belt-and-suspenders fallback form Step 0 item 6 already uses five times for its own siblings, where the literal is the non-vendoring checkout's safety net rather than a restatement of the default, and build.config.sh remains the value's single home. -->
- **New gate script? Register it first (temperloop#1931).** Right after the `--scoped` bullet above, the worker is pointed — via `build-level.mjs`'s self-contained `gateRegistrationChecklistSection()` (mirrors `discriminationEvidenceSection()`'s shape) — at every registry a new/renamed check-surface, gate, exec-bit entry, coverage-manifest claim, or setting must land in before that run, so an unregistered script is never the omission its own `--scoped` run fails to catch; see that function for the full list, not restated here.
- **Class-A activation-proof pass-through — load-bearing (temperloop#1934).** If the item carries a class-A `activation:` block, the **first** worker prompt you author for it MUST render the item's `activation.proof` verbatim as the reachability predicate §3e.6 will run against this worktree before push — not a paraphrase — and tell the worker to name and wire its own artifacts so that predicate passes, treating the surface it names (a function/symbol, a file path, a config key) as fixed. If the predicate genuinely contradicts the acceptance bullets, the worker returns `blocked` with a question naming the conflict — never a silent rename. An absent `activation` block, or a class B/C block, carries no such instruction. Both paths carry this as a first-spawn instruction, exactly like the clauses above: the Workflow path embeds it in `build-level.mjs`'s `workerPrompt()` (its own self-contained `activationProofSection()`, gated on `activationClass(item) === 'A'`, temperloop#1934); the conversational path MUST include it, verbatim per the item's `proof:`, in the **first** worker prompt it authors for a class-A item. Keep build.md §3c and `workerPrompt()` in lockstep here too (static guard in `test_workflow.sh`).
- **Design-fork escalation — do NOT guess on a contract-changing decision.** If, while implementing, you discover that ≥2 viable designs exist and choosing between them would change this item's *contract or approach* — its interface, schema shape, the meaning of an acceptance bullet, or a no-behavior-change guarantee (e.g. a byte-exactness trap, or an acceptance criterion that assumes a mechanism that turns out not to exist) — do **not** pick one and build it, and do **not** file it as `blocked`. Return `status: "design-fork"` with the `design_fork` object (decision, viable options + trade-offs, your recommendation, the evidence that surfaced it). The orchestrator gets a human verdict and re-spawns you against this same worktree with it appended. Rule of thumb: a *missing fact you could simply be told* is `blocked`; a *design choice a human must own* is `design-fork`.
- **Verification surface (per the project rule) — write it to a FILE, return only the path (#418 inflow-cut).** Assemble a verification-surface markdown block appropriate to the change type — eval/artifact → before/after numbers; scorer/prompt → per-fixture diffs with examples; config → corrected-vs-prior side by side; refactor → explicit no-behavior-change claim + the proving diff/tests. **Write that block to `<worktreePath>/.build-verification.md`** (in the worker's own worktree) and return its path as `verification_surface_path` in the verdict — do **not** inline the block in the verdict JSON. This keeps the large surface OUT of the orchestrator's context: it never round-trips through the worker's return message; `pr.sh open` reads the file directly at 3f (`--verification-surface-file`). Quote the `## PR verification surface` rule (extracted in Step 0 item 4 — global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, or the project `CLAUDE.md` if it overrides) into the prompt so the worker meets it. (Back-compat: an inline `verification_surface` field is still accepted as a fallback — `pr.sh` resolves the surface by precedence file-flag → `verification_surface_path` → inline → recap — but the file path is preferred.)
- **No long-running background work — foreground the gate, never `run_in_background`.** The worker runs **every** verification command it *does* run — the path-scoped gate subset of the bullet above, plus evals, builds, sweeps — in the **foreground**. NEVER launch one with `run_in_background: true`: a subagent's backgrounded process is **reaped when the worker yields**, and there is no `SendMessage` to resume it — a backgrounded gate/eval silently dies. **This clause governs what the worker CHOOSES; it is NOT, on its own, preventive (temperloop#2145).** The `Bash` tool **auto-backgrounds** a command that exceeds its ~590s foreground ceiling — the tool's decision, not the worker's — so "never pass `run_in_background: true`" structurally cannot stop it. A scoped gate that widened to the full suite gets auto-backgrounded, reaped at yield like any other backgrounded process, and the turn ends with **no verdict object at all** and the work stranded uncommitted: the #1219 shape arriving through a door this prohibition does not cover (measured on epic temperloop#2133 level 0 — four such stalls across two items, ~1M subagent tokens, zero commits from those attempts, each stall leaving its gate still running: 14 concurrent `scripts/quality-gates.sh` trees were inventoried, six stacked in one worktree, so the next run there contended with its own predecessors and reported false REDs that both passed in clean isolation with no code change between). What actually prevents it is upstream and mechanical — the `$BUILD_GATE_SLICE_SECS` budget the `--scoped` bullet above puts on the gate, bounding the call below that ceiling by construction. Read the two together: this clause for the worker's own choices, the budget for what the tool would otherwise do anyway. **A turn that ends while awaiting a background-task notification is the #1219 bug, not a valid return** — the notification never reaches a subagent, so the worker hangs and yields no verdict object at all. If one command would exceed the ~10-min `Bash` foreground cap (e.g. a mega-fixture eval) — or the tighter ~5-min prompt-cache TTL budget the bullet above sets — the worker **narrows/splits it, or returns `blocked`/`failed`** and lets the orchestrator run that verification parent-side — it never backgrounds-and-waits. **Both paths carry this as a first-spawn instruction, not merely as prose:** the Workflow path embeds it in `build-level.mjs`'s `workerPrompt()` (the `## Quality gate & long-running work — FOREGROUND ONLY (#1219)` section, included by both the main-worker and spike prompts); the conversational path MUST include this clause in the **first** worker prompt it authors (prevention), not only in the §3d re-spawn cure. **Prose is prevention, not the guard — §3d's mechanical detection is (temperloop#993).** An LLM-executed instruction rots, and this one was ignored twice in a single run (#982, 8 modified files / 0 commits; #983, 3 modified files / 0 commits), so the clause above is deliberately paired with a machine check that needs no worker cooperation: `pr.sh recover-probe` reports the shape (dirty worktree, zero commits) as **`RECOVER_DIRTY`** and `driveItem` auto-resumes on it — see §3d. Never ship one half alone. <!-- cite: B.24 incident:F#1219 -->
- **Gitignored files are absent from the worktree** (e.g. `.env`) — `git worktree add` populates from the git **index**, so a gitignored host-local file is never carried in, on any host, ever. Narrow allowance, unchanged: when a tool needs a *value* in order to **run**, source it into the environment from the parent checkout (`set -a; . <repo>/.env; set +a`) — into the shell env, never onto disk here. (The eval harness loads repo-root `.env` itself per stageFind#203, but other tools may not.) **Two hard limits on that allowance (temperloop#1182):** (i) never `cp`/write a host-config or secret file **into** the worktree, and never go hunting for a credential's value elsewhere on the host — a secret landing in a non-gitignored path is the exposure the `/assess` A.8 host-config seam exists to prevent, and a strictly worse failure than any unverified criterion; (ii) never use that allowance to **confirm an acceptance criterion** — see the deferral bullet below.
- **Host-config / secret acceptance criteria are DEFERRED by the worker and verified PARENT-SIDE by the orchestrator (temperloop#1182).** An acceptance bullet that turns on a gitignored, host-local file — a credential file such as `workflows/scripts/build/build.config.local.sh`, an operator-placed secret, an env var sourced from one — is **structurally unverifiable from a worktree**: the reading is absent/`false` in *every* worktree on *every* host regardless of the truth, so it is a **guaranteed false negative**, not an intermittent one. Measured instance (foundation#1556): an item whose acceptance was *`pipeline-retro-judge-spawn.sh --dry-run` reports `credential_present`* read `credential_present: false` in the worktree and `true` in **both** real checkouts moments later; the worker escalated `acceptance-incomplete` over nothing. Worse, the "helpful" resolution — copying the token in — is exactly limit (i) above. So the contract is:
  - **The worker reports such a criterion DEFERRED, never passed.** It sets `passed: false` (it did not confirm it) **and** `deferred_host_config: <the file / env var>`. That **pair** is the marker; neither half alone changes anything (a bare `passed: false` still reads as blocked per 3d, and the marker alone never manufactures a pass). It must not claim to have confirmed what it structurally cannot see, nor set the marker to route around a criterion it merely failed to meet. **3d then treats a marked deferral as neither pass nor failure**, so it does not stall the level (see 3d's `passed: false` bullet). Implemented, not merely described, on the Workflow path: `build-level.mjs`'s `hostConfigDeferralSection()` embeds the instruction in every worker prompt (**ungated, and every consumer has a verification seat** — the worktree-vs-index fact holds on `/build`, `/sweep` and `/fix` alike, so all three embed it; the parent-side seat that settles the resulting deferral is **§4a here**, the **per-chunk merge pass** in `claude/commands/sweep.md`, and the **Step 5 modal gate** in `claude/commands/fix.md`. Keep those three in lockstep with this section: an ungated instruction whose path has no seat would let a deferral auto-merge unverified), `isHostConfigDeferral()` excludes the pair from 3d's `anyFailed`, `driveItem` logs a named warning at 3h, and the parked record carries `host_config_deferrals: [{criterion, host_config}, …]` (present only when non-empty). `pr.sh`'s acceptance recap renders each as a `DEFERRED — host-config …` line, so an unchecked box in the PR body cannot be misread as a worker failure. The conversational path MUST include the same instruction in the **first** worker prompt it authors. **The orchestrating session then verifies each deferral itself, in the real checkout, at §4a** — before that item is eligible for the merge set; it runs in a checkout that *does* carry the gitignored file, so the check that is meaningless worker-side is trivial there. **None of this relaxes the A.8 confirmed-set bar:** `/assess`'s host-config seam still demands *confirmed set*, never merely *location named*. What changes is **who** confirms, not **whether** — and never by carrying the named file into the worktree.
- **Return contract — a compact structured verdict, as the worker's FINAL message.** End the turn with exactly the fenced JSON block below and **nothing after it** — no prose transcript, no narration of the build. This object *is* the worker's report: the orchestrator drives **every** state transition in 3d (and the PR body in 3f) from these fields alone, so a tight, fully-specified verdict keeps the orchestrator reasoning over a small object instead of ingesting a transcript — which is what bounds orchestrator-context growth across a long run. Return the smallest object the status requires: populate the fields marked **required when status=…** for the returned status, and omit the rest. **Shape is schema-enforced; SIZE is not, so it is bounded here (temperloop#1080):** a JSON schema fixes a field's type and can never fix a string's length, and the unbounded prose slots measured 2-4x past what this spec asked for across 83 real worker verdicts (`summary` ran to a median 119 words against "1-3 sentences"; per-criterion `evidence` to a median 33 against "`file:line` or test name") — every word of it an output token the orchestrator then ingests. So `summary` is capped at `$BUILD_WORKER_SUMMARY_MAX_WORDS` words and each `evidence` / `failure_reason` / `design_fork` free-text slot at `$BUILD_WORKER_EVIDENCE_MAX_WORDS` (both resolved at Step 0 item 6; `build-level.mjs` interpolates them into the worker prompt's `## Output shape` section — the two surfaces are one contract and move together), and **no process narration** belongs in the return value at all: what the worker read, ruled out, or tried first is not a finding. That is a routing rule, not information loss — the worker's full argument goes to the verification-surface FILE, which 3f splices into the PR body by path without it ever entering orchestrator context.

  **Field ownership — what the *worker* sets vs what the *orchestrator* writes:**
  - The worker owns **only** the fields below. It does **not** set `branch`, `pr`, or `pushed_sha` — the orchestrator owns the branch (3b), and records `pr:`/`pushed_sha:` itself on push (3f). The worker surfaces `commits` (the sha array it produced); the orchestrator pushes those and writes the PR pointer.
  - The `status` enum is **closed** at exactly the four variants below, and each maps 1:1 to a handling branch in 3d — `done` → 3d (all-passing → 3e; any `passed:false` → blocked re-loop), `blocked` → questions loop, `design-fork` → verdict loop, `failed` → retry/skip/abort. Never return a status outside this set; if you can't pick one, you're in `blocked` (missing fact) or `design-fork` (design choice a human must own). <!-- cite: B.25 class:half-specified-contract -->

  ````
  ```json
  {
    "status": "done" | "blocked" | "design-fork" | "failed",   // required ALWAYS — closed enum, 1:1 with 3d branches
    "summary": "<what changed + why it satisfies the item>",   // required when status=done — OUTCOME only, no process narration; ≤ $BUILD_WORKER_SUMMARY_MAX_WORDS words
    "acceptance_results": [                              // required when status=done — one entry per acceptance bullet
      { "criterion": "<verbatim bullet>", "passed": true, "evidence": "<file:line / test name / command+verdict — a POINTER, not the argument; ≤ $BUILD_WORKER_EVIDENCE_MAX_WORDS words>", "discrimination_evidence": "<mechanism removed/broke + RED without it + GREEN restored — a pointer, ≤ $BUILD_WORKER_EVIDENCE_MAX_WORDS words; required on /build (requireDiscriminationEvidence), temperloop#1319>" }
    ],
    "commits": ["<sha>", "..."],                         // required when status=done — worker-produced shas; orchestrator pushes them (3f)
    "verification_surface_path": "<path>",               // required when status=done — path to <worktreePath>/.build-verification.md (the surface block, written to a FILE so it stays OUT of orchestrator context); pr.sh open reads it via --verification-surface-file (3f). Legacy inline "verification_surface":"<markdown>" still accepted as a fallback.
    "questions": ["...", "..."],                         // required when status=blocked — one self-contained question per entry, no preamble/recap
    "design_fork": {                                     // required when status=design-fork — each free-text slot ≤ $BUILD_WORKER_EVIDENCE_MAX_WORDS words
      "decision": "<the design question whose answer changes the item's contract/approach>",
      "options": [ { "label": "<option>", "tradeoff": "<what it costs / implies>" } ],
      "recommendation": "<which option you'd pick + one-line why>",
      "evidence": "<file:line / test / constraint that surfaced the fork>"
    },
    "failure_reason": "..."                              // required when status=failed — the blocking fact, not the attempt; ≤ $BUILD_WORKER_EVIDENCE_MAX_WORDS words
  }
  ```
  ````

  Fields the worker **never** sets (orchestrator-owned, listed here so the boundary is explicit): `branch` (3b), `pr` / `pushed_sha` (recorded in 3f). The shape above is **contract-stable** — the orchestrator's 3d match keys on `status` plus the per-status fields, so a worker emitting the prior shape (no `required when` annotations — those are documentation, not new keys) parses identically. The `//` comments inside the block aren't valid JSON anyway — any parser strips them; they exist for the worker-as-reader, not the orchestrator-as-parser — so the annotations are purely additive.

  **On the Workflow path, this object is the `agent({schema})` JSON schema — machine-validated by the runtime, not hand-parsed.** The same closed contract above is encoded as **`WORKER_VERDICT_SCHEMA`** in `claude/workflows/build-level.mjs` (the schema passed to every worker `agent({schema})` call): the runtime *validates the worker's return against it* and hands `driveItem` a typed object rather than free text, so a malformed verdict fails structurally at the workflow boundary instead of mis-parsing. The conversational path (`--no-workflow`) parses **this same JSON by hand** from the worker's final message — same shape, same `status` enum, same per-status required fields; the only difference is *who* validates it (the runtime on the default Workflow path, the orchestrator's own read otherwise). `WORKER_VERDICT_SCHEMA` is the single as-built source — keep this prose contract and that schema in lockstep (a new field/status added to one must be added to the other).

#### 3d. Handle the agent's return (synchronous — halts the level)

Branch **solely on the worker's returned verdict object** (3c) — its `status` plus the per-status fields — never on prose it emitted. The four branches below are the **closed, 1:1 image** of the 3c `status` enum (`done` / `blocked` / `design-fork` / `failed`): every enum variant has exactly one branch here, and every branch consumes exactly one variant's required field(s). Keep them in lockstep — adding a status to one without the other is the half-specified-contract bug this gate exists to prevent.

**First, the verdict object must EXIST (#1219).** Branching *solely on the verdict object* presumes there **is** one. If the worker's final message carries **no parseable verdict JSON with a `status` in the closed enum** — the common cause is a worker that backgrounded a gate/eval (a `run_in_background: true` `quality-gates.sh`) and ended its turn awaiting a notification that never arrives (§3c **No long-running background work**) — do **NOT** wait on it and do **NOT** invent a status. Treat it as an **incomplete return**: re-spawn the same worker **once** against the same `worktreePath`, **on the item's stamped `model:` tier** (this is a mis-applied *instruction*, not evidence the tier was too weak, so the escalate-on-retry top-tier bump does **not** apply to this first re-spawn), with an explicit instruction to *run the gate in the FOREGROUND and end the turn with the fenced verdict JSON and nothing after it*. If the second return is still non-conforming, handle it as **`failed`** with a synthesized `failure_reason` of `"worker returned no parseable verdict after a foreground-instructed re-spawn (#1219)"` (there is no verdict object to source one from) — i.e. enter the `failed` branch below; its own retry/skip/abort re-spawns then follow the normal escalate-on-retry top-tier rule. This is a guard on the **presence** of a valid object, not a branch on emitted prose — so the "branch solely on the verdict object" invariant is preserved. (On the Workflow path this case is handled structurally: `workerPrompt()` embeds the `## Quality gate & long-running work — FOREGROUND ONLY (#1219)` contract in **every** worker prompt (prevention), `agent({schema: WORKER_VERDICT_SCHEMA})` validates every return, and on a `null` verdict `driveItem` **retries the main worker once with `FOREGROUND_CURE` appended** — so the retry prompt DIFFERS from the first attempt, curing the stall rather than re-stalling identically — **then escalates `worker-error`** (`build-level.mjs` ~644–656); the read-only spike path is covered by the same embedded foreground contract and escalates a `null` immediately with no retry (`build-level.mjs` ~555–557). Keep this guard, the `workerPrompt` foreground section, and `FOREGROUND_CURE` in lockstep per the schema↔prose mandate above.) **Conversational-path re-spawn hazard — confirm the first worker is dead before re-spawning.** A worker that *looks* stalled may still be alive and commit late; re-spawning a second worker against the same worktree then races the first (observed 2026-07-24, resolved by `TaskStop` + adopt, no loss). Before the conversational re-spawn above, verify the first worker has actually terminated (`TaskStop` it, or confirm its task is done) — never re-spawn onto a live worker's worktree. This is a conversational-path hazard only: `build-level.mjs`'s retry `await`s the `null` return first, so its re-spawn is strictly sequential and cannot race.

**Before you classify an absent return as a failure, LOOK (#939).** A missing verdict is a failure of the **return channel**, which says nothing about the **work** — in the #939 run it hit 2 of 5 workers, one of which had already committed, pushed, opened its PR and gone green. So the *first* action on an absent return (before the re-spawn above) is a **staged probe of the observable side-effects**, never an escalation and never a re-spawn: (1) does the worktree hold commits ahead of `origin/<default>`; (2) is the branch on `origin`; (3) is there an **open PR** for `item.branch`. If **nothing** landed, the incomplete-return handling above applies unchanged. If anything did, **reconstruct** the record from that ground truth and finish the machinery from whatever stage it reached — **do not re-spawn the worker** (it would build on a worktree that already holds the finished commit) and **do not open a second PR** (adopt the existing one). What is genuinely unrecoverable is the worker's own self-check, so the reconstructed record marks **every acceptance criterion UNVERIFIED — never passing** — and the orchestrator re-verifies them itself before the merge gate. (On the Workflow path this is structural: `pr.sh recover-probe <worktreePath> <branch>` is the deterministic ladder — `RECOVER_NONE` / `_DIRTY` / `_COMMITTED` / `_PUSHED` / `_PR_OPEN` — `driveItem` runs it on any absent return, `callWorker()` catches the **thrown** StructuredOutput-absent case that is not a `null`, and the parked record carries `acceptance_unverified: true` + `recovered_from:`. An already-pushed recovery **skips the 3f-0a rebase**, whose rewrite would make the following push a non-fast-forward. Keep this paragraph, `pr.sh recover-probe`, and `driveItem`'s recovery branch in lockstep.)

**The dirty-worktree rung — mechanical detection of the background-stall shape (temperloop#993).** The probe's stage-0 bucket is split in two, because "the worker touched nothing" and "the worker has real work on disk it never committed" want opposite handling. **`RECOVER_DIRTY`** = no verdict **AND** the worktree dirty (`git status --porcelain` non-empty — tracked edits or untracked files, but with 3b's own `.build-guard` marker excluded by pathspec, since that is orchestrator machinery and counting it would make every fresh worktree read dirty) **AND** zero commits ahead of `origin/<default>` **AND** no PR — the exact signature of a worker that backgrounded the gate and got reaped mid-flight (the §3c prevention clause's failure mode; observed on #982 and #983). It is **not** a *landed* stage — nothing is committed, so there is nothing to push or open a PR from, and it never produces a reconstructed parked record. Its disposition is instead an **auto-resume, not an escalation**: re-spawn on the **same `worktreePath`** (the harness has no resume-this-agent seam, so the worktree *is* the inherited context) with the foreground cure **plus** a dirty-resume note naming the uncommitted file count and telling the worker to read `git status`/`git diff` and continue from that work rather than rebuild it — which is exactly the recovery that worked live on both incidents. `RECOVER_NONE` (clean tree, nothing anywhere) keeps its unchanged one-retry-then-escalate handling. If the auto-resume itself returns no verdict and the tree is *still* dirty with zero commits, the `worker-error` escalation payload carries `shape: "foreground-stall"` + `dirty_files` + `worktree` — load-bearing, because the escalation's **skip** option prunes the worktree and would destroy that uncommitted work unseen. `dirty` / `dirty_files` ride **every** probe outcome; only stage 0 branches on them.

Each re-spawn is a **fresh** `Agent`, re-run against the **same pre-created `worktreePath`** from 3b (its `.build-guard` marker persists with the worktree, so the guard stays armed — no re-arming step) — there is no `SendMessage` to continue the prior one, but the worktree persists between spawns (the orchestrator only removes it in 3h or on abort). A re-spawn that must build on already-committed work is told to `git fetch origin <item.branch> && git reset --hard FETCH_HEAD` first (only meaningful once 3f has pushed that branch); pre-push re-spawns just start fresh from the worktree's `origin/<default>` base. Re-spawn model tier follows the 3c **escalate-on-retry** rule: after a `failed` return (or a CI failure), re-spawn on the top tier (omit `model:`) regardless of the item's stamped `model:`; `blocked`/`design-fork` re-spawns keep the stamped tier.

- **`blocked`** — surface the questions via `decision_sink_ask(<the worker's `questions[]`>, <one choice per question / freeform answer>, ask-now)` (operator-present → modal `AskUserQuestion`, the identical prompt as before; operator-absent → async decision-issue posted on this item's GH issue, item parks, tick stops for this item — the drain step translates the operator's typed reply into a `## User answers` block, and the next tick's re-spawn picks it up as if the modal path had answered), then re-invoke `Agent` with the original prompt plus a `## User answers` section. Loop until status changes.
- **`design-fork`** — the worker hit a contract-changing design decision (see the 3c escalation rule). **Branch on operator-presence:**
  - **Operator-present:** surface via `decision_sink_ask(<`design_fork.decision`>, <one option per `design_fork.options` entry (label + trade-off), with the worker's `recommendation` as the first/recommended option>, ask-now)` — modal `AskUserQuestion`, the operator sees the identical option set. **Then, AFTER the ask returns** (the seam carries only the ask), **capture the verdict durably *before* re-spawning** so a resume or re-spawn never re-litigates it: a one-line stamp on the plan item's `notes:` for a local call, or — for an architectural / load-bearing fork (a schema, a module seam, a public contract) — a `Decisions/` note per the global decision-capture rule, linked from the item. Then re-invoke `Agent` against the **same `worktreePath`** (the `.build-guard` marker is still in place, so the guard stays armed) with the original prompt plus a `## Design verdict` section. Loop until status changes. The item stays `[~]` throughout — a design fork halts the *level* (like `blocked`) but keeps the *build* delegated to the worker; this is what prevents the drop-to-manual context blowup ([[Mistakes/foundation - build design-fork drops to manual, orchestrator context blowup]]). (Order preserved: `decision_sink_ask` → durable verdict capture → re-spawn.)
  - **Operator-absent (v1 exclusion — async backend does NOT apply here):** a live worktree cannot be held across a cron gap. Do NOT open a decision issue. Instead: (1) stamp the plan item's `notes:` field with the fork's decision question and offered options (so the next claim has the context it needs); (2) move the item back to **Ready** via the board adapter (`board_set_status "$(board_item_id <n>)" "$BOARD_OPT_READY"`); (3) remove the `.build-guard` marker from the worktree and discard the worktree; (4) stop processing this item for this tick. The item will be claimed fresh on the next operator-present run or claim, which will see the `notes:` stamp and surface the fork via the modal path. Log: `design-fork on operator-absent run — parked back to Ready (worktree discarded); verdict stamped in notes: for re-spawn.`
- **`failed`** — surface via `decision_sink_ask(<`failure_reason`>, [retry, skip this item, abort the run], ask-now)` (operator-present → modal `AskUserQuestion`, the identical prompt as before; operator-absent → async decision-issue posted on this item's GH issue with the failure reason and the three options, item parks, tick stops for this item):
  - **Retry** (re-spawn with failure context)
  - **Skip this item** — discard the worker's worktree (`workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh remove <repo-root> <item.slug>` — removes worktree, branch, and marker), mark `[-]` on plan, move on to the next item. **Read the escalation's `committed_work` field first** (temperloop#2020, the same guard `fix.md` §4a step 4 states in full — this is a pointer, not a second spec): `build-level.mjs` pushes an escalating item's branch to `origin` before returning, at one choke point covering every escalation kind, and records the result on the payload. `preserved: true` means a remote copy exists and this removal can only destroy a local one; `preserved: false`, `WORK_PRESERVE_FAILED`, `SPINE_DENIED`, or an absent field (an older workflow) all mean **the worktree may be the last copy** — report that and keep it rather than discarding on a skip.
  - **Abort the run** — clean up before yielding (per *park, don't abandon*): remove every worktree captured this run (`workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh remove <repo-root> <slug>` per item — worktree, branch, and marker, and per item **subject to the same `committed_work` read as Skip above**), run `"$RELEASE"`, and leave any already-pushed PRs as `[m]`/`[~]` with their `pr:` recorded. The level merge gate does not fire on aborted runs. Do **not** leave worktrees + claims dangling — that strands board items In Progress under a dead session and orphans locked worktrees (Step 0.5 reconciles these on the next resume).
- **`done` with any `passed: false`** — treat as `blocked`; ask user whether to accept partial completion or send the worker back. **One exclusion, and only one (temperloop#1182): a host-config DEFERRAL** — an entry carrying `passed: false` **together with** a non-empty `deferred_host_config`. That pair is not a failure; it is a criterion the worker structurally could not observe from a worktree, re-homed to the orchestrator's own parent-side check at §4a. Escalating it would stall the level on a reading that is `false` in every worktree on every host regardless of the truth (foundation#1556). The exclusion is the **pair**, never the boolean alone — a bare `passed: false` with no marker blocks exactly as before, so this can never swallow a real failure. Such an item still parks, but its criteria are **UNVERIFIED** (the `acceptance_unverified` family): §4a MUST verify them before the item joins the merge set.
- **`done` with all passing** — proceed to 3e.

#### 3d-esc. Escalation-resume continuation loop (Workflow path — the default)

When the level runs via the **Workflow path** (the default; the `build-level.mjs` Workflow — item #426), the per-item driver (`driveItem`) returns `escalations: [{slug, kind, payload}]` instead of halting inline (the item level escalates rather than the parent session). At the **level boundary**, after `parallel()` collects all results, the orchestrator handles the batched escalation set as follows. This is the seam that keeps the orchestrator context bounded to one small `{parked, escalations}` object per level even when some items need a human round-trip.

**Orchestrator↔workflow boundary (state it explicitly).** On the Workflow path (the default), responsibilities split on a hard line:
- **The Workflow (`build-level.mjs`) owns 3a–3h for one level** — claim, worktree, worker spawn, review, acceptance gate, push-by-SHA, PR open, CI poll, park — driven via the `runMachinery` bridge + `agent({schema})` worker, with `worktree.sh`-created worktrees and the `.build-guard` write-jail (NOT `isolation:'worktree'`). It **returns** `{parked, escalations}` and **NEVER writes the plan note** and **NEVER merges** (so **Step 3h.5 never runs on this path** — temperloop#1452; its scope paragraph carries the reasoning and the accepted trade-off) — escalated items keep their worktrees intact for the orchestrator to re-drive; the workflow removes no worktrees.
- **The conversational orchestrator (this session) retains, on the Workflow path:** (1) **Step 4 (the merge gate) in full** — regime detection, the timed/modal approval, the NATIVE/MANAGED backend merge paths, the deferred `## Questions` drain (4f), and the 4d Done-moves / epic-close; (2) **ALL plan-note writeback, serialized as a single writer** — the `[~]`/`[m]`/`[>]`/`[x]`/`[v]`/`[-]` sentinel flips, `pr:`/`pushed_sha:` sub-lines, the `## Run status` dashboard, and the 3d-esc verdict blocks — the workflow returns *what* to write and the orchestrator writes it, one `vault_patch` per item in sequence (concurrent writes race); and (3) **escalation resolution** — the 3d-esc continuation loop below (surface the batched escalations, capture verdicts durably, re-invoke the workflow scoped to `onlySlugs`).

This split is what bounds orchestrator context: the workflow absorbs the per-item machinery/worker churn, the orchestrator reasons only over the `{parked, escalations}` summary plus its own merge + writeback + escalation duties.

**Re-derivation inputs (checkable, no live workflow state required).** A continuation is fully re-derivable from two durable sources only:

1. **Plan-note level sentinels + `pr:` sub-lines** — which items are `[~]` (worktree intact, no PR yet), `[m]` (worktree removed, PR parked), which hold `speculative: true` (held before the 3f currency check), and which hold **`escalated: true`** (worktree intact, verdict block appended, awaiting continuation — the discriminator stamped in step 3 below). These sentinels survive a crash and are the authoritative status. A `[~]`+no-`pr:`+`escalated: true` item unambiguously identifies an escalated item awaiting continuation (vs. `speculative: true` for a held speculative worker, and no sentinel at all for a stuck worker).
2. **The appended verdicts map** — the `## User answers` / `## Design verdict` blocks appended to the plan note per the durable-capture step below. These carry the human's choice for each escalated slug.

No in-memory workflow state is needed. A resumed orchestrator reconstructs the continuation by: reading the plan note, finding `[~]` items with **`escalated: true` and no `pr:`** (the durable sentinel stamped in step 3 below — see "Stamp `escalated: true`"), cross-referencing the verdicts map for their slugs, and re-invoking the workflow with those slugs as `onlySlugs`. The `escalated: true` sentinel (stamped in step 3 when the verdict block is appended) is the unambiguous discriminator: a `[~]`+no-`pr:`+no-`escalated:` item is a stuck non-speculative worker (ask resume/reset); a `[~]`+no-`pr:`+`speculative: true` item is a held speculative worker (3f currency check); a `[~]`+no-`pr:`+`escalated: true` item is an escalated item awaiting continuation (re-enter 3d-esc). The `pr:` sentinel (written in 3f step 3) is what distinguishes "worktree intact, escalated" from "PR already open, just watching CI" — never conflate the two.

**Step-by-step loop:**

1. **Partition the level result.** From the workflow's return `{parked, escalations}`:
   - **`zeroDisposition` present → partition NOTHING yet (temperloop#2004).** Both arrays are empty for a set that was asked to drive at least one slug, so there is nothing to partition and no conclusion this return supports. Take Step 3's **third arm** (above) — run each named slug's `reprobe`, dispose on what the reads say — before any sentinel write. This matters most **here**, in the continuation loop: the observed case was a resumed run that returned an empty object having re-run nothing, which is exactly the shape step 5's "loop terminates when `escalations` is empty" would otherwise read as success.
   - `parked` items — already drove to `[m]`; write their plan-note sentinels now (`[m]` + `pr:` + `pushed_sha:` sub-lines, per 3h step 1), remove their worktrees (3h step 3), and release their claim markers (3h step 2). **Serialize these sentinel writes — one `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_patch` per item in sequence** (concurrent `patch_vault_file` on one note races and silently drops a write, per the within-level coordination rule). These siblings are **done for this level boundary** and must not be re-driven by any continuation.
   - `escalations` items — **leave their worktrees intact** (the workflow guarantees this); their plan-note sentinels stay `[~]` (no `pr:` sub-line, since 3f never ran for them). They re-enter the loop below. **Quota-death cross-check (temperloop#1819):** before step 2 groups by kind, read the Workflow tool result's own `<failures>` block — the harness's out-of-band agent-failure lines, a channel the orchestrator sees and the workflow script cannot. An escalated item still reported as `machinery-denied` or `worker-error` whose matching `<failures>` line carries the harness session-limit text (`You've hit your session limit · resets …`) is a quota death the in-process classifier missed (its agent-liveness canary can be inconclusive), so **reclassify it to `quota-exhausted`**, carrying that line's reset time into the payload's `reset_time`, before surfacing — the #1819 channel collapse was exactly this: the real cause lived only in `<failures>` while the workflow's return value named two kinds whose documented cures (rewrite the command / re-drive from scratch) are both wrong for, and destructive to, a quota death whose work is sitting intact in the worktree.

2. **Surface as one batched `decision_sink_ask(...)` per escalation kind.** Group by `kind` for clarity; within a group, one option-set per slug. Operator-present → modal `AskUserQuestion`, each `decision_sink_ask(...)` here surfaces the **identical batched modal prompt** as before. Operator-absent → async decision-issue per slug (one issue per escalated item with the kind + payload, `decision` label, operator assigned, item parks) — except `design-fork` slugs on an operator-absent run: those follow the v1 exclusion (park to Ready, worktree discarded, `notes:` stamped) instead of posting a decision issue. The seam carries only the ask; the durable verdict capture (step 3) and the `escalated: true` stamp happen **after** it returns (modal) or after the drain step translates the reply (async), never inside the seam call. **Serialization order (preserved, load-bearing — per [[Decisions/foundation - Autonomous pipeline driver + GitHub decision queue]] CORRECTION):** for each slug the `decision_sink_ask` (this step) **precedes** the verdict-block append (step 3) **precedes** the `escalated: true` stamp (step 3). Present `payload` verbatim (the worker's `design_fork` or `questions` object, depending on `kind`):
   - `design-fork` — surface via `decision_sink_ask(<`design_fork.decision`>, <options from `design_fork.options`, the worker's `recommendation` pre-selected>, ask-now)`. One question per slug (the design decisions are item-specific and must not be merged across slugs — a human must ratify each separately).
   - `blocked` — surface the `questions[]` array via `decision_sink_ask(<the `questions[]`>, <freeform / per-question answer>, ask-now)`; one ask per slug (or a single multi-slug batch when the questions are identical and an answer applies to all — judge by the payload).
   - `failed` — surface `failure_reason` via `decision_sink_ask(<`failure_reason`>, [retry, skip, abort], ask-now)`. A **skip** here marks the item `[-]` and prunes its worktree; an **abort** cleans up as in 3d-failed above.
   - `stale-worktree` (temperloop#1937 — the pre-§3e.5 freshness rebase onto `origin/main` hit a real content clash; `pr.sh`'s own rebase already `git rebase --abort`s on conflict and this step does the identical thing, so the worktree is left intact on its pre-rebase commit and the gate never ran — **never** `acceptance-gate-failed`, since there is no gate verdict to report. The payload names the conflicting files, git's own rebase output tail as `detail` (round 3), and the rebase disposition. The useful dispositions are **retry after resolving the rebase by hand** (fetch, rebase onto `origin/main`, resolve, push — then re-drive) **or skip**. Round 2 adds a second arm, `payload.reason: "timeout"`: the OUTER Bash-tool timeout killed the freshness step itself, possibly mid-rebase — a follow-up probe checks for and aborts any in-progress rebase (`payload.rebase_in_progress` / `payload.aborted` say what it found — round 3: SNAKE_CASE, and **only trusted when the probe itself actually resolved**; if the probe's OWN outer timeout or inner watchdog fires first, both fields are `null` and `payload.disposition` says the rebase state is UNKNOWN rather than falsely asserting nothing was in progress) before this same kind is raised, so a timeout is **never** treated as the fail-open `FRESHNESS_ERROR` path; the disposition is the same retry-or-skip. Round 3 adds a third arm, `payload.reason: "rebase-failed"`: the rebase failed for a reason OTHER than a content conflict (a rejecting `pre-rebase` hook, a missing commit identity, a leftover in-progress rebase) — `conflict_files` is always `[]` on this arm (never fabricated), and `detail` carries git's own output; the disposition is the same retry-or-skip, since the worktree's base is still what needs resolving either way), `ci-failed`, `ci-poll-bad-argument` (temperloop#2014 — `ci-poll.sh` refused to RUN on its arguments, or the driver's own pre-flight refused to spawn it: **not a CI verdict**, so never dispose it as a red PR. The payload's `stage` says where the hand-off broke (`push-to-poll-handoff` — push and PR-open both succeeded but reported no usable head SHA; `ci-fix-push` — the CI-fix re-push did; `ci-poll` — `ci-poll.sh` itself rejected an argument, with its own object on `ciOut`), and `sha_seen` shows the literal value the poll would have received. The PR named in `pr` is **open and untouched** and its checks may well be green, so the dispositions are inspect-the-PR-and-finish-by-hand or re-drive — never a re-push and never a discard), `acceptance-incomplete`, `acceptance-gate-failed`, `acceptance-gate-timeout` (§3e.5 ran out of *time*, so the suite's verdict is UNKNOWN — the branch is **not** known-red; the useful dispositions are retry, or raise `$BUILD_GATE_SLICE_SECS`/split the gate list), `push-rejected`, `push-unwatched-branch` (temperloop#1688 — the push **landed**, on a ref no open PR references while an open PR for the same slug sits on the `pr_head_ref` the payload names; **never** re-push the same ref or open a PR past it, since the PR is serving pre-push content and an unpinned CI poll would green it: the disposition is re-push onto `pr_head_ref` with `--allow-rewrite` — the non-classifier-tripping spelling `pr.sh` itself recommends in the payload's `error`, per §3f step 1 — then a `--sha`-pinned re-poll), `closing-keyword`, `dirty-worktree` (the worker left tracked-file edits uncommitted, so a rebase never started — **not** a conflict and **never** a discard: the dispositions are commit-the-leftover-and-re-drive or drop-the-debris-and-re-drive.
