Two sites raise this same kind: 3f step 0.25 (payload has no `step` field), and — round 2, temperloop#1937 — the pre-§3e.5 freshness step, which probes dirtiness BEFORE attempting its own rebase onto `origin/main` and never misreads the refusal as `stale-worktree`'s conflict shape; its payload carries `step: "gate-freshness"` and `dirty_paths` to distinguish it), `review-agent-timeout` (temperloop#2003 — a **mandatory** §3e reviewer did not return inside the review fanout's wall-clock ceiling, so its gate never ran; the item is **not** pushed. The payload names `mandatory` / `timed_out` and carries the computed `review` tally, so `mandatory_ok` reads the truth instead of being left unevaluated. This is an UNKNOWN, never a red review: the dispositions are **re-drive the item**, or raise `$BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_CEILING_SECS` only if that review is legitimately this slow — never skip the mandatory reviewer), `machinery-step-timeout` (a machinery step outlived the workflow's per-step liveness ceiling and was killed — its result is UNKNOWN, **not** failed; the payload's `probeStage` says what `pr.sh recover-probe` found actually landed, and nothing was re-issued, so the dispositions are re-drive or finish by hand), `activation-failed` / `absence-proof-vacuous-at-merge-base` / `activation-control-unavailable` / `activation-proof-missing` (§3e.6, temperloop#1219 — the item is **not** pushed; the fix is always the missing WIRING or a sounder `proof:`, **never** a weakened predicate or a deleted `activation:` block, so the useful dispositions are retry-after-fixing-the-wiring, retry-after-re-authoring-the-predicate in plan-schema's wrap-immune form, or skip — and `activation-control-unavailable` is an UNKNOWN, not a Fail: it means the merge-base control could not be **established**, so re-run it rather than reading it as a red branch), `quota-exhausted` (temperloop#1819 — the harness's session-usage quota ran out mid-run: an ENVIRONMENTAL death, **not** a classifier refusal (`machinery-denied`, whose rewrite-the-command cure would be wasted on a command that was never the problem) and **not** a content failure (`worker-error`, whose re-drive cure would discard work that already landed). `build-level.mjs` classifies it through two seams: the thrown agent error text (the reset time is extracted into the payload's `reset_time`), and — for the bare-null shape that carries no text at all — an agent-liveness canary, a cheap probe spawn that fails only when the harness can spawn nothing (a classifier denial is per-command; a quota death kills every spawn; the canary re-probes per bare-null and memoizes only a dead verdict, so a quota that dies late in a level is still caught). The item's work is usually **intact**: `worktree_left_intact: true` in the payload means the escalation cleaned up nothing, so the disposition is **wait for `reset_time`** (or check `quota-gate.sh` / `~/.claude/rate-limits.json` when none was reported), then inspect the worktree — `pr.sh recover-probe` says what actually landed — and **resume** from there; never rewrite the command and never re-drive from scratch without checking the worktree first), and all other non-enumerated kinds — **generic escalation path**: render the raw `payload` as a text block with the slug and kind as a heading, then ask the user how to proceed via `decision_sink_ask(<the rendered payload>, [retry, skip, abort], ask-now)`. A non-enumerated kind must **never produce a silent stall** — unknown payload shape escalates visibly rather than dropping the item.

**`level-pick` — the LEVEL-scoped escalation (temperloop#2083, epic #2065's dual-build harness).** Every in-scope item of a dual-built level raises this kind carrying the SAME level payload, so step 2 groups them into **ONE** ask rather than one per item. The payload carries the pre-registered tally (`tally`, plus each item's own `reason`), the proposed `winner` and why (`tally` / `tally-tie-cheaper` / `tally-tie-incumbent`), both arms' whole-job `cost`, the `calibration` reading that triggered the gate, and every arm's intact worktree. **It carries NO default and is never timed** (`default: null`) — the pairwise judge is not yet known to agree with a human on this repo, so a timeout is not consent (`claude/CLAUDE.kernel.md` § Merge autonomy & consent); on an operator-absent run the level parks with **no PR opened for any in-scope item**, worktrees intact. **Its async decision issue is posted ONCE, keyed by LEVEL, on the plan note's epic issue — not any single item's issue** — overriding step 2's generic per-slug disposition exactly as 4b's risky-set approval already does, and for the same reason: this kind fires on every in-scope item of the level carrying one identical payload, and its answer is ONE `levelPick` object, not a per-slug map. A per-slug fan-out would post N duplicate issues with no reconciliation rule — the level then either stalls forever waiting on N closures, or collects a subset of inconsistent replies with nothing defining how to collapse them into the single value `driveLevelPick` expects. So: one issue per level (title it for the plan + level, `decision` label applied, operator assigned), and its single reply IS the level's verdict. **If the epic issue is unavailable** — board integration OFF, or no `epic:` in the plan frontmatter — there is no durable receptacle to key by level, so fall through to a **loud terminal halt** exactly as 4b and 4e-keystone do when they lose the same surface; never fall back to the per-slug fan-out. Surface it through `decision_sink_ask(...)` as one `ask-now` gate whose options ARE the verdict grammar: **`confirm`** (route the tally's winner), **`override-level <arm>`** (route that arm for every item), or **`override-item <slug> <arm> "<reason>"`** (route that arm for the named items only — which makes the level's own pick **`mixed`**, stamps `override: item` on that item's ledger row, and writes an override-sourced calibration pair). Capture the answer as a `## Escalation resolution — <slug>` block per step 3, then re-invoke the workflow with the level's in-scope slugs in `onlySlugs` **and** `levelPick` carrying the verdict. Once `calibration.json` reads `calibrated` the confirm is **no longer required** — the pick routes itself and the same grammar stays available as an **optional override** the operator may pass up front. `level-pick-input-invalid` is the malformed-answer arm: the workflow refuses to route rather than silently discard an override, so the fix is the `levelPick` object, never a re-run without it.

**A parked item carrying `merge_blocked` is INELIGIBLE for Step 4's merge gate.** `arms-trailer-unstamped` means the winning PR has no *verified* `Model-comparison-arms:` trailer (ADR 0040), so merging it would ship work chosen by a model comparison the merged record no longer discloses. Land the trailer by hand — `workflows/scripts/model-comparison/tagging.sh stamp-arms …` appended to the PR body, then confirmed with that script's own `parse-arms` inverse — and clear the field before merging; never merge past it. The level summary's `dualBuild.pick.merge_blocked` names every such item, so the gate reads a list rather than re-deriving one.

3. **Capture the verdict durably before re-spawning.** For each escalated slug:
   - **`design-fork`** — append a `## Design verdict — <slug>` section to the plan note via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_append` (**not** `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file` — the em-dash in the heading causes that tool to misfire; use the builtin-REST server):
     ```markdown
     ## Design verdict — <slug>
     Decision: <the decision text from design_fork.decision>
     Chosen: <the label of the selected option>
     Rationale: <one sentence from the user's reply or the recommendation>
     ```
     If the fork is architectural / load-bearing (a schema, a module seam, a public contract), **also** file a `Decisions/` vault note per the global decision-capture rule. Either way, the plan-note block is what the continuation reads — the vault note is the long-lived record.
   - **`blocked`** — append a `## User answers — <slug>` section via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_append` (**not** `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file` — same em-dash heading caveat):
     ```markdown
     ## User answers — <slug>
     <verbatim answers to each question[], one per line>
     ```
   - **Other kinds** — append a `## Escalation resolution — <slug>` section (also via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_append`) with the user's choice (retry / skip / action taken).
   
   **Stamp `escalated: true` (load-bearing for resume).** After appending the verdict block for a slug, **immediately** stamp `  - escalated: true` on that plan item via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_patch` (mirroring the `speculative: true` precedent exactly — same durable-sentinel pattern, same tool). This sentinel is what Step 0.5 reconcile and Step 1.4 resume key off to route a `[~]`+no-`pr:` item to the 3d-esc continuation (re-enter with the `onlySlugs` filter) instead of the "stuck worker → ask resume/reset" default. Without it, a crash between appending the verdict block and re-spawning the worker leaves the item indistinguishable from a stuck non-speculative worker, and the reset path discards the worktree plus the human verdict. **Clear the sentinel** (remove the sub-line, via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_patch`) when the continuation drives the item to `[m]` (parked) or `[-]` (skipped) — same lifecycle as `speculative: true`.
   
   These appended blocks are the **only** durable escalation state beyond the `escalated: true` sentinel. The continuation workflow reads the plan note, finds them, and injects the relevant block as the `## Design verdict` / `## User answers` section in the worker's re-spawn prompt (step 4 below). This is what makes the re-derivation checkable: if the block is present and `escalated: true` is stamped, the verdict was captured; if either is absent, the escalation is unresolved.

4. **Invoke the scoped continuation.** **Immediately before this re-invocation**, print the **Workflow launch line** template (`claude/message-schema.md`) with the incremented round number and the escalation kind(s) being re-driven — the fields this line names exist precisely for a continuation round, so a 3d-esc re-drive is never silently indistinguishable from the fresh drive that preceded it (temperloop#1941). **Immediately after it returns**, print the **Workflow return line** template. Re-invoke the **same Workflow** (`build-level.mjs`) with `args.onlySlugs = [<slug>, ...]` containing only the still-unresolved slugs (those whose worktrees are intact and for which a verdict block was just appended). Pass the **full `args.items` array** unchanged (the workflow's `onlySlugs` filter selects the active subset) plus a `verdicts` map:
   ```json
   {
     "verdicts": {
       "<slug>": { "kind": "<escalation-kind>", "verdict_section": "<the FULL appended markdown block — heading + body>" }
     },
     "onlySlugs": ["<slug>", "..."]
   }
   ```
   `verdict_section` is the **full markdown block** the orchestrator appended to the plan note for this slug — the `## Design verdict — <slug>` (or `## User answers — <slug>`) heading **and its body** — not merely the heading. The workflow injects it **verbatim** into the re-spawned worker's prompt, so the value must be self-contained (the worker has no access to the plan note). The workflow's `driveItem` for each continued slug re-spawns the worker with the original prompt **plus** that block appended as an extra `## Design verdict` / `## User answers` section (read from `args.verdicts[slug].verdict_section`), so the worker sees the human's decision instead of re-forking. The re-spawn does **not** re-run 3a (claim already held — `driveItem` skips it on a slug in `onlySlugs`) or 3b (**the existing worktree is reused** — `driveItem` skips `worktree.sh create`, whose force-remove-and-re-add would otherwise discard the escalated build; the `.build-guard` marker is still in place, so the guard stays armed without re-arming). It resumes at **3c** (worker re-spawn), continuing forward through 3d–3h as normal. Re-spawn model tier follows the 3c **escalate-on-retry** rule: `failed` → top tier; `blocked`/`design-fork` → keep the stamped tier.

5. **Loop until done.** Collect the continuation's `{parked, escalations}`. Write parked siblings' sentinels and remove their worktrees (step 1). If `escalations` is non-empty, loop back to step 2 (a re-spawned worker may discover a further fork or remain blocked). The loop terminates when `escalations` is empty (all items either parked or skipped/aborted). A stuck loop (the same slug escalates on consecutive iterations with no change in payload) is surfaced after 3 identical rounds — prompt the user to skip or abort the slug rather than looping indefinitely.

**Acceptance invariants (falsifiable):**
- A design-fork/blocked/failed escalation **re-drives its slug to parked** via a verdict-seeded continuation; siblings that are already `[m]` are left untouched (their worktrees were removed in step 1 and they carry `pr:` on the plan note — the `onlySlugs` filter excludes them).
- The continuation is **re-derivable from {plan-note level sentinels + `pr:`} + {the appended verdicts map} ALONE** — the exact inputs are: (a) every `[~]` item in this level with no `pr:` sub-line **and `escalated: true`** (= worktree intact, verdict captured, awaiting continuation — the sentinel stamped in step 3); (b) the `## User answers — <slug>` / `## Design verdict — <slug>` / `## Escalation resolution — <slug>` sections appended to the plan note. The `escalated: true` sentinel is the unambiguous discriminator — a `[~]`+no-`pr:` item without it is a stuck non-speculative worker (ask resume/reset), not an escalated item. No live workflow state is needed; these two durable sources reconstruct a continuation fully after a crash.
- Any non-enumerated terminal kind (not `design-fork`, `blocked`, `failed`) **surfaces as a generic escalation** with its raw `payload` rendered as a text block — it never stalls silently.

#### 3e. Optional pre-push review

##### Routing — which reviewers run

If project CLAUDE.md `## Subagents` lists a review subagent matching any of the axes below, invoke as read-only pass. <!-- cite: B.30 incident:F#1007 -->

- the item's `review:` override;
- the item's change *kind* (`architectural` → `architecture-reviewer`);
- the extension/path-glob axis — `workflows/scripts/config/reviewer-routing.tsv` is the **single source of truth for that axis** (ADR 0008, `docs/adr/0008-reviewer-routing-tsv-extension-axis-scope.md`; consult the tsv for the full extension/glob → reviewer list, which includes the docs-prose glob);
- or the prose fallback of that axis — **any other stranger-facing prose `*.md` not matched by a `reviewer-routing.tsv` row → `docs-reviewer`** (a non-extension catch-all ADR 0008 keeps in-prose rather than a tsv row, which would over-match);
- **except** a workflow spec under `claude/commands/*.md`, which always routes to `workflow-reviewer` regardless of any tsv row or the prose fallback (foundation#1007; invariants, not style).

A change matching more than one axis (a `review:` override, the change kind, a tsv route, the prose `*.md` fallback, or the `claude/commands/*.md` exception) runs each matching reviewer. `docs-reviewer` is advisory only — its findings are a review pass like this step's other reviewers, never a `checks` gate entry (`scripts/quality-gates.sh` / `.github/workflows/ci.yml` name no LLM-judgment gate, by design: non-reproducible judgment doesn't belong in the deterministic set).

**When invoking, pass the effective (merged) principle set** — the resolution **Step 1.8** already computed for this item's `(repo, project)` pair (§ Step 1.8 "Resolve the effective engineering principle set"; § 3c embeds the identical pair into the worker prompt) — to the reviewer as **additional evaluation criteria** ("judge the diff against these too — cite the principle's name and origin, `kernel` or `project`, in any finding").

**Reuse that resolution; do not re-resolve it here** — the kernel set always applies, so there is no "no declared principles" branch left to fall into; an item's project slot being empty, or the section being empty/malformed, is a *resolved outcome* already carried forward from Step 1.8; state it once, in the Step 6 summary's `principles:` line, never as per-item noise in this hot loop.

##### Availability, degradation, and the coverage tally

**Availability is a MECHANICAL PROBE, never a judgment call (temperloop#1462).** Resolve every matched reviewer through the kernel helper `workflows/scripts/lib/agent_declared.sh` — source it, then read `agent_declared_state <agent>`, which prints exactly one of `installed`, `source-only`, or `absent` (ADR 0029, `docs/adr/0029-agent-declared-probe.md`) across the same three surfaces `command_declared` walks, with `agents/` for `commands/`, plus the `CLAUDE.md ## Subagents` clause this step opens with.

**`installed` is the spawn gate** — do *not* gate a spawn on bare `agent_declared`, which is deliberately true for `source-only` too. And do **not** re-derive availability by eye from "is there a `.claude/agents/` directory" (what that reading cost: § Why these rules are shaped this way, below).

If the state is anything but `installed`, state the skip for the item rather than silently passing the gate, using the two-form degradation line (`claude/message-schema.md` § Degradation notice) — and let the state pick the form, one each: `source-only` (the reviewer ships as source at `claude/agents/<agent>.md`, flat — e.g. `architecture-reviewer`/`docs-reviewer`/`requirements-auditor`/`workflow-reviewer` — or under `reviewers/`, e.g. the per-language reviewers, but isn't live here) → the remedy-bearing `skipped — <agent> available as source; run workflows/scripts/install/project-agents.sh to enable`; `absent` (nothing ships, nothing to install) → the bare `skipped — <agent> unavailable`.

**Mandatory for a `claude/commands/*.md` diff (foundation#1007):** the `workflow-reviewer` pass is *required*, not worker-discretion — for any item whose diff touches a workflow spec under `claude/commands/*.md` it MUST either run or emit the legible skip notice per the two-form rule above, with the form selected by `agent_declared_state workflow-reviewer` and never guessed (`source-only` → `skipped — workflow-reviewer available as source; run workflows/scripts/install/project-agents.sh to enable`; `absent` → `skipped — workflow-reviewer unavailable`), and is **never** omitted or self-run informally.

Tally each command-doc item's reviewer outcome (`ran` / `skipped — unavailable`) for the Step 6 summary, so coverage is legible at session end — on the Workflow path this rides `park()`'s `review: { ran, skipped, mandatory_ok, routed_not_run }` field (temperloop#1450/#1984; see the I/O CONTRACT in `build-level.mjs`), merged across the original 3e pass and any CI-fix re-review (§3g) into ONE tally per item. `mandatory_ok` covers the foundation#1007 command-doc rule alone; `routed_not_run` names every OTHER routed-but-unrun reviewer — a visibility field, never a second gate (ADR 0037, `docs/adr/0037-routed-but-unrun-reviewers-are-visible-not-blocking.md`), so a skipped tsv route can no longer leave the tally reading fully clean.

The cross-PR coverage rate over merged command-doc PRs is reported out-of-band by `workflows/scripts/workflow-reviewer-coverage.sh` (a reporting rollup, **not** a `checks` gate — LLM-judgment gates stay out of the deterministic set by design).

##### Blocking — HIGH severity only

**Blocking means HIGH-severity, and only HIGH** (every reviewer in this catalog shares the `### [HIGH | MEDIUM | LOW] <name> in <file>` output contract, ADR 0007) — a HIGH finding loops back to 3c with the review feedback as context; MEDIUM/LOW findings are advisory and never block. They are never silently discarded either (temperloop#1450): the Workflow path splices every ran reviewer's full findings text into the PR body as a `## Review notes` section, so a clean-but-advisory review is still visible to the human reviewer rather than computed, checked for HIGH, and thrown away.

##### Execution boundary, and the wall-clock bound

**Why this runs INSIDE the Workflow, not the orchestrator (temperloop#1430):** on the default Workflow path, `build-level.mjs`'s `driveItem` runs this step directly, between 3d and 3e.5, spawning each matched reviewer itself via `agent({agentType})` — never delegated to the 3c worker (which has no way to spawn a nested subagent at all) and never deferred to the conversational orchestrator. The 3d-esc orchestrator↔workflow boundary (above) is not a no-judgment/judgment split — the orchestrator retains real judgment-shaped work on this path (the Step 4 merge gate, escalation resolution).

The orchestrator↔workflow boundary is irreversible-action plus single-writer, not no-judgment: `build-level.mjs` owns the irreversible per-item actions it can take without the orchestrator's two reserved seats — push and PR-open (**not** the merge: merging needs the consent seat and the plan-note write seat, which is exactly why 3h.5 is conversational-path-only, temperloop#1452) — and §3e must run *before* 3f (push) — a step the workflow owns by that same rule, so a loop-back to 3c is reachable only from **inside** `driveItem`: by the time the workflow *returns* to the orchestrator, the item is already pushed and its PR open (irreversible), and the orchestrator's own post-return partition removes the parked item's worktree — the very tree a review would need to inspect.

The static lockstep guard for this ordering lives in `workflows/scripts/build/tests/test_workflow.sh` (search `K1430`).

**Bounded by construction (temperloop#2003):** `build-level.mjs` spawns every routed reviewer **concurrently** and waits on the whole fanout under one wall-clock ceiling (`$BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_CEILING_SECS`, with a progress notice at `$BUILD_REVIEW_AGENT_SLOW_SECS` first) — so a reviewer that never returns can neither keep a later one from launching nor stall the level in silence (what happened before the bound: § Why these rules are shaped this way, below).

A reviewer still unreturned at the ceiling is **abandoned** and dispositioned by kind — **advisory** → the same two-form degradation line above; **mandatory** → an escalated `review-agent-timeout` (Step 3d-esc) carrying the computed tally, never a silent pass. The bound **fails open** to the old unbounded wait if its own timer cannot run, so it can never manufacture a false timeout on a healthy review.

##### Why these rules are shaped this way (incident history)

- **Availability re-derived by eye (temperloop#1462).** Read that way, the predicate reported every reviewer unavailable on the kernel's own dogfooding checkout — where `.claude/agents/` is gitignored and `CLAUDE.md` has no `## Subagents` heading, yet all eleven agents are installed and spawnable at `$HOME/.claude/agents/` — so this very step emitted an all-skip line for agents that spawn fine, which is a mandatory gate silently not running.
- **Worker-discretion phrasing, before temperloop#1430.** The boundary above is exactly why the pre-#1430 worker-discretion phrasing could never discharge the mandatory command-doc rule on this path — the worker cannot spawn `agent({agentType})`, so "run it or emit the skip notice" collapsed to a guaranteed skip on every command-doc PR.
- **Unbounded review waits, before temperloop#2003.** What the ceiling now prevents is exactly what happened before: one hung agent, ~41 minutes with the workflow no longer writing its journal, and the mandatory `workflow-reviewer` never spawned.

#### 3e.5. Parent-side acceptance gate (no-op if the project declares no gates)

Before pushing, the orchestrator independently verifies the worker's claims rather than trusting the return JSON. Run the project's **single source of truth for the static gate set — `scripts/quality-gates.sh`** (the same script CI and CLAUDE.md § Dev workflow invoke, so the acceptance gate can't drift from a hand-maintained inline copy) against the worker's commit, in a throwaway checkout of the worker SHA (or the worker's worktree, before 3h removes it). What makes this the *authority* is that it is **the orchestrator's own run, not the worker's report** — the PR #309 silent-red class is a worker's self-assessment standing in for a real gate run, and that is what this step removes. **No-op on a project that ships no `scripts/quality-gates.sh`** (e.g. a no-CI repo, or foundation itself). **The gate runs on a tree freshly rebased onto current `origin/main`, WHEN a gate script is present (temperloop#1937).** On the Workflow path `driveItem` runs a pre-gate freshness step, strictly before this one, that fetches origin and rebases the worktree branch onto `origin/main` whenever it is behind — so a validator that ratchets against `origin/main` (`validate-check-surface-degenerate-coverage.sh`, `validate-exec-bit-registry.sh`, `validate-mandatory-step-signal.sh`) never false-fails this gate on rows main gained after the worktree's base was cut; a conflicting rebase escalates `stale-worktree` (below) instead of ever reaching this gate. **Round 3 (HIGH):** the freshness step is itself gated behind the SAME `scripts/quality-gates.sh` presence check this gate makes — a project with no vendored gate script has nothing for it to protect, so it reports `FRESHNESS_NO_GATE` and takes the byte-identical pre-#1937 path (no fetch, no rebase, no extra spawn) straight to this gate's own `GATE_ABSENT`.

- **Fail** → loop back to 3c with the gate output as context; do **not** push a known-red branch.
- **Pass** → proceed to 3f.
- **The gate is DIFF-SCOPED by default — `$BUILD_GATE_SCOPED` (temperloop#1663).** It runs only the gates the item's changed paths can reach, resolved through `workflows/scripts/config/gate-paths.tsv` by `workflows/scripts/lib/gate-selection.sh`. **Why:** a full per-item suite could not survive within-level parallelism, and the ceiling it hit is not tunable. Measured on a 3-item level: **55 minutes, 21 agents, a seven-figure subagent-token spend, ZERO items landed** — all three escalated `acceptance-gate-timeout` with every worker finished and committed and only the verdict missing. N concurrent items means N concurrent full suites, each with its own `$QUALITY_GATES_JOBS` workers; contention inflated the gate tail 200–300%, while the slice budget's own maximum sits barely above the budget that failed and is clamped by the executor agent's ~10-minute Bash cap. The suite had to get **shorter**, not the budget longer. **Why it's safe:** this is the same map and selector CI's `checks` job has used on the `pull_request` event since temperloop#1024, so scoping here adds no failure mode the PR check does not already carry; every resolution failure in the selector widens to the **full** set, an unmapped gate always runs, and a scoped run **names every gate it skipped**. What gates `main` is untouched — the **`merge_group`** run of `checks` is unscoped and always runs everything. **The seam is an env var (`QUALITY_GATES_SCOPED`), not the `--scoped` flag**, for the same reason the slice budget is: a consuming repo vendoring an older `quality-gates.sh` ignores an unknown env var and runs the whole suite (the pre-#1663 behavior, still correct), whereas an unknown **flag** exits 2 `usage` and reads back as a gate **failure**. **The escape hatch is `$BUILD_GATE_SCOPED`, set in a config FILE — not the environment.** This is the one setting whose env layer does not reach its consumer, structurally rather than by oversight: the same #1241 scrub two bullets down erases every `build.config.sh` value-setting from the gate's environment before it runs, and this name is in that set, so an exported override is gone before it is read. The scrub deliberately spares the config-**file** layers, so use `build.config.local.sh` (`$BUILD_CONFIG_LOCAL`), the machine config (`$BUILD_CONFIG_MACHINE`), or the tracked default. Turning it off re-exposes the parallel-item timeout, so prefer fixing the wrong `gate-paths.tsv` row.
- **Budget exhausted is a THIRD outcome — never a Fail (temperloop#1021).** A run the time budget killed proves nothing about the tree, so it must not wear the Fail verdict: the Workflow path escalates it as **`acceptance-gate-timeout`** (distinct from `acceptance-gate-failed`), and a hand-run conversational gate must report it the same way rather than looping back to 3c as though the branch were red. This is not hypothetical tidiness — it is the bug: a single flat timeout for the whole suite was SIGTERM'ing a **green** suite mid-run and reporting `GATE_FAIL`, twice (temperloop#115 raised the number 2min → 8min; the suite then outgrew that too), and an escalation payload could not be told apart from real breakage. The fix is structural rather than a third raise, because the executor agent's ~10-minute Bash ceiling cannot be raised at all: `build-level.mjs` hands `scripts/quality-gates.sh` a per-**slice** budget (`QUALITY_GATES_BUDGET_SECS`, from `gateSliceSecs`/`$BUILD_GATE_SLICE_SECS`) plus a resume index (`QUALITY_GATES_START_AT`), the suite stops cleanly *between* gates and reports where to resume, and the driver loops slices — so total suite runtime is unbounded by any one invocation and gate-list growth can no longer produce a false failure. Both knobs are **env vars, not flags**, so a consuming repo vendoring an older `quality-gates.sh` ignores them and runs the whole suite in one go exactly as before. A green run also reports its elapsed time and slice count, which is the **decay signal**: growth is visible as shrinking margin on passing runs instead of surfacing the day it blows a budget. **The escalation payload must agree with the kind it ships under (temperloop#1587).** That split is only readable if the numbers under it are: the Workflow path keeps ONE record of what the run found — a per-slice `sliceLedger` — and derives every reported figure from it (`verdict`, `RED`/`UNKNOWN`/`GREEN`, the single field a consumer may trust; `failedGates`, the ledger's sum; `slices`, its length) rather than maintaining a second counter beside it. The pre-#1587 payload shipped an accumulated `failedGates:1` *next to* the terminal slice's own `gateOut:{outcome:'GATE_PASS',failed:0}` — the kind said failed, the embedded object said passed — and an operator reading the gate log's closing `OK — gates 96..162 passed (final slice)` line reasonably called the escalation a false positive. It was not: an earlier slice had really failed, and that green line covers only the gates the last slice ran. Two consequences to preserve: a `GATE_FAIL` whose count could not be parsed still reports at least one failure (never a failure verdict with zero failures beneath it), and an **observed failure dominates an unfinished remainder** — a run that failed in slice 1 and was then killed by the Bash ceiling escalates `acceptance-gate-failed` with a reason naming both halves, so `acceptance-gate-timeout` can never launder a known-red branch into an unknown one. A hand-run conversational gate reports the same way.
- **`set -o pipefail` plus a DIALECT-SAFE exit capture is mandatory whenever the gate is piped (temperloop#68; the exit-capture half corrected dialect-safe by temperloop#801).** The Pass/Fail verdict is the gate's **own exit code** — so it must not be swallowed, and it must not be read through a variable that silently expands empty on whichever shell actually ran the command (§ Tool invocation discipline's "check the platform's dialect" rule applies directly here: this harness's Bash tool executes through **zsh** on macOS, not bash, so a literal `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` — a BASH array — expands to the EMPTY STRING there, not the gate's exit code; zsh's equivalent is `$pipestatus`, lowercase AND 1-INDEXED, i.e. `${pipestatus[1]}`). If you capture the gate's output through a filter or `tee` (e.g. `scripts/quality-gates.sh 2>&1 | tee /tmp/qg.log`), a bare pipe's exit status reflects the **last** stage (`tee`'s `0`), **masking a red gate**: the gate degrades to a silent no-op and a locally-red branch sails through to push, leaving CI (3g) as the only catch — the exact PR #309 silent-red class this gate exists to pre-empt, and reading an empty-string `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` as "not a failure" under zsh reintroduces that identical class on this platform (temperloop#801). **Prefer running the gate un-piped and branching on its exit directly** — dialect-agnostic, no PIPESTATUS/pipestatus variable needed at all. If the gate must be piped, `set -o pipefail` first and then read **both** forms rather than guessing the host shell — bash's `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` (0-indexed) and zsh's `${pipestatus[1]}` (lowercase, 1-indexed) — never bare `$?`. The Workflow path's §3e.5 invocation in `claude/workflows/build-level.mjs` gets this right and still needs neither variable, though since temperloop#2094 it IS piped: the gate streams `2>&1 | tee <sliceLog> >>gateLog` so one slice's output can be parsed in isolation while the cumulative operator log keeps filling live — including on the timeout path, where the executor kills the command and any after-the-fact copy step would never run. It carries `set -o pipefail` at the head of the composed command and reads a **bare `$?`**, which under pipefail is the gate's own non-zero and never `tee`'s `0`, on both bash and zsh — chosen precisely because neither dialect's PIPESTATUS form is safe to hard-code there. A hand-run conversational-path gate must prefer the un-piped shape, or that same pipefail-plus-bare-`$?` form, or the dual bash/zsh form above if it can't. Do **not** hand-edit either invocation to pipe the gate without carrying pipefail AND a dialect-correct exit read through — that silently reintroduces the swallow. <!-- cite: B.31 incident:K#801 -->
- **The gate runs hermetically — the pipeline's own `build.config.sh` value-settings are scrubbed first (foundation#1241).** The Workflow path's §3e.5 invocation in `claude/workflows/build-level.mjs` `unset`s the setting set `workflows/scripts/build/build-config-settings.sh` prints (SSOT-derived from `build.config.sh` itself — the union of its `: "${NAME:=…}"` declarations AND the names its top-level `export` list exports, since a name the file exports without declaring would otherwise escape the scrub entirely, temperloop#1709) *before* running the gate, so the suite runs at **tracked defaults** — matching what CI's `checks` job sees — instead of inheriting the pipeline-drive session's ~40 exported settings. Without the scrub those exports win the config-precedence layer (`env > machine-conf > repo-local > tracked-default`) and false-**Fail** the precedence tests (`test_config.sh` / `test_stranger_config.sh` / `test_pipeline_cron.sh`) on a change CI passes green — the #1241 merge-tier blocker. The two config-**file** resolvers `BUILD_CONFIG_MACHINE` / `BUILD_CONFIG_LOCAL` are deliberately **not** scrubbed: a file-based machine-local leak is a distinct mechanism (foundation#1055) an env scrub can't fix. A hand-run conversational-path gate is not exposed today (its fresh Bash calls don't inherit the pipeline exports), but must preserve this hermeticity if that ever changes — mirror the scrub, exactly as with pipefail above. <!-- cite: B.32 incident:F#1241 -->
- Require the worker's `acceptance_results` evidence to be **machine-checkable** (exact test node id / command + exit code), not prose — tighten the 3c prompt accordingly.
- Scope to the fast **static** gates here; the slow pytest/eval still runs in CI (3g) where the project has it. No-op on projects that declare no gates (e.g. a no-CI repo).

#### 3e.6. Class-A activation gate — confirm the built thing is actually wired in (no-op unless the item declares `activation: class: A`)

"Merged + CI-green" proves the artifact is *correct*, not that it is *reachable* — a new runner never registered, a flag left off, a rollup computed but never rendered all sail through 3e.5 (see `Decisions/temperloop - Activation-completeness contract`). This gate closes the **synchronous / in-repo** class of that gap; the cross-repo (class B) and soak (class C) classes are ledger-discharged and out of scope here (temperloop#317). <!-- cite: B.33 incident:K#317 -->

Applies **only** when the item carries an `activation:` block with `class: A` (plan-schema § activation). No-op otherwise — an item with no activation block, or a `class: B`/`C` block, skips this step. On the **Workflow path** (the default Step-3 path since temperloop#998) this gate is `build-level.mjs`'s `runActivationGate()`, called from `driveItem` strictly between 3e.5 and 3f; it reaches the block only via the `activation` arg in the Step 3 items contract above, which is why the two ship as one pair (temperloop#1219 — the gate was specified here and implemented nowhere, so `activation:` was dead on the default path). A control pass that cannot be **ESTABLISHED** (no merge-base, the throwaway worktree won't materialize) is a THIRD outcome on the #1021 pattern, never a Pass and never a Fail: it escalates `activation-control-unavailable` rather than letting a possibly-vacuous proof through.

- **Explicit predicate** — if the block carries a `proof:` predicate, run it against the worker's worktree (the same throwaway checkout 3e.5 used, before 3h removes it), from the worktree root. `proof:` reads **false until the built code is genuinely reachable** — `grep -q GeminiRunner evals/runners/__init__.py` (registered), `test "$(cfg get feature.x.enabled)" = true` (flag flipped), a one-line drive that exits non-zero when the panel is absent. Exit 0 = activated. **Absence-asserting predicates get a merge-base control run first (temperloop#944).** When `proof:` negates its check (opens with `!` — author it in the wrap-immune form plan-schema.md § activation documents, `! tr '\n' ' ' < <file> | tr -s ' ' | grep -q '<phrase>'`, never a bare `! grep -q '<phrase>' <file>`: grep is strictly line-oriented, so a phrase that line-wraps can never match and a bare negated grep reads Pass whether or not the removal happened — the temperloop#930 case, green on main@f41a93a before any work), run the identical predicate against the item's **merge-base** first — `base="$(git -C <worktreePath> merge-base HEAD "origin/$default")"`, materialized via a throwaway `git -C <worktreePath> worktree add --detach <tmp> "$base"`, predicate run from `<tmp>`, then `<tmp>` removed — **before** trusting a Pass on the worker's own copy. Also passing at merge-base means the predicate is vacuous (it would read Pass on an untouched tree, proving nothing about this item's work): escalate `absence-proof-vacuous-at-merge-base` and loop back to 3c exactly like a Fail, without even checking the worktree copy. A **presence** proof (the `GeminiRunner` example above) needs no such control run — false on an untouched tree by construction, so there is nothing for it to vacuously pass.
- **No predicate → structurally unreachable, and a hard escalation if it ever arrives (temperloop#1451).** `proof:` is **required** on a `class: A` block: plan-schema **rule 13** fails validation for a class-A block carrying none (`workflows/scripts/build/plan.sh`, checked at Step 1 before any worker runs; cases in `workflows/scripts/build/tests/test_plan.sh`), so a no-`proof:` class-A item cannot reach this step on a validated plan. If one nonetheless arrives — a plan note hand-edited past Step 1, or a resume against a mutated note — escalate **`activation-proof-missing`** and loop back to 3c to author the predicate, exactly as a Fail. **There is no fallback actor and no skip arm here: the predicate IS the gate.** *(Until temperloop#1451 this bullet routed the case to a `/verify` capability that has never existed in this repo — no `claude/commands/verify.md`, no other definition — paired with a legible-degradation clause that let the "mandatory" class-A gate resolve to its own skip notice on every no-predicate run: the temperloop#1387 shape, a pre-authorized degradation clause making a structurally-dead route read as an accepted fallback. Do not reintroduce a degradation arm here unless a real actor ships to back it.)*
- **Fail** → loop back to 3c with the activation output as context; do **not** push a branch whose feature is dormant. The worker's fix is to add the missing wiring (register / flip / render), never to weaken the predicate.
- **Pass** → proceed to 3f.
- **`set -o pipefail` plus the dialect-safe exit capture** applies here exactly as in 3e.5 — prefer un-piped and branch on the direct exit, or, if piped, `set -o pipefail` then read bash's `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` **and** zsh's `${pipestatus[1]}` (lowercase, 1-indexed) together, never bare `$?` — whenever the `proof:` predicate is piped: the Pass/Fail verdict is the predicate's own exit code and must not be swallowed by a `tee`, nor silently misread as empty under the wrong shell's dialect. **But do NOT impose `set -o pipefail` on the `proof:` PREDICATE ITSELF (temperloop#1219)** — that is the opposite of belt-and-suspenders: `pipefail` reports the rightmost NON-ZERO status, and any predicate whose tail exits early on a match (`grep -q`, `head`) SIGPIPEs its upstream writer, which dies 141 — so the wrap-immune ABSENCE idiom plan-schema.md documents inverts on the case that matters (phrase PRESENT, must FAIL: `141` → `!` → **0, a false PASS**; without pipefail `0` → `!` → 1, the correct Fail). Run the predicate un-piped inside a command substitution and read its own `$?` — the preferred shape this bullet already names — and leave the author's own pipeline semantics alone. `build-level.mjs`'s `activationProofCmd`/`activationControlCmd` carry the same DO-NOT-ADD note, and `scripts/lint-pipe-grep-q.sh` (temperloop#1050) is the tree-wide guard for the identical footgun.

#### 3f. Push and open the PR

From the **parent checkout** (not a worktree — the worker's commit is in the shared object store; for an item carrying `repo:`, this is the **same cross-repo `<repo-root>` resolved in 3b**, not the plan's own checkout — the worktree shares its object store, so push/PR-open must target the same repo the worktree was created against), via the deterministic-machinery script `workflows/scripts/build/pr.sh` (epic #253: each mechanical step below is a pure function of observable machine state with a closed outcome set, so its mechanics live in code — the orchestrator invokes the subcommand and branches on the structured `.outcome`; the judgment-shaped halves — rewording an offending commit, the `BASE_STALE` rebase/conflict handling, branch-collision triage — stay orchestrator-driven here):
0. **Closing-keyword pre-push backstop (enforce, don't merely prompt).** Before pushing, scan the worker's commit message(s) for issue-closing keywords — the prose prohibition in 3c is backstopped here so a slip is caught structurally:
   `workflows/scripts/build/pr.sh scan <worktreePath>`
   → `SCAN_CLEAN` (proceed) or `SCAN_BLOCKED` + the offending lines (non-zero exit; the script greps the `origin/<default>..HEAD` commit messages for the closing-keyword pattern). `SCAN_BLOCKED` means a worker commit message carries a closing keyword (the `ec8d5fd` failure mode — GitHub auto-closes the issue on default-branch merge regardless of the PR body). Do **not** push such a branch as-is: either re-spawn the worker against the same `worktreePath` (the `.build-guard` marker is still in place, so the guard stays armed) instructed to amend/reword the offending message to a plain description and re-commit (no push), or strip it locally (`git -C <worktreePath> commit --amend`/`rebase --reword`) — then re-run the scan until `SCAN_CLEAN` before the push-by-SHA below. Issue linkage belongs in **one place only** — the orchestrator's PR-body `Closes #<gh_issue>` (step 2 below), never a worker commit.
0.25. **Unconditional rebase onto fresh `origin/<default>` — the stale-base guard (#525).** Independent of speculation: **every** worker branched off `origin/<default>` at the start of its run, and on a fast-moving default a long run lets the default advance mid-build — so by push/PR-open time the worker's base may be stale and a straight push lands a PR whose cumulative diff **reverts whatever merged in between** (the W49 PR#82 / W52 PR#83 stale-base reversions). Before the scan/push, `build-level.mjs` (3f) runs `workflows/scripts/build/pr.sh rebase <worktreePath>` to fetch the default fresh and replay the worker's commits onto its tip (a no-op when already current): <!-- cite: B.34 incident:F#525 -->
   - **`REBASED`** → the base is now current; the PR diff carries ONLY the worker's own changes. Proceed. (`rebase_needed: false` says base already == tip, so no rebase was run at all.)
   - **`DIRTY_WORKTREE` and `REBASE_CONFLICT` are two states, never one (temperloop#735).** **`DIRTY_WORKTREE`** → git refused to *start* the rebase because the worker left tracked files uncommitted; that refusal exits non-zero exactly like a clash, and collapsing the two reported a **finished** worker as a conflict with `base == tip` (no rebase needed, none attempted, nothing to abort — the edits are still in the worktree), which the conflict disposition would then discard. build-level escalates its own `dirty-worktree` kind, and the disposition is **commit the leftover, never discard the worker**: inspect `dirty_paths`, amend forgotten work into the worker's commit (or `git checkout --` genuine debris), then re-drive from this step; `rebase_needed` says whether a stale base still has to be replayed once the tree is clean. **`REBASE_CONFLICT`** → a real content clash on replay: pr.sh has already `git rebase --abort`ed (worktree left clean, the worker's commits intact, **never** a silent revert). build-level escalates `rebase-conflict` for a human to resolve — it does **not** push the stale SHA. This is the always-on counterpart to the speculative-only `base-check`/`BASE_STALE` flow below (which stays for the speculative-promotion path); the rebase here makes the guard unconditional so a non-speculative long worker on a fast-moving default never reverts intervening merges.
0.5. **Speculative-base currency check — load-bearing (speculative workers only; the sufficient half of the speculation contract).** When this worker was **speculatively pre-spawned** during the prior level's merge-wait (per "Speculative next-level execution" in the within-level section), its worktree was branched from `origin/<default>` *before* that level merged — so its base may now be **stale** (the prior level advanced `main`). Skip this step for a non-speculative worker (its base is current by construction — 3b branched it from the freshly-advanced `origin/<default>` per 4e). For a speculative worker, **before the push-by-SHA below**, check base currency against the now-advanced default tip:
   `workflows/scripts/build/pr.sh base-check <worktreePath>`
   → `BASE_CURRENT` / `BASE_STALE` (the script fetches `origin/<default>` and compares `merge-base(HEAD, origin/<default>)` against the advanced tip). The check is mechanical; what follows a `BASE_STALE` — the rebase and its conflict handling — stays orchestrator-driven:
   - **`BASE_CURRENT`** (merge-base == advanced tip) → the base is up to date; proceed to push-by-SHA (step 1).
   - **`BASE_STALE`** (merge-base behind tip) → do **NOT** push the stale SHA — pushing a stale speculative base **silently drops the merged level-k changes in overlapping regions, and CI on the speculative head won't catch it** (the failure this check exists to prevent). Bring it current first:
     - **Rebase-then-reverify (preferred) — push BEFORE watching CI:** `git -C <worktreePath> rebase origin/<default>`. The rebase changed the diff, so the prior green is stale and must be re-earned — but **CI check-runs only exist after a push**, and an **unpushed** SHA is not the same case 3g's `NO_CI` outcome covers (temperloop#605): the remote has never heard of an unpushed local SHA at all, so `ci-poll.sh` querying `commits/<sha>/check-runs` against it 404s and resolves to a hard `ERROR` (after `gh_retry`'s bounded retry budget) — **never** `NO_CI` and never a green. `NO_CI` fires *only* for a SHA the remote genuinely has (a real, pushed commit) that happens to carry zero check-runs configured against it (e.g. this repo has no CI at all). This is exactly why push-before-watch stays mandatory post-#605: watching the unpushed local SHA would not let an un-reviewed diff quietly wave through the merge gate as a benign no-CI skip (the 404→`ERROR` path stops that outright) — but it would still be polling a SHA that doesn't exist anywhere but this worktree, which is the wrong thing to watch regardless. So order the steps so CI is always watched on a **pushed** head, never an unpushed local SHA. On a clean rebase:
       1. **Force-push the rebased SHA** to the plan branch: `workflows/scripts/build/pr.sh push <worktreePath> <item.branch> --force`. (Force is required — the rebase rewrote history relative to any prior push of this branch.)
       2. **Re-run the 3e.5 parent-side acceptance gate** against the rebased SHA — this is the local static gate (no network), so it may run before or after the push; either is fine.
       3. **Watch CI (3g) on the pushed head** — the just-pushed rebased SHA, never the unpushed local SHA.
       Only after both the 3e.5 gate passes *and* CI re-confirms green on the pushed head does the speculative worker promote (it is already pushed; record the `pr:`/`pushed_sha:` pointer per step 3 — no second push-by-SHA in step 1 is needed for this path, the force-push above already placed the branch).
     - **Discard-and-respawn (fallback on rebase conflict):** if the rebase conflicts, remove the worktree (`workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh remove <repo-root> <item.slug>`), **re-create it from fresh `origin/<default>`** per 3b (`worktree.sh create` — re-drops the marker), and **re-run the worker** (3c) against the current base — then proceed normally through 3d–3e.5 and this step again (now `BASE_CURRENT`).
   - **Promote to a real PR only after the currency check passes.** This is the sufficient half the eligibility predicate ("no `depends-on`") is *not* — it catches the overlapping-but-independent-lines case eligibility can't see. See [[Decisions/foundation - Speculative next-level execution worktree-isolation contract]].
1. Push the worker's commit to the plan branch **by SHA**:
   `workflows/scripts/build/pr.sh push <worktreePath> <item.branch> --allow-rewrite`
   → `PUSHED {sha, branch}` — pushes the worktree's `HEAD` by SHA (`<worktreePath>` is the **deterministic path the orchestrator pre-created in 3b** (`<repo>.wt/<item.slug>`), not a value the worker reports; its `HEAD` is the worker's top commit). This honors the plan's `branch:` name regardless of the worktree's throwaway `build/<item.slug>` local branch.
   - **Remote-branch collision** → `PUSH_REJECTED {forced, lease, refused_reason, remote_tip, remote_only_commits, error}` + non-zero exit. `--allow-rewrite` (temperloop#2103) has already handled the ordinary case this used to leave to a human: a **continuation round** whose branch an earlier round pushed and 3f-0a then rebased can never fast-forward, so `pr.sh` rewrites it under `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<sha>` over a value it read first (`forced:true` + `lease:<sha>` in the payload), downgrading to a plain push on any provable fast-forward (#335) and issuing no force at all when the remote value is unreadable. **A rewrite is never issued on the lease alone** (temperloop#2103 round 3): because this step requests one on *every* item's push rather than only on a rescue, `pr.sh` additionally requires that the local history **supersede** the remote tip — every commit the remote has and `HEAD` does not must be patch-equivalent to one in `HEAD`, the same `rev-list --cherry-pick --right-only` test the escalation-time rescue push uses. A lease only catches a writer who moves the ref *between* the read and the push; it does nothing about content that was already sitting there, so without this gate the genuine collision below would be silently overwritten and reported as an ordinary `PUSHED`. A `PUSH_REJECTED` that survives all of that is therefore **not** a stale-branch case, and `refused_reason` says which of three it is: **`null`** → the lease went stale under a concurrent writer (`forced:true` — re-run to re-read it), or the remote value was unreadable (`forced:false`); **`"remote_not_superseded"`** → the branch name **genuinely collides with unrelated work** — `remote_tip` names the sha `pr.sh` refused to overwrite and `remote_only_commits` how many commits it carries that this worktree does not; **no force was issued and origin is untouched** — surface to the user (pick a new branch name → patch the plan's `branch:`, or abort); **`"supersede_probe_failed"`** → that question could not be *answered* (the remote tip's object never became available locally), an UNKNOWN and never an asserted conflict — re-run it. Never hand-issue a bare `git push --force` here, and never hand-force past a `remote_not_superseded` refusal without first reading what is actually on that ref.
   - **Landed on a ref no open PR watches** → `PUSHED_UNWATCHED {pr_number, pr_head_ref, stale_head_cause, error}` + non-zero exit (temperloop#1688). The push **succeeded** — do not re-push believing it didn't — but it wrote a ref no open PR references, while open PR `#pr_number` for the same slug sits on `pr_head_ref`. Because the worktree's local branch is `build/<slug>` and the PR is opened on the plan's `<type>/<slug>`, a hand-typed or driver-issued re-push aimed at the *worktree's* branch lands a second remote ref the PR never sees. **Never proceed to pr-open or the CI watch on this outcome**: the PR keeps serving its pre-push content, and an unpinned `ci-poll.sh` would resolve to that stale head's already-green checks and report `CI_GREEN` for content that is not what would merge (#254's false green, by a new route). The disposition is to re-push onto the PR's own ref — `pr.sh push <worktreePath> <pr_head_ref> --allow-rewrite` — then re-verify with a `--sha`-pinned poll. (`--allow-rewrite`, not `--force`: it is the same request spelled without a classifier-visible force token (temperloop#2103, #437), and this is exactly the wording `pr.sh` itself emits in the `PUSHED_UNWATCHED` `error` message — a literal `--force` on a recovery path that only exists because something already escalated is how an otherwise-healthy autonomous run gets parked a second time.) **`stale_head_cause: "branch-mismatch"` is the field that keeps this from being misread as GitHub's post-force-push head lag** (the lag `ci-poll.sh`'s header documents): the lag shows the PR on the *same* head ref with a trailing head sha and converges on its own; this is two different refs and never converges. The two look identical from the PR page, which is exactly how the live 2026-08-21 sighting was first misdiagnosed.
2. Open the PR — write the worker's verdict JSON (the 3d return object) to a temp file and run:
   `workflows/scripts/build/pr.sh open --repo <repo-root> --branch <item.branch> --title "<item.title>" --verdict <verdict.json> [--gh-issue <N>] [--also-closes <M,M2,…>] [--verification-surface-file <worktreePath>/.build-verification.md] --plan-link "Plans/<date> <project> - <title>#<item.slug>" --source "<item.source>"`
   Pass `--verification-surface-file <worktreePath>/.build-verification.md` when the worker set `verification_surface_path` (the #418 inflow-cut). **The orchestrator uses the deterministic path it pre-created in 3b — not the verdict's `verification_surface_path` value** — so the surface body never enters orchestrator context; `pr.sh` emits a structured ERROR if no file exists at that path, so a non-standard worker that wrote elsewhere surfaces immediately rather than silently. Omit the flag when the worker produced no surface; `pr.sh` then falls back through the verdict's `verification_surface_path` → inline `verification_surface` → the acceptance recap.
   → `PR_OPENED {pr_number}` (`--verdict -` reads stdin; `--body-only` prints the assembled body without creating the PR — for inspection, and the dry mode the build tests assert on). The script assembles the body from the verdict's `summary` / `acceptance_results` + the resolved verification surface plus the plan fields, per this contract:
   - The agent's `summary`
   - **If the item has `gh_issue:`** (pass `--gh-issue`), a literal `Closes #<gh_issue>` line — **not** wrapped in backticks / a code span. GitHub silently ignores closing keywords inside code spans (observed in plan #265/#266, where a backticked `Closes` failed to auto-close and the issues had to be closed by hand). This is what auto-closes the issue on merge; the 4d board→Done move is the belt-and-suspenders complement. **This PR body is the ONLY place an issue-closing keyword belongs** — the orchestrator owns issue linkage here; worker commit messages must never carry one (forbidden in 3c, enforced by the 3f step-0 pre-push scan above). **Cross-repo `repo:` honor point:** when the item's `gh_issue:` is tracked in a *different* repo than the one this PR opens against (the plan's home repo, tracking a kernel-classified item's issue — the mirror image of the `repo:` case, since `gh_issue:` normally lives wherever the item was triaged), pass the fully-qualified `owner/repo#N` form instead of the bare number, so `pr.sh` emits `Closes owner/repo#<gh_issue>` — never bare `Closes #N`, which is **same-repo only** and silently fails to reach a tracker in another repo (per the global `CLAUDE.md` § Issue linkage cross-repo-closes caveat). The common, same-repo case is unaffected — bare `Closes #<gh_issue>` as before. **Both paths implement this identically** (temperloop#852): on the default Workflow path, `build-level.mjs` performs this same qualify-vs-bare decision itself at its 3f pr-open call site, keyed off `item.repo` vs. the `ownerRepo` it's already handed (see that file's own comment there) — the conversational path is not a special case, the two cannot diverge on this mechanic.
   - **If the item has `also_closes:`** (a list of additional issue numbers; pass `--also-closes`), the script emits **one bare `Closes #M` line per entry**, each on its own line, **alongside** the primary `Closes #<gh_issue>` above — never combined (`Closes #1 and #2` closes only #1; GitHub requires each issue carry its own keyword). Same bare-text rule: no backticks / code span. This is how one PR closes several issues atomically on merge — the opportunistic same-PR fix (a `capture.sh`'d defect fixed alongside the planned work) and the root-cause-collapse atomic close (a survivor's fix PR closing the survivor plus its absorbed symptoms together). `also_closes:` absent emits nothing extra. Any `also_closes:` entry tracked in another repo is qualified the same way as `gh_issue:` above (`owner/repo#M`).
   - Bulleted recap of `acceptance:` items with `passed/evidence` (and `discrimination_evidence` when present — 3c, temperloop#1319). **Surface the degraded case here too (don't let it fail silently) — the sibling of the verification-surface clause immediately below:** `WORKER_VERDICT_SCHEMA` does not mark `discrimination_evidence` required, §3d branches solely on `.status`, and §3e.5 never inspects it, so a worker that simply omits the field on an otherwise-`passed: true` entry produces a `done` verdict that sails through the whole pipeline unnoticed. So: when this run armed `requireDiscriminationEvidence` (Step 3 `args`) AND the `done` verdict carries any `acceptance_results[]` entry with `passed: true` and an empty/absent `discrimination_evidence`, emit a named warning at 3f — `PR #N: acceptance criterion "<criterion>" passed with no discrimination evidence — the worker never proved this check can fail (temperloop#1319)` — and tally such items in the Step 6 summary. **Advisory, never a hard failure or a new blocking gate** (kernel principle 7, advisory over enforced discipline): the item still parks and the PR still opens; a `passed: false`/`blocked`/`failed` entry is untouched by this clause — only a *claimed* pass with no proof is suspect. On the Workflow path this is implemented, not merely described: `build-level.mjs`'s `discriminationGaps()` computes the gap set once `verdict.status === 'done'` and all bullets pass, `driveItem` logs the named warning at 3h once the PR number is known, and the parked record it returns carries `discrimination_gaps: [<criterion>, ...]` (present only when non-empty) for the orchestrator to roll into Step 6.
   - A `## Verification` section = the **resolved** verification surface (per-change-type proof: before/after numbers, per-fixture diffs, config side-by-side, or a no-behavior-change claim + proving diff). `pr.sh` resolves it by precedence — `--verification-surface-file` → the verdict's `verification_surface_path` (the file the worker wrote, #418) → the inline `verification_surface` (back-compat) → the acceptance recap. The recap fallback fires **only** if the worker produced no surface at all — the bare recap alone does not satisfy the project's PR-verification-surface rule. **Surface the degraded case (don't let it fail silently):** when the orchestrator passed no `--verification-surface-file` AND the verdict carries neither `verification_surface_path` nor an inline `verification_surface`, emit a named warning at 3f — `PR #N: no verification surface, acceptance recap only — does not satisfy the PR-verification-surface rule` — and tally such items in the Step 6 summary. This catches a worker that missed the file-write instruction (e.g. a crash-resumed worker built from a stale prompt) *before* CI, while the PR body is still editable, instead of leaving the reviewer to discover it.
   - Back-link: `Tracked in: [[Plans/<date> <project> - <title>#<item.slug>]]` (pass the wikilink target via `--plan-link`)
   - Source ref: `Derived from: <item.source>` (pass via `--source`)
   - Standard `🤖 Generated with [Claude Code]` footer.
3. Capture the PR number, then **immediately** record durable PR identity on the item — `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file` to add `  - pr: <N>` and `  - pushed_sha: <worker-sha>` sub-lines — *before* starting the CI watch (3g). The sentinel stays `[~]`, but the item now carries a recoverable pointer to the open PR (Step 1.4 resume re-attaches via `pr:` instead of re-spawning; Step 0.5 reconciles against it). **If the item carried a `  - speculative: true` sentinel** (it was speculatively pre-spawned, per 3b), **clear it now** — remove the sub-line in the same patch. Recording `pr:` is the promotion event; past it the item is an ordinary `[~]` whose `pr:` pointer governs resume, so the speculative sentinel must not linger. **Also emit an issue-touch record for this PR-open** (foundation #916/#919, the issue-touch-stream — skip when the item carries no `gh_issue:`, there being no issue to attribute the touch to): `workflows/scripts/emit-issue-touch.sh --repo <owner>/<repo> --issue <item.gh_issue> --kind pr-open || true`. Telemetry only — `|| true`-isolated so a missing/unwritable raw lake never blocks the PR-open path; its presence here is mechanically enforced by `workflows/scripts/validate-issue-touch-emit.sh` (wired into `scripts/quality-gates.sh`). Claim touches are already covered by `claim.sh`'s own emit (3a) and are never duplicated here.

#### 3g. Monitor CI

Watch CI with the toolkit's CI poll (deterministic machinery, epic #253), run via `Bash { run_in_background: true }`:

```bash
workflows/scripts/build/ci-poll.sh <owner>/<repo> <pr>
```

**Headless one-shot runs (`PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`) — run the poll in the FOREGROUND, never backgrounded (#626).** A `claude -p` headless session has **no re-invoke-on-background-completion loop**, so a backgrounded poll would yield and end the session before CI finishes — the bug that left pipeline PRs green-but-never-merged (the full reason, why `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` is the marker, and why it is *narrower* than the `--unattended OR PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` operator-absent predicate — the interactive walk-away `--unattended` case has a working loop and keeps the backgrounded/timed path: [[Decisions/foundation - build headless (claude -p) foreground merge — no re-invoke loop (#626)]]). All three #626 foreground branches (here, 4b timed gate, 4b MERGED-poll) key on `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`. When `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`, instead run `ci-poll.sh` as an ordinary **blocking** `Bash` call (it loops to a terminal outcome itself) with an explicit bounded `--timeout "$BUILD_HEADLESS_POLL_TIMEOUT"` (under the ~10-min Bash foreground cap): `ci-poll.sh <owner>/<repo> <pr> --timeout "$BUILD_HEADLESS_POLL_TIMEOUT"`. The session itself is un-timeout'd, so if CI is still pending at the bound **re-issue the foreground poll once more before parking** (one retry within 3g, not zero — most CI finishes inside a second window); if it is *still* pending after that retry, park `[m]` with the `pr:` pointer and **stop this item** — the `funnel-merge-pending` hand-off marker (#624) resumes it next tick when CI is long-green. Do **not** yield expecting a re-invoke. (This branch is headless-only; operator-present runs keep the backgrounded two-sweep poll above unchanged.) <!-- cite: B.36 incident:F#626 -->

The script owns the poll mechanics: it resolves the PR head SHA **once** over REST, polls `commits/<sha>/check-runs` at a **≥30s** interval (gh api calls are core-REST, but there's no reason to poll tighter than CI completes; `--interval <secs>` overrides), and emits ONE structured JSON line from a **closed outcome set** — the orchestrator branches on `.outcome`, never parses prose. **Do not hand-roll the poll, and never use `gh pr checks --watch`** — that is GraphQL-backed and polls the *scarce* GraphQL bucket every few seconds; with parallel fanout that's N concurrent GraphQL pollers and a primary driver of rate-limit exhaustion (GH #53). CI check state is on the **core REST bucket** (roomy, barely used), so the script polls there. Flags: `--sha <sha>` pins the head explicitly (use it on a re-poll right after a force-push, before the PR object catches up); `--timeout <secs>` (default 3600) bounds the watch. <!-- cite: B.35 incident:F#53 -->

On the script's outcome:
- **`{"outcome":"CI_GREEN",…}`** → 3h.
- **`{"outcome":"CI_FAILED","failed_run_ids":[…],…}`** → the script reports *what* failed; **diagnosis — flake vs real, and what to tell the re-spawn — stays an LLM seat (the orchestrator's judgment), never the script's.** Inspect each failed run (`gh run view <run-id> --log-failed`, ids straight from `failed_run_ids`); re-invoke `Agent` against the **same pre-created `worktreePath`** (the `.build-guard` marker is still in place, so the guard stays armed), instructed to first `git fetch origin <item.branch> && git reset --hard FETCH_HEAD` (build on the pushed work), then "CI failed; fix and commit; do not push." **Before pushing the fix, re-run §3e against it (temperloop#1450)** — a CI-fix commit can touch anything, including the very command doc whose edit tripped the original failure, and skipping the review here would silently relocate §3e's structurally-guaranteed-skip class one stage later; the Workflow path's `ciPollLoop` does this by re-invoking `runReviewers()` (the same routing decision, re-read off the now-fixed worktree), escalating `review-blocking` on a HIGH finding exactly as the original 3f would, before any push. Then the orchestrator **force-pushes** the new SHA — `git push -f origin "$(git -C <worktreePath> rev-parse HEAD)":refs/heads/<item.branch>` — and re-runs `ci-poll.sh` on the new head, **pinning the head with `--sha <the same SHA you just force-pushed>`** (reuse the `git -C <worktreePath> rev-parse HEAD` value computed for the `git push -f`; loop within 3g). **Do NOT let the poll re-resolve the head from the PR API after a force-push** (i.e. never re-read `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<pr> --jq .head.sha`): GitHub's `pulls/{pr}` object can briefly lag and still return the **stale pre-push head**, whose check-runs are already green from the prior run — so a re-poll on that SHA prints a **false `CI_GREEN`** and advances the item to merge without ever running CI on the fixed code (#254, observed in the #508 retro). The locally-pushed SHA is authoritative by construction; the API round-trip only adds the race. (The *initial* 3g poll above takes no `--sha` and lets the script resolve `head.sha` once — that read is race-free because no force-push has occurred.)
- **`{"outcome":"NO_CI","waited":…,…}`** (temperloop#605) → zero check-runs ever appeared on the head SHA through the script's bounded grace window (never the full poll window) — a repo with no CI configured, not a hang. This is **not a failure and not a TIMEOUT**: emit a legible skip notice — `PR #N: no CI configured on this SHA — skipping the CI gate` — then **→ 3h (all of its steps 0–4 apply unchanged)**, with the skip recorded in the level summary (4a) alongside the parked item so it is never mistaken for a confirmed-green PR. Never re-enter 3g for this outcome and never block the item on it. <!-- cite: B.37 incident:K#605 -->
- **`{"outcome":"TIMEOUT",…}` / `{"outcome":"ERROR",…}`** (non-zero exit) → the poll never completed with CI genuinely pending or unreachable (zero-check-runs no longer routes here — that's `NO_CI` above). Never treat it as green: surface it, fix the cause (push the head, or re-run the poll), then re-enter 3g.

#### 3h. Park as `[m]` and move on

PR is green — **or** 3g resolved `NO_CI` (temperloop#605: no CI configured on this repo, a legible skip, not a failure). No human gate yet — that fires at the end of the level. Do all of, **in this order** (record durable success *before* destroying recovery material): <!-- cite: B.38 class:evidence-destroyed-before-record -->

0. **Parent-checkout cleanliness assertion — per-worker leak backstop (Option C, the detective layer).** `git status --porcelain` in the parent checkout, run **once per worker** (in the parallel fanout, at the join — see the within-level execution note). It must be **empty**: the guard hook (Option B) should have *prevented* any out-of-worktree write, and a pre-created worktree (Option A) means the worker never had a reason to touch the parent — so a dirty parent here means a leak slipped past both (e.g. the guard was un-armed, or a write path the guard's allow-list permitted). **Attribute and auto-discard by committed paths (bisect-by-committed-paths):** list the dirty paths (`git status --porcelain | awk '{print $2}'`), and for each, check whether it is a subset of the paths the worker already committed to its branch (`git -C <worktreePath> diff --name-only origin/<default>...HEAD`). A dirty path that **matches** a committed path is a confirmed leak of that worker's own edit — `git checkout -- <path>` to discard it (the committed copy on the branch is canonical). A dirty path that does **not** match any committed path is unexplained — surface it to the user rather than blind-discarding. Then tighten the next worker prompt's worktree-path instruction and confirm the worktree's `.build-guard` marker was present (the guard arms from it; a missing marker means 3b didn't run via `worktree.sh create`). Do **not** proceed with a dirty parent tree — Step 0's dirty-tree guard runs only at run start, so an uncaught leak here rides silently into the next item's branch.
1. **Record success first.** `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file` on `$1`: `[~] **<title>** ...` → `[m] **<title>** ... — PR #N parked for batch merge` (the `pr:`/`pushed_sha:` sub-lines from 3f are already present). Doing this *before* worktree removal guarantees a crash never leaves the gap "state says `[~]` but the worktree/evidence is gone." **If 3g resolved `NO_CI`** (temperloop#605), also stamp a `  - no_ci: true` sub-line in the same patch — same durable-sentinel pattern as `pr:`/`pushed_sha:`/`speculative: true`. **If the returned record carries `acceptance_unverified: true`** (temperloop#939 — a lost worker return recovered from side-effects), stamp `  - acceptance_unverified: true` in the same patch too, and treat that item's acceptance as **still owing verification**: re-verify it yourself before it rolls into the Step 4 gate. This is what lets Step 1.4's resume revalidation and the 4a summary tell "no checks were ever going to appear" apart from "checks appeared and are stuck/gone" for a parked item with no `statusCheckRollup` to point to.
2. **Board integration ON — best-effort marker clear; the claim is HELD until Done.** The board claim legitimately **stays In Progress** through the park — this is the sanctioned **claim-until-Done** flow (K#275, CLAUDE.md § Park, don't abandon): a parked `[m]` item is not done, so its claim is *held*, and the board half only leaves In Progress via the 4d Done write on merge or a deliberate skip. Clearing the local terminal marker here is therefore an **optional convenience, never a required step**: run `"$RELEASE" <gh_issue>` (the entrypoint resolved by the Step 0 probe) to clear the orchestrator window's claim marker (status bar → "No Issue Claimed") if it clears cleanly, but **do NOT fail or block the park on a non-zero release.** `release.sh` is per-window and one-marker-per-window, so in a **multi-claim window** — the documented within-level parallelism, where one session claims several items in a level — the marker holds only the *latest* claim and `release.sh` will refuse a *non-latest* issue (`this window holds a claim for #B, not #A — refusing`). That refusal is **expected and non-fatal**: leave the earlier claim held until Done; 4d's Done write and `reconcile.sh` catch it. The next item's 3a claim re-sets the marker regardless.
3. **Remove the orchestrator-created worktree LAST** (pure scratch once the SHA is pushed in 3f) at its **deterministic path** from 3b: `workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh remove <repo-root> <item.slug>` — removes the `.build-guard` marker, the worktree (force semantics built in, covering any uncommitted scratch the worker left behind), and the throwaway `build/<item.slug>` branch; structured outcome `REMOVED` (or `NOT_FOUND` on a re-run). `gh pr merge` at the gate is a remote op from the parent checkout, so no worktree is needed past this point. If the run aborts before this step, the leftover worktree is recoverable via `worktree.sh prune <repo-root>` (and the path is re-derivable from the slug) — strictly safer than losing the `[m]` record.
4. Move to the next item in this level. If this was the last item, fall through to Step 4 (batch merge gate).

#### 3h.5. As-you-go merge — land the clean-disjoint tier at its own green (conversational path only; temperloop#1026)

**SCOPE — this step runs on the `--no-workflow` CONVERSATIONAL path ONLY; on the default Workflow path it does not run at all (temperloop#1452).** Its actor is the conversational orchestrator driving 3a–3h inline: only that session is simultaneously *inside* the per-item loop (so "at its own green" is a moment it can act on) and holder of the two seats this step needs — the plan-note single-writer seat (the `[>]` flip that must be durable *before* the merge call) and the merge/consent seat. Neither seat can move to the Workflow: `build-level.mjs` **NEVER writes the plan note** and **NEVER merges** (§ Step 3 dual-path, and the orchestrator↔workflow boundary under 3d-esc), and giving it a merge seat would put an irreversible, un-consented merge inside a process that has no consent surface — against the kernel's § Merge autonomy & consent. Nor can the orchestrator do it from outside: the same #1430 boundary reasoning that puts §3e *inside* `driveItem` applies here in mirror image — the workflow's `parallel(driveItem)` returns only at the **level boundary**, so the orchestrator first sees an item's `parked` record when every sibling is already terminal-for-level, and "its own green" has long passed. **The accepted trade-off, stated plainly:** on the default path every item parks `[m]` at 3h and takes the single Step-4 level-boundary gate — i.e. the pre-#1026 merge-queue pileup this step was built to avoid is re-paid by default, and `--no-workflow` is the way to get as-you-go merging back (the same shape as speculative next-level execution's conversational-path-only NON-GOAL above). Building a merge-capable Workflow runtime, or a mid-level orchestrator merge callback, is separate epic-sized work, deliberately not attempted here.

**What this changes, and what it deliberately does not.** Parking every item until the level boundary charged the first-finished PR the slowest sibling's remaining CI *plus* the gate's response time, and then delivered the whole level to the merge queue as one burst (measured 2026-08-02: three PRs opened together took 68/69/145 min open-to-merge against 10–33 min for solo PRs — within-level parallelism converted into a merge-time pileup). This step lets an item that the **existing** Step-4a regime partition already places in the **clean-disjoint** tier take its consent and land now. It moves **when** an already-consented clean item merges — never **whether** consent is required, never **which** items count as clean-disjoint, and never the risky path: a risky / structurally-overlapping set is untouched here and takes the Step-4 modal gate at the level boundary byte-identically to before. The level boundary also stays a **dependency barrier for STARTING level *k+1*** (Step 4e advances only after this level's gate) — that is unchanged.

**Governed by `BUILD_MERGE_AS_YOU_GO`** (`build.config.sh`, sourced at Step 0; `${BUILD_MERGE_AS_YOU_GO:-1}` in a checkout that doesn't vendor it — named here, never valued, per § Named-setting convention). Off ⇒ skip this step entirely; the item stays `[m]` and the run is pure level-boundary batching, exactly as before this step existed. The setting is read **only on the conversational path** per the SCOPE paragraph above — on the default Workflow path its value is inert, and pure level-boundary batching is what happens regardless.

**Eligibility — five gates, ALL required. Any failure (or any UNKNOWN) leaves the item `[m]` and defers it to Step 4.** This asymmetry is the point: the fast path is only ever *narrower* than the level gate's tier, so it can never widen what counts as clean-disjoint.

1. **The switch is on and the always-modal kill-switch is not set.** `BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW=0` means "every set hard-blocks" (4a), so it disables this tier too.
2. **Risk predicates (b) and (c), over this one PR.** Run `gate.sh risk <owner>/<repo> <pr>` — the same machinery 4a runs, over a one-PR set — and read the PR with `gate.sh read`. `{"outcome":"RISKY",…}`, a `hold`/`risky` PR label, a `notes:` risk flag on the plan item, or a `mergeStateStatus` that is not `CLEAN` (after 4a's single re-poll for a lagging `UNKNOWN`/`BEHIND`) ⇒ **not eligible**.
3. **No unverified acceptance — and that means EITHER field, not just the first.** An item carrying `acceptance_unverified: true` (3h step 1) still owes the orchestrator's own re-verification and must never ride the fast path. **The identical bar applies to an item whose parked record carries a non-empty `host_config_deferrals` (3h, temperloop#1182)** — a *separate, non-overlapping* field, so a check written against `acceptance_unverified` alone does **not** cover it. Its criteria are equally UNVERIFIED, and the actor that settles them is **§4a — which this step by construction never reaches** ("its own green" has long passed by the level boundary).
