So an item carrying **either** field ⇒ **not eligible**: it stays `[m]` and takes the level-boundary gate, where §4a's parent-side verification runs before it joins the merge set. Letting a host-config deferral ride the fast path would merge a credential-gated criterion with **nobody having verified it** — the exact silent loss (#637/#1556) the deferral mechanism exists to close — and Step 6's `V + U + E MUST equal D` audit could not close either.
4. **Sibling-disjointness — the gate that keeps predicates (a) and (d) honest.** 4a's hunk-overlap probe **(a)** and the 4a.5 combined-tree pre-check **(d)** are *pairwise/union* predicates: over a set of one they are vacuous, and merging on that vacuity would silently widen the tier. So evaluate them against this level's siblings before merging. For **every other item in this level** that is not already terminal (`[x]`/`[v]`/`[-]`): resolve its changed paths from its **`origin/<sibling.branch>` remote-tracking ref** (`git diff --name-only origin/<default>...origin/<sibling.branch>` — the same ref 4a.5 uses, which exists from the moment 3f pushed and survives 3h's local `branch -D`) and intersect them with this item's own changed paths.
   - **A sibling with no pushed `origin/<branch>` yet makes disjointness UNKNOWN ⇒ not eligible.** Unknown is never read as disjoint.
   - **A non-empty path intersection with any sibling ⇒ not eligible.** At this seam file-overlap is *disqualifying*, not advisory as it is in 4a: there is no union tree to test and no second PR to hold, so an overlapping pair simply falls back to the Step-4 gate where the full two-tier (a) probe and the 4a.5 union pre-check run exactly as today.
5. **Ordinary item state.** The item carries a `pr:` and is not stamped `speculative:`/`escalated:`.

**Consent — the same seat, not a new one.** An eligible item is by construction a **clean-disjoint-independent set of one**, so it takes the *existing* **4b timed consent path** verbatim over that one-PR set — no new question, no new severity, no new backend. `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` ⇒ the headless immediate-merge branch (synchronous `OPEN`+`CLEAN` re-poll, then merge; no window, no objection scan). A loop-capable run announces the `BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW` window on the run's existing objection surface (in-session turns, or the epic-issue `OBJECT` receptacle on an operator-absent cron run) and re-validates at wake. **An objection, or a failed wake-time re-validation, cancels as-you-go for that item only: it re-parks `[m]` and rejoins the Step-4 gate** — it never escalates into a per-item modal ask of its own. Record the consent line in `## Merge gate log` exactly as 4b step 1 does (level, ISO-8601 timestamp, consent mode, the one-PR list), so resume-without-re-consent keeps working unchanged.

**Merge mechanics — and why stale-base handling runs PER MERGE.** Merge through the **same 4b backend paths**, selected by `gate.sh backend`: `NATIVE` ⇒ `gh pr merge <pr> --auto` plus the queue-aware poll / `diagnose-queue` / stall-recovery flow; `MANAGED` ⇒ `gate.sh managed-merge <owner>/<repo> <pr> --strict|--non-strict`. Never hand-roll a merge, and never batch two as-you-go merges into one call. Because each as-you-go merge is its **own** invocation of that per-PR machinery, the stale-base handling is exercised **per merge rather than once per level** — on `NATIVE` the queue re-tests and updates each entry's branch itself; on `MANAGED` `--strict` re-runs `update-branch` + a SHA-pinned CI re-poll on the *updated* head for every PR. That matters precisely because as-you-go merging advances `main` underneath still-open siblings: a sibling knocked to `BEHIND`/`DIRTY` is caught by its own gate 2 above if it has not parked yet, and by 4a's fresh mergeability read at the level gate if it has.

**Sentinels.** Flip `[~]`/`[m]` → **`[>]`** (merging as-you-go) at the moment consent is recorded and *before* the merge call — the same record-durable-success-first ordering 3h uses. `[>]` is **not** a repurposed `[m]`: `[m]` is *parked, awaiting consent*; `[>]` is *consented, in flight*, and Step 4a must never re-consent it. On confirmed `state == MERGED` run the **full 4d treatment** for that item (`[>]` → `[x] … merged in #N`, the post-merge worktree convergence, the issue-touch + item-efficiency emits, the backend-conditional board Done write, and the 4d-epic / 4d-pipeline / 4d-archive / 4d-retro checks) — 4d is already item-scoped, so it needs no change beyond accepting `[>]` as an input state. On anything short of confirmed `MERGED` — a `TIMEOUT`, an `EJECTED`, a `CONFLICTING`, or a stall recovery cannot clear — **demote `[>]` → `[m]`**, append the reason, and let the Step-4 gate dispose it: a merge-in-flight state is never left dangling.

**Ordering with 3h.** 3h runs first and in full (leak assertion, the durable `[m]` record, claim held, worktree removal); 3h.5 then acts on that recorded state. A crash between the two leaves an ordinary `[m]` item, which Step 1.4's resume revalidation handles exactly as today.

## Step 4 — Batch merge gate (per level)

Fires once all items in the current level have reached terminal-for-level state (`[m]`, `[>]`, `[x]`, `[v]`, `[-]`, or aborted). Spike items are already terminal at `[v]` (closed in the per-item kind-fork) and do not enter the gate. **The gate's selected set is the `[m]` items only, MINUS every item whose parked record carries a non-null `merge_blocked` (temperloop#2083)** — everything the 4a summary, the 4a risk partition, the 4a.5 union pre-check, and the 4b consent path operate over. **Apply that subtraction HERE, before 4a partitions anything**: a `merge_blocked` item parks `[m]` exactly like every other item, so this filter is the only thing standing between it and a consented merge — it is never a 4a risk-partition member, never a 4a.5 union candidate, and never offered as a 4b option or swept up by a 4b **Merge all**. It is *held*, not failed, so it is also never `[-]`; 3d-esc's `merge_blocked` disposition owns the hand fix that clears it, and the level summary's `dualBuild.pick.merge_blocked` list is where the flagged slugs come from. An item already `[x]` merged as-you-go at 3h.5 is done and never enters the gate; an item still `[>]` is **already consented and in flight**, so the gate only *confirms* it (poll to `MERGED` → the 4d treatment; on a failed/stalled merge demote it to `[m]` per 3h.5 and fold it into this gate's selected set) and **never re-consents it**. When no item was as-you-go eligible — the risky / structurally-overlapping case, **and every run on the default Workflow path, where 3h.5 does not run at all** (temperloop#1452) — every item is `[m]` and this gate behaves byte-identically to before 3h.5 existed. The `[>]`/`[x]`-already-merged arms above are therefore live only on the conversational path, or on a resume that inherited a `[>]` from one. For each `[m]` item:

### 4a. Surface the level summary

Print a single block to the user before the question, e.g.:

```
=== Level 0 merge gate: 6 parked PRs (backend: MANAGED · strict) ===
[m] sweep-watchdog-orphan-sleep         PR #201  CI ✓                    MERGEABLE/CLEAN
    ↳ defect #198: the watchdog sleeps forever on an orphaned sweep, so a stalled chunk never times out.   # arm 1 — one Problem-summary line per PR, under its own row
[m] tier2-baseline-partial-promote      PR #202  CI ✓                    MERGEABLE/CLEAN
    ↳ defect <org>/ssmobile#77: tier-2 baselines promote on a partial run, so a half-finished sweep overwrites a good baseline.   # arm 1, cross-repo item — qualified owner/repo#N per 3f
[m] b2b-artist-preservation             PR #203  CI ✓                    MERGEABLE/CLEAN
    ↳ no linked issue — chore: no tracker filed for this rename.   # arm 2 — item carries no gh_issue:
[m] diff-value-render-consistency       PR #204  CI ✓                    MERGEABLE/CLEAN
    ↳ summary unavailable — gh issue view #199 exited 1 (rate limit); NOT inferred from the title.   # arm 3 — never fabricated
[m] adapter-first-ci-run                PR #206  CI —  (no CI configured) MERGEABLE/CLEAN
[m] gemini-35-flash-detected-lang       PR #205  CI ✓                    CONFLICTING/DIRTY
# MANAGED+strict ⇒ the 5 CLEAN PRs (incl. #206's no-CI skip) merge serially via gate.sh managed-merge --strict (update-branch + re-CI each after the first); #205 → 4c
```

**Then print the level's disposition roster — TOTAL over the level, not just the merge set (temperloop#1310).** The gate's *selected set* is unchanged (the `[m]` items); what changes is what the operator **reads**. An item that merged as-you-go at 3h.5, captured a `[v]` verdict, was skipped, or is still escalated otherwise leaves the gate block entirely, so the one surface a reader who never opens `/workflows` sees at the level boundary answers for only *some* of the level's items. Right after the `[m]` rows above, run `"$machineryBinDir/plan.sh" roster "<planFile>" --level <k> --stage gate --owner-repo "$ownerRepo"` and print its stdout verbatim — the example below is the same level § 3-launch's example announced, at its next boundary. Each row's marker is the item's own plan sentinel (`claude/plan-schema.md` § Status sentinels) — the roster coins none of its own; `[?]` is the single exception and is **not** a sentinel, report-only for an item whose state could not be read, deliberately outside the schema's seven so nobody mistakes it for a state `plan.sh writeback` can produce.

```
--- Level 1 disposition: 4 items · 2 in the merge set · 2 outside it ---
[x] level-composition-report  #1310     merged in PR #1801 — as-you-go merge at 3h.5
[m] flaky-ci-detector         #1201     in the merge set — mergeability detailed above (PR #1802)
[v] keystone-changelog-spike  #1311     verdict captured — routed #1330
[m] pr-recap-round-trippable  no issue  in the merge set — mergeability detailed above (PR #1798)
```

Mergeability comes from `gh pr view <pr> --json mergeable,mergeStateStatus` per PR — surface **both** fields: `mergeable` (MERGEABLE / CONFLICTING / UNKNOWN) and `mergeStateStatus` (CLEAN / BEHIND / BLOCKED / DIRTY / …). If `mergeable: UNKNOWN`, wait ~3s and re-poll once before classifying it as non-mergeable. Note `mergeStateStatus` can **lag** right after a CI pass — a PR whose parent already *is* `main` HEAD may briefly read `BEHIND`; don't act on a single stale read. **Revalidate liveness too:** include `state,statusCheckRollup` in that read — a `[m]` parked in a prior session whose PR was since closed/merged out-of-band or went red must be surfaced (re-open / skip / re-run), never presented as a stale green tick (cf. Step 1.4).

**A `merge_blocked` item renders distinctly too, and is rendered EXCLUDED — `MERGE BLOCKED — <merge_blocked value>` in place of a mergeability verdict, with no consent option of its own** (temperloop#2083). It was subtracted from the selected set above, so the one risk this render closes is it vanishing from the operator's view entirely: a held item that is simply absent from the block reads identically to an item that merged. Print its row, name the literal (`arms-trailer-unstamped` today) and the hand fix 3d-esc gives it, and state the count in the same breath as the merge set's (`N parked PRs · M held: merge-blocked`) — so the gate's own text says what it is NOT merging and why, at the point it asks for consent on the rest.

**An `acceptance_unverified: true` item (stamped by 3h per temperloop#939) likewise renders distinctly — `acceptance ⚠ RECOVERED, unverified`, never a bare acceptance ✓** — since its worker's self-check never ran; the same reasoning below applies to it verbatim. **A `NO_CI` item (the item's `  - no_ci: true` sentinel, stamped by 3h per temperloop#605) renders distinctly in this summary — `CI —  (no CI configured)`, never `CI ✓`** — so a genuinely-untested-by-CI PR is never visually indistinguishable from a confirmed-green one at the one point a human (or a log reviewer) sees the set before it merges. This distinction matters most on the `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` headless immediate-merge path (3g, 4b) — nobody reads the transcript live before that merge fires, so the rendered summary line is the *only* record of "this PR's mergeability was never actually vetted by CI" surviving into the level's audit trail.

**Verify every host-config deferral HERE, parent-side, before the item joins the merge set (temperloop#1182).** An item whose parked record carries `host_config_deferrals: [{criterion, host_config}, …]` (3h) has acceptance criteria its worker **structurally could not observe** — they turn on a gitignored, host-local file, and a worktree is populated from the git index, so the reading is absent/`false` in every worktree on every host regardless of the truth. Same family as `acceptance_unverified` above: these are **UNVERIFIED, not passed**, and they render distinctly in the summary block — `acceptance ⚠ N host-config DEFERRED (verify parent-side)`, never a bare `acceptance ✓`. **You are the actor that can settle them**: this session runs in the real checkout, which *does* carry the file. So settle every deferral **here — after the summary block is rendered and BEFORE the merge regime is computed below** (the ordering is load-bearing: an unsettled deferral must never reach the risk partition, because a `clean-disjoint` verdict is what puts an item in the timed tier). Per deferral: **run the criterion's own check in the parent checkout** — the repo root you were invoked from, never the item's worktree, and **never** by copying the named `host_config` file into a worktree (that is the secret-in-worktree exposure `/assess` A.8 exists to prevent, and it is not an option here at any urgency). Prefer the criterion's own named command (`… --dry-run`, a health-check target); a bare presence probe (`[ -f <file> ]` / a `grep` for the var name) is acceptable **only** when the criterion is itself about presence — the A.8 bar is *confirmed set*, not *location named*, so a file that exists with the value unset does **not** satisfy it. And **never** resolve a deferral by re-spawning the worker: a re-spawn lands in a worktree again and reproduces the identical uninformative reading — that loop is the foundation#1556 failure, not a fix for it. **Dispose by outcome, and tally it in the Step 6 summary (`Host-config deferrals`).** **Confirmed** → render `acceptance ✓ (host-config verified parent-side: <host_config>)` and the item is merge-eligible as normal. **Not confirmed** (the value is genuinely unset on this host) → the acceptance really has failed: the item is **not** merge-eligible; treat it as the 4c non-mergeable case and surface it (`decision_sink_ask`, `ask-now`) naming the exact file/env var the operator must populate. **Cannot establish** (the check itself errored, or this checkout is not the host the criterion is about — e.g. a criterion about the cron host verified from an operator checkout) → say which, hold the item out of the merge set, and route it as an `ask-now`; **never** silently promote an unestablished deferral to ✓. None of these arms relaxes the A.8 bar — they move *who* confirms, never *whether*.

**Every `[m]` row carries its issue's defect line — problem first, then the fix (temperloop#1485).** Render `claude/message-schema.md`'s **Question block** template § Problem summary slot as the `↳ defect #<n>: …` line under each PR's own row **in this text block**, never as part of a 4b option label — those labels are length-capped and the tool caps at 4 options, which is exactly why the per-issue defect belongs here instead. That template owns the slot's shape (a compressed one-to-two-line restatement of the issue's own defect statement, **not** its title — the title already rides in the row above it), owns its **three arms**, and owns the **never-fabricate rule**; do not restate any of that here.

**Read the defect prose here — the run does not already hold it.** Knowing an item's `gh_issue:` is knowing a *number*: nothing earlier in `/build` fetches an issue **body** (`issue-state.sh resolve` reads `state,labels,assignees,url` only, and 4a/4b never call it at all), so a line sourced from "what the run has" would take the unavailable arm on every item. Instead run **one** `gh issue view <n> -R <issue-repo> --json body -q .body` **per `[m]` item, here at the gate** — the gate fires once per level and is already issuing `gh` calls (the `backend` probe below, the per-PR `gh pr view` mergeability read), so one issue read per parked PR is negligible against what it buys the operator. Render the arm the read selects, per `[m]` row:

- **Arm 1 — summary rendered.** Non-empty body → `↳ defect #<n>: <the compressed restatement>`.
- **Arm 2 — `↳ no linked issue — <one-line reason>`.** The item carries **no** `gh_issue:` (a refactor or chore with no tracker) — never a silently-omitted line, so a reader can tell "nothing was filed" from "somebody dropped the summary".
- **Arm 3 — `↳ summary unavailable — <one-line reason>`.** The read exited non-zero or returned an empty body — issue deleted, `gh` auth failure, rate limit, network error, blank body. Name which, and **do not** substitute the item's title, the PR title, or the slug: the template forbids inferring the defect, and this summary is the artifact a fabrication would corrupt.

**Cross-repo qualification — mirror 3f's `Closes` rule.** When the item's `repo:` differs from the plan's home repo (`ownerRepo`), the issue is tracked in `ownerRepo` while the PR opens against `repo:` — exactly the split 3f's "Cross-repo `repo:` honor point" already handles. So fetch with `-R <ownerRepo>` and render the ref **fully qualified**: `↳ defect <ownerRepo>#<n>: …` (and `↳ no linked issue`/`↳ summary unavailable` unchanged). The common same-repo case stays bare `#<n>`, as 3f's bare `Closes #N` does. An item's `also_closes:` entries each get their own `↳ defect …` line, on these same rules.

**Detect the merge regime here, before any merge** — it decides 4b. Two scripted probes run here, both read-only against `main`, both returning closed JSON the orchestrator branches on (never prose-parsed):

- **`backend` probe (scripted — selects the 4b merge path).** Run `workflows/scripts/build/gate.sh backend <owner>/<repo>`. Closed outcome set: `{"outcome":"NATIVE"}` | `{"outcome":"MANAGED"}` | `{"outcome":"MANAGED","probe_failed":true}` (always exit 0). Mechanics (as built): the `BUILD_MERGE_BACKEND` setting (`build.config.sh`) short-circuits an explicit `native`/`managed` override **without probing**; otherwise the script probes the branch ruleset (`repos/<owner>/<repo>/rules/branches/main`) for a `merge_queue` rule. **A probe failure resolves to `MANAGED`, never `NATIVE`** (fail-safe: MANAGED never silently arms a merge nobody chose), flagged `probe_failed:true` so "no queue" and "couldn't tell" stay distinguishable — **surface `probe_failed:true` in the 4a summary line** (e.g. `backend: MANAGED (probe failed — could not read branch rules; set BUILD_MERGE_BACKEND=native if this repo has a queue)`), never swallow it. `NATIVE` → the 4b native-queue path; `MANAGED` → the 4b managed path.
- **`strict` probe (scripted — picks the managed sub-flag only).** Run `workflows/scripts/build/gate.sh strict <owner>/<repo>` → `{"outcome":"STRICT"}` | `{"outcome":"NON_STRICT"}` (reads `required_status_checks.strict`; a 404 *"Branch not protected"* → `NON_STRICT`). Its **sole remaining role** is selecting `gate.sh managed-merge`'s flag on the MANAGED backend: `STRICT` → `--strict`, `NON_STRICT` → `--non-strict`. On `STRICT` the managed batch merges **serially** (each merge advances `main`; each subsequent PR's `--strict` update-branch re-runs CI) — say so in the summary so the serial cadence (≈ one CI-length wait per PR after the first) isn't a surprise. On the NATIVE backend the probe is informational only (the queue itself enforces the up-to-date guarantee).

**The `backend` axis is the SUCCESSOR of the old 4a `queue` axis** — the regime table is now 1×2:

| backend | 4b merge path |
|---------|----------------|
| NATIVE | Native queue: `gh pr merge <pr> --auto` per PR (no `--queue`/`--merge`/`--delete-branch` — the queue owns strategy + branch lifecycle); the queue's `merge_group` trial branch serializes/batches and updates branches itself. `update-branch` nudge RETIRED. **The live state of both org build repos** (queues stood up after the #330 org migration; see [[Decisions/foundation - build native merge-queue adoption]]). |
| MANAGED | Managed serial loop (temperloop#13 — a repo with no native queue, e.g. a free personal repo that can't provision one): orchestrator-driven per-PR `gate.sh managed-merge <owner>/<repo> <pr> --strict\|--non-strict` calls in plan-item order (flag from the `strict` probe above). See 4b "Merge — MANAGED backend". |

**Migration note (retires the old cell names):** the old 2×2's `queue=ON` cells **C/D** ≡ `NATIVE`; its `queue=OFF` cells **A/B** are **absorbed by `MANAGED`**, with the strict/non-strict split demoted from a path-selecting axis to `managed-merge`'s `--strict|--non-strict` sub-flag — `--non-strict` keeps cell A's cheap immediate-merge cost profile (no update-branch, no CI re-poll), `--strict` replaces cell B's hand-rolled `--auto` + `update-branch`-nudge serialization with scripted per-PR mechanics. There is no third axis over the old 2×2.

(`backend`/`strict` select only the *mechanical* merge path — they are **orthogonal** to the block-vs-timed regime computed next, which decides whether the run blocks for a human first.)

**Compute the block-vs-timed regime here too — it decides whether 4b is modal or timed.** Per [[Decisions/foundation - Timed merge-gate auto-merge (supersedes never-auto-merge-main)]], the selected set is either **risky → hard-block (modal, explicit approval, never timed-auto-merge)** or **clean-disjoint-independent → timed (countdown then auto-merge unless objected)**. The set is **risky** iff **ANY** of the following structural conditions holds — evaluate them mechanically over the selected PRs, no judgment call:
- **(a) Overlapping *hunks*** — two selected PRs touch the **same file AND their changed line ranges actually intersect**. Same-file-touch alone is **NOT** risky: git's 3-way merge auto-resolves disjoint hunks even within one file, so "both touch `foo`" ≠ "will conflict" — this predicate over-approximated conflict as file-collision and forced the modal gate on sets that merge perfectly cleanly (e.g. L1's #317/#318, two disjoint same-file PRs that tripped the gate then merged clean). The check is now two-tier: <!-- cite: B.39 incident:PR#317 -->
  - **File-overlap probe (cheap, advisory).** For each selected PR, `git diff --name-only origin/main..<branch>`; compute the pairwise path intersection. A non-empty intersection is **surfaced as an advisory note** in the 4a summary (e.g. `note: PRs #X and #Y both touch claude/commands/build.md`) but does **NOT** by itself make the set risky — a same-file/disjoint-hunk set stays **timed-eligible** (auto-merges after the window unless objected, or lands via the native queue on a `NATIVE` backend).
  - **Hunk-overlap probe (only on a same-file pair → decides risky).** For each pair that shares a file `F`, test whether their changes to `F` genuinely conflict. **First refresh the two refs — `git fetch origin <branchA> <branchB>` — then trial-merge the `origin/`-qualified refs, never the bare local branch names.** Run a trial 3-way merge per shared file — `git merge-tree --write-tree origin/<branchA> origin/<branchB>` (or, on older git, `git merge-tree $(git merge-base origin/main origin/<branchA> origin/<branchB>) origin/<branchA> origin/<branchB>`) and check for a conflict marker / non-zero conflict status; **a detected textual conflict** on any shared file makes the set **risky** (→ modal). *(Why `origin/` + an explicit fetch: a bare `<branch>` resolves against whatever the local clone last saw — a leftover from a removed worktree, or a ref diverged from what was actually force-pushed — so the probe would trial-merge the wrong trees and report a **spurious** conflict, converting an unattended timed merge into an operator interrupt. The fetched `origin/<branch>` ref is the pushed PR-head the queue would actually integrate — the same load-bearing reasoning § 4a.5 states for `combined-tree-precheck.sh`.)* If the trial merge is clean, the same-file overlap is **disjoint-hunk** → advisory only, set stays timed-eligible. (A genuine conflict that only surfaces later, at merge time, still routes to **4c** via the `CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY` `mergeStateStatus` in (c) — the queue, on a `NATIVE` backend, is itself the safety net that serializes/re-tests, which is *why* same-file-disjoint no longer needs a blocking tax here.)
- **(b) Flagged PR** — any selected PR carries a `hold` or `risky` **label** (`gh pr view <pr> --json labels`), **or** its plan item has a `notes:` **risk flag** (a `notes:` field on the plan item marking risk).
- **(c) Not-CLEAN mergeStateStatus** — any selected PR's `mergeStateStatus` is **not `CLEAN`** (i.e. `BEHIND` / `BLOCKED` / `DIRTY` / `UNKNOWN`), read from the same `gh pr view --json mergeable,mergeStateStatus` above (after the single re-poll for a lagging `UNKNOWN`/`BEHIND`).
- **(d) Combined-tree pre-check failed** (foundation#865) — the **Step 4a.5** union pre-check below returned `CONFLICT` (a parked branch will not merge into the accumulating union — a textual collision the pairwise (a) probe's ordering can miss) or `GATE_FAILED` (the union merges cleanly but fails the full gate suite as a *combined* tree — a **semantic** collision two green-alone PRs create together: a new gate plus the files it scans, a target/`.PHONY` registered in only one branch). Either verdict makes the set **risky** → the 4b modal/park path, **never** a timed auto-merge — because no merge *consent* can make a gate-failing union land (the native queue's own `merge_group` would reject it downstream anyway); the operator holds the offending PR(s) and merges the rest, or aborts. Evaluated only when the set has **>1 PR** and `BUILD_COMBINED_TREE_PRECHECK` is not off (see § 4a.5). <!-- cite: B.40 incident:F#865 -->

**Read the deferred `## Questions` batch here too — it is drained at this gate (4f).** At gate-entry, re-read `$1` and collect every **unanswered** (`[ ]`) entry in the plan note's `## Questions` section (the `ask-at-gate` decisions deferred during this level — Step 2.5 issue creation, Step 2.6 epic creation, Step 0.5 reconcile auto-fix, Step 4c conflict dispositions). These are surfaced as **ONE batch before merge consent is recorded** (4f), in whichever regime this gate runs — they do **not** add a second interruption. An empty/absent `## Questions` section is the common case (no deferrals this level) → the gate runs exactly as before.

If **none** of (a)/(b)/(c)/(d) holds, the set is **clean, disjoint, and independent → timed** (trigger (d) is resolved by the Step 4a.5 pre-check below and folds into this determination before the regime is finalized) — a same-file/disjoint-hunk advisory note (the (a) file-overlap probe firing without a hunk conflict) does **not** flip this; it is printed in the summary and the set stays timed. **Window:** `BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW` (seconds); **`BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW=0` forces every set to hard-block** — the always-modal kill-switch that restores the prior always-explicit-approval behavior. **The kill-switch is evaluated here, at gate-entry (4a), so it must be set BEFORE the gate fires to take effect.** Changing `BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW=0` *mid-window* does **not** preempt a countdown already running — once 4a has selected the timed regime and scheduled the wake, the only mid-window intervention is the objection mechanism (any operator response → modal, per 4b), not the env var. State the selected regime (and, when timed, the window) in the 4a summary so the operator knows whether a countdown is running.

### 4a.5. Combined-tree pre-check — the union gate before enqueue (foundation#865)

**Why here.** Steps (a)–(c) above are cheap, per-PR/pairwise predicates read from the GitHub API; they catch textual overlap and flagged/non-CLEAN PRs but are **blind to a semantic collision** — two PRs each green in their own CI whose *combination* breaks the build (a new gate plus the files it scans; a `make` target or `.PHONY` entry one branch registers and the other's files trip; the Epic-B retro's #811×#812 / #840×#841 / #801, F#847). Today those surface only **inside** the native queue's `merge_group` trial branch — an eject → diagnose → rebase → requeue cycle costing ~1h each. This step moves that discovery **left of the enqueue**, where a collision costs one local gate run.

**When it runs.** Only when the selected set has **more than one** parked PR **and** `BUILD_COMBINED_TREE_PRECHECK` is not off (the opt-out setting, `build.config.sh` — Step 0 sources it; named here, never valued). A single-PR level cannot collide with a sibling (the queue's own `merge_group` still tests it), and the setting disables the check outright — both cases skip straight to 4b.

**Invoke** `workflows/scripts/build/combined-tree-precheck.sh <repo-root> <ref> <ref> [...]` passing each parked PR's **`origin/<item.branch>` remote-tracking ref** — NOT the bare local `build/<slug>` branch. This is load-bearing: on the conversational path 3h step 3 already removed each parked item's worktree **and its local branch** (`worktree.sh remove` runs `git branch -D`) at park time, *before* this gate — so a bare local ref would no longer resolve and the pre-check would `die` → `ERROR` → fail-open → a silent no-op on the default path (the exact defeat this note prevents). The `origin/<item.branch>` ref survives that local `branch -D` (it lives under `refs/remotes/origin/…`, which the 3f push updated and 3h never touches) and is precisely the pushed PR-head SHA the queue would integrate. Base defaults to `origin/main` — the ref the queue integrates against. The script builds a throwaway detached worktree, `git merge`es each ref into the union in plan-item order, runs the full gate suite (`scripts/quality-gates.sh`, == CI's `checks` job) against the merged tree, and tears the worktree down. Closed JSON, one line — **branch on `.outcome`, never parse prose**:

- **`CLEAN`** → the union merges and passes every gate → proceed to 4b with the regime 4a computed (unchanged).
- **`CONFLICT`** (`.branch` names the offending merge) → the parked branches **will not merge together** — a 3-way union conflict the cheap pairwise (a) probe missed. This fires risk-trigger **(d)** the same as `GATE_FAILED` below → the set is **risky** → the **4b modal/park path**, where the operator **holds the offending PR** (its `.branch`) and merges the rest, or aborts; the held PR takes a fresh gate in a later pass against an advanced `main`. **Do NOT** route it to 4c and merge it standalone — 4c only re-tests against `main` (where a union-only conflict is invisible: the branch is `CLEAN` against `main` individually), so merging it there would silently re-introduce the very collision this check caught. Naming the offender is a label on the *offending merge in this order*, not a root-cause claim.
- **`GATE_FAILED`** (`.failed_gates` names the gate(s) the suite's roll-up blamed, `.output` carries **those gates' own inline sections** — the `FAIL:` reason itself, not a blind tail of the stream — `.suite_log` the path to the retained full log, `.exit_code` the suite's code; temperloop#880) → the set **cannot batch-merge as-is**; this fires risk-trigger **(d)** → the set is **risky** → the **4b modal/park path**. Surface `.failed_gates` **and** the `.output` reason in the 4a summary, pointing at `.suite_log` for the untruncated log. On an **attended** run the modal (the risky-set `decision_sink_ask`) lets the operator hold the offending PR(s) and merge the rest, or abort; on an **operator-absent** run it takes the **existing** risky-set operator-absent path (async decision-issue on the epic, park the level `[m]`, stop) — no new writer surface, and never a timed auto-merge of a union the queue would reject.
- **`SKIP`** (fewer than two branches — a defensive echo) → proceed to 4b.
- **`ERROR`** (the pre-check's own tooling failed — bad ref, worktree-add failure) → **fail-open**: note it in the 4a summary and proceed to 4b **without** the pre-check's protection, exactly as a missing quota snapshot resolves to "proceed". The native queue's `merge_group` remains the downstream backstop; the run must never stall on the pre-check's own failure.

**Cost.** One full local gate-suite run per multi-PR level — a modest tax that buys back a ~1h queue round-trip per collision it catches. The opt-out setting (named at *When it runs* above) disables the check for a repo/run that would rather rely on the queue's `merge_group` alone.

### 4b. Mergeable set — approval (modal or timed), then merge

**Select the approval regime from the 4a block-vs-timed computation.** A **risky** set (4a (a)/(b)/(c), or `BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW=0`) takes the **hard-block modal** path; a **clean, disjoint, independent** set takes the **timed** path. Both paths converge on the identical merge mechanics below — the regime changes only *when/whether the run blocks for a human*, never the merge incantation. Per [[Decisions/foundation - Timed merge-gate auto-merge (supersedes never-auto-merge-main)]].

**Surface the deferred `## Questions` batch first (4f) — modal path.** Before the merge-approval question, present every unanswered `## Questions` entry collected in 4a as **one batch** alongside the merge gate (resolve them per 4f, recording each answer/default back into the section). Showing them here — at the modal gate the operator is already attending — is what makes the deferral free: the operator answers the deferred decisions and the merge in one sitting, never mid-level.

**Approval — risky set (hard-block, modal).** Require explicit approval; **never** timed-auto-merge a risky set. Surface via `decision_sink_ask(<the risky set + why it blocked>, <one selectable option per mergeable PR (label = slug + PR # + title; default = all selected, user deselects any to hold) + an **Abort the run** option that short-circuits everything else>, ask-now)` with `multiSelect: true` — operator-present → modal `AskUserQuestion`, the **identical multi-select approval prompt** as before; operator-absent → async decision-issue posted on the **plan note's epic issue** (not any single item's issue) with the risky-set summary and the three options (Merge all / Hold some — specify which / Abort), `decision` label applied, operator assigned, tick stops for this level. **The tool caps at 4 options** — when more than 4 PRs are parked, you can't list one per PR. Instead print the per-PR summary as text (4a) and ask a single single-select `decision_sink_ask(...)`: **Merge all** / **Hold some — I'll name them** / **Abort the run**. The seam carries only the ask; the deferred `## Questions` (4f) drain that is shown alongside it and the merge incantation both stay **outside** the call (and the 4f drain precedes the merge, unchanged). Surface *why* the set hard-blocked (the offending predicate — overlapping *hunks* (a real same-file conflict detected by the 4a trial merge, **not** mere same-file overlap, which is advisory) / flagged PR / not-CLEAN, or the `=0` kill-switch) so the operator knows the gate isn't timed. **The per-PR defect lines stay in the 4a text block, never in the option labels** (temperloop#1485) — the labels are length-capped and cap at 4, so 4a's `↳ defect #<n>: …` rendering above is the only place the operator sees what each PR fixes; print that block immediately before this ask (both regimes — the timed path's announce presents the same 4a summary), so the problem is in view when consent is given. This ask therefore **satisfies** `claude/message-schema.md`'s **Question block** template § Problem summary slot **via 4a's rendering** — including its three arms (`↳ defect …` / `↳ no linked issue — …` / `↳ summary unavailable — …`) and its never-fabricate rule — and never restates the slot's shape in its own payload.

**Approval — clean-disjoint-independent set (timed auto-merge).** Present the green set (the 4a summary) plus the countdown, then auto-merge the whole set via the merge mechanics below **after the window elapses, UNLESS an operator objection arrives first OR a wake-time re-validation finds the set no longer clean**:
- **Headless one-shot runs (`PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`, per the 3g definition) skip the objection window entirely and merge immediately (#626).** Load-bearing reason — no re-invoke loop → a `ScheduleWakeup` never returns to this plan's gate → no timed window can run; and the structural risky-set hard-block, not a per-merge window, is the real supervision (a risky set never reaches this timed branch): [[Decisions/foundation - build headless (claude -p) foreground merge — no re-invoke loop (#626)]]. So when `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`: do **not** `ScheduleWakeup`, do **not** announce a window, and **do NOT run the objection scan** (check 2 below) — **no window was opened, so there is no objection period and no `<announce-ts>` anchor; any epic-issue `OBJECT` comment that exists is stale from a prior window and MUST NOT be read here** (reading it would silently park every future headless merge on this plan — the failure this branch must avoid). Run **only check 1 synchronously, now**: re-poll every PR `OPEN`+`CLEAN` (a fast `gh pr view`, no wait). If all pass, apply any unanswered `## Questions` (4f) defaults inline, then proceed **straight to the merge incantation** for the re-confirmed-clean set. If any PR is no longer `OPEN`+`CLEAN`, fall back to the modal hard-block path exactly as the timed path does (operator-absent → async decision-queue on the epic issue, park, stop). State `immediate-merge (headless, no window)` in the 4a summary. The bullets that follow — the `ScheduleWakeup` window, the re-homed announce, **and the operator-absent objection receptacle** — serve **loop-capable runs only** (operator-present, and the interactive walk-away `--unattended` case, both of which have a working re-invoke loop); under #626 a `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1` run is headless-no-loop and takes this immediate-merge branch instead, so the cron objection-receptacle bullet is **superseded for the pipeline** by this branch.
- **Reuse the existing approval-poll / `ScheduleWakeup` mechanism** — the same self-paced wake loop `assess` Step 6 uses to watch a plan's `status`. Schedule a wake `BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW` seconds out; **encode the absolute deadline timestamp AND the announce timestamp in the `ScheduleWakeup` `prompt` itself** (not just working memory) so they survive context summarization across the window, exactly as the Step-6 poll encodes its `ASSESS_POLL_BUDGET` deadline. Announce the window and the set up front, **including any unanswered `## Questions` entries collected in 4a** so the operator can act on them within the window: *"Merging N PRs in 5 min unless you object — reply to cancel. P deferred questions will take their defaults at the deadline unless you answer: <one-line per entry with its default>."*
- **Re-homed objection receptacle (operator-absent / cron runs).** On an operator-present run the in-session turn stream is the objection surface — any reply cancels the timer. On an **operator-absent / cron run** that in-session surface does not exist (there are no operator turns to scan), so "no turns found" would silently mean "no objection" and the gate would always auto-merge — a structural degradation. **Fix:** at announce time, when the run is operator-absent, post a comment on the **plan note's epic issue** (the same issue the risky-set modal path already uses) that announces the merge window and names the `OBJECT` keyword:
  ```
  Merge window open — Level <k> (<N> PRs): <PR list>.
  Merging in ~<window>s unless you reply OBJECT on this issue within the window.
  Deadline: <ISO-8601-deadline>.
  ```
  Encode the **epic issue number** in the `ScheduleWakeup` `prompt` alongside the deadline and announce timestamps, so it survives context summarization. The objection check at wake-time (check 2 below) then reads comments on that issue, not in-session turns. **The epic issue is the per-window-level objection receptacle on cron runs, not a new decision issue** — no `decision` label is applied (it is the merge-window announcement, not a pending decision). Operator replies with `OBJECT` on this issue to cancel. If the epic issue is unavailable (board integration OFF or no `epic:` in the plan frontmatter), fall through to the **modal hard-block path above** instead of running the timed path (the timed path requires a durable objection surface that exists across ticks; without a GH issue it has none).
- **Window-elapse applies each `## Questions` entry's default (4f, timed path).** A deferred entry still `[ ]` when the window elapses **takes its `default`** — the same auto-proceed the timed gate already applies to the merge itself (absence of objection = consent). Resolve each such entry per 4f (tick `[x]`, record `→ default taken: <value>`) **before** executing the merge. This is what lets a fully-unattended timed gate proceed end-to-end with no silent stall — every deferred decision has a value to proceed on.
- **Objection cancels — and routes the deferred questions into the modal fallback.** **Any operator response within the window** cancels the auto-merge and falls back to the **modal hard-block path above** (the `decision_sink_ask(...)` risky-set approval), where the operator selects/holds/aborts **and** answers the deferred `## Questions` batch (4f, modal path) rather than letting them default. The post-announce objection scan (the durable check below, check 2) stays **outside** the seam and **precedes** the fallback ask and the merge incantation, unchanged. (No response is the walk-away case the timed gate exists to serve — there the defaults are taken.)
- **Wake-time re-validation (BLOCKER guard — required before any merge).** When the wake fires at the deadline, **BEFORE executing any merge**, the orchestrator must re-confirm the set is still safe — the 4a risk predicate (esp. `mergeStateStatus`) was computed once at gate-entry and a PR can silently flip `CLEAN → BEHIND/BLOCKED/DIRTY` during the window (e.g. another merge advanced `main`). Two checks, both required: <!-- cite: B.41 incident:F#626 -->
  1. **Re-poll every PR in the presented set:** `gh pr view <pr> --json state,mergeable,mergeStateStatus`. **Proceed with the timed auto-merge only if ALL PRs still read `state == OPEN` + `mergeStateStatus == CLEAN`.** If **any** PR is no longer `OPEN`+CLEAN, the timed consent is void: fall the **whole set** back to the **modal hard-block path above** (the `decision_sink_ask(...)` risky-set approval) — except a PR that has developed a genuine content conflict (`CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY`) routes to **4c** while the rest re-enter the modal gate. (`mergeStateStatus` lag still applies — re-poll once after ~3s before treating a lone `BEHIND`/`UNKNOWN` as a real flip, per 4a.)
  2. **Re-check for a post-announce objection — surface depends on operator-presence (re-homed, not inherited):**
     - **Operator-present path (unchanged):** scan for any operator turn that arrived since the **announce timestamp** encoded in the wake prompt. Do **not** rely solely on implicit harness turn-ordering to have surfaced an objection — if any operator turn is present since the announce, treat it as an objection: cancel the auto-merge and fall back to the modal path.
     - **Operator-absent path (cron/tick — re-homed onto the epic issue):** scan comments on the **plan note's epic issue** (whose number is encoded in the wake prompt — see the announce step above) posted after the **announce timestamp**. If any comment body contains the word `OBJECT` (case-insensitive, anywhere in the line), treat it as an objection: cancel the auto-merge and fall back to the modal hard-block path (`decision_sink_ask(...)` on the epic issue, as the risky-set path already does — operator-absent backend). Implementation: `gh issue view <epic> -R "$REPO" --json comments --jq '[.comments[] | select(.createdAt > "<announce-ts>") | .body] | map(test("OBJECT";"i")) | any'`. A `true` result cancels. The epic issue comment is the durable, cross-tick objection surface that a ScheduleWakeup-alone cannot provide on a cron run.
  **"Absence of objection = consent" applies only to a set re-confirmed clean at merge time.** Only when both checks pass — all PRs OPEN+CLEAN and no post-announce objection (via session turns or epic-issue comment, per path above) — does the wake proceed. **Serialization order at wake-time (preserved, load-bearing — none of these is inside any seam call; the timed walk-away path issues no `decision_sink_ask`):** (1) the post-announce objection scan (check 2 above) → (2) apply each unanswered `## Questions` entry's default (4f, timed path: tick `[x]`, record `→ default taken: <value>`, take the deferred action) → (3) the merge incantation below. So when both checks pass: **apply each unanswered `## Questions` entry's default first** — then merge the presented set via the mechanics below. (If check 2 found a post-announce objection, the deferred batch routes into the modal fallback per the objection bullet above, where the operator answers it via the `decision_sink_ask(...)` risky-set approval — defaults are taken only on the clean walk-away path.)
- **Strict `main` still requires MERGED-confirmation on the timed path.** The timed/modal distinction does **not** relax the #130 premature-close guard: the timed path still requires poll-until-`state == MERGED` confirmation before any 4d Done-move — on a `NATIVE` backend via the "Merge — NATIVE backend" poll predicate (a `--auto` enqueue is never landed), on a `MANAGED` backend via `gate.sh managed-merge`'s internal confirm poll (its `MERGED` outcome *is* that confirmation) — a timed-gate merge is never treated as landed merely because the wake fired and the merge was queued/issued.
- This **only** removes *blocking* friction on a set that is provably safe **both at announce and at wake time**; it does **not** change *which* PRs merge (the whole presented green set) or *how* (the unchanged incantation below).

**Select the merge path from the 4a `backend` probe first — the backend is resolved at run time by `gate.sh backend <owner>/<repo>`, never hard-coded per repo.** `NATIVE` ⇒ the **"Merge — NATIVE backend"** path immediately below. `MANAGED` ⇒ the **"Merge — MANAGED backend"** path after it. Because the probe reads the repo's *current* branch ruleset each run, **a repo's backend can change** (a native merge queue can be stood up or torn down), so always take the path from this run's probe rather than assuming one from the repo's identity. Today the **native-queue path is the live path on the org build repos and on temperloop** — all currently probe `NATIVE`; in particular **temperloop probes `NATIVE`** (its `main` has a native merge queue), so despite being the free-tier kernel repo it is **NOT** the MANAGED case. The managed path is the live path only on a repo with no native queue (one that can't provision or has disabled its queue) and is the fallback when the backend probe fails.

**Merge — NATIVE backend (`NATIVE` — native merge queue; formerly "queue ON", cells C/D — unchanged in behavior):** for **every** selected PR, run **`gh pr merge <pr> --auto`** — the queue-ON incantation from Step 0 (**no** `--queue`/`--merge`/`--delete-branch`: `--queue` is not a real gh-2.93 flag, and a queue branch owns the merge strategy + branch deletion). `--auto` arms auto-merge, which enqueues the PR once its required checks pass. The queue serializes/batches via its own `merge_group` trial branch (an integration branch GitHub builds + tests as a combined candidate, then merges FIFO once branch-protection checks pass), and **updates branches itself** — so the per-PR `update-branch` nudge loop (the #83 workaround, now folded into `gate.sh managed-merge --strict`'s own mechanics on the MANAGED path) is **RETIRED for queue repos**. Then drive the queue-aware poll/stall path:

1. **Poll predicate.** After each `--auto`, poll `gh pr view <pr> --json state,mergeStateStatus,mergedAt`. **gh 2.93 exposes NO merge-queue-entry field** — `mergeQueueEntry` is not a valid `--json` field (it returns nothing), and `autoMergeRequest` reads **null even while a PR is queued** (it tracks classic auto-merge, not the merge queue). So queue *position/state is not observable via the gh CLI*; classify on `state`/`mergeStateStatus` plus the timeout ceiling (step 2) as the stall/dequeue backstop:
   - **In-queue / processing (keep waiting):** `state == OPEN` AND `mergeStateStatus` NOT in `DIRTY`/`CONFLICTING` (it routinely reads `CLEAN`/`BLOCKED`/`UNKNOWN` while queued — **do not treat that as failure on its own**). A dequeue that leaves the PR `OPEN` is indistinguishable from "still queued" **from the `gh pr view` fields alone**, so the **`BUILD_QUEUE_TIMEOUT` ceiling still bounds this wait** — but a stall is **no longer opaque**: run **`gate.sh diagnose-queue <owner>/<repo> <pr>`** (foundation#1150), which classifies it from the two channels `gh pr view` cannot see — the GraphQL `mergeQueueEntry` membership signal (still-in-queue) + the `merge_group` run history (why it left) — returning `QUEUED` / `MERGE_GROUP_FAILED` / `MERGE_GROUP_INFRA` / `DEQUEUED` / `MERGED` / `QUEUE_STALLED` (still enqueued, but past `BUILD_QUEUE_STALL_AFTER` with **zero** `merge_group` runs ever dispatched for it — a stalled queue, not a slow one, so stop waiting rather than re-polling to the ceiling). `MERGE_GROUP_FAILED` is itself split by `gate.sh` on per-job step data (temperloop#1175): a workflow-defined step (any step after the runner-provided "Set up job" step) itself concluding failure stays `MERGE_GROUP_FAILED`; the run failing before reaching any workflow-defined step — a GitHub Actions infra hiccup, not a real gate failure — reads `MERGE_GROUP_INFRA` instead, so a transient outage never permanently ejects a healthy PR. Use its verdict to route recovery (step 3). **Cadence** (each call spends a GraphQL + REST request — do **not** run it every poll tick): invoke it **at most twice** per PR — once when the in-queue wait first crosses the stall-suspicion point (about half the `BUILD_QUEUE_TIMEOUT` ceiling — so a `MERGE_GROUP_FAILED`/`MERGE_GROUP_INFRA` is caught early rather than after the full wait), and once at the ceiling before step-3 recovery.
   - **Merged (success → 4d):** `state == MERGED` (`mergedAt` set). **Only this triggers the 4d Done-treatment** — preserves the #130 guard (`--auto` only *enqueues*; "added to queue" / "auto-merge enabled" is NOT landed).
   - **Failed (→ recovery):** `state == OPEN` AND `mergeStateStatus == DIRTY`/`CONFLICTING` → a real content conflict → route that one PR to **4c**, as today. OR `state == CLOSED` without `mergedAt` (abandoned/dequeued-and-closed) → recovery (step 3). OR `gate.sh diagnose-queue` returns **`MERGE_GROUP_FAILED`** (a workflow-defined step itself concluded failure — a semantic conflict or a transiently-broken main, with the failed run id) → route straight to **4c** without waiting out the ceiling. OR `gate.sh diagnose-queue` returns **`MERGE_GROUP_INFRA`** (the `merge_group` run concluded failure but never reached a workflow-defined step — a GitHub Actions infra hiccup, e.g. a transient outage resolving action download info, temperloop#1175) → recovery (step 3), which **re-enqueues** rather than ejecting to 4c: the failure carries no signal about the PR's own correctness. OR `gate.sh diagnose-queue` returns **`DEQUEUED`** (left the queue with no failing group run) → recovery (step 3), the same recovery target as a `CLOSED`-without-merge state.
2. **Timeout ceiling — `BUILD_QUEUE_TIMEOUT` (seconds)** per selected PR's time in queue. **Encode the absolute deadline timestamp in the poll's `ScheduleWakeup` `prompt`** (same pattern as the 4b timed-gate window and assess Step 6), so it survives context summarization across the wait. **Headless one-shot runs (`PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`) poll for `MERGED` in the FOREGROUND, not via `ScheduleWakeup` (#626)** — a blocking `gh pr view` poll loop **at a ≥30s interval** (the same floor `ci-poll.sh` enforces in 3g — no reason to poll tighter than the queue lands) bounded by `"$BUILD_HEADLESS_POLL_TIMEOUT"` per Bash call (under the ~10-min cap; chain another foreground poll if still queued and the session budget allows). On `state == MERGED` → 4d in-session. If the bound elapses with the PR still `OPEN`+enqueued (`--auto` armed, not `DIRTY`/`CONFLICTING`), do **not** dequeue and do **not** yield for a wake — leave it enqueued and **stop**: the `funnel-merge-pending` marker (#624) resumes next tick, re-checks, finds `MERGED`, and clears the marker. (The step-3 classify-and-recover flow still applies on a *confirmed* stall/conflict, just driven by the foreground bound rather than a backgrounded wake.)
3. **Recovery on stall** (timeout elapsed, or a confirmed persistent dequeue-without-merge). **Classify first** — `gate.sh diagnose-queue <owner>/<repo> <pr>` (foundation#1150) turns the stall into a structured verdict; branch on it:
   - **`MERGE_GROUP_FAILED`** (the queue's `merge_group` CI concluded failure AND a workflow-defined step itself concluded failure — a real group conflict / broken main, carrying the failed run id) → **dequeue** (`gh pr merge --disable-auto <pr>`) and route straight to **4c**: a `managed-merge` retry would just re-hit the same group failure, so skip it.
   - **`MERGE_GROUP_INFRA`** (the `merge_group` run concluded failure but NEVER REACHED a workflow-defined step — a GitHub Actions infra hiccup during runner setup, temperloop#1175/foundation#1563, not a real gate failure) → **re-enqueue once** via `gate.sh queue <owner>/<repo> <pr>` (idempotent `--auto`) — the same re-arm-once pattern `DEQUEUED` uses just below, never a dequeue-to-4c: the failure carries no signal about the PR's own correctness, so ejecting it to conflict-resolution would send a healthy PR down a path with nothing to resolve. If it fails the same way again next pass, fall through to the managed fallback below.
   - **`DEQUEUED`** (the entry dropped during queue churn — no failing group run) → **re-arm once** via `gate.sh queue <owner>/<repo> <pr>` (idempotent `--auto`); if it stalls again next pass, fall through to the managed fallback below. If that re-arm returns **`DRAFT`** (exit 9 — the PR is a draft, which GitHub will never auto-merge), do **not** retry and do **not** flip it ready yourself: report the named state and its `gh pr ready` remedy, since a draft is always a deliberate human decision (temperloop#1180).
   - **`QUEUED`** (still legitimately enqueued) → it is not actually stalled; keep waiting unless the `BUILD_QUEUE_TIMEOUT` ceiling is genuinely hit, in which case take the managed fallback below. **`QUEUE_STALLED`** (still enqueued, but past `BUILD_QUEUE_STALL_AFTER` with zero `merge_group` runs dispatched — the queue is stuck, not slow) is the opposite call: **stop waiting immediately** instead of burning the rest of that ceiling — **dequeue** (`gh pr merge --disable-auto <pr>`) and take the managed fallback below.
   - **`MERGED`** → it landed after all → treat as success (go to 4d).
   - On an `ERROR`/unclassifiable verdict, or the `QUEUED`-past-ceiling case, fall through to the mechanics that recover any stall regardless of cause:
     1. **Dequeue** the PR — `gh pr merge --disable-auto <pr>` (removes it from the queue / disables the pending auto-merge).
     2. **Fall back to the managed per-PR mechanics** for that PR: `gate.sh managed-merge <owner>/<repo> <pr> --strict` (the scripted successor of the old cell-B `--auto` + `update-branch`-nudge fallback — same behavior: make the branch current, revalidate CI on the updated head, land, confirm `MERGED`).
     3. If the managed fallback also can't land it (real conflict — `CONFLICTING`, exit 3), route to **4c** exactly as today.
4. **Unattended default = classify-and-recover, recorded not silent.** On the `--unattended`/cron arm, the step-3 recovery is taken automatically (never spin forever) AND recorded: append one `### open` entry to the pending-decisions surface (`Pipeline/pending decisions.md` vs the legacy `Context/pipeline - pending decisions.md` — target pinned by the append-target resolution rule, `claude/commands/check-in.md`; in the knowledge store) via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_append` (this is an `ask-at-checkin` auto-disposition — `claude/CLAUDE.md` § Unattended pending-decisions surface — so the next `check-in` reviews it). **The record must name the actual `diagnose-queue` verdict and the branch step 3 took** — the `Default taken:` line is the durable surface `check-in` trusts, so a fixed action would misreport the three branches that don't dequeue-and-fallback:
   ```markdown
   ### <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM> · build Step 4b queue-stall · <host>:<sess8>
   - **Decision:** native merge queue stalled on PR #<N> (<slug>) at level <k> after BUILD_QUEUE_TIMEOUT — `diagnose-queue` → <QUEUED|QUEUE_STALLED|MERGE_GROUP_FAILED|MERGE_GROUP_INFRA|DEQUEUED|MERGED|ERROR>
   - **Default taken:** <the branch actually taken — MERGE_GROUP_FAILED → "dequeued, routed to 4c (merge_group run #<id> failed)"; MERGE_GROUP_INFRA → "re-enqueued via gate.sh queue (merge_group run #<id> never reached a workflow-defined step — GH infra hiccup)"; DEQUEUED → "re-armed via gate.sh queue"; MERGED → "confirmed MERGED, no recovery needed"; QUEUE_STALLED → "queue stalled (enqueued <S>s, 0 merge_group runs) — dequeued (--disable-auto) + managed-merge --strict fallback"; QUEUED-past-ceiling / ERROR → "dequeued (--disable-auto) + managed-merge --strict fallback">
   - **Disposition:** auto-taken (unattended run; no live operator)
   - **Status:** open
   ```
   On an **attended** run, surface the stall as a modal `AskUserQuestion` whose **context names the `diagnose-queue` verdict** (so the operator sees the cause, not just "stalled"), offering the verdict-appropriate options — e.g. on `MERGE_GROUP_FAILED`: route-to-4c / leave-queued / abort; on `MERGE_GROUP_INFRA`: re-enqueue / leave-queued / abort; on `DEQUEUED`: re-arm / dequeue-and-fallback / abort — instead of writing the surface.
5. **MERGED-confirmation gates 4d (load-bearing — #130).** Every arm above gates the 4d Done-move on confirmed `state == MERGED` (`mergedAt` set) — including a dequeue-and-fallback that ultimately lands the PR via the strict-serial path. A still-queued PR stays `[m]` (its item keeps In Progress) until a later pass confirms `MERGED`. **Never** treat "added to queue" or "auto-merge enabled" as landed (the exact #130 bug). <!-- cite: B.42 incident:F#130 -->

---

**Merge — MANAGED backend (`MANAGED` — no native queue; absorbs the old `queue=OFF` cells A/B, temperloop#13):** a serial per-PR loop the **orchestrator owns** — the loop, the plan-item processing order, and the stop/continue policy are orchestrator-side; `gate.sh managed-merge` stays strictly **per-PR mechanics** (a set-loop inside gate.sh would move merge-order policy into the deterministic machinery). Also the **queue-stall fallback** target from "Merge — NATIVE backend" step 3.

1. **Record consent durably FIRST — the gate-log consent line.** The loop's first action, immediately after consent exists (modal approval given, timed window elapsed with no objection, or the headless immediate-merge re-poll passed) and **before the first `managed-merge` call**, is to append one consent line to the plan note's **`## Merge gate log`** section (schema: `~/.claude/plan-schema.md` § Orchestrator-written `## Merge gate log` section) via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_append`: level, ISO-8601 timestamp, consent mode (`modal-approved` | `timed-elapsed` | `headless-immediate`), and the **exact consented PR list**. This line is what makes **resume-without-re-consent** mechanical (state table below): a crash mid-loop resumes the remaining consented PRs without re-asking. Consent pins that exact PR list — a PR outside it (or re-opened under a new number) is NOT covered and takes a fresh gate. <!-- cite: B.43 incident:K#13 -->
2. **The serial loop.** For each consented PR **in plan-item order** (the within-level source order from Step 1.4), run `workflows/scripts/build/gate.sh managed-merge <owner>/<repo> <pr> --strict|--non-strict` — the flag from the 4a `strict` probe (`STRICT` → `--strict`, `NON_STRICT` → `--non-strict`). Per-PR mechanics (as built): `--strict` (the default) runs `gh pr update-branch` → resolves the **new** head SHA → SHA-pinned CI re-poll on that updated head (REST check-runs, never `gh pr checks --watch`) → on green, merges via the Step 0 canonical incantation (`--merge --delete-branch`, **not** `--auto` — mergeability is already established) → polls to **confirmed `state == MERGED`**; a red re-poll **ejects with no merge attempted**. `--non-strict` skips the update-branch + re-poll entirely (cell A's immediate-merge cost profile — no CI-cost regression) and goes straight to merge + confirm. On `--strict`, budget ≈ one CI-length wait per PR after the first (each merge advances `main`; the next PR's update-branch re-runs CI) — the old cell-B serial cadence, now scripted.
3. **Branch on the closed outcome set** (`.outcome` of the one JSON line; never parse prose). Persist each PR's disposition to the plan note **as it resolves** (`[x]` / stays `[m]` / `[-]`), so an interrupted loop reconstructs from sentinels + the consent line:
   - **`MERGED` (exit 0) → 4d.** The success outcome **is** the internal poll-until-`state == MERGED` (`mergedAt` set) confirmation — so the **#130 confirmed-MERGED guard applies verbatim on the managed path**: the 4d Done-treatment keys on this confirmed `MERGED` outcome, never on "the merge call returned" (and never on anything short of `state == MERGED`; a PR closed-without-merge can never read as merged).
   - **`EJECTED` (exit 5 — CI red on the updated head; no merge was attempted; `failed_run_ids` in the payload) → its OWN disposition set, and the loop CONTINUES with the remaining consented PRs** (an eject never halts the level's queue). This is **not** a 4c case — 4c stays real-content-conflicts-only; an eject is a CI failure freshly surfaced by folding `main` into the head. Three dispositions:
     - **Re-spawn worker to fix the CI failure** — re-invoke `Agent` against the item's pre-created `worktreePath` (re-arm the `.build-guard` marker per the 4c re-spawn mechanics if 3h removed it), handing it the `failed_run_ids`; worker fixes + commits, orchestrator re-pushes, re-watches CI (3g), item re-parks `[m]` for the next gate pass.
     - **Skip permanently** — mark `[-]` + reason.
     - **Leave for manual triage (default)** — stays `[m]`; append `- ejected at level-k managed merge: CI red after update-branch (runs <failed_run_ids>)`.
     The disposition is a **`ask-at-gate`** decision: on an attended modal gate ask it inline (the operator is already present); otherwise append one `## Questions` entry (`` `step: 4b` `pr: <N>` — EJECTED PR #<N> (<slug>) disposition? **default: leave for manual triage** ``, with an `auto-proceed:` sub-line) and proceed on the default — non-destructive, exactly like 4c's conflict default.
   - **`MERGE_REJECTED` (exit 6 — the platform itself refused the `gh pr merge` call) → re-probe the backend, then dispose.** The likeliest cause is a **queue-armed repo mis-probed as MANAGED** (a `probe_failed:true` verdict, or a stale `BUILD_MERGE_BACKEND=managed` override) — a queue-required branch rejects a direct merge. Re-run `gate.sh backend <owner>/<repo>`: if it now reads `NATIVE` (or the operator confirms a queue), route the PR and the rest of the consented set through the **NATIVE** path above (the consent line still covers them — same PR list, same level). If it still reads `MANAGED`, the residual disposition is a **`ask-at-gate`** decision, not an `ask-now` halt — leaving the PR `[m]` is a safe, non-destructive default (nothing merges; nothing is lost): on an attended gate ask inline; otherwise append a full `## Questions` entry (`` `step: 4b` `pr: <N>` — MERGE_REJECTED PR #<N> (<slug>): platform refused the merge (<error>). Disposition? **default: leave `[m]` for manual triage** ``, with an `auto-proceed:` sub-line) and proceed on the default; the loop continues with the rest.
   - **`CONFLICTING` (exit 3 — a content conflict against the *current* `main`, or the post-merge confirm poll hit `CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY`) → route that one PR to 4c**, which runs the shared **`rebase-and-retry`** disposition (**4c-retry**) first and only falls through to the per-PR question on a **genuine** content conflict; the loop continues with the rest. A queue-time `CONFLICTING` most often means the base merely *moved* while the PR sat in the gate — that case rebases cleanly and re-lands **within this same run, with no second merge approval** (4c-retry step 4), so it never spends an operator's attention.
   - **`TIMEOUT` (exit 4 — a poll deadline passed) → surface it; the PR stays `[m]`** (keeps In Progress) for a later pass to re-check; the loop continues with the rest. Two poll loops can raise it (temperloop#23): the **SHA-pinned CI re-poll** running out its deadline with checks still pending (`--strict` arm; **no merge attempted** — the head is not yet green), or the **post-merge confirm poll** running out its deadline (the merge call was issued but `state == MERGED` wasn't confirmed in the window — the #130 guard keeps it `[m]`, not merged). Either way nothing is treated as landed. On an unattended run, also record it (`ask-at-gate`): append a `## Questions` entry (`` `step: 4b` `pr: <N>` — managed-merge poll timed out (CI re-poll or confirm poll). **default: leave `[m]` — a later pass re-checks** ``, with an `auto-proceed:` sub-line) so the stall is legible, not just parked.
   - **`ERROR` (exit 1 — a gh/git call inside `managed-merge` failed: the update-branch call, the head-SHA resolve, or the CI re-poll query itself erroring) → surface the error verbatim; never treat it as green.** A CI re-poll that merely runs out its deadline is **not** ERROR — it is `TIMEOUT`/exit 4 above (temperloop#23; ERROR is reserved for a failed call, per gate.sh's header exit-code contract). The PR stays `[m]` (nothing was merged); the loop continues with the rest, and the unattended run records it exactly like `TIMEOUT` above (same `## Questions` shape, default: leave `[m]`).
4. **No queue/resume label — sentinels + consent line + live probe only.** Resume state on the managed path rides the plan-note sentinels (`[m]`/`[~]`), the `## Merge gate log` consent line, and a live `gh pr view` probe — **no new label anywhere** (a `tl:queued`-style PR label was considered and subtracted at design review: dual-state drift between label and plan note). `PIPELINE_MERGE_PENDING_LABEL` (`funnel-merge-pending`, `build.config.sh`) remains the **pipeline-plane** cross-tick marker on the board item (3g/#624) — a different plane, cross-referenced here, never duplicated as a build-plane merge-state label.
5. **Resume-without-re-consent — the pinned state table.** On resume (Step 1.4 `[m]`/`[~]` revalidation, or Step 0.5 reconcile), route each item of a partially-merged level by: **consent line present for this level × sentinel × live PR state**:

   | Consent line covers this PR | Sentinel | Live PR state | Action |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | yes | `[m]` | `MERGED` (`mergedAt` set) | **Mark merged** — flip to `[x]` and run the 4d treatment (the crash happened after this PR landed). |
   | yes | `[m]` | `OPEN`, CI green — or pending/red (a crash mid-`managed-merge`'s own update-branch/re-poll window; a parked `[m]` was green *before* the loop touched it) | **Resume the managed loop** — re-issue `gate.sh managed-merge` for this and the remaining consented PRs, in plan-item order, **without re-asking consent**. Re-issuing is idempotent: it redoes the update-branch + re-poll itself, and a genuinely-red head simply resolves to `EJECTED` and its disposition set. |
   | no (line absent, or PR not in its list) | `[m]` | `OPEN` | **Fresh gate** — no consent was recorded for it; the level's remainder re-enters 4a/4b normally. |
   | yes | `[~]` | any | **Fresh gate after re-park** — the item regressed out of `[m]` post-consent (e.g. an EJECTED re-spawn); it re-earns `[m]` via 3g and takes the *next* gate; the old consent does not carry to a re-pushed head. |
   | any | `[m]`/`[~]` | `CLOSED` without `mergedAt` | **Escalate** — PR closed-unmerged is an `ask-now` halt (re-open / re-run / skip `[-]`), never inferred merged (#130 class). |

**The 4b merge mechanics are unchanged by the timed gate** — the **incantation is exactly Step 0's** (NATIVE uses the smaller `gh pr merge <pr> --auto`; MANAGED merges inside `gate.sh managed-merge` via `gh pr merge <pr> --merge --delete-branch`, with `--strict` folding an update-branch + CI re-poll in front). The timed/modal regime decides only *whether the run blocks for a human first*; once the set is approved (explicitly in the modal path, or by absence-of-objection in the timed path), the merge runs identically. On the MANAGED `--strict` arm, budget ≈ one CI-length wait per PR after the first (each merge advances `main`); the old hand-driven `update-branch` babysitting is now inside `managed-merge`'s own mechanics. The integration-branch **merge train** (4b-train) is **retired** — absorbed by this managed loop (see 4b-train's retirement note); on a NATIVE repo the queue's `merge_group` does the batching itself.

### 4b-train. Integration-branch merge train (RETIRED — absorbed by the MANAGED backend)

**RETIRED (temperloop#13) — do not run.** The train's only live condition (strict `main`, queue absent, ≥3 disjoint PRs) no longer exists as a merge path: on a `NATIVE` repo the queue's `merge_group` trial branch *is* the integration branch, and on a `MANAGED` repo the scripted serial loop (`gate.sh managed-merge`, 4b "Merge — MANAGED backend") is the train's replacement. Rationale for retiring it (one scripted path over a third unscripted merge axis; the ≥3-disjoint-PR serial-CI trade accepted): [[Decisions/foundation - build native merge-queue adoption]] §4. The heading is retained so historical cross-references resolve.

### 4c. Non-mergeable set — rebase-and-retry first, then a per-PR question

This is for **real content conflicts** only — `CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY` (or `UNKNOWN` after re-poll). It is **not** for `BEHIND`: a `MERGEABLE/BEHIND` PR has no conflict, just a stale base, and is resolved in 4b — on the NATIVE backend the queue updates branches itself; on the MANAGED backend `gate.sh managed-merge --strict` folds the `update-branch` + re-CI into its per-PR mechanics — **not** a manual rebase. (Nor is it for a managed **`EJECTED`** — CI red after update-branch is a CI failure with its own disposition set in 4b, not a content conflict.) Only escalate to 4c if a PR develops an actual content conflict (`CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY`) that updating the branch can't clear. <!-- cite: B.44 incident:F#83 --> **But a queue-time `CONFLICTING` is two states wearing one label** (temperloop#1093): GitHub reports it both for a branch whose base merely *moved* while the PR sat in the gate — it replays onto the new tip with no conflicted hunk — and for a genuine content conflict. Only the second deserves an operator; the first is the common queue-time case and is fully mechanical. So **4c never parks on the label**: it runs **4c-retry** first and reaches the per-PR question below **only** on a real conflict (or a non-green re-verify). **4c-retry — the shared `rebase-and-retry` disposition.** ONE implementation with TWO callers — `/build` 4b (both backends' `CONFLICTING` routes) and **`/fix` Step 5** (its `gate.sh poll` / `managed-merge` exit-3 arm), which references this section **by name rather than re-encoding it**, so the two cannot drift. It is composed **entirely from existing machinery** — `pr.sh rebase`, `pr.sh push --force`, `ci-poll.sh --sha`, and the caller's already-probed `gate.sh queue` / `gate.sh managed-merge` — adding no new script, flag, or outcome value. *Precondition:* a worktree sitting on the **PR tip** — a `/build` item reuses its 3b `worktreePath` (re-create per 3b if 3h removed it, so the `.build-guard` marker is re-armed); a caller owning none (`/fix`'s adopt path) makes a disposable one, `worktree.sh create "$repoRoot" <slug>`; either way `git -C <worktreePath> fetch origin && git -C <worktreePath> reset --hard origin/<branch>` first (`<branch>` = `gh pr view <pr> --json headRefName -q .headRefName`), never a stale local ref.

1. **Rebase — `pr.sh rebase <worktreePath>`.** Its own closed outcome set is the branch point; never pre-judge the conflict from the `CONFLICTING` label. **`REBASED`** → the base merely moved and the payload's `.sha` is the replayed tip → step 2. **`REBASE_CONFLICT`** (non-zero exit) → a **genuine content conflict**: `pr.sh` has *already* run `git rebase --abort`, leaving the worktree clean and the worker's commits intact (0.25/#525) — **rely on that abort-and-restore, never re-implement it** or leave a half-rebased tree. Name the conflicting files with a read-only trial merge on the same refs the rebase used (its `base`/`tip` payload) — `git -C <worktreePath> merge-tree --write-tree --name-only origin/<default> <tip>`, whose conflicted-path list prints after the tree oid at exit 1 (the probe 4a's hunk-overlap prober already uses) — and fall through to the per-PR question below **carrying that list**. **A content conflict is never auto-resolved**: that is this disposition's deliberate policy line, not an unfinished edge — the cheap, high-value half is the cleanly-rebasable case, and a machine-resolved hunk is exactly what an operator must see.
2. **Re-push by SHA — `pr.sh push <worktreePath> <branch> --force`.** Its `PUSHED` payload carries the authoritative `.sha` (a `--force` that is a pure fast-forward downgrades to a plain push, `forced:false` — #335). `<branch>` is the **PR's own head ref** (the `gh pr view <pr> --json headRefName` value the precondition already resolved) — **never** the worktree's local `build/<slug>` branch, which is a different ref no PR watches (temperloop#1688). A `PUSH_REJECTED` ends the retry: leave the PR `[m]` and surface the error verbatim. A **`PUSHED_UNWATCHED`** (non-zero) says that mistake was just made — the push landed on an unwatched ref while the PR tracks `pr_head_ref`; re-run step 2 against `pr_head_ref` rather than continuing, since step 3's poll would otherwise be watching a head this push never moved.
3. **Re-verify the NEW head — a `--sha`-pinned `ci-poll.sh`, never an auto-resolved one.** `ci-poll.sh <owner>/<repo> <pr> --sha <the SHA step 2 just pushed>`, with the same bound/foreground arm 3g uses (`--timeout "$BUILD_HEADLESS_POLL_TIMEOUT"` under `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`). The pin is **required, not optional** (#254): after a force-push `pulls/{pr}` can still report the stale pre-push head whose checks are already green, so an unpinned re-poll prints a **false `CI_GREEN`** on un-CI'd code — reuse step 2's SHA, never re-read `head.sha`. **`CI_GREEN`** → step 4. **`CI_FAILED`** → a real failure against the new base, which is a **CI failure, not a conflict**: dispose it exactly as 4b's `EJECTED` set (re-spawn / skip `[-]` / leave `[m]`, default leave). **`TIMEOUT`/`NO_CI`/`ERROR`** → leave `[m]` and surface it; **nothing short of `CI_GREEN` proceeds.**
4. **Re-enqueue WITHIN THE SAME RUN — with NO second merge approval.** Take the caller's already-probed backend (4a's `backend` verdict; `/fix` Step 5's): `NATIVE` → `gate.sh queue <owner>/<repo> <pr>`; `MANAGED` → `gate.sh managed-merge <owner>/<repo> <pr> --strict|--non-strict`. Confirm through the caller's own existing confirm path (4b's poll predicate, `/fix`'s `gate.sh poll`) — **only a confirmed `state == MERGED` reaches 4d**; #130 is untouched. A **second** `CONFLICTING` stops the retry and falls through to the question below: one retry per PR per gate pass, never a loop. **Why no second approval (the merge-autonomy warrant).** The kernel's § Merge autonomy & consent scopes a grant to the run that earned it and forbids reading silence as consent — neither is bent here, because **the merge decision is unchanged**: consent was given to merging *this PR's content*, and a clean rebase replays exactly those commits onto a newer base without touching a hunk, so re-enqueueing decides nothing new. The one thing that did change — the base — is **re-verified mechanically** by step 3's SHA-pinned poll before anything is enqueued, so the green the consent rested on is re-earned rather than assumed, and every genuinely new decision still halts (content conflict → park; `CI_FAILED` → the `EJECTED` set; second `CONFLICTING` → stop). Worked example: temperloop#1080 / PR #1086, hand-resolved in exactly this shape.

For each PR that 4c-retry left as a **genuine content conflict** (`REBASE_CONFLICT` at step 1, or an `UNKNOWN` still unresolved after re-poll), the disposition is a **`ask-at-gate`** decision — **append one `## Questions` entry per conflicting PR** rather than issuing a per-PR interrupting `AskUserQuestion`, so the conflict dispositions fold into the **single batched gate question** (4f) alongside any other deferred entries. Entry: `` `step: 4c` `pr: <N>` — Conflicting PR #<N> (<slug>): rebase-and-retry hit a content conflict in <the files named at step 1>. Disposition? **default: leave open for manual rebase** `` with an `auto-proceed:` sub-line (unanswered → leave the PR open, mark `[m]`, append `- open PR #N awaiting manual merge`; nothing destructive is done). Naming the conflicting files is **required, not decorative** — it is the difference between "this PR conflicts" and something an operator can act on without re-deriving the conflict. The three resolutions an operator may pick at the gate: **leave open for manual rebase** (the default — the PR stays open / `[m]`); **skip permanently** (mark `[-]`); or **re-spawn worker to rebase** — re-invoke `Agent` against the item's pre-created `worktreePath` (its `.build-guard` marker persists with the worktree; if 3h already removed it, re-create it per 3b first — `worktree.sh create` re-drops the marker), instructed to `git fetch origin`, `git reset --hard origin/<item.branch>` (the PR tip), then `git rebase origin/main`, **resolve the conflicts a machine deliberately would not**, commit (do NOT push); the orchestrator then force-pushes the rebased SHA (`git push -f origin "$(git -C <worktreePath> rev-parse HEAD)":refs/heads/<item.branch>`), re-watches CI, and re-checks mergeability, merging inline if newly mergeable. The conservative default is non-destructive — an unanswered conflict at gate resolution never skips or rewrites a PR; it just parks it for a human, exactly as if no one was watching, and 4c-retry does not weaken that: it never runs on a PR whose conflict is real (step 1 hands those straight here), and all it does run is the replay-and-re-verify a human would do by hand.

### 4f. Drain the deferred `## Questions` batch (one batch, before merge consent)

This is where the `ask-at-gate` decisions that were **deferred** during the level (appended to the plan note's `## Questions` section instead of interrupting — Step 2.5 issue creation, Step 2.6 epic creation, Step 0.5 reconcile auto-fix, Step 4c conflict dispositions, Step 4b managed-merge EJECTED dispositions from a *prior* gate pass) are surfaced and resolved as **ONE batch, before merge consent is recorded**. It runs in BOTH gate regimes, off the unanswered set collected in 4a:
