- **Modal regime** (risky set, or `BUILD_MERGE_GATE_WINDOW=0`, or an objection arrived): present every unanswered `## Questions` entry **alongside the merge `decision_sink_ask(...)`** (4b modal path) — the operator answers the deferred decisions and the merge in one sitting. Group the entries into a single question (e.g. one `multiSelect`, or a per-entry option set when ≤4); each entry's stated `default` is the pre-selected/recommended choice.
- **Timed regime** (clean walk-away, window elapsed with no objection): each unanswered entry **takes its `default`** when the window elapses (4b timed path) — no interruption, consistent with the timed gate's absence-of-objection consent. The announce message (4b) lists the deferred entries + their defaults up front, so an attending operator can answer them within the window before they default.

**Resolve each entry** by ticking `[ ]` → `[x]` in the `## Questions` section and appending `→ answered: <choice>` (modal) or `→ default taken: <value>` (timed) via `mcp__obsidian__patch_vault_file`, then performing the entry's deferred action keyed off its `step:` (e.g. `step: 2.5` skip-creation retracts the just-created issues; `step: 4c` `pr:` re-spawns the rebase worker; `step: 0.5` runs the safe auto-fixes). A resolved entry is never re-asked on resume.

**No silent stall — load-bearing.** Every `## Questions` entry carries a `default` (the plan-schema invariant), so an entry unanswered at gate resolution **always** has a value to proceed on — the timed regime takes it on window-elapse, the modal regime pre-selects it. An unattended timed gate therefore drains the whole batch to defaults and proceeds end-to-end; it never hangs waiting on a non-blocking question. (This is precisely why `ask-now` decisions are **excluded** from `## Questions` — they have no safe default, so they must keep their own interrupting halt in 3d/3a/4b-risky, never defer here.)

### 4d. Update plan-note sentinels, move merged items to Done, and close the epic if drained

After the gate completes, **for each item whose PR is confirmed `state == MERGED` (`mergedAt` set) — not merely `--auto`-queued —** patch `$1` (a still-queued/unconfirmed item stays `[m]` + In Progress, handled by the "Left open" arm below). **This applies identically on the queue path:** `gh pr merge <pr> --auto` only *arms auto-merge / adds the PR to the queue*; the 4d Done-treatment fires only on confirmed `state == MERGED`, never on "added to queue" / "auto-merge enabled" (the queue extension of the #130 guard — see the 4b "Merge — NATIVE backend" poll predicate and the callout below; the MANAGED path satisfies the same guard inside `gate.sh managed-merge`, whose `MERGED` outcome is itself the confirmed poll).
- Merged: `[m]` (or `[>]`, for an item consented at 3h.5) → `[x] **<title>** ... — merged in #N (YYYY-MM-DD)`
- Left open: `[m]` stays `[m]`; append `- open PR #N awaiting manual merge`
- Skipped at gate: `[m]` → `[-]` + append `- skipped at level-k merge gate <date>: <reason>`

**Re-print the roster once the writebacks above are done — the level's close-out (temperloop#1310).** 4a's roster answers *what is going into* the gate; this one answers *what came out*, which is the half "a reader who never opens `/workflows` can tell what happened to each item" actually turns on. Same call with `--stage close`, plus `--driver "<what happens next>"` — the next level and its item count, or that the plan is complete — so the operator can stop reading here and still know where the run stands: `"$machineryBinDir/plan.sh" roster "<planFile>" --level <k> --stage close --owner-repo "$ownerRepo" --driver "level 2 (3 items)"`. It renders from the sentinels this step just wrote, so it runs **after** them and before the board Done-write, issue-touch and worktree-convergence steps below.

```
--- Level 1 closed: 4 items · 2 merged · 1 left open · 0 skipped · 1 unchanged ---
[x] level-composition-report  #1310     merged in PR #1801
[x] flaky-ci-detector         #1201     merged in PR #1802
[v] keystone-changelog-spike  #1311     verdict captured — routed #1330
[m] pr-recap-round-trippable  no issue  left open — awaiting manual merge (PR #1798)
# next: level 2 (3 items)
```

**This section is item-scoped and is the SOLE post-merge treatment on both paths.** It runs at the level gate for a `[m]` item — the only arm a default Workflow-path run ever reaches — and, on the conversational path, at 3h.5 the moment an as-you-go `[>]` item confirms `MERGED` — same steps, same confirmed-`MERGED` guard, no duplicate. (An as-you-go merge therefore reaches 4d-epic / 4d-pipeline / 4d-archive / 4d-retro at its own merge; those checks are already idempotent and data-driven, so firing them mid-level rather than at the boundary changes nothing about their verdicts.)

**Post-merge worktree+branch convergence (in-lane, idempotent).** For **each item just confirmed `MERGED`** above, reclaim its worktree + throwaway local branch **now**, in the acting session's OWN lane: `workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh remove <repo-root> <item.slug>` (the same `<repo-root>` resolved in 3b — the plan's own checkout, or the `repo:` cross-repo root — **never** a foreign canonical checkout, per the working-tree-ownership rule). This closes the **post-merge-convergence gap**: cleanup happens deterministically at the moment of merge rather than relying solely on the next session-start sweep. It is **idempotent by construction, not by a new guard** — `worktree.sh` `cmd_remove` already returns `REMOVED` when a worktree or `build/<slug>` branch existed and `NOT_FOUND` when neither did (see 3h step 3 and its own header contract), so this call is a **safe no-op on re-run and safe when the happy-path 3h already removed the worktree** (its `NOT_FOUND` arm *is* the already-gone case — no double-remove error). The two paths converge here: the **`--no-workflow` conversational path** removed the worktree pre-gate at 3h (this step then reads `NOT_FOUND`), while the **default Workflow path** left it intact (the workflow removes no worktrees — see the Step 3 dual-path note), so this is where a workflow-driven item's worktree is actually reclaimed. Left-open and skipped items are untouched — their worktrees, if any, persist for the re-drive/manual path. *(Backstop: the **deploy-mini session-start sweep** (F#653) — which prunes merged locals in every clean-on-main checkout at session start — plus `worktree.sh prune <repo-root>` remain the **crash-path** net for a session that died before reaching this step; this step is the deterministic in-lane convergence, the sweep is the safety net, not the primary mechanism.)*

**Also emit an issue-touch record for each item just confirmed `MERGED`** (foundation #916/#919, the issue-touch-stream — skip when the item carries no `gh_issue:`): `workflows/scripts/emit-issue-touch.sh --repo <owner>/<repo> --issue <item.gh_issue> --kind merge || true`. Telemetry only, same `|| true`-isolated contract as the 3f PR-open emit above, and covered by the same `validate-issue-touch-emit.sh` presence-lint. Left-open and skipped items do **not** get a `merge` touch — only items that reached confirmed `MERGED` in this pass.

**And emit ONE per-item efficiency record for each item just confirmed `MERGED`** (temperloop#943, the item-efficiency stream — the overhead-per-shipped-change metric): `workflows/scripts/emit-item-efficiency.sh --slug <item.slug> --repo <owner>/<repo> [--epic <plan.epic>] [--issue <item.gh_issue>] --pr <N> --level <k> --build-run <WF[,WF...] — the wf_ ids of ALL build-level.mjs Workflow invocations that drove this level, comma-separated: the initial invocation plus every 3d-esc escalation-continuation round's re-invocation> [--design-run <the wf_ id(s) of the /workshop + /assess runs that produced this plan>] [--driver-prep-run <this /build session's own pre-worker workflow run, when it had one>] [--ci-sha <the SHA 3g polled>] [--merge-group-sha <the queue's merge-group SHA, when the repo uses one>] [--gate-wait-ms <ms between 3h and the merge that landed this item — the `[m]` wait to this gate, or the shorter `[>]` wait of a 3h.5 as-you-go merge>] [--end-to-end-ms <ms from the 3a claim to this confirmed merge>] || true`. Telemetry only, same `|| true`-isolated contract as the two emits above; its presence here is asserted by `workflows/scripts/tests/test_item_efficiency.sh` (wired into `scripts/quality-gates.sh`), which is why the flag names matter — **`--build-run` is the load-bearing one**: without it every token phase records `null` and the item contributes an identity-only row. And it takes the **full set**, never a single id — collect the wf id of **every** `build-level.mjs` invocation for the level (the fresh drive **plus each 3d-esc escalation-continuation round's re-invocation**) and pass them comma-separated, per the script's own `--build-run <WF[,WF...]>` usage contract; naming only the initial invocation leaves every continuation round's spend structurally unattributed. Pass only the flags you can state honestly, and **never invent a duration to fill a slot** — an omitted leg records `null` ("nobody measured this"), whereas a fabricated one silently becomes a real number in the very ceremony-cost trend this metric exists to make trustworthy. Token figures are not computed here: the script composes `pipeline-spend-report.sh`, which owns the dedupe-by-requestId and cost-weighting traps. Left-open and skipped items get no record — only confirmed `MERGED`, so the denominator is always "shipped changes".

**Board integration ON — the explicit Done write is REQUIRED** (K#902). **Nothing moves the item to Done on its own — there is no cascade and nowhere to hook one** (`workflows/scripts/board/ISSUES-ONLY-BACKEND.md`), and omitting this write is what left 9 of 9 closures wearing `fnd:status:backlog`. So for **each item just confirmed `MERGED`** that carries a `gh_issue:`, issue the adapter Done write through the helper — `if declare -F board_close_done >/dev/null 2>&1; then board_close_done "$BOARD" <gh_issue>; else board_resolve_item "$BOARD" <gh_issue> >/dev/null && board_set_status "$(board_item_id <gh_issue>)" "$BOARD_OPT_DONE"; fi`. `board_close_done` (temperloop#1217/#1337) strips the residual `fnd:status:*` label and the `fnd:host/session:*` claim stamp — the issue is already closed by `Closes #N`, so no second close fires. **That already-closed state is what makes this the helper's sanctioned repair case, not its misuse case:** its header forbids calling it where a merged PR's `Closes #N` still *owes* the close, which is why this call sits behind the confirmed-`MERGED` gate below and must never be hoisted ahead of it. Routing through the helper also fires the adapter's write-through cache invalidation internally (`_board_cache_dirty_after_write`, `board.sh:769`, fired at `:849`/`:923`), so the guarded `lib/cache.sh` source this step used to carry is no longer needed — leaving that hook inactive is what let a following board read keep serving this issue as open/In-Progress after it had merged (temperloop#1164). The `declare -F` guard is the cross-repo skew fallback. That write stays gated on **confirmed `MERGED`**, never on "the gate completed" — that gating is the whole of what keeps the #130 premature-Done surface closed (see the callout below) — and a non-zero return is **warn-and-continue** naming the issue (same unchecked-return family as the 3a epic stamp, foundation#1220). **`reconcile.sh` is the safety net** (Step 0.5 reconcile flags a merged/`[x]` item still showing In Progress; `--status`/`--labels` flag a closed issue still wearing `fnd:status:*`). Left-open and skipped items keep their current board status (In Progress). <!-- cite: B.45 incident:F#130 -->

> **Never let anything move the card to Done before the PR is confirmed `MERGED`.** On strict `main` the 4b merge is async (`--auto`), and on a native-queue repo it is *also* async via `--auto` (which enqueues the PR; the gate "completing" means the PRs are *enqueued in the `merge_group`*, not landed — the queue merges FIFO minutes later): in both async cases the gate "completing" means the selected PRs are *queued*, not landed — and a Done-move at queue time marks work complete against **unmerged code** (observed in #130: issue #490 was set Done / closed at 00:51 while PR #510 was only queued, and the PR didn't actually merge until 01:37 — a 46-min window where the issue was closed against unmerged code, and would have stayed wrongly closed had auto-merge been cancelled). Since the Done write above is explicit and hand-placed, **its `MERGED` gate is the only thing standing between you and #130 again** — there is no automation whose own timing makes this safe. Never move an item to Done on "the gate completed" rather than confirmed `MERGED`.

**If the plan has `epic:`,** closing a merged child auto-ticks the epic's sub-issue list when linked via the typed sub-issues API; for the task-list fallback (Step 2.6), tick `- [ ] #<child>` → `- [x]` in the epic body and post a one-line `Level k: merged #…` rollup comment on the epic.

#### 4d-epic. Epic auto-close (board ON + plan has `epic:`)

After the per-item Done-moves above, check whether the epic still has open children and close it if not. This is what gives the epic a **death** — and it belongs here because a merge (or a spike's `[v]` close) is the only thing that closes a child.

1. **Count open children — data-driven, not "plan finished."** Route through the adapter (temperloop#1140): in this same Bash call, `source "$BOARD_LIB"` then guarded-source `lib/cache.sh` alongside it (`if [ -f "$(dirname "$BOARD_LIB")/cache.sh" ]; then . "$(dirname "$BOARD_LIB")/cache.sh"; fi` — the `worklist.sh:50-53` guarded form, never `[ -f … ] && .` under `set -e`; shell state does not persist between Bash calls, so an earlier step's sourcing can't be assumed here), then `board_sub_issues "$BOARD" <epic> open | wc -l | tr -d '[:space:]'`. **This read MUST stay LIVE.** `board_sub_issues` carries no cached arm today — it was removed in temperloop#1163 because the cached path answered from a snapshot field that was never populated and silently returned 0 open children for every epic, which is exactly the false-drained signal that armed a wrongful epic-close in temperloop#1030. If a cached arm is ever reintroduced to `board_sub_issues`, this call site is not where to point it without re-litigating that incident. (Sub-issues API unavailable → fall back to the epic-body task-list: count unticked `- [ ] #<n>` lines, or query each listed child's state.)
2. **If the count is 0 — account for activation obligations before closing** (temperloop#317; see `Decisions/temperloop - Activation-completeness contract`). "All children closed" proves the epic's work *merged*, not that it *activated* — the same merged/activated split 3e.6's class-A gate draws for a single item, here applied at the epic. Run both sub-steps against the **pending-activations ledger** (`Pipeline/pending activations.md`, falling back to the legacy `Context/pipeline - pending activations.md` — path fallback convention, `claude/commands/check-in.md`; record grammar per `claude/commands/check-in.md` § Pending-activations ledger) before touching step 3: <!-- cite: B.46 incident:K#317 -->

   a. **Emit-on-close (idempotent).** For each item in **this plan note's** `## Items` list that reached a terminal `[x]`/`[v]` state and carries an `activation:` block with `class: B` or `class: C` — sourced straight off that item's own `activation:` block — append one ledger record per the check-in.md grammar via `mcp__obsidian-builtin__vault_append`. **Class-A obligations are excluded outright**: a class-A block is proven synchronously at 3e.6 and is never ledgered, so it plays no part here. First `Read` the ledger (resolved file path) and skip emitting for any item whose record already exists (the idempotency check — see the heading convention below). Name each new record's heading `<epic>-<item-slug>` — e.g. `### 317-my-item — <one-line what-must-go-live>` — so it is identifiable as belonging to *this* epic (2b relies on that prefix). Populate the record from the item's own `activation:` block: `class:` verbatim; `proof:`, `locus:`, and the class-appropriate `watermark:` (class B) / `soak-until:` (class C) copied verbatim whenever the item's block states them. Plan-schema does not yet require those sub-fields on a B/C block (temperloop#317 machinery is still landing) — when one is missing, state your best inference from the item's `notes:`/`files:`/`repo:` context and prefix it `(inferred)` rather than leaving the field blank, so an under-specified block still produces a reviewable record instead of blocking the emit. Every freshly emitted record starts `status: open`.
   b. **Refuse-close-while-undischarged.** Re-use the ledger read from 2a (or read fresh) and check for **any** `### <epic>-*` heading — this prefix is exactly how "a record for this epic" is identified, whether it was emitted just now in 2a or by a prior run of this or another plan targeting the same epic — whose `status:` still reads `open`. If **any** such record is open, **do not close the epic**: skip step 3 entirely this pass, leave the epic open, and post one flag comment on the epic issue (mirrors the existing sub-issue rollup-comment pattern below) — `gh issue comment <epic> -R <repo> --body "Activation-incomplete: N record(s) still open on the pending-activations ledger — close deferred."` — skipping the comment if the epic's most recent comment already carries that marker, so a resumed run doesn't repost it every pass. Also note it in the Step 6 run summary: `epic #<epic> children drained but N activation record(s) still open — close deferred`. This is a **flag, not a stall**: only `/check-in` and `/tidy`'s env-hygiene probe ever flip a record's `status:` to `discharged` (check-in.md's ledger section), so the epic closes on a later 4d-epic pass — a resumed run, a later plan against the same epic, or the "every merge gate" re-check below — once every `<epic>-*` record reads `discharged`. (4d-pipeline/4d-retro/4d-archive all key off "the epic just transitioned open→closed in this run," so a close deferred here correctly holds them dormant too — no separate change needed there.)
   c. **No-regression fallthrough.** An epic with open-children == 0 and **no** `<epic>-*` activation record at all — no item declared a B/C obligation, or the plan is grandfathered/exempt per plan-schema rule 14 — has nothing for 2a to emit and nothing for 2b to find, so it falls straight through to step 3 exactly as before this section existed. This is the **no-regression invariant**: an epic carrying zero activation records closes on the unchanged open-children==0 path.
   d. **Refuse-close-while-body-acceptance-open** (temperloop#458). "All children closed" also does not prove the epic's **acceptance** ran: acceptance / verification legs written as **prose checkboxes in the epic body** — rather than materialized as their own sub-issues — are invisible to the open-children count and would vanish silently on this close (the ssmobile#328 incident: four hands-on e2e scenarios carried only in the epic body, one merge from disappearing). So before closing, read the epic body — `gh issue view <epic> -R <repo> --json body -q .body` — and count **unchecked** acceptance boxes: `- [ ]` lines that sit under a heading whose text matches `accept` or `verif` (case-insensitive), **excluding** bare child-reference checkboxes (`- [ ] #<n>` or `- [ ] <owner>/<repo>#<n>` — those are tracked by the open-children count, not body prose). If **any** remain, **do not close the epic**: skip step 3 this pass, leave it open, and post one flag comment on the epic issue — `gh issue comment <epic> -R <repo> --body "Acceptance-incomplete: N unchecked acceptance/verification checkbox(es) remain in the epic body — close deferred. Tick them, or break each work-bearing leg out into its own sub-issue, then the epic closes on the next 4d-epic pass. (Deliberate override: close by hand, or re-run with the box(es) ticked.)"` — skipping the comment if the epic's most recent comment already carries the `Acceptance-incomplete:` marker (same anti-repost guard as 2b). Also note it in the Step 6 run summary: `epic #<epic> children drained but N body acceptance checkbox(es) still open — close deferred`. Like 2b this is a **flag, not a stall** and holds 4d-pipeline/4d-retro/4d-archive dormant (no open→closed transition this run); the epic closes on a later pass once every body acceptance box is ticked (or each work-bearing leg has been broken out into its own now-closed sub-issue). The **explicit operator override** is to tick the boxes, or close the epic by hand — never a silent auto-close.
3. **If no `<epic>-*` record is open AND no unchecked body acceptance/verification checkbox remains** (2b and 2d both passed, or were a no-op per 2c): close the epic — **comment first, then land Done**, the same ordering discipline the split-parent close in 2.6 step 3 above (and `/triage` Step 4.8) uses, so a failed comment leaves the epic open rather than closing it with no explanation. Post the rollup **only when the epic is not already closed** (`gh issue view <epic> -R <repo> --json state -q .state` != `CLOSED`, then `gh issue comment <epic> -R <repo> --body "All sub-issues closed; epic complete."` — same idempotency reasoning as 2.6 step 3), then land Done via `if declare -F board_close_done >/dev/null 2>&1; then board_close_done "$BOARD" <epic>; else board_resolve_item "$BOARD" <epic> >/dev/null && board_set_status "$(board_item_id <epic>)" "$BOARD_OPT_DONE"; fi`. `board_close_done` closes the epic *and* strips its `fnd:status:*` label and claim stamp in one idempotent call — a bare `gh issue close` would leave both behind. The adapter owns this close outright (an epic has no PR whose `Closes` is owed), takes no `--comment` by design (hence the separate, state-gated rollup comment), and fires the adapter's cache invalidation internally, so this step's former guarded `lib/cache.sh` source is no longer needed. The `declare -F` guard is the cross-repo skew fallback. A non-zero return is warn-and-continue, with `reconcile.sh` as the safety net (Step 0.5 reconcile flags an open-but-drained or mis-statused epic). **If open children > 0, or 2b/2d deferred the close:** do nothing further (the epic stays open).
4. **Idempotent:** an already-closed epic is a no-op — `board_close_done` closes only when the issue is not already closed, and step 3's rollup comment is state-gated so a re-run cannot repost it; a ledger record already present for an item (2a) is never re-emitted; a `discharged` record never reverts to blocking a later pass.

**Why data-driven, not plan-status-driven** (per [[Decisions/foundation - Triage stage and the logical-technical pipeline split]]):
- A plan may cover only a **subset** of an epic's children (a focused batch). Closing the epic when *this plan* finishes would prematurely close it while other children are still open. Counting open children closes it only when the epic is *actually* done — whether the last child was closed by this run, another run, a triage cull, or by hand.
- Run this check at **every merge gate** after the Done-moves (and once more at end of run), and in the spike kind-fork after a `[v]` close — so the epic closes as soon as its true last child closes, in whichever step that happens. The same repeated-check cadence is what lets a 2b- or 2d-deferred close resolve later without a dedicated re-poll: the next time this step runs and finds open-children == 0 again, it re-reads the ledger (2b) and re-scans the epic body (2d) and closes only if every `<epic>-*` record has since been discharged **and** every body acceptance box is now ticked.

#### 4d-pipeline. Surface the pipeline un-freeze at epic close (foundation #864)

Fires when 4d-epic just transitioned the epic open→closed in *this* run **AND** the plan note carries a `## Pipeline freeze` section recording a freeze this run (or a prior run of this plan) took at Step 1.7 that is **not yet resolved** (no resolution line appended). The freeze's standing obligation is that its un-freeze is **surfaced — never silent** — the moment the interference window closes: <!-- cite: B.47 incident:F#864 -->

- **Operator-present:** `AskUserQuestion` — **Re-enable the pipeline now** (flip the schedule note `enabled: no` → `yes` via full-file `vault_write`, same contract as the Step 1.7 flip) vs **Leave frozen** (the operator re-enables later by hand: schedule note `enabled: yes`, plus re-arming any deploy-host LaunchAgent/cron wake they tore down manually). Append the chosen resolution as one line to the plan's `## Pipeline freeze` section (`vault_append`), so the section reads as resolved.
- **Operator-absent:** do **not** auto re-enable — never resume autonomous spend with no operator present (the same fail-closed philosophy as the schedule gate). Append one `### open` entry to the pending-decisions surface (`Pipeline/pending decisions.md` vs the legacy `Context/pipeline - pending decisions.md` — target pinned by the append-target resolution rule, `claude/commands/check-in.md`; ask-at-checkin format; default taken: **leave frozen**) for `check-in` to review, and append a `left frozen (operator-absent epic close <date>)` line to the plan's `## Pipeline freeze` section.
- **Runs that end with the freeze still in place** — including partial runs where the epic stays open and 4d-pipeline never fires — MUST state it in the Step 6 summary (`pipeline remains FROZEN — frozen by this run at Step 1.7`). The freeze may outlive the run, but never invisibly.

#### 4d-archive. Archive the done plan note into the repo (board ON + plan has `epic:`)

Fires on the **same trigger as 4d-retro** — only when 4d-epic just transitioned the epic open→closed in *this* run (the step-2 close above ran). It may sit just **before or after** 4d-retro; both fire on the identical open→closed transition. Skip entirely when the epic did not close in this run, when board integration is OFF, or when the plan has no `epic:` frontmatter.

This is the **"machines to git"** half of the vault/git boundary (epic #252). While the epic is open the plan note lives **only in the vault** `Plans/<note>.md` as the live, mutable cross-session ledger (status flips, item ticks). At epic close — when the plan has reached `status: done` and the work is sealed — build copies that done plan note into the repo so the **durable record rides git history** instead of depending on the un-versioned vault. The vault copy stays the live copy; the repo copy is an immutable snapshot.

**Mechanism — copy-to-repo-dir via the shared protected-main lander (#408).** The snapshot lands in the repo `Plans-archive/` directory so it rides normal **git history** (diffable, blame-able, survives vault loss) rather than living as a GitHub issue attachment (opaque, un-diffable, lost if the issue is deleted). Run the deterministic-machinery script — do **not** inline a bare `git commit`: <!-- cite: B.48 incident:F#408 -->

   ```bash
   workflows/scripts/build/archive-plan.sh "$PLAN_SRC" "<epic>" "<repo-root>"
   ```

- **`$PLAN_SRC`** — the run's plan note, the vault path `Plans/<note>.md` this run executed (the same `Plans/<date> <project> - <title>` referenced in 4d-retro's Links). It is `status: done` by the time 4d fires (Step 5 set it, or it was already done on a resume).
- **`<epic>`** — the epic number whose close triggered this archive (for the commit message).
- **`<repo-root>`** — the repo build operates on (where `Plans-archive/` lives). The script copies `$PLAN_SRC` to `Plans-archive/$(basename "$PLAN_SRC")` there.

**Why a script, not an inline commit (the #408 fix).** A bare `git commit` to the orchestrator's local `main` can never reach `origin` on a protected branch — since the #330 merge-queue ruleset a direct push is rejected (GH013), the *same* strand #404 fixed for the session-transcript archive. `archive-plan.sh` drives the shared kernel `workflows/scripts/lib/land-on-protected-main.sh`, which lands the snapshot **durably and idempotently**: on a protected `main` it builds the commit in a throwaway worktree off the archive branch's base (never touching the orchestrator's checkout), pushes the stable `chore/plan-archive` branch, opens/adopts one PR, and arms `gh pr merge --auto` so the queue lands it; on an unprotected branch or no remote it commits in place. Re-runs converge (stable branch + diff-against-base short-circuit) — no duplicate snapshot, no empty commit. **The archive branch ACCUMULATES; it is never rebuilt over a pending snapshot (temperloop#1523).** `chore/plan-archive` is force-pushed every run, so basing it unconditionally on `origin/main` destroyed any prior run's snapshot whose PR had not merged yet — CI red, a queue stall, a PR left open — and the run that destroyed it still printed a success-reading line. The kernel now bases the worktree on `origin/chore/plan-archive` whenever that branch carries commits `origin/main` does not, so an un-merged snapshot is **carried forward and extended**, and rebuilds off `origin/main` only when nothing unlanded sits on it. **Never work around a lingering archive PR by deleting or resetting that branch** — it is the only copy of every snapshot on it.

**Handle the one status line the script prints — the four are NOT interchangeable, and only the first is success (never treat any as a failure that aborts the run):**
- `plan-archived: <rev>` — **LANDED.** The snapshot is on the default branch (pushed / no-remote / already on origin). Done.
- `plan-archive-pending: <pr>` — **NOT landed.** `main` is protected; the snapshot is on the `chore/plan-archive` branch behind PR `<pr>`, with auto-merge armed. It lands only when the queue merges that PR. Report it in the run summary as `plan archive PENDING on PR #<pr> — not yet on main`; do **not** record it as archived. Nothing is gated on the merge (unlike a `/tidy` transcript stub there is no stub-deletion coupling), and a later epic-close run is a harmless no-op once it merges — but the run summary must not claim a durability the payload does not have.
- `plan-archive-failed: <why>` — **NOT landed and NOT pending:** the snapshot never reached git, or it reached a PR whose auto-merge could not be armed (so nothing will merge it). Non-fatal to the run, but report `<why>` in the summary as a failure — the vault note is the only copy, and a re-run is what recovers it.
- `plan-archive-skipped: <why>` — the archive was not attempted (no such note, target is not a git repo); log `<why>` and continue.

**Does NOT touch `/tidy`.** This is a mechanical build archive step, **not** a Capture/Backstop extraction rule — it adds no capture/backstop pair, so no `tidy.md` Step-3 backstop is required and none is added.

#### 4d-retro. Mint the per-epic retro tracker (board ON + plan has `epic:`)

**Mint the per-epic retro tracker.** Fires **only when 4d-epic just transitioned the epic open→closed in *this* run** — i.e. this run's merges / `[v]` verdict-captures closed the epic's last open child and the step-2 close above ran. It fires from **BOTH** the Step-4 merge-gate path and the spike kind-fork path — the trigger is identical: 4d-epic just transitioned the epic open→closed in this run. This closes the contract-decomposition loop: every epic build completes **mints** its own retro tracker — a cheap, durable record of the epic's build health, filed at close so the decomposition learnings get a durable home instead of depending on someone remembering to open one.

The tracker is a **mint, not a questionnaire**: it records *what happened* (merge friction + build-health signals) and hands the *judgment* — was the threshold right, did the seam hold — to the overlay `/retro` judge, which later picks up `retro-pending` trackers. The four decomposition-retro questions and the handoff-defect taxonomy that used to live here have **moved out of the kernel** into that judge's sixth axis; the mint deliberately carries no judgment questions. <!-- cite: B.49 incident:K#533 -->

**Gated by `RETRO_MINT_ENABLED`** (`workflows/scripts/build/build.config.sh`, already in scope from Step 0, default on): when it is **off** (`0` / empty), this entire step no-ops — no idempotency probe, no tracker, **zero footprint**. Skip entirely when `RETRO_MINT_ENABLED` is off, when board integration is OFF, or when the plan has no `epic:` frontmatter.

**Does NOT fire for:** an epic that was already closed **before** this run (4d-epic was a no-op); a **partial-coverage** plan whose epic stays open (4d-epic didn't close it — open-children > 0); or a triage-cull / by-hand close that happened **outside** build. The trigger is specifically *build drove this epic to completion in this run*, not "an epic is closed."

**DOES fire for the reconcile-driven close.** When Step 0.5 reconcile auto-fixes a *missed* 4d-epic close (an epic left open with zero open children), that close is still 4d-epic driving the epic open→closed in this run — so 4d-retro **should** run after it; the body-marker idempotency probe (step 1) safely no-ops if the retro was already filed on a prior run.

**Synchronous at epic-close — not deferred to `/tidy`.** Mint the tracker **the moment** 4d-epic closes the epic, in this same step — the mint is the *primary* filing, *not* a `/tidy` extraction rule. Rationale (the architecture-review alternative, rejected): a drain-time extraction would lose the retro if the session ends before the next drain; minting synchronously guarantees the tracker exists the instant the epic dies. `/tidy` still carries a **report-only** backstop for this mint (the registered Capture/Backstop pair — `tidy.md` Step 3 § `Retro mint backstop`), but it is a periodic *net* that only reports a missed mint, never the filing path.

1. **Idempotent — probe before create (body-marker, not title), corpus-rg-first.** Mirror Step 2.5's body-marker pattern (same helper, same resolve-once `$PROBE_LIB` seam — re-source it here if this is a fresh bash block): probe for the unique machine-readable marker line `Retro-for-epic: #<epic>` embedded in the retro issue **body**, so a resumed / re-run build never files a duplicate: `issue_marker_probe "$repo" "Retro-for-epic: #<epic>"` — searches the rendered issue corpus first (zero `gh` calls when fresh), falling back to the identical live `gh issue list -R "$repo" --search "Retro-for-epic: #<epic> in:body" --state all --json number` whenever the corpus is absent or stale-beyond-limit (or `PROBE_LIB` doesn't exist in this checkout — same graceful degrade as 2.5). A match (`jq -r '.[0].number // empty'` off the returned array) → already filed; do nothing. **Probe the body, not the title** — a `#<epic>` token inside an `in:title` search is parsed by GitHub as an issue-reference and can silently return empty even when the issue exists (→ a duplicate retro on every resume); `in:body` is literal text search, not a reference token, and this helper's corpus path never reads title at all (see issue-marker-probe.sh's header on the in:title trap), so the same protection holds on both the corpus and the gh-fallback path.
2. **Gather the mint's signals and decide its labels — no judgment, only what the run observed.** From this run's own record of the epic's build, collect:
   - **Merge friction** — conflicts encountered (count + which PRs) and each conflict's resolution-cost bucket (`trivial-inline` resolved in place / `rebase-respawn` needed a rebase round-trip / `real-rework` a contract or edge had to change); `0/none` if clean. (This is the content the `## Merge friction` body section renders.)
   - **Build-health signals**, each recorded as a `(value, gate-state)` pair — where gate-state is **`passed`** (the gate actually ran this epic and verified this value) or **`did-not-run`** (the gate never executed this run, so a clean value is *unverified*, not confirmed). This clean-verified vs clean-unverified distinction is load-bearing — never render a bare `0` that conflates "the gate ran and saw zero" with "the gate never ran":
     - **CI retries** — total CI-retry count across the epic's PRs; gate `did-not-run` on a spike-only epic (no `code` item ⇒ no PR ⇒ CI never ran).
     - **worker ejections** — count of workers ejected-and-respawned during the build; gate = worker execution (ran iff any item built).
     - **design forks** — count of items that returned a `design-fork` verdict this run.
     - **merge gate** — whether the epic's PRs actually cleared a merge gate (`passed`), or never reached one (`did-not-run`, e.g. a spike-only epic).
   - **State label** — resolve whether an overlay `/retro` judge is installed to pick this tracker up, via the shared `command_declared` predicate (`workflows/scripts/lib/command_declared.sh`; resolve-once like `$PROBE_LIB`: `CMD_DECL_LIB="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2>/dev/null || echo .)/workflows/scripts/lib/command_declared.sh"; [ -f "$CMD_DECL_LIB" ] && source "$CMD_DECL_LIB"`). Then: **`retro-pending`** iff `command_declared retro` is true (a judge exists — the tracker is *pending* judgment); else **`retro-info`** (a **terminal** label — no judge is installed, so nothing will ever pick it up; the tracker stays a durable record only). If `command_declared` can't be sourced in this checkout, treat it as false ⇒ `retro-info`. **Never apply a `spike` label** — the mint is not a spike.
   - **Urgency** — the mint is *past the urgency bar* iff **any** worker ejection (≥1), **any** design fork (≥1), or **CI retries ≥ `RETRO_URGENT_CI_RETRIES`** (`build.config.sh`). Past the bar ⇒ the tracker **also** carries `retro-urgent`, in addition to its state label.
3. **Ensure the tracker's labels exist first, then file exactly one tracker into Backlog via the board `capture` entrypoint.** `capture.sh` passes a non-work-class `--label` value straight through to `gh issue create`, which **aborts with no issue created** if the label doesn't exist on the target repo — and a fresh-history kernel/adopter repo won't have the `retro-*` labels yet, so an un-provisioned mint would fail on every epic close with no tracker and no `Retro-for-epic:` marker, defeating the step-1 idempotency probe (it would find nothing and re-fail identically forever). Provision them idempotently first, using the same helper `capture.sh` uses for its own work-class labels — `_board_issues_ensure_label` is in scope from `$BOARD_LIB`; its inner `gh label create` is `|| true`, so this is a harmless no-op wherever the labels already exist:

   ```bash
   _board_issues_ensure_label "$repo" "retro-pending" "fbca04"
   _board_issues_ensure_label "$repo" "retro-info"    "c5def5"
   _board_issues_ensure_label "$repo" "retro-urgent"  "d93f0b"
   ```

   Then file the tracker (the same adapter front door 2.5/`capture.sh` use — lands in Backlog, no `--milestone`), carrying the state label from step 2:

   ```bash
   "$(dirname "$BOARD_LIB")/../capture.sh" "Process retro: epic #<epic>" \
     --body "<body>" --board "$BOARD" --label "<retro-pending|retro-info>"
   ```

   Then, **only when the mint is past the urgency bar** (step 2), add the urgency label to the issue `capture` just created: `gh issue edit <n> -R "$repo" --add-label retro-urgent` (`<n>` = the number `capture` printed). If that add-label call fails (a transient `gh` error), **do not silently drop the escalation** — the tracker still exists with its state label, but its urgency is lost and the step-1 probe will short-circuit ("already filed") on every later run, so nothing re-applies it: log the failure and fold `retro-urgent add FAILED on #<n>` into the Step 6 summary so a human can re-apply the one label, rather than leaving it invisibly missing. Both labels ride the same tracker.
4. **Tracker body** — the machine-readable marker (**unchanged** — the idempotency probe in step 1 keys on it), a one-line reader gloss carrying the honest state label, the merge-friction and build-health stamps, then the links. **No retro questions, no handoff-defect taxonomy** — the mint records *what happened*; the overlay `/retro` judge asks the decomposition questions later:

   ```markdown
   Retro-for-epic: #<epic>

   Process retro (mint) for epic #<epic> (closed by /build on <date>). State: <retro-pending|retro-info>.
   This is a durable **mint** — the build-health record for this epic, filed at close: it records what
   happened, and the retrospective *judgment* is done later by the overlay `/retro` judge, which picks up
   `retro-pending` trackers. A `retro-info` tracker is **terminal** — no `/retro` judge is installed here
   to pick it up, so it stays a durable record only. Findings accumulate in the decomposition note linked below.

   ## Merge friction
   What merging this epic's PRs actually cost — so the "merge more aggressively" posture is data-driven.
   - **conflicts encountered** — count + which PRs hit a conflict (e.g. `2 — #284, #285`); `0/none` if clean.
   - **resolution cost** — each conflict bucketed `trivial-inline` / `rebase-respawn` / `real-rework`; `0/none` if clean.

   ## Build health
   Per-signal stamp — each signal's **value** and whether its **gate actually ran** (`passed` = the gate ran
   and verified this value; `did-not-run` = the gate never executed, so a clean value is *unverified*, not confirmed).
   - **CI retries** — <count> (<passed | did-not-run — spike-only epic, no PR>)
   - **worker ejections** — <count> (<passed | did-not-run>)
   - **design forks** — <count> (<passed | did-not-run>)
   - **merge gate** — <cleared | did-not-run> (<passed | did-not-run — spike-only epic>)

   Past the urgency bar (any worker ejection or design fork, or CI retries ≥ RETRO_URGENT_CI_RETRIES) ⇒ this
   tracker also carries `retro-urgent`.

   ## Links
   - Epic: #<epic>
   - Plan: [[Plans/<date> <project> - <title>]]
   - Findings accumulate in: [[Decisions/stageFind - Contract-based epic decomposition]] (the canonical, board-agnostic decomposition note)
   ```
   (The `Plans/` path is `$1` — the plan note this run executed; on foundation it is the `foundation`-scoped plan.)
5. Record `(retro tracker #, created | already-existed, state label, urgent?)` for the Step 6 summary.

### 4e-quota. 5-hour quota gate (after each level)

After Step 4 fully completes for this level (all merges done, 4d sentinels + board updated) and **before advancing to the next level**, check the 5-hour usage quota — so a long run pauses at this clean, state-consistent boundary instead of risking being cut off mid-merge in the next level. **Skip when no levels remain** (fall through to Step 5; there is nothing to protect). **Skip when `$quotaGate` is empty** (Step 0 found no gate script — a non-vendoring repo): the optional gate is a no-op, advance normally. <!-- cite: B.50 guard:workflows/scripts/build/quota-gate.sh -->

Run the decision script resolved in Step 0 (its config is already sourced, so the threshold is in scope):
```
"$quotaGate"
```
Branch on the one-line verdict JSON:
- **`{"action":"proceed",…}`** or **`{"action":"unavailable",…}`** → continue to **4e-keystone** (which then falls through to 4e Advance). **`unavailable` fails open** — a missing / stale / unparseable quota signal (or an account with no 5h window) never pauses a run.
- **`{"action":"pause","wait_secs":S,"reset_ts":T,"remaining_pct":N}`** → the remaining 5h quota is below `BUILD_QUOTA_PAUSE_PCT`. **Pause, then auto-resume in-session:**
  1. Record a one-line marker in the plan note's `## Run status` — `paused for 5h quota at <HH:MM>, remaining <N>%, resuming ~<reset HH:MM>` — so the pause reads as intentional, not a dead session.
  2. Launch the wait as a **background** Bash command — `sleep <S>` with `run_in_background: true`. Foreground `sleep` is blocked in the harness; the backgrounded process re-invokes this session when it exits. End the turn announcing the resume time.
  3. **On the wake re-invocation, re-run `quota-gate.sh`.** `proceed`/`unavailable` → continue to 4e-keystone (then Advance). Still `pause` (the window hadn't rolled, or a no-`reset_ts` short re-check) → repeat step 2. Then continue to 4e-keystone.

This gate is **path-agnostic** — it runs at the orchestrator's level boundary under **both** the default Workflow path and the `--no-workflow` conversational path (the orchestrator owns the boundary, and the merge gate, in both). All four settings (`BUILD_QUOTA_PAUSE_PCT` / `_CACHE` / `_WAIT_BUFFER` / `_MAX_AGE`) live in `workflows/scripts/build/build.config.sh`.

### 4e-keystone. Keystone-spike review halt (temperloop#526)

**This gate is path-agnostic** — like 4e-quota, it runs at the orchestrator's level boundary under **both** the default Workflow path and the `--no-workflow` conversational path, and it is **plan-note-driven** (it re-reads `$1` and keys off sentinels, never an in-memory flag — so nothing has to cross the orchestrator↔workflow return boundary). <!-- cite: B.51 incident:K#526 -->

**Before advancing, resolve any keystone-spike halt.** Re-read the plan note. If the level **just processed** contains an item carrying `keystone: true` (§ plan-schema `keystone:`) that is now `[v]`, **and** non-terminal items (`[ ]`/`[~]`/`[m]`) remain elsewhere in the plan, the run **STOPS HERE instead of advancing**: a keystone verdict reshapes downstream contracts, so the operator reviews it before any dependent level builds. This is an `ask-now` gate with **no safe default** — `--unattended` controls the run *start*, never a mid-run gate, so it does **not** bypass this halt (§ Operating principles: "`--unattended` controls the *start*, not the *gates*").

**Resume is self-limiting — no double-halt.** The check keys on the level *just processed this run*. On a resumed RUN 2 the keystone spike is **already `[v]` at run start**, so Step 1.4 excludes its (terminal) level from the level list — that level is never re-processed, the kind-fork never re-runs, and this check never re-fires for it. RUN 2 processes only the dependent levels, whose just-processed set contains no keystone `[v]`, so it advances/completes normally. Re-running `/build` **is** the operator's approval to proceed; there is no separate approval flag to flip. (If the verdict says the plan itself should change, the operator edits/abandons the plan rather than re-running.)

On the halt:
1. **Record the boundary in `## Run scope`** (plan-schema § `## Run scope`) via `vault_append`: `- RUN <n> (spike) · <ISO-ts> · keystone spike \`<slug>\` (level <k>) [v] — HALTED before levels <k+1>–<N>; verdict: <verdict-note wikilink> · review then re-run /build to proceed`.
2. **Leave `status: executing`** (do **not** demote to `done`) so a later run resumes at the next level — the parked-not-finished state. **Skip Step 5 entirely** for this stop (it is guarded on "all items reach terminal state", which is false here). **Leave the epic In Progress with its claim HELD** — do **not** run Step 5's epic park-back: the plan is mid-flight awaiting RUN 2, not finished, so this is the sanctioned "Claim held until Done" state (`claude/CLAUDE.kernel.md` § Task workflow), and RUN 2 resumes under the same claim. (`/tidy`'s stale-claim sweep is the backstop for a keystone halt the operator genuinely abandons.)
3. **Reach the operator, then surface the halt by regime.** This is an `ask-now` halt that parks the run with **no inline ask**, so it is the one such halt that does *not* route through the `decision_sink_ask` seam (there is nothing to answer). It therefore emits the operator-phone ping **directly** at this point — the same reach the seam gates get, via the same helper: run `decision-notify.sh ask-now "<repo> /build halted — keystone-spike review (<slug>) needed before dependent levels build"` and relay its printed line via the harness `PushNotification` tool (self-suppressing when the operator is still at the terminal; foundation#863, § Operating principles). Do this once, in **all** regimes below, before the regime-specific surfacing. Then:
   - **Operator-present:** print the keystone summary — the spike slug, the verdict-note path to review, and the held levels — and stop. No `AskUserQuestion` is needed (the operator's *action* is to review then re-run; there is nothing to answer inline).
   - **Operator-absent (`--unattended` / `PIPELINE_OPERATOR_ABSENT=1`), board ON + plan has `epic:`:** **park and post, never proceed.** Post a `decision`-labelled verdict-review issue on the plan's **epic** (not any item's issue), body naming the keystone spike, the verdict-note wikilink, and the held levels, with the instruction "review the verdict, then re-run `/build <plan>` to build the dependent levels" — assign the operator (same `$ASSIGNEE` resolution as the async-decision backend, § Operating principles), stop the tick. Add the posted issue number to the `## Run scope` line (`· decision issue: #N`). This mirrors the 4b risky-merge async-decision pattern — an `ask-now` decision parks and reports; silence is never consent. **Do not** also write the pending-decisions surface: that surface is `ask-at-checkin`-only (safe-default, auto-proceed), and a no-default `ask-now` block written there would mis-signal its urgency to `/check-in`; the labelled+assigned epic issue is the correct durable tracking surface (as the async-decision backend already is, and it writes no pending-decisions entry either).
   - **Operator-absent, board OFF or no `epic:` (no epic issue to post to):** fall back exactly as 4b does when the epic issue is unavailable — there is no async receptacle, so raise a **loud terminal halt**: the `## Run scope` line (step 1) is the durable record, and the run stops with the keystone summary printed prominently in the Step 6 summary. A later human re-run past the review is still required.
4. **When the run later resumes past this boundary** (RUN 2, the keystone spike already `[v]` at start and this level's dependents about to build), append the matching resume line to `## Run scope`: `- RUN <n+1> (build) · <ISO-ts> · resumed after operator review — building levels <k+1>–<N>`.

If the level just processed contains **no** keystone `[v]` (or no non-terminal work remains), fall through to Advance unchanged.

### 4e. Advance

If more levels remain: loop back to Step 3 with the next level (which now has its `depends-on` deps merged into main, so workers will see them in their base). A non-speculative worker spawned here branches its worktree (3b) from the **freshly-advanced** `origin/<default>` — its base is current by construction, so it skips the 3f step-0.5 currency check.

- **Reattach any in-flight speculative workers (opt-in optimization on).** If the orchestrator pre-spawned eligible items of this next level during *this* level's merge-wait (per "Speculative next-level execution"), those workers already ran 3a–3e against the pre-merge base. Do **not** re-spawn them — instead resume each at the **3f step-0.5 base-currency check**, now that this level has merged and advanced `origin/<default>`: `BASE_CURRENT` items promote straight to push-by-SHA; `BASE_STALE` items rebase-then-reverify (or discard-and-respawn). Items of the next level that were **not** speculation-eligible (a `depends-on` into the just-merged level) spawn fresh here as usual, on the current base.

If no more levels: fall through to Step 5. (Any speculative workers pre-spawned for a level that turns out not to exist were gated by eligibility against a real next level, so this case does not arise — speculation only pre-spawns items that are actually in the plan's level list.)

## Step 5 — Demote plan status

When all items reach terminal state, patch frontmatter:
- All items `[x]` or `[v]` (terminal-complete) → `status: done`
- Any `[m]`, `[>]`, or `[-]` remaining → `status: executing` (next run can resume from the parked / in-flight / skipped items). A `[>]` at this point is an as-you-go merge that had not confirmed `MERGED` when the run ended — Step 1.4's `[>]` resume row picks it up next run; it is never treated as terminal.

**Epic closure is independent of plan status.** It is the data-driven 4d-epic check (open-children == 0), *not* a function of this plan reaching `done`. A plan that covers only some of its epic's children will correctly reach `status: done` while the **epic stays open** (other children remain) — the epic closes later when its real last child closes, via 4d-epic in this or another run. Never close an epic from Step 5.

**Epic park-back (board ON + plan has `epic:`) — the inverse of the run-start claim-stamp (3a-3).** *Closing* a drained epic is 4d's job; *parking* a still-open one is Step 5's, and it is what stops the epic being stranded In Progress under a now-ending run (the "ghost In-Progress epic" — observed on epics left In Progress for days under dead sessions, cleaned up by hand). After the plan-status patch above, if the epic is **still open**: <!-- cite: B.52 incident:F#85 -->
- **Park it iff this run owns it.** Read the epic's `Host/Session` via the adapter (`board_resolve_item "$BOARD" <epic#>`). If the stamp **equals this run's** stamp AND no child is in flight (this run reached terminal state, so none is), **move the epic → `Ready` and clear the stamp**: `eid="$(board_item_id <epic#>)"; board_set_status "$eid" "$BOARD_OPT_READY"; board_stamp "$eid" "$BOARD_FIELD_HOSTSESSION" ""`. **Check both calls' exit codes** — the same unchecked-return gap as the 3a stamp (foundation#1220): if either exits non-zero, **warn loudly naming the epic and the failure** (`WARNING: epic #<epic#> park-back failed: <error>`) and **name it in the Step 6 summary** (the Board line's `park-back:` slot) — a failed park-back is never silent; the epic stays In Progress under this run's stamp until `/tidy`'s stale-claim sweep (the backstop below) or a manual park clears it. This is `park-don't-abandon` applied to the epic — **not a close** (the epic re-enters In Progress + re-stamps when its next child is claimed, by this or a later run).
- **Never clear an epic stamped by a *different live* session** — that is a concurrent run legitimately owning it (the cross-session parallelism contract-scoped decomposition enables), not drift. Only un-own what this run stamped.
- **Backstop:** a stamp a *crashed* run never cleared is swept cross-session by `/tidy`'s stale-claim sweep (foundation #85) — Step 5's park-back only covers the clean end-of-run path.

## Step 6 — Final summary

```
/build — wrote-through [[Plans/<date> <project> - <title>]]
- Levels processed: K
- Merged: M (slugs → PR #s) — of which A merged as-you-go at 3h.5, B at a level merge gate
- Open PRs awaiting manual merge: M (slugs → PR #s)
- As-you-go merges still in flight (`[>]`): M (slugs → PR #s — consented, awaiting confirmed MERGED; resumes next run)
- Skipped: M (slugs → reasons)
- Worktrees remaining: M (paths — should be 0 if all terminal)
- Board (when ON): created I issues (slugs → #s); epic #E (created | adopted-from-triage; linked C children); epic stamp: landed on first claim | **NEVER LANDED — epic unstamped** (last error); park-back: cleared | **FAILED** (error) | n/a (epic closed, not owned by this run, or stamp never landed); moved J merged items → Done; epic closed → Done: yes/no (open children remaining: R); plan archived → `Plans-archive/<basename>` (committed | already-archived | n/a — epic still open); retro tracker: #P (created | already-existed; state `<retro-pending|retro-info>`; urgent? yes/no [+ `retro-urgent add FAILED` if the urgency add-label failed] | n/a — epic still open | n/a — `RETRO_MINT_ENABLED` off)
- Pipeline: no overlap | overlap check inconclusive (no `files:` fields) | frozen at Step 1.7 → re-enabled at 4d-pipeline | frozen at Step 1.7 → **remains FROZEN** (left frozen at 4d-pipeline, or epic still open) | overlap acknowledged — ran unfrozen
- Principles (resolved once at Step 1.8, embedded into every worker prompt by §3c and reused by §3e): kernel (7) ∪ project (<resolved path>: M, phrasing wins on D dupe(s)[, override(s): <name>]) | kernel (7) only — no project § Principles declared | kernel (7) only — project § Principles present but empty/malformed, treated as absent | project (<resolved path>: M) — mode: replace, kernel set discarded | none — project opted out (<resolved path> § Principles: none) — one resolution per distinct `(repo, project)` pair this run; a second pair (a cross-repo item) gets its own bullet in this same form, named by that pair — e.g. a plan whose primary pair is `(temperloop/temperloop)` and one item carries `repo: ssmobile` renders a second line `- Principles (ssmobile/ssmobile): kernel (7) only — no project § Principles declared`
- Discrimination-evidence gaps (temperloop#1319, only when `requireDiscriminationEvidence` was armed this run): G items with a `passed: true` criterion and no proof it can fail (slugs → criteria) | 0 — none this run | n/a — requirement not armed
- Host-config deferrals (temperloop#1182, from each item's `park()`-returned `host_config_deferrals`): D criteria deferred by their worker (slugs → criterion + `host_config`), of which V verified parent-side at §4a ✓, U not confirmed (operator must set), E could not be established | 0 — no host-config criterion this run. **V + U + E MUST equal D** — an unaccounted deferral means a criterion reached merge with nobody having verified it, which is the failure this line exists to make visible.
- Review (§3e, temperloop#1430/#1450/#1970/#1984/#2020, from each item's `park()`-returned `review` field): ran R review(s) (slugs → reviewer names) | skipped S (slugs → reviewer: reason) | routed but NOT run on N item(s) (slugs → `routed_not_run` names — every resolved-then-skipped reviewer, advisory, never a block; the tally merges the 3e pass with any §3g CI-fix re-review, so a reviewer skipped in one round and run in another is named in BOTH `ran` and `routed_not_run` — the skip was real, not a contradiction) | mandatory command-doc rule genuinely degraded on D item(s) (slugs — `mandatory_ok: false`) | n/a — no item this run matched a review axis — **and, rendered as its own trailing clause on this line whenever any item's `review.residual_blocking` is non-empty, never folded into the `ran` count:** `residual_blocking: B item(s) shipped a PR with an UNRESOLVED HIGH` (per item: `<slug> — round <round>/<max_rounds>` from that entry's own `round`/`max_rounds`, plus its PR #) — **that PR's `## Review notes` MUST be read before merging it**: the findings are carried there verbatim, never suppressed, and the §3e convergence bound stopped the re-escalation loop, it did not resolve them; render `residual_blocking: 0 — every reviewed item converged inside the bound` when none did. This clause is **mandatory** precisely because a converged-but-residual item still reports `ran` and `mandatory_ok: true` above — without it that item reads byte-identically to a clean one and the only trace of its unresolved HIGH is buried in one PR body. **And, as a SECOND trailing clause on this same line, rendered on exactly the same terms whenever any item's `review.routing_degraded` is present (temperloop#2020):** `routing_degraded: G item(s) reviewed on a PARTIAL roster` (per item: `<slug> — <the gap's own missing/mismatch/content_mismatch figures>`, plus its PR #) — the `reviewer-routing.tsv` relay did not survive, so that item's **extension-axis and prose-`*.md` routes were never resolved** and whichever reviewer those rows would have named did **not** run; the table-independent routes (the mandatory command-doc rule, `review:`, `kind: architectural`) did, so `ran` and `mandatory_ok: true` above can be fully clean for an item whose `.sh`/`.mjs` diff went unreviewed. Render `routing_degraded: 0 — every reviewed item routed on a complete table` when none did. Same rationale as the clause before it: without it a degraded roster is visible **only** inside one PR body's `skipped — …` line, never at rollup, which is exactly how the six unrun shell reviews of temperloop#1984 went unnoticed.
- Spikes verdict-captured: V (slugs → verdict notes)
- Keystone halt (when a `keystone: true` spike fired this run): HALTED after level k for verdict review — verdict `<note>`; held levels k+1–N; re-run `/build` to build them | n/a (no keystone spike this run) — see `## Run scope`
- Reconciliation (resume only): drift items flagged / fixed
- State-graph (resume only, temperloop#1910 L6): state-graph: skipped — board integration OFF | state-graph: unavailable (<reason>) | state-graph: built <n> nodes, <m> edges, sources <ok> ok/<absent> absent/<error> error/<stale> stale, resume verdict → `<cache-dir>/resume-<run-id>.json`
- Plan status: done | executing
```

## Failure modes

- **Plan note edited mid-run.** Re-read `$1` at the start of each item, not just once.
- **Worktree leaks.** `git worktree list` shows them. The orchestrator-created per-item worktrees live at the deterministic path `<repo>.wt/<item.slug>` on branch `build/<item.slug>`; `workflows/scripts/build/worktree.sh remove <repo-root> <slug>` reclaims one (worktree + branch + marker). 3d/3h do this per item; a crashed run can leave stragglers to sweep with `worktree.sh prune <repo-root>` (removes merged-into-`origin/<default>` worktrees only, skipping dirty ones unless `--force`; the path is re-derivable from each item's slug).
- **Branch already exists.** Handled in 3b — surface and ask user to pick alternative or use existing.
- **CI flake vs real failure.** If logs show no real failure, surface to user before re-spawning the worker.
- **Strict branch protection serializes the whole batch.** When `main` requires up-to-date branches (`required_status_checks.strict: true`), only the *first* PR at the gate merges with a plain `gh pr merge`; every other parked PR flips `MERGEABLE/CLEAN` → `MERGEABLE/BEHIND` and errors `Required status check "<ctx>" is expected (mergePullRequest)`. This is **not** a conflict — even fully disjoint-file PRs serialize this way; it's just slow. On a `NATIVE` repo the queue serializes and updates branches itself; on a `MANAGED` repo the 4a `strict` probe reading `STRICT` selects `gate.sh managed-merge --strict`, whose per-PR mechanics fold the `update-branch` + CI re-poll in before each merge (the scripted successor of the #83 hand-nudge — observed pre-script: a 7-PR level where only PR #1 merged until each was `update-branch`ed and re-CI'd, and a PR that sat `BEHIND` 25 min under `--auto` until nudged). Do **not** misroute `BEHIND` to 4c.
- **Cross-item merge conflict at batch gate.** A genuine `CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY` — distinct from the `BEHIND` case above — happens when items in a level touch the same file *and the hunks overlap* (git's 3-way merge auto-resolves disjoint hunks, so same-file ≠ conflict). Mitigation: (a) the plan's `## Sequencing notes` should flag likely collisions; (b) Step 4c handles `CONFLICTING` PRs explicitly (leave-open / skip / rebase); also an `update-branch` in 4b that surfaces a conflict escalates there. Authors who want zero risk should encode the ordering as `depends-on` so the items land in separate levels.
- **`mergeable: UNKNOWN` doesn't resolve.** Re-poll once after ~3s in 4a; if still unknown, treat as `CONFLICTING` and route through 4c.
- **`mergeStateStatus` reads `BEHIND` on an up-to-date branch.** GitHub lags right after a CI pass — a PR whose parent already is `main` HEAD can momentarily show `BEHIND`. Under the NATIVE `--auto` queue a *transient* stale read self-corrects once checks are green. A `BEHIND` that **persists** past the prior merge, though, is the real-stale-base case from #83 — on the MANAGED backend it is cleared mechanically by `gate.sh managed-merge --strict`'s built-in `update-branch` (or, one-off, `gate.sh nudge`) rather than hand-polling indefinitely.
- **User aborts mid-batch-gate.** Selected-but-not-yet-merged PRs stay `[m]`; non-selected stay `[m]`; the plan demotes to `status: executing` so the next session resumes at Step 1 with the parked set surfaced.
- **Worker can't satisfy an acceptance bullet.** Workers must return `done` only when all bullets pass. If genuinely unsatisfiable, worker should return `blocked` with the bullet as a question, not lie.
- **Worker ignores return contract.** No JSON block → treat as `failed: "no structured return"`. User decides whether to retry with a stricter prompt.
- **Board claim fails or is contended.** A non-zero claim (the `$CLAIM` entrypoint — `gh` unauthenticated, issue not on the board, network) or a pre-check showing the issue already In Progress under a *different* `Host/Session` **halts that item** (retry/skip/abort) — build never silently works an unclaimed or someone-else's item. Fix an auth failure with `gh auth login`; the default `repo` scope is all a claim ever needs.
- **Issue creation must not double-run on resume.** Step 2.5 creates issues only for items *missing* `gh_issue:` and writes the number back into the plan note immediately, so a resumed run sees the existing `gh_issue:` and creates nothing twice. If a created issue never made it onto the board, the 3a claim fails (halt) rather than proceeding untracked.
- **`release.sh` is marker-only as `/build` calls it, and the claim is held until Done.** It clears the tmux marker, not the board column — and `/build` passes no `--board`, so its board-side parked-claim-stamp clear (temperloop#979: issues-only board + not In Progress + this session's own stamp) never engages here, and would refuse anyway on an In-Progress item. Parked `[m]` items legitimately stay In Progress until the merge gate moves merged ones to Done (4d); items left open at the gate stay In Progress by design (work isn't finished). The board only ever leaves In Progress via 4d (merge → Done) or a deliberate skip. This is the **claim-until-Done** sanctioned flow (K#275): the marker clear at 3h step 2 is optional and best-effort, so a `release.sh` **refusal on a non-latest claim in a multi-claim window** (`this window holds a claim for #B, not #A`) is expected — the earlier claim stays held and is cleared by 4d's Done write / `reconcile.sh`, never by forcing a per-window marker that points elsewhere. Never fail a park on a release refusal.
- **Epic doesn't close though all children merged.** The 4d-epic close reads the live open-children count via the sub-issues API. If that API is unavailable, fall back to the epic-body task-list (all `- [x]`) as the close signal; if even that is ambiguous, leave the epic open and flag it in the Step 6 summary for a manual close. **Never close an epic while any child is still open** — that is the failure the data-driven count exists to prevent.
- **Split member must not share one issue across its items (#73).** When `/assess` splits one coarse sub-issue #N into several items, every item must end up with its **own** `gh_issue:` — never a copied `gh_issue: #N` on all of them. The mechanism: `/assess` leaves `gh_issue:` unset + stamps `split_from: #N`; Step 2.5 mints a fresh issue per split item (1:1 restored); Step 2.6 step 3 closes the coarse #N once all its leaves are minted. If items ever share a `gh_issue:`, the first PR's `Closes #N` (3f) closes the issue while the rest of the chain is unmerged and the board moves #N to Done prematurely — the exact failure this guards against (observed on stageFind epic #247: #258→3 items, #262→2, hand-worked around by minting #440-444).
- **Partial-coverage plan must not close the epic early.** A plan covering only a subset of an epic's children reaches `status: done` with the epic still open by design. The 4d-epic check (open-children == 0, not "plan done") is what prevents a premature close; Step 5 never closes the epic. Step 0.5 reconciles the inverse drift (an epic left open after its true last child closed out-of-band).
